Resume is restart-from-0 by deliberate MVP policy (per-chunk addrs make resume-from-K a future increment). The fetch preview advertises its keys (space/a/arrows/d/Enter/Esc) on a hint line.
- [x] ISC-C77: Key classes are non-substitutable at the type level. The AEAD seal-key types `PublicRoomKey` and `CircleKey` are distinct (both `AeadKey256`); the tier-split seal functions accept only their own key type, so sealing a circle payload under the public room key (or a public payload under a circle key) is a compile-time error. Uniform sealing of shares and chat can never silently cross the public / circle tier boundary.
- [x] ISC-C78: Client-side unread indicator. A room (a circle or the Lobby) that is not the active room shows an unread dot on its rail row when a chat message arrives there from another sender; the dot clears the moment that room gains focus, and a sender's own echoes never raise it. It is derived entirely from the inbound message stream (`NetEvent::Message` / `NetEvent::CircleMessage` → `GuiState::push_message`, focus-gated on `active`), so the relay holds no read/unread state (ISC-A-S2 preserved) and it is purely client-local (same affordance family as @-mention highlight and mute). Chat-only — share discovery does not raise it. (#64)
- [x] ISC-C79: The client detects a DEAD live connection and tears it down. A subscribe stream's exit no longer tears down directly (revised by ISC-C87, #80); instead the actor re-subscribes that stream, and a **failed** re-subscribe (the connection is genuinely gone) — or a flapping stream (repeated rapid re-subscribes) — drives `handle_disconnected`, which emits a `Disconnected` event and clears its live state (session, server-id, identity, joined room, joined circles, served shares) so no stale half-open session is reused. The half-open socket is surfaced by the application channel's h2 keepalive (`http2_keep_alive_interval` + `keep_alive_timeout` + `keep_alive_while_idle`); teardown stays on the actor's `&mut self` and is idempotent, so sibling readers of one dead connection collapse to a single teardown. (#72, #80)
- [x] ISC-C80: The client auto-reconnects after a drop. While disconnected and holding a prior successful connect plan, the actor re-issues the connect on a capped exponential backoff timer (never a busy-loop), carrying the persisted display handle, `rejoin_circles`, and `republish_roots` so a recovered connection silently restores circles and shares exactly like a fresh launch. A successful reconnect resets the backoff; a failed attempt schedules the next with a longer delay until the connection returns. (#71)
- [x] ISC-C81: A second client on the same resolved profile root refuses to start. Startup takes an advisory PID lockfile in the resolved profile root (after `profile::resolve`); a second instance resolving the SAME root finds a live holder and exits rather than opening the single-writer storage layer (redb + seeds + CAS) concurrently, while a `--portable` instance resolving a DIFFERENT root is unaffected. A stale lockfile left by a crashed holder is reclaimed by a PID-liveness check. Refuse-to-start only; focusing the existing window (cross-process IPC) is out of scope. (#60)
- [x] ISC-C82: An unlocked identity can be renamed — a new display name re-seals the at-rest blob (M13 write-through under the cached key), so the chosen name persists across the next unlock; a profile created nameless before #65 can likewise be given a name (the recovery path). Invalid names (`is_valid_display_name`: empty, line-break, over-length) are rejected, leaving the prior name intact. The cryptographic identity (the handle hash) is unchanged — only the presented name. The command-palette entry that drives this and the live-wire-handle update are felt-deferred. (#66)
- [x] ISC-C83: A connected member emits an AES-256-GCM-sealed, ML-DSA-87-self-signed `MemberHeartbeat` into each subscribed room — the lobby under its `PublicRoomKey`, each joined circle under its `cot_key` — on a jittered ~10–15 s interval, over the existing `CircleOfTrust.Subscribe` stream. It is the member-plane presence beacon (`daemonseed_core::heartbeat`), the counterpart to the relay-plane subscription-refcount presence (ISC-S20): the beacon carries the member's self-asserted presence inside the seal, where only room members can read it. Surfacing the resulting roster is deferred to #75 (lobby) / #77 (circle). (#74)
- [x] ISC-C84: A heartbeat frame is wire-shape-identical to every other sealed room frame — the same `CotFrame` envelope (`nonce ‖ ciphertext ‖ tag`) posted at the room's existing rendezvous address — separated only by a distinct AEAD AAD (`daemonseed/presence/heartbeat/v1`) and provenance domain. It adds no new relay-distinguishable flow (ISC-A-S2 traffic-shape), and the domain separation means a heartbeat can never be opened as — or substituted from — a chat message, a public-room message, a share announcement, or a roll-call under a coincidentally-equal key. (#74)
- [x] ISC-C85: A receiver folds verified heartbeats into a per-room `PresenceTracker` (`daemonseed_core::presence`) keyed by sender pubkey and reaps a member after ≥3 consecutive missed intervals — TTL = interval × miss-count, measured from local monotonic receive time, never the advisory wall-clock. The interval (presence resolution) and the miss-count (liveness window) are separate knobs, biased to forgiveness so a brief wobble of ≤2 missed beacons never reaps a still-live member while a genuine departure clears within the TTL; a stale (older-timestamped) beacon never moves a member's clock backwards. (#74)
- [x] ISC-C86: Share liveness rides the presence heartbeat (#76). A sharer's `MemberHeartbeat` (ISC-C83) carries a `live_share_ids` digest of the shares it is currently serving into that room, bound into the beacon's provenance signature. A receiver refreshes the receive-time of that sharer's catalog shares present in the digest and removes any of that sharer's shares absent from it (a drop without an explicit withdraw — self-healing), and prunes all of a sharer's shares when its heartbeat lapses past the presence TTL (ISC-C85). Consequently a full `ShareAnnouncement` fires only on change (publish/unpublish) plus the startup/manual-refresh roll-call — the periodic re-announce is retired. This revises ISC-S30's two-speed model: ongoing liveness rides the heartbeat digest, not a jittered reconcile-timer roll-call. (#76)
- [x] ISC-C87: A graceful per-stream end-of-stream on a LIVE connection re-subscribes that one stream and leaves the rest of the session intact. Because a half-open connection's GOAWAY ALSO surfaces as `Ok(None)`/end-of-stream, the reader cannot tell a graceful per-stream EOS from a dead connection by the result code — so each stream-exit posts a `ResubscribeRoom` / `ResubscribeCircle` and the re-subscribe ATTEMPT is the active probe: it succeeds only while the authenticated connection is alive (the tonic channel rides one already-opened stream — `AppSession::open`, no transparent re-dial). On success the lobby or that single circle is re-established without disturbing the other streams; on failure the actor falls through to teardown + reconnect (ISC-C79). A per-stream flap guard escalates repeated rapid re-subscribes to a teardown so a flapping link converges to backoff reconnect rather than hot-looping. (#80)
- [x] ISC-C88: The GUI surfaces the live Lobby roster (#75). The GUI net actor emits and ingests the member-plane heartbeat (ISC-C83/C84/C85) into/from the lobby exactly as the TUI does — a per-lobby `PresenceTracker` rebuilt fresh on each (re)join, a single self-scheduling emit timer, and the replay-freshness gate (ISC-A-C41) at ingest. A persistent right-hand people column in the Lobby Chat view renders the set of currently-present OTHER members from that tracker: a row appears on a member's heartbeat and vanishes when the member is reaped past the TTL — appearing/disappearing is the only indication (the Demonsaw roster model). A row shows the member's display name; the `#12hex` fingerprint (the ISC-C4 binding to the verified pubkey) is revealed on hover for on-demand verification, never inline. The roster is keyed by pubkey, so two members presenting an identical display name render as two distinct rows. Circle presence is #77. (#75)
- [x] ISC-C89: A whitelisted signer authors public-space content client-side, symmetric with the read/verify helpers (`daemonseed_cli::public_space`): `sign_post` builds + signs a `PostPayload`, `sign_motd` enforces the shared ISC-S9 single-line-plaintext rule (`motd_text_is_valid`) before signing a `MotdPayload`, and `sign_post_delete` signs a `PostDeletePayload` over a target content address. Each produces a `SignedArtifact` carrying the local identity's ML-DSA-87 signature + full pubkey over the verbatim `signed_payload` (never re-encoded, D-M6-7), so the result re-verifies against the relay's published signer whitelist via `verify_served_post` / `verify_artifact` — the authoring counterpart to verify-and-serve (ISC-S7/S9/A-S3). (#90)
- [x] ISC-C90: Signer self-determination — `local_key_is_whitelisted` is true iff the local pubkey is authorized by the relay's *published* whitelist (`GetSignerWhitelist`, via `whitelist_from_wire` + `Whitelist::authorizes`, matching a full-key or `name#hash` handle entry per ISC-12). This is the cryptographic predicate that gates the in-client authoring composer (#92) — no separate admin login; a non-member key gets a read-only view. Whitelist membership itself stays operator-only / out-of-band (ISC-S8 / ISC-A-S4); this only *reads* the published list. (#90)
- [ ] ISC-C91: The GUI renders the connected relay's announcements + MOTD in a dedicated display pane (port of the TUI panes, #91). The GUI net actor fetches the public space (`GetSignerWhitelist` + `GetMotd` + `ListPosts`) over the authenticated connection and runs the testable data-prep `daemonseed_gui::state::build_announcements_view`, which re-verifies client-side trusting nothing the relay asserts (ISC-A-S3): each post is checked with `verify_served_post` and **dropped** if it fails; the MOTD is checked with `verify_served_motd` (its whitelist carries the connected relay's server pubkey so a server-signed MOTD verifies, ISC-26) and shown only if it passes. The MOTD renders verbatim via `render_motd` and the MOTD area hides when unset (ISC-S9 parity); each announcement renders topic/body/timestamp via `post_render_fields`. The pane is reachable via the Ctrl-K command palette (and a tab); display-only — the signer composer is #92 and the unread-gated auto-landing is #93. (#91)
- [ ] ISC-C92: A whitelisted signer gets an in-client MOTD/announcement composer; the composer is shown iff the local **stable** identity key is on the relay's *published* whitelist (`daemonseed_cli::public_space::composer_visible`, fail-closed on a malformed/empty whitelist — the boolean both the GUI and TUI gate on, D3). Composing signs with the persistent identity key (`derive_identity_keys(seeds.mnemonic, Identity::Primary).signing` — the key behind the whitelisted `name#hash` handle, NOT the ephemeral connection key) via `sign_post` / `sign_motd` and uploads via `UploadPost` / `UploadMotd` (#89); the MOTD composer enforces the ISC-S9 single-line-plaintext rule before signing and surfaces the rejection rather than dropping. A non-signer key gets a read-only pane (no composer); whitelist membership stays operator-only / out-of-band (ISC-S8 / ISC-A-S4). (#92)
- [ ] ISC-C93: On connect the client computes a single combined hash of the relay's *verified* MOTD + announcements (`daemonseed_gui::state::combined_content_hash` — a deterministic, server-order-independent SHA-384 over the MOTD and the posts in canonical `(topic, sent_unix_ms, body)` order) and compares it to a per-relay last-seen hash held in the profile's `Seeds`, keyed by `server_id` (the additive `announce-seen <server_id> <hex_hash>` directive; `Seeds::announce_seen` / `set_announce_seen`). A mismatch — or no stored hash (a first arrival) — auto-opens the announcements/MOTD pane (`landing_decision` → `Landing::Announcements`, active-tab 3); an exact match opens the Lobby (`Landing::Lobby`). The auto-land fires only on the connect-time fetch (`NetEvent::PublicSpaceSnapshot { connect_time }`), never on a manual Refresh / poll / post-upload refresh. Viewing the pane updates the stored hash + re-seals (`persist_announce_seen`), so a returning user who saw the old version and a brand-new user alike are shown the pane while an already-seen user is bypassed. The pane stays Ctrl-K-reachable regardless. Client-derived, no new server state / RPC; a deletion flips the hash → mismatch (D5). (#93)
- [ ] ISC-C94: The GUI net actor emits a sealed `MemberHeartbeat` into each joined circle (sealed under that circle's `cot_key` via `seal_circle_heartbeat`, self-signed for provenance ISC-C57, on the single shared emit timer) and folds verified per-circle beacons into a per-circle `PresenceTracker` (one tracker per `CircleSub`, rebuilt fresh on join, reaped on the heartbeat tick — the timer is the reap clock). The circle inbound reader opens an inbound frame under that circle's key (`open_heartbeat`) and routes it by the GUI `circle_id`; `handle_apply_heartbeat` applies to exactly that circle's tracker (the lobby routes by room name). Presence is members-only and relay-blind (ISC-A-S2 — the relay forwards only the opaque sealed beacon and holds no circle key), the own-beacon is self-filtered per-circle (a member never lists itself), and the replay-freshness gate (ISC-A-C41) holds. A roster-changing apply or a reap that changed a circle's tracker emits a room-scoped `NetEvent::Roster { circle_id: Some(..) }`; the binary feeds the ACTIVE room's roster into the existing right-hand people column, so a circle's roster shows when that circle is active and the lobby's when the lobby is. Ports the TUI's per-circle presence into the GUI net actor; the visible circle-roster render is felt-test-gated. (#77)
- [ ] ISC-C95: An unlocked user can rename their identity from the Ctrl-K command palette ("Rename identity"). The overlay prefills with the current display name (blank for a profile created nameless before #65 — the recovery path); on confirm the name is validated (`is_valid_display_name`: empty / `#` / over-length / control chars rejected, leaving the prior name and the overlay's inline error visible), then `GuiState::rename_identity` re-seals it on the at-rest blob (ISC-C82 core). On success the presented identity updates LIVE without a reload: the rail identity line (`my-handle`) is refreshed in place and the net actor's `my_handle` is updated via `NetCommand::SetMyHandle` so subsequent local echoes, heartbeats, and `mine` detection present the new name without a reconnect. The cryptographic identity (the handle hash) is unchanged. The visible palette flow is felt-test-gated. (#66)
- [ ] ISC-C96: The circle-detail sheet shows three name vectors for the active circle, with copy that distinguishes them: `Name` (the chosen display name, `circle.name`); the relay-scoped adj-noun label (`GuiState::circle_deterministic_label_of` — `default_circle_label` once the rendezvous is known, the fingerprint before), captioned to make plain it matches only between members on the SAME relay (rendezvous = `SHA-384(cot_key ‖ server_id)`); and the relay-INDEPENDENT `#<12hex>` fingerprint (`GuiState::circle_fingerprint_of`), captioned as the universal out-of-band verification vector. The label and the fingerprint diverge once connected; pre-join (no rendezvous) the label falls back to the fingerprint. All three are blank for the Lobby (no net contract), which hides the vector rows. The values are display-only (`main::apply_circle_detail`, set over the existing ISC-C62 accessors — no new data path). The visible 3-vector sheet render is felt-test-gated. (#36)
- [ ] ISC-C97: Every primary text-entry field in the GUI exposes a right-click context menu offering Cut / Copy / Paste / Select all — the chat composer, the Join-phrase input, the publish/share-name input, the rename-identity input, the new-circle phrase input, the announcement MOTD/topic/body composers, and the first-start/unlock passphrase, recovery-word, and display-name fields. The menu items invoke the field's OWN built-in `TextInput` clipboard functions (`cut`/`copy`/`paste`/`select-all`) via a single reusable `MenuTextInput` wrapper (a `Rectangle` containing a builtin `ContextMenuArea` over a `TextInput`, mirroring Slint's `LineEditBase`); Cut/Copy use Slint's backend (winit) clipboard, which retains X11 selection ownership correctly (no transient `arboard` write that would drop ownership on drop). The Join field's Paste routes via `custom-paste` to the existing `paste-into-join` (`read_clipboard_text`) path, preserving its clipboard-read + strength-refresh + refocus behavior (#63 unregressed). The menu is a declarative Slint affordance with no new Rust logic to unit-test, so it carries no registered automated coverage; the visible right-click menu and its clipboard behavior are felt-test-gated. (#67)
- [ ] ISC-C98: A share named in the publish overlay persists its name and auto-republishes under it. The publish path resolves a blank name field to the folder basename; the GUI write-through (`GuiState::persist_published` → `Profile::persist_published`) persists the user's CUSTOM name as `Some(name)` and the basename default as `None` into the at-rest blob's `PublishedShare { root, name }` slot (`daemonseed_core::storage::seeds`), keyed on the root so a re-publish never clobbers a stored name. On the next launch the connect-time auto-republish reads the persisted name (`republish_name`) — re-asserting a named share under its chosen name and an un-named share under the folder basename — so a fetching peer sees the chosen name. The persistence (the seal/open name round-trip + keyed idempotency) is covered by `published_share_name_persists_across_seal_open` and the GUI write-through unit test `state::tests::profile_round_trips_published_shares_across_reload`; the visible peer-sees-the-name round-trip is felt-test-gated. (#41)

### Client — anti-criteria (ISC-A-C*)

- [ ] ISC-A-C29: A chat line received on one surface must never render in another surface's pane (ISC-C61). The split view's per-pane filter is keyed strictly on the line's `surface` tag; a circle's plaintext must not appear in the lobby pane, nor in any other circle's pane. Cross-surface bleed in the transcript is forbidden.
- [ ] ISC-A-C30: Joining or cycling circles (ISC-C59/C60) must not mix `cot_key`s or sender attribution across circles. A composed post is sealed under exactly the active circle's `cot_key` (ISC-C8 / `seal_message`), and an inbound `CotFrame` is attributed to the single circle whose `cot_key` opened it — never guessed, never broadcast to all panes. Posting to the wrong circle, or attributing a message to a circle other than the one that decrypted it, is forbidden.
- [ ] ISC-A-C31: A share fetch must never persist or surface unverified or falsified content. Chunk bytes reach disk only after SHA-384 content-address verification (ISC-S28), and only inside the fetch's reserved staging area, which no manifest path can name (ISC-A-C32 extension); a file appears under its real name only by promotion after every one of its chunks has verified against the user-confirmed manifest; the downloads browse manifest records only promoted files of fully-completed fetches. An integrity failure (ISC-A-S20) destroys all of the fetch's staged partials, aborts the share's fetch, flags the share against automatic re-fetch, and is never resumed past. A transient-transport failure retains verified units (promoted files and staged verified chunks) for resume; a resume is bound to the stored user-confirmed manifest, re-verifies every retained byte against its content address before reuse, and re-fetches only what is missing. The staging area is part of the panic-erasure surface. A persistence failure after verification surfaces as a fetch error rather than a silent partial success.
- [ ] ISC-A-C32: Writing a fetched download (ISC-C63) must never write a file outside its share folder, and the share folder must never escape the downloads root. Every manifest `rel_path` is validated to be strictly relative with only normal components — an absolute path, a `..` component, or a root/prefix component is refused — and the per-share folder name (derived from the untrusted share name) is reduced to a single safe path component (separators/control chars neutralised, `.`/`..` rejected). So a hostile sharer's name or paths cannot escape the downloads root via traversal.
- [x] ISC-A-C33: No blind download (A1, paired with ISC-C66). A fetch must never request or write any chunk before the user has seen the manifest preview and confirmed. The preview path (`net::handle_fetch_share`) pulls only the manifest and drops the stream, so a fetch that is previewed and then cancelled (`Esc`) writes nothing to disk and opens no download. The sole chunk-pulling path is `handle_confirm_fetch`, reachable only via the user's `Enter`-driven `ConfirmFetch` — there is no auto-download path that bypasses the preview.
- [x] ISC-A-C34: No duplicate publish (M16 smoke fix, paired with ISC-C69). A second publish of an already-published share must never create a second relay listing. Client primary guard: the published list is keyed by the client-local defined root (`daemonseed_tui::app::PublishedShare`), and `[p]` on a root that is already published — or still queued in-flight — is a status-hint no-op (`[u]` first to re-publish); a failed publish never reaches `published`, so the guard stays open and `[p]` stays retryable. Relay backstop: `SharePublishRegistry::publish` REFUSES (never replaces) a second live listing for the same `(owner, name)` — replace semantics would silently drop a distinct share that merely reuses a display name. The key is per-connection, so two daemons can share a display name. The defined root never rides the wire: `NetEvent::PublishStarted` echoes it client-side only, and the `●` marker binds per defined row by root (never by name join), so a duplicate can never be invisible to its publisher again.
- [x] ISC-A-C35: Anti: No publish- or serve-path operation may park the net actor's single-threaded runtime or hold an entire share's bytes in RAM. The hash pass and every per-chunk disk read run on blocking threads; the actor loop keeps draining commands throughout (the define-path ISC-A-C7 discipline, extended to publish/serve). The server performs only cheap fail-closed checks before sending (IO error, size mismatch) — the receiver's per-`ChunkResponse` SHA-384 re-derivation remains the integrity guarantee, so a same-size local tamper is rejected by the fetcher, not the server.
- [x] ISC-A-C36: Anti: Unlocking a profile with multiple persisted share roots must never fail the share-index open with a lock error. The actor opens `share-index.redb` once per session and never closes/reopens it; a new define cancels the prior scan and clears the single-active index (latest-wins) instead of cycling the file lock.
- [x] ISC-A-C37: Anti: a share whose encoded manifest would exceed the relay frame budget (MANIFEST_FRAME_BUDGET, headroom under tonic's 4 MB default) is REFUSED at publish with a loud self-explaining error — never sent, never a silent stall. The ceiling is file-count-driven as much as byte-driven (many small files inflate the manifest); the refusal message says how big the manifest got. Manifest paging is the documented future lever (with raising configured frame caps + 2 MiB chunks).
- [x] ISC-A-C38: Anti: a `MemberHeartbeat` (ISC-C83) carries no roster, no peer list, and no content beyond the sender's own presence assertion — its fields are exactly room, sender pubkey, self-asserted handle, timestamp, and signature. A heartbeat never enumerates other members; presence is learned only by each member observing the set of beacons it receives, never by any member or the relay publishing a member list. (#74)
- [x] ISC-A-C39: Anti: heartbeat liveness is never persisted. The `PresenceTracker` is RAM-only and a fresh process starts with an empty roster (live-only, consistent with ISC-S20); no presence state is written to the at-rest blob, redb, or any file, so a relaunch reconstructs the roster solely from freshly-received beacons. (#74)
- [x] ISC-A-C40: Anti: a graceful per-stream end-of-stream MUST NOT tear down the whole session — it must not clear unrelated joined circles or the lobby, must not abort served-share tasks, and must not flip the session to disconnected while the underlying connection is alive (this is the v0.30.0 regression: a single graceful lobby/circle EOS cleared every circle and blocked all sends). A re-subscribe targeting a stream already torn down, or a `circle_id` no longer joined, is a no-op and never resurrects state. (#80)
- [x] ISC-A-C41: Anti: a replayed or stale-dated `MemberHeartbeat` MUST NOT refresh presence (or share-liveness) for a member who has departed. The ingest path drops a beacon whose advisory `sent_unix_ms` is outside the freshness window `[now − REPLAY_FRESHNESS_PAST, now + REPLAY_FRESHNESS_FUTURE]` before it reaches the `PresenceTracker` or the share-digest reconcile, so an untrusted relay replaying a captured beacon can pin a departed member present for at most the window, not indefinitely. This BOUNDS replay; it does not eliminate replay of a still-recent beacon (a nonce/sequence anti-replay is deferred) and the window depends on loosely-synchronised wall clocks — both open design points (#78). (#78)
- [x] ISC-A-C42: Anti: the GUI Lobby roster renders no presence decorator — no dot, colour, badge, "last seen", typing indicator, or join/leave toast — and no offline rows or persistent all-members list; it renders only the live set, and an empty set shows a quiet empty-state, never a roster of absent members. Own presence is never a roster row (the self-beacon is filtered net-side). The roster is RAM-only and not persisted (consistent with ISC-A-C39): a fresh process starts with an empty column. (#75)


- [ ] ISC-A-C1: The client persists no plaintext identifiers, no session-activity logs, no message content, and no recently-contacted lists — only the encrypted at-rest blob (ISC-C3) and a minimal configuration file. Optional ephemeral debug logs must be opt-in, auto-truncated, and exclude identifiers and message content.
- [ ] ISC-A-C2: The BIP-39 mnemonic is the only mechanism for cross-device setup and post-loss/post-passphrase-loss recovery — no server-side recovery, no email reset, no backup service. It exists on the device only transiently and is zeroed from RAM immediately after seed derivation. Circle entropy, though persisted in the at-rest blob (ISC-C59, M13), is **not** derived from the mnemonic; a mnemonic-only recovery on a clean device therefore does not restore circle memberships — this preserves the invariant that the mnemonic alone never reconstructs the social graph.
  - rationale: Real limitation — the mnemonic regenerates the identity seeds but not the circle-of-trust seeds (user-chosen entropy not derivable from the mnemonic), so recovery via mnemonic alone regains identity but loses circle access unless the circle passphrase is retained separately. This is intentional (circles are shared secrets, not personal-recovery surface) and must be told to the user at enrollment.
- [ ] ISC-A-C3: Mute lists (ISC-C15) and hide-shares lists (ISC-C16) never leak to the server — no enumeration, no presence-by-prefix, no per-prefix analytics. The server cannot tell whether one daemon has muted another.
- [ ] ISC-A-C4: The @mention feature (ISC-C17) introduces no new server-visible distinction beyond what regular chat already exposes — no mention-index, no server-side notification dispatch, no membership inference.
  - rationale: Honest scope — existing chat side-channels (length, timing, burstiness) are unchanged and may still statistically distinguish mention-bearing messages; closing those channels is separate scope.
- [ ] ISC-A-C5: The client never auto-shares content from a rating tier the user filtered out and never auto-fetches the ciphertext of a filtered share until the user explicitly opts in. Filtering is render-time and fetch-time, not just visual.
- [ ] ISC-A-C6: The share index (ISC-C21) is never persisted in plaintext — it is encrypted under the two-stage passphrase KDF with its own HKDF `info`, separate from the at-rest blob key. Index ciphertext yields no filenames, sizes, paths, or content hashes to an offline attacker.
- [ ] ISC-A-C7: The daemon — and the share indexer (ISC-C21) specifically — must never make the host unusable for other work. Locked-out failure modes: gating any user-facing function on indexing completion; failing the "civility" floor on Raspberry Pi 4 (4 GB, USB-3 storage)-class hardware even under a worst-case cold-index of a multi-terabyte share; share-wide rescans on single-file changes (even under platform-event-limit pressure); silent unbounded work (any worse-than-O(changed-paths) work must be user-visible and cancellable); and fate-sharing between the indexer and the network surface (separate task budgets so the indexer cannot starve the network task).
- [ ] ISC-A-C8: The client must not display, write, or auto-accept content encrypted under a `suite_id` below the circle's effective floor (the `cot_key` crypto family per ISC-C8 combined with the server's signed deprecation policy per ISC-S16 and the local registry; no per-circle `min_suite_id`, no founder). Sub-minimum content surfaces a deprecated-suite warning at render time requiring explicit user action, with the exception recorded locally. The client must not write new artifacts under a `read-only-deprecated` suite. Cross-family migration is treated as a new-circle event (a "create a new circle to migrate" UX), not in-place rekeying.
  - rationale: In-place cross-family migration would require simultaneous re-derivation by every active member, which needs a synchronization primitive the protocol does not yet have.
- [ ] ISC-A-C9: The client must not silently bypass, dismiss, or work around a server's announced suite-deprecation cutoff (ISC-S16, ISC-C25). The persistent warning surfaces on every start while a notice is active, dismissible only per `(suite_id, server-id)`. The client must not auto-rotate identity material to escape a cutoff (migration is always explicit), must not treat a missing/unparseable/signature-invalid policy as permission to use any suite (it falls back to the local registry with no auto-downgrade), and must not skip a policy fetch when the cached policy is past its TTL. Offline acceptance of stale cached policies is forbidden.
- [ ] ISC-A-C10: The client must not silently rotate identity keys to circumvent a server rate limit (ISC-S17) — switching identity is always explicit. The client must not exceed a small documented per-server concurrent-connection cap (default 4) so legitimate clients do not present as an attack pattern to a low-powered operator, and must not cache or pre-emptively replay identity-proofs, CoT-subscriptions, or signed-post submissions across connection-close in a way that intensifies reconnection load — the ISC-C26 backoff applies before any retry, and the retry uses a freshly-derived identity-proof signature.
  - rationale: Accepted limitation — these are reference-client commitments, not enforceable against a hostile non-conformant client; Sybil resistance proper is deferred.
- [ ] ISC-A-C11: The client must not install any release binary whose multi-sig ML-DSA-87 signature fails the bundled trust anchor's N-of-M threshold (sub-quorum, unknown keys, or expired anchor keys are all hard refusals). It must not auto-install any update — including emergency security updates — without explicit user confirmation, must not silently downgrade to an older signed version (downgrades surface as explicit user decisions), must not cache/persist/replay update payloads after a verification failure (failed artifacts are wiped immediately, the failure logged within ISC-A-C1 limits), and must not trust a server-relayed update (ISC-S18) any more than any other channel — the trust anchor is the project release-signing key, never the relaying server.
  - rationale: Silent auto-update is the coercion vector a compromised maintainer would use to ship a backdoor before users notice.
- [ ] ISC-A-C12: The client must not silently suppress any trust-state event in ISC-C28 — every non-transient event is logged and surfaces at its assigned class (except log-only). It must not permit global dismissal of a persistent-non-blocking class (dismissal is always per `(event-key, scope)`), must not degrade a blocking event to a lower class via preference/config/"expert mode", and must not ship a "disable security warnings" or "skip blocking prompts" toggle. It must not retain ISC-A-C1-forbidden data in the audit log, must not export the log out-of-band (no telemetry, remote reporting, or crash-dump inclusion), and must not skip the audit-log entry for any non-transient event even when the user dismisses the affordance.
- [ ] ISC-A-C13: First-start must not complete on a passive "I have saved my phrase" checkbox — backup verification must be demonstrably performed via the round-trip decrypt (ISC-C33) or the type-back (ISC-C34). A click-through that does not demonstrate phrase capture is forbidden, including any hidden "let me skip this" toggle.
  - rationale: Checkbox-only paths produce users who lose their seed phrase and blame the software; the no-long-term-history property softens recovery loss but does not justify the foot-gun.
- [ ] ISC-A-C14: The first-start sequence is purely local — no network connection except the explicit first connection at ISC-C29 step 7. The client must not upload the mnemonic, the recovery file, the at-rest blob, the session passphrase, or any derived key material to any remote endpoint as part of install or first-start. No cloud backup, no telemetry, no analytics.
  - rationale: Cloud backup of seed phrases is privacy-hostile in this threat model; introducing a server-side attack surface for the most sensitive artifact contradicts the architecture. The user's own deliberate off-device copying of the recovery file is explicitly different — that is the user moving an artifact they already own.
- [ ] ISC-A-C15: First-start must not initiate any network connection — including the first bootstrap-relay connection — until backup verification has succeeded (ISC-C33 or ISC-C34). The bootstrap decision and any network resolution it requires are deferred to ISC-C29 step 7, after backup-verify.
  - rationale: If the bootstrap connection happened before backup-verify, an interruption or coercion at that moment would leave the user network-exposed under an unrecoverable identity — backup-first ordering preserves the "identity recoverable before identity exposed" invariant.
- [ ] ISC-A-C16: The client must not derive the profile-id (ISC-C36) from any user-visible or filesystem-derived input (display name, path, hostname, username, mnemonic, passphrase) — it must come from cryptographic randomness alone. It must not proceed with per-profile key derivation when the profile-id is empty/missing/unparseable, must not silently regenerate or rotate the profile-id during a profile's life (rotation is profile re-initialization, not continuation, and a detected change should surface a warning), and must not include the profile-id in any out-of-band log, telemetry, or crash-dump.
- [ ] ISC-A-C17: The client must not use a constant or environment-derived value as the Argon2id salt — the salt is the profile-id (ISC-C36) and only the profile-id, itself from cryptographic randomness (ISC-A-C16). It must not silently change a profile's persisted Argon2 params mid-life (param change is profile re-initialization; migration is an explicit user-initiated re-key re-encrypting both blob and recovery file), must not omit the Argon2 params from the `.dseed` cleartext header (their absence must fail recovery closed rather than fall back to current defaults and risk silent data loss), and must not share Argon2 params across profiles via a shared file.
- [ ] ISC-A-C18: The client must not accept a server identity-proof envelope (ISC-S19) whose signature is not bound to the current TLS session via the channel-binding value — captured envelopes from earlier sessions must fail closed. It must not skip the handle-pubkey hash verification (anchors ISC-C4's self-verifying-handle property), must not proceed past a verification failure with partial trust ("almost valid, tentatively continue" is forbidden — failed identity-proof closes unconditionally), and must not speculate to the user about which specific check failed (the close-shape is uniform by design per ISC-A-S12).
- [ ] ISC-A-C19: The client must not attempt zero-config bootstrap-relay discovery at first-start (ISC-C37) — no mDNS, DHT, broadcast probe, or introducer-without-a-relay handshake; the two paths in ISC-C37 are exhaustive. It must not proceed past first-start with zero configured servers, and must not silently substitute a default relay different from the bundled bootstrap-anchor — binding the default to the binary is the integrity guarantee, so any divergence implies tampering and the client must refuse rather than fall back to a runtime-overridable value.
- [ ] ISC-A-C26: With no circle joined, the client must not append the composed message to the local transcript and must not transmit anything when the send action is invoked — Enter is a strict no-op (ISC-C48). A false local echo that makes an unsent message look sent is forbidden: the message never left the machine, so it must not appear in the user's own transcript. The composed draft may be preserved, but it must not masquerade as a posted message.
- [ ] ISC-A-C27: "Back"/Esc on the main view must never strand the user at the enrollment wizard (ISC-C29) — it routes to a logged-in menu or confirm-disconnect, never to the cold first-start flow that would prompt re-enrollment or seed re-entry. Disconnect drops to the daily-login Unlock screen (ISC-C51), not the enrollment wizard.
- [ ] ISC-A-C28: First-start (ISC-C29) must not silently overwrite an existing profile blob (ISC-C3) at the resolved profile root (ISC-C35). An existing-profile launch routes to Unlock (ISC-C51) rather than enrollment; a re-run of first-start against a root that already holds a blob requires an explicit user confirm before any overwrite, and absent that confirm fails closed rather than destroying the existing identity.

### Direct messaging (ISC-C38–C46 / ISC-A-C20–A-C25) — alpha2 scope; FROZEN to DRAFT v6 2026-07-28

The direct-messaging family is deferred from the MVP gate to alpha2. The MVP client ships without DM; users who want 1-to-1 private conversation in the interim use a 2-member circle-of-trust with a shared phrase (ISC-C8). These ISCs take **permanent main-line IDs from conception** (C38–C46 / A-C20–A-C25) — never renumbered when the feature ships.

**FROZEN 2026-07-28 to the design-of-record `docs/design/direct-messaging.md` DRAFT v6** (adjudicated + hardened over 8 adversarial panel rounds; caraka ratified 2026-07-28). The family is re-cut **in place** from the relay-era originals (which folded in `server_id` and mandated no-offline-delivery). Each ISC below is a concise verifiable end-state; the frozen construction, byte layouts, and rationale live in the design doc (canonical home). **Build slices mint additional ISC-C / ISC-A-C IDs** for the finer v6 mechanisms (paging, the double-ratchet, the ack) as they are implemented. **Forces #177** (relay-era client-ISC reconciliation). One-sentence shape: *a DM is a circle with one other person, keyed by a per-contact pseudonym over an ML-KEM-1024 encapsulation to the recipient's published static key, delivered as sender-performed offline store-and-forward with a fail-safe ack.*

- [ ] ISC-C38: A DM reuses the circle mechanism for exactly two parties (sealed frame, rendezvous engine, transcript), keyed by a **per-contact pseudonym** bound in-seal to the sender's long-term identity, over an ML-KEM-1024 encapsulation `ss0` to the recipient's published static key. First contact needs no handshake round-trip (KEM encapsulation is asymmetric); the ongoing channel runs a **two-directional post-quantum double ratchet** (per-direction symmetric chains with a bounded skipped-message-key cache, an ephemeral-ML-KEM step per reply carrying a `(ratchet_gen, PN)` header and a redundant `eph_ct` per generation) — content forward-secret after B's first reply.
- [ ] ISC-C39: DM delivery is offline-capable, sender-performed store-and-forward, **fail-safe**: the sender defaults a message to *not delivered* and flips it to *confirmed-collected* only on a verified authenticated monotonic high-water ack — never on a guess. Three truthful UI states (on-DHT / peer-reachable / **never "delivered"** until the ack). Geometric backoff re-seed (per-emission jittered), hard give-up at 7 days marks *undelivered*. Veilid has no TTL (eviction-only), so a value survives exactly while re-seeded.
- [ ] ISC-C40: The static key record (`dflt(1)`, world-derivable owner = HKDF over the identity's ML-DSA-87 pubkey) publishes `{version (u64 monotonic), kem_ek, invite_only, signature}` signed by the identity key; readers cache the highest verified version and never regress (rollback is DoS-only — the record is world-writable but inner-signed, so no forgery). It is a **presence-independent static publish** (no online/offline rhythm). It also advertises the first-contact anti-replay epoch `fc_epoch = floor(now/FC_PERIOD)`.
- [ ] ISC-C41: The **sender-blind doorbell** (`dflt(32)`, recipient-keyed `HKDF(PK_lt_B)`) holds a **self-contained first-contact entry** in slot `HKDF(sender_secret_A, PK_lt_B) % 32` (sender-secret ⇒ stable-per-pair, idempotent, not observer-computable — no pairwise contact-graph oracle). The entry carries `ct0` + a sealed body {pseudonym + in-seal binding to the long-term identity, `eph_ek_A`, hashed intended-recipient, `fc_epoch`, a reserved `key_selector` (=STATIC in alpha), body} ≈ 18 KB < 32768. Admission is A+B+C (PoW rate-bound + grantee-bound one-time invite token under an invite-only policy + the sender-blind self-contained entry). Idempotent retry via A-persisted `ss0`. Accepted limit: 32 concurrent unknown senders (post-alpha lever: pointer+body split).
- [ ] ISC-C42: The ongoing channel is **per-page scattered records** `page_addr(dir,p) = HKDF(AR ‖ dir ‖ p)` (K=16 messages per `dflt(16)` record; `AR = HKDF(ss0)`), owner-write gated by the AR-derived owner so **no third party can write, forge, or erase** it (the load-bearing win). Messages are AES-256-GCM-sealed under the ratchet message key with a `msg_sig` binding `chan_id ‖ dir ‖ seq ‖ eph_ek ‖ recipient` (no epoch — addressing is decoupled from content-binding). Paging fixes the write budget (opens amortized K:1, watchable, no wrap); the frozen decision #4 closes at ≈3.13/min with a **client-global aggregate ack cap**.
- [ ] ISC-C43: Established contact rides the AR-derived channel directly (contact cache holds the channel key + pseudonym); the doorbell is consulted only for first contact, so doorbell erasure is a first-contact nuisance, never a way to suppress an established conversation.
- [ ] ISC-C44: The contact cache lives in the at-rest blob — per contact: long-term + pseudonym pubkeys, the channel/`AR` material, `ss0`, first/last timestamps — encrypted at rest under the passphrase that protects mute/hide (the binding of these public values to the local user is sensitive social-graph metadata). Enables established-contact delivery across restart; B additionally persists provisional-handshake state and the receive high-water cursor.
- [ ] ISC-C45: First-contact UX is explicit-accept — an unknown sender surfaces as a contact request; the thread renders only on accept. A known sender whose cached identity key matches is delivered directly; a mismatch surfaces a trust event ("known handle, new key") via the ISC-C28 taxonomy. The gate is local UI, not a protocol round trip. **User-education requirement:** the pre-establishment first message is labeled **hello-grade** — "not yet secured; say hello, not secrets" — because it has no forward secrecy until B's first reply forks the ratchet.
- [ ] ISC-C46: The block list is the DM revocation primitive (a `blocked` set keyed on identity pubkey): doorbell suppression (blocked sender's entry dropped at sweep on the sealed sender hash) + channel suppression (client stops sweeping the blocked channel). The blocked sender's experience is byte-identical to "never came online." Reversible (cache preserved). Mute stays scoped to chat-render suppression.
- [ ] ISC-A-C20: The DHT and any storage node never see DM content or forgeable authorship — every message and first-contact entry is AES-256-GCM-sealed, the signature lives inside the seal, and ongoing-channel writes are AR-owner-gated; ISC-A-S2 holds as for chat circles.
- [ ] ISC-A-C21: A DM message is never delivered twice as new and never replayed across pairs, epochs, or carrier records: `msg_sig` binds `chan_id ‖ dir ‖ seq`; the seal-key KDF binds both identities; the monotonic high-water + RLE gap-bitmap ack confirms collected-past-a-gap messages without ever asserting a false "delivered" (fail-safe).
- [ ] ISC-A-C22: DM authorship forgery requires the sender's ML-DSA private key. The in-seal pseudonym binding + mandatory-and-verified `msg_sig` under the pseudonym key is the **sole** proof-of-possession path (applied symmetrically to B's ACCEPT); the sender-blind doorbell entry's binding proves possession, so an unknown-key-share or handle-spoof cannot impersonate another sender.
- [ ] ISC-A-C23: The accepted-for-alpha metadata residuals are named, not hidden: F1 lobby-rhythm (activity-timed writes correlate to lobby online windows) + bucket-class size; first-contact anonymized in-degree (sender-blind); universal address-plane harvest-now (a future `DK_lt` compromise recovers the AR-derived address graph — content stays FS via deleted ratchet chains); **opening-burst no-FS, mitigated by user education** (ISC-C45). Cover-traffic / prekey / mixnet mitigations are post-alpha.
- [ ] ISC-A-C24: The ongoing-channel addresses `HKDF(AR ‖ dir ‖ p)` are derivable only by the two parties (`AR = HKDF(ss0)`, `ss0` secret), scatter across DHT nodes per page, and are third-party-opaque; an observer holding only a candidate recipient pubkey cannot derive or confirm any ongoing channel. The first-contact `fc_addr` was deliberately made recipient-keyed + sender-blind (not pairwise-derivable) to avoid a global contact-graph oracle.
- [ ] ISC-A-C25: The contact cache never leaves the at-rest blob — no wire message carries any portion of a user's contact list, and no observer can enumerate a user's contacts. Key exchange happens only via the recipient's own published key record and the sender-blind first-contact entry addressed to that recipient — never a who-knows-whom broadcast.

### Veilid write budget (WB-ISC-1–15) — presence + scheduler + discovery (`docs/design/veilid-write-budget.md`)

The write-budget family takes permanent `WB-ISC-N` IDs from the FROZEN 2026-07-09 design. WB-ISC-1–8 (WB-1 presence model, below), WB-ISC-9–13 (WB-3 write scheduler), and WB-ISC-14 (WB-4 steady-state re-surfacing) are built; WB-ISC-15 (a late-converging client still discovers a share) is DEFERRED to its two-node orinoco oracle and is registered in `daemonseed-isc` only once that live probe lands. The presence oracles are unit-covered in `daemonseed-core::{heartbeat,presence}` (fixed-length sealing, leave-dominance, same-room bound, reap-in-calm, chat-fold — all pure/clock-injected) plus a paused-time scheduler oracle in `daemonseed-veilid-net::schedule` (keepalive dispatch independence); the scheduler criteria are covered by paused-time oracles in `daemonseed-veilid-net::schedule` against an injected write-sink + clock (the WB-2 oracle seam).

- [x] WB-ISC-1: Anti — presence emit timing takes no input from user activity, AND keepalive dispatch timing is statistically independent of other-class write load (probe: `daemonseed_core::presence::next_keepalive_interval` is an activity-free CSPRNG draw in the [180,220]s band; `schedule::wb_isc_1_keepalive_dispatch_timing_independent_of_chat_load` — a paused-time test asserting the keepalive dispatch-delay distribution under a chat burst is identical (KS distance 0) to idle, not merely within the jitter band).
- [x] WB-ISC-2: Anti — presence/roster state for room R never consumes input from any record outside R's sibling set (probe: `presence::wb_isc_2_cross_room_write_never_refreshes` — a member write tagged with a different room is refused by a scoped `PresenceTracker`).
- [x] WB-ISC-3: A connected-but-silent member remains on the roster indefinitely while its keepalives land (probe: `presence::wb_isc_3_silent_member_stays_while_keepalives_land` — a 3×TTL horizon, keepalive every 200s, never reaped in calm).
- [x] WB-ISC-4: A member's roster freshness advances on any verified same-room member write without a presence beacon (probe: `presence::wb_isc_4_chat_only_member_stays_fresh_past_ttl` — a member that only chats after joining stays fresh past the TTL; wired into the gui/tui inbound chat paths).
- [x] WB-ISC-5: Reaping is suspended while enqueue-to-ack latency is elevated and resumes in calm (probe: `presence::wb_isc_5_reap_suspended_while_congested` — elevated signal + expired TTL → no reap; calm → reap. The production signal is the scheduler's per-write enqueue-to-ack latency, exposed via `VeilidNetHandle::last_write_latency_ms`).
- [x] WB-ISC-6: All presence-class writes — join, keepalive, and leave tombstone — seal to ONE constant byte length across all payload shapes (probe: `heartbeat::wb_isc_6_all_presence_writes_seal_to_one_constant_length` — fixed-length padded sealing to `HEARTBEAT_SEALED_LEN`; the digest is moved out of the veilid beacon and a too-large payload is rejected rather than sealed longer).
- [x] WB-ISC-7: A leave tombstone dominates freshness — no same-room write with `sent_unix_ms ≤ leave_ms` re-freshens a departed member, while a genuinely newer write rejoins (probe: `presence::wb_isc_7_leave_tombstone_dominates_freshness`; the leave marker is a provenance-bound `is_leave` field so a relay cannot forge or strip it).
- [x] WB-ISC-8: Anti — no freshness-inference or reap transition schedules any network emission (probe: `presence::wb_isc_8_read_side_is_emission_free` + code inspection — the read-side API (`apply`/`apply_member_write`/`reap`) takes no emitter and returns only local data; the scheduler's I9 enforces the same on the write half).
- [x] WB-ISC-9: Steady-state non-chat DHT writes ≤ 4/min per client under the WB-2 load model (lobby + 3 circles + 2 shares + operator MOTD), with the connect window ≤ 8 one-shot writes in the first 120s (probe: the paused-time scheduler oracle driven ≥ 30 simulated minutes).
- [x] WB-ISC-10: A chat write to an idle record begins DHT dispatch ≤ 2s after enqueue at any queue depth, with a reserved dispatch slot never counted against the non-chat cap (probe: paused-time test with a saturated class-4 backlog).
- [x] WB-ISC-11: Anti — no chat ring write is ever coalesced or dropped by the scheduler, and pending chat flushes at graceful close (probe: property test — every enqueued chat write reaches the write-sink exactly once, in per-record order, across a close).
- [x] WB-ISC-12: Anti — a queued withdraw/leave tombstone is never coalesced away, superseded, or reordered behind by a same-id current-state write; a same-id current-state enqueued while a withdraw is pending is dropped (probe: unit test — withdraw then watchdog refresh → withdraw dispatches, refresh dropped; the #121/#118 resurrection guard).
- [x] WB-ISC-13: Slot/seq assignment for ring writes occurs inside the record lock at dispatch — the scheduler's per-record single-flight never lets two same-record writes overlap in the sink (probe: code inspection + a serial-dispatch interleaving oracle, complementing the `rendezvous::record_lock` oracle).
- [x] WB-ISC-14: Anti — no discovery-class surface relies on warmup-only sweeps plus a passive watch; every subscribed chat/discovery record (lobby chat, share-advert, circle chat, operator) has a declared steady-state re-surfacing mechanism (probe: code inspection of the gui/tui net-actor `steady_resweep` arm — a post-warmup round-robin reader resweep over the current subscribed set, presence excluded as it self-heals via keepalive re-writes — plus `next_resweep_seed_is_key_based_and_survives_set_changes`, the key-based round-robin cursor oracle proving a join/leave never skips a record).
- [DEFERRED-VERIFY] WB-ISC-15: Share adverts (and lobby/circle chat) are discoverable by a client that holds only the room secret and converged AFTER the write, with no prior knowledge of any `share_id` and no reliance on warmup timing (probe: the two-node orinoco oracle — B joins and settles first, A publishes/sends after B's warmup window closes, B still discovers/receives. Follow-up: the orinoco re-test of `feat/veilid-migration` after this commit; live Veilid attach is impossible on the build VM's SLIRP NAT).

- [x] CRSH-ISC-1: The steady resweep's GET accounting surfaces per-record attempted/failed/found counts — a failed GET is distinct from an empty slot (no longer swallowed by `.ok().flatten()`), the enabling observability for consumer-side session-health tracking (#180 §RS-1.1) (probe: `rendezvous::crsh_isc_1_sweep_outcome_accounts_failed_empty_and_found_separately`; existing #157 resweep behavior regression-guarded).

- [ ] CRSH-ISC-14: Anti — peak un-gated DHT-op concurrency (open + watch) never exceeds margin(2) under any composition of connect, repair, and Refresh; every un-gated site acquires the enforced limiter (#180 §RS-2). Limiter core + connect-path wiring landed (probe: `dht_gate::crsh_isc_14_ungated_ops_bounded_by_margin`); the full connect+repair+Refresh composition probe completes with steps 3/7.

- [x] CRSH-ISC-2: K consecutive all-failed sweep passes in calm weather flag a record repair-due exactly once, at the K-th pass's own cursor tick (latched, no duplicate) — #180 §RS-1.2 (probe: `daemonseed_core::session_health` paused-time `crsh_isc_2_*`; K = `REPAIR_K_THRESHOLD`, repro-tunable).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-9: a repair-due transition is suppressed while the WB-5.1 weather estimator reads elevated and resumes (not cancels) in calm — the streak is retained across the elevated regime (#180 §RS-1.2) (probe: `session_health::crsh_isc_9_elevated_suppresses_then_calm_resumes`).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-11: Anti — the repair-due decision input (`SweepHealthInput`) carries only network-state fields (sweep-result counts, watch state, weather); no user-event-derived value can enter, so the signal is a pure function of network state (#180 §RS-0/§RS-1.2) (probe: `session_health::crsh_isc_11_*`, type-level + unit assertion).

- [x] CRSH-ISC-3: The repair re-establishes a record session (invalidate open-cache → optional close-first → re-open → re-watch → full 0..64 sweep) **holding the record's `record_lock` across the entire span**, open/watch on the margin limiter and each re-sweep GET on a per-GET read permit (#180 §RS-1.2) (probe: `rendezvous::crsh_isc_3_repair_ordered_trace_lock_span_and_permit_discipline`; `REPAIR_CLOSE_FIRST` default true, repro-gated §RS-1.3).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-18: A same-record write concurrent with a repair serializes behind the repair's `record_lock` — never lost, never targeting a torn-down handle; on acquiring the lock it reads the freshly re-cached session (#180 §RS-1.2) (probe: `rendezvous::crsh_isc_18_chat_write_serializes_behind_repair_and_targets_fresh_session`, paused-time).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-17: Anti — no site holds a read-pool permit while acquiring the un-gated-op limiter, or vice versa; the repair's open/watch (limiter) and re-sweep GETs (read pool) are separate phases (#180 §RS-2) (probe: the CRSH-ISC-3 trace assertions + acquire-site inspection; Refresh sites at step 7 are bound by the same rule).

- [x] CRSH-ISC-4: Anti — a fetch failure, browse, render, or roster event enqueues no DHT op (write/GET/open/watch) and no `app_call`; error paths mutate local state only (#180 §RS-0) (probe: gui `crsh_isc_4_*` — the reactive path takes no network handle; `mark_share_unresolved` mutates local state + one `SharesSnapshot`).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-5: A share whose fetch fails — preview (import-fail, manifest-fail) OR download (chunk-fetch) — stays listed `Unresolved`; it is removed from `discovered`/catalog only on verified withdraw or catalog TTL, never by any fetch-failure path (`prune_unreachable_share` fully retired) (#180 §RS-1.5) (probe: gui/tui `crsh_isc_5_*` incl. `crsh_isc_5_download_fail_keeps_share_listed_with_route`).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-6: A share's re-resolve (`Unresolved`) flag is cleared — the share marked fetchable-again — at the route-rotation fold (`apply_discovery`, when the route blob rotates: a local no-network op), so it recovers however long the sharer is away and the user re-initiates the download; a content-only re-advert (route unchanged) keeps it re-resolving. The parked browse retry is housekeeping only: a Fire tick drops a now-stale park (generation bumped without a rotation) and window expiry surfaces failure (naming the share) without pruning (#180 §RS-1.4, reframe 2026-07-17) (probe: gui/tui `crsh_isc_27_route_rotation_reimports_on_catalog_unchanged` [rotation clears], `crsh_isc_6_content_readvert_does_not_mark_fetchable` [content re-advert does NOT clear], `crsh_isc_6_fire_drops_stale_park_without_clearing`, `crsh_isc_6_window_expiry_*` + `daemonseed_core::browse_retry::crsh_isc_6_*`).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-15: Anti — no NETWORK op (re-fetch/GET/open/watch) fires from the advert-fold event; the parked-retry housekeeping decision is queried only at a cursor tick, never inside `apply_discovery`. A route rotation clears the local `Unresolved` flag at the fold, but that is a no-network UI-state op that emits nothing, so it does not breach decorrelation (#180 §RS-1.4, reframe 2026-07-17) (probe: `browse_retry::crsh_isc_15_*` + CRSH-ISC-4's zero-network reactive-path probe, architectural).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-19: Parked retries carry a `(share_id, discovered-generation)` tag; a withdraw/re-add mid-park drops the stale retry so it never fires against a new advert's route (#180 §RS-1.4) (probe: `browse_retry::crsh_isc_19_*` + gui/tui).

- [x] CRSH-ISC-8: A chat `NetCommand` (SendLobby/SendCircle) enqueued while a `FetchShare` is in flight is processed before the fetch resolves — the net actor loop spawns the fetch and never awaits it inline (#180 §RS-2) (probe: gui/tui `crsh_isc_8_actor_never_awaits_a_fetch_inline`).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-7: Anti — a failing-fetch storm never delays a chat write past the WB-3 I4 bound (≤ 2s); the self-heal retry path is off the actor loop (#180 §RS-2) (probe: gui/tui `crsh_isc_7_failing_fetch_storm_never_delays_chat`, paused-time).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-29: A chat `NetCommand` (SendLobby/SendCircle) enqueued while a `ConfirmFetch` (chunk download) is in flight is processed before the download resolves — the net actor loop spawns the download and never awaits it inline; the terminal outcome folds on-loop, applying the share's mark/clear-Unresolved mutation (#197, mirroring the CRSH-ISC-8 `FetchShare` restructure) (probe: gui/tui `crsh_isc_29_actor_never_awaits_a_download_inline` + `crsh_isc_29_fold_applies_mark_clear_per_outcome`).
- [x] DL-ISC-10: The share-fetch boundary types transient-transport, integrity, not-served, and local failures apart, replacing the blanket `Send(String)` that made a transient timeout indistinguishable from a content-integrity failure (#205 blocker). A SHA-384 content-address mismatch, a chunk-address mismatch, and a malformed/oversized frame classify as `Integrity` (fatal for the share — never persisted, never resumed past); transport/route/actor failures as `Transient` (resumable); the reachable owner's authoritative negative as `NotServed`; the engine's own disk/path faults as `Local` (contributed downstream, never returned by the transport). `FetchErrorClass` is derived at the site where the knowledge exists — the SHA-384 verify, the malformed-frame guards, the retry loop — never re-inferred from message strings (`docs/design/download-subsystem.md` Part 3) (probe: veilid-net `error::tests::fetch_class_maps_each_variant_to_its_class` + `fetch_class_never_returns_local_from_a_transport_error`).
- [x] DL-ISC-1: Peak concurrent in-flight fragment `app_call`s to one serving route never exceeds that route's budget `W(route)`, under any composition of parallel files/chunks/fragments (probe: veilid-net `route_budget::tests::peak_in_flight_never_exceeds_the_route_window`).
- [x] DL-ISC-2: A fragment timeout / route-death collapses the route's budget to `W_FLOOR` and records a learned ceiling of half the killing width keyed by the sharer's long-term pubkey; a later attempt against the same sharer — including a resume on a rotated route with a fresh `RouteId` — cannot open wider than that ceiling, and the ceiling only ratchets DOWN within a session (probe: `route_budget::tests::learned_ceiling_survives_route_rotation` + `learned_ceiling_only_ratchets_down` + `failure_collapses_to_floor_and_learns_half_the_killing_width`).
- [x] DL-ISC-3: Additive increase is window-clocked — at most +1 per full window's worth of clean completions, and the healthy-climb counter resets on any decrease (probe: `route_budget::tests::increase_is_window_clocked_and_resets_on_decrease`).
- [x] DL-ISC-4: Downloads to distinct routes hold independent budgets and progress concurrently (probe: `route_budget::tests::distinct_routes_are_independent_and_bounded_by_g`).
- [x] DL-ISC-5: Total in-flight fragment `app_call`s across all routes never exceeds the fetcher-global cap `G_GLOBAL` (probe: same two-route counting fake, global-peak bound).
- [x] DL-ISC-6: Anti: no fetch path issues a fragment `app_call` without budget admission, and no admission is held across a retry backoff sleep — dropping a permit frees the route + global slot and wakes a waiter (probe: `route_budget::tests::dropping_a_permit_frees_the_slot_and_wakes_a_waiter` + the seam probe `share::tests::budgeted_fetch_reassembles_and_admits_through_the_route_budget`).
- [x] DL-ISC-15: Liveness — under sustained clean completions on one route the window reaches `W_CEIL` within a bounded number of windows and never exceeds it (probe: `route_budget::tests::window_climbs_to_ceiling_under_sustained_health`).
- [x] DL-ISC-16: Liveness — N units on one healthy route achieve peak in-flight > `W_FLOOR` (genuine parallelism under the budget), still bounded by the window (probe: `route_budget::tests::files_parallelize_under_a_climbed_window`).
- [x] DL-ISC-17: The latency valve steps the window down on a sustained breach (≥3 of the last 5 completions at/over `FRAGMENT_LATENCY_THRESHOLD`) and NOT on a single slow sample (probe: `route_budget::tests::latency_valve_steps_down_only_on_sustained_breach`).
- [x] DL-ISC-19: Anti: `W_CEIL < G_GLOBAL` (compile-time `const _` assert) and no single route ever holds more than `W_CEIL` global slots, so a route at its ceiling never blocks another route from admitting (probe: the compile-time assert + `route_budget::tests::a_saturated_route_never_blocks_another`).
- [x] DL-ISC-9: Anti: two concurrent managed-dir downloads — in one process or two — never lose a `downloads.idx` entry; the idx read-modify-write is serialized by an OS advisory file lock (`flock`/`LockFileEx` on a `.idx.lock` sibling, `fs4`), re-reading under the lock so no entry is dropped via a stale snapshot (probe: `storage::fetched::tests::concurrent_record_share_never_loses_an_entry` — 8 concurrent `record_share`s on one root via separate stores, all entries land).
- [x] DL-ISC-22: Anti: the staging sweep deletes only staging state that is NEITHER registered as an active fetch (the live-fetch registry) NOR resumable-with-progress (the frontend keep-predicate: a stored confirmed manifest is present AND ≥1 verified chunk was staged — A1/#214: a preallocated-but-empty failure, manifest persisted but no chunk fetched, has nothing to resume and IS reclaimed rather than surfaced as a phantom 0% partial) — the sweep skips any registered id, and a directory whose name is not a valid staging component is never deleted on a name pattern alone (probe: `storage::fetched::tests::sweep_reclaims_only_unregistered_unresumable_debris` + `sweep_keeps_a_registered_resume` + `sweep_leaves_foreign_dirs` + `sweep_reclaims_a_resumable_area_with_no_verified_progress`). The registry↔sweep happens-before is a **caller-ordering contract** (the sweep runs at startup/idle when nothing is registering), not a code-atomic guarantee — the step-5/6 wiring owns that ordering.
- [x] DL-ISC-11: Chunk bytes reach disk only after verification, only inside the reserved `.dspart/<share_id>/` staging namespace, written at their manifest-derived byte offsets (files pre-sized sparse; partial state is a SET of verified chunks, not a prefix); a file appears under its real name only by promotion, after every chunk has verified. Mid-download no bytes sit under a final name (probe: `storage::fetched::tests::staging_writes_at_offsets_then_promotes` — out-of-order offset writes + fs inspection + promote).
- [x] DL-ISC-18: Anti: the full computed destination-relative path (selection-root basename + subpath) passes the traversal guard as ONE unit, and the reserved `.dspart` staging component is refused in any manifest `rel_path`, so no hostile manifest or selection can write outside the dest or into the staging namespace (probe: `storage::fetched::tests::place_guards_the_full_computed_path` + `staging_namespace_is_refused_in_manifest_paths` — `sanitize_rel_path`/`record_share`/`place_at_dest` all refuse `.dspart`).
- [x] DL-ISC-21: Anti: at a user-chosen dest, promotion never overwrites a pre-existing path that is not provably this download's own artifact — the incoming file collision-suffixes (`name-N`, before the extension) instead (probe: `storage::fetched::tests::promote_never_clobbers_a_preexisting_file`). (The overwrite-if-provably-ours case is a step-8 resume refinement.)
- [x] DL-ISC-7: Each (selection × destination) case of the placement table lands exactly per the design §Part 2 table, including normalized overlapping roots. For a user-chosen dest, `place_at_dest` is a total function of the selection roots (`SelectionRoot::{File,Dir}`, ISC-C72): a single file → its basename; a folder (incl. exactly one file) → the folder's name + subpath (arrives whole); scattered folders → each as a top-level entry; the whole-share root → full rel_paths; nested roots subsumed; between-roots basename collisions suffix the later root (`name-2`). The managed downloads dir keeps the full `<share-folder>/<rel_path>` layout (`record_share`, unchanged). Replaces the shape-guessing `rebase_to_selection_root` (fixes the one-file-folder collapse and the scattered-selection ancestry) (probe: `storage::fetched::tests::place_*` — single/one-file-folder/scattered/whole-share/nested/collision, plus the `record_*` managed-dir tests).

- [x] DL-ISC-8: `ConfirmFetch` carries the user's selection roots (a `RootKind` tag + the selected manifest indices), and the GUI download scheduler makes placement a function of them: the net-side `selection_roots` maps `RootKind::Share` → the whole-share root (`Dir("")`), `RootKind::File` → `File(rel_path)`, and `RootKind::Dir` → `Dir(common_prefix_dir(selected))` (the selected files' common parent-directory prefix, so a one-file folder keeps its folder), then `place_at_dest` lands each root per the DL-ISC-7 table — a scattered selection lands each root as a top-level entry, a one-file folder keeps its folder. A **whole-share** download to a chosen dest additionally never scatters: the share-root folder name is NOT part of the file rel_paths (they are relative to the share root), so files not already under one common top-level folder are re-rooted under the share name (`no_scatter`) — a single-depth share lands as `<dest>/<ShareName>/…` rather than loose, a nested share is unchanged (probe: `veilid_net::tests::selection_roots_*` + `storage::fetched::tests::no_scatter_wraps_a_flat_share_and_leaves_a_nested_one`).
- [x] DL-ISC-13: Anti: an integrity failure destroys every staged partial of the fetch, keeps already-promoted files, sets the durable poison flag (session state keyed by `share_id`), never parks a one-shot retry, never marks the share Unresolved, and is never resumed past — the engine's `DownloadOutcome::IntegrityFailed` folds to `ConfirmOutcome::IntegrityFailed` (staging already destroyed by the engine), whose fold emits `FetchError` + sets `poisoned_shares` and does NOT mark Unresolved or park (probe: `veilid_net::tests::download_outcome_maps_to_confirm_outcome` + `fold_integrity_failed_poisons_without_mark_or_park`).
- [x] DL-ISC-14: A panicking download worker still yields a terminal outcome and leaves only staged (quarantined) state: the spawn seam (`spawn_confirm_task`, mirrored at the browse `spawn_fetch_task` seam) retains the worker's `JoinHandle` and maps a `JoinError` (panic) to a terminal `LocalFailed`-class outcome, so a panicking worker never strands the share (probe: `veilid_net::tests::a_panicking_download_worker_yields_a_terminal_outcome`).

- [x] DL-ISC-20: Anti: a resume never uses a stored confirmed manifest it cannot prove is untampered. The confirmed manifest is persisted into the fetch's staging area (`StagingArea::persist_manifest`, a deterministic canonical serialization under the reserved `.dspart` component so no staged file can collide with it) and its SHA-384 digest is anchored in the profile's own trusted state (`storage::manifest_digest::ManifestDigestStore`, a redb store under the profile dir — NOT the co-resident-writable downloads root). Before any reuse, `verify_stored_manifest` re-serializes + re-digests the staging copy and constant-time-compares it to the profile-anchored digest; a tampered staging manifest, a swapped (different) manifest, or a missing manifest each returns `Err` and the resume MUST halt (re-gate on a fresh preview/confirm) — never proceed on an unverified stored manifest. Without the profile anchor a co-resident tamper of the staging copy plus a colluding sharer serving the matching manifest would bypass the confirm gate (`docs/design/download-subsystem.md` Part 3) (probe: `storage::fetched::tests::verify_stored_manifest_rejects_a_tampered_staging_manifest` / `_rejects_a_swapped_manifest` / `_rejects_a_missing_staging_manifest` / `_accepts_the_matching_digest`).
- [x] DL-ISC-12: A resume re-fetches only missing/unverified chunks; every retained byte re-verifies against its content address before reuse. `derive_resume_state` re-derives per-file state from BYTES ON DISK, never from bookkeeping: each chunk region (`[i*CHUNK_SIZE, min((i+1)*CHUNK_SIZE, size))`, boundaries from `CHUNK_SIZE` + the stored size, never from the file self-describing) is re-hashed at BOTH the promoted final file and the staged partial, and counts as present iff either re-verifies against the stored content address — per-chunk SET semantics, no prefix assumption (a later chunk can be present while an earlier one is missing). A chunk that mismatches, is short/sparse, or is unreadable is MISSING (discard + re-fetch); a file whose every chunk verifies is Complete. A resume can never be tricked past an integrity check by tampered at-rest state (`docs/design/download-subsystem.md` Part 3) (probe: `storage::fetched::tests::derive_resume_returns_verified_and_missing_as_a_set` — non-prefix {0,2}/{1,3} — + `_classifies_a_tampered_partial_as_missing` + `_on_a_fully_staged_file_is_complete` + `_reads_a_promoted_file`). The wiring of re-derivation into the confirm/fold path is step 8b.

- [x] CRSH-ISC-10: Consumer-imported share routes are tracked in an in-use guard and released on the later of advert-blob replacement or in-flight-fetch completion; every release site routes through `release_tolerant()` (treating veilid `InvalidArgument` as already-evicted, exactly one raw `release_private_route` remains, inside the helper); `RouteChange::dead_routes` drops matching `advert_routes` by value (#180 §RS-3) (probe: `daemonseed_core::route_guard` unit tests + `actor::release_private_route_only_called_through_release_tolerant` + `drop_dead_advert_routes_removes_only_dead_by_value`).

- [x] CRSH-ISC-13: The gui manual Refresh button executes the §RS-4 contract — immediate re-render, then a sweep-first wave over every share-bearing subscribed record (`resweep_rendezvous`, GETs only, off the actor loop, serialized behind `resweep_busy`), arming each swept record for evidence-gated re-establishment; an armed record re-establishes on a SINGLE failed pass in calm weather (`refresh_or_cadence_due`), healing a dead session in one action without a consumer restart — including when the triggering sweep was busy/warming: the arm survives into `pending_repairs` and the drain dispatches it (#180 §RS-4, R1) (probe: gui `crsh_isc_16_refresh_gate_reestablishes_only_on_failure_evidence` on the fold-decision core + `crsh_isc_13_armed_refresh_survives_a_busy_enqueue_and_the_drain_honors_it` on the arm/drain lifecycle; attended one-action heal folds into CRSH-ISC-12).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-16: Anti — a manual Refresh over a HEALTHY record emits only sweep-shaped GETs, never an `open_dht_record`/`watch_dht_values`: re-establishment fires only on a repair-due or just-failed record, so `refresh_or_cadence_due(_, armed=true, healthy) == false` and the wave uses `resweep_rendezvous` (GETs only) exclusively (#180 §RS-4) (probe: gui `crsh_isc_16_refresh_gate_reestablishes_only_on_failure_evidence`; healthy-armed case asserts no re-establishment).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-20: Anti — the Refresh contract is exclusively user-initiated: `handle_refresh_shares` is reached only from `NetCommand::ResweepShares`, itself sent only by the UI Refresh button (`main.rs` `on_refresh_shares`); no render, focus, timer, tab-open, or state-change path sends `ResweepShares` (the ~3s liveness auto-poll sends only `RefreshShares`) (#180 §RS-4) (probe: grep — the sole `NetCommand::ResweepShares` sender is the button; any new sender fails review against this ISC).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-21: An identical advert re-read (same owner + timestamp + metadata) folds as `CatalogChange::Unchanged` — no spurious generation bump — and refreshes `received_at` (re-hearing keeps the share alive for the TTL prune) while folding Unchanged; a same-metadata NEWER-timestamp re-announce still folds `Updated` (#180 §RS-1.4, F3) (probe: `share_catalog` unit tests `identical_reread_refreshes_received_at_but_folds_unchanged` / `reheard_share_survives_ttl_prune` / `same_metadata_newer_timestamp_reannounce_folds_updated` / `equal_timestamp_differing_metadata_folds_updated`).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-22: RepairRendezvous is dispatched OFF the transport actor loop (spawned, never awaited inline) while the record_lock still spans the whole close/open/watch/sweep re-establishment (atomicity preserved, CRSH-ISC-3/18), guarded by a per-record in-flight set so a re-dispatch for a record already repairing is skipped — a slow repair cannot park the loop or double-repair — and the in-flight marker is cleared via RAII (Drop) so a panic in the spawned repair cannot permanently disable a record's self-heal (#180 F1) (probe: code inspection — the arm contains tokio::spawn and no inline .await of repair_rendezvous; guard + record_lock-serializes unit tests; `repair_in_flight_guard_clears_marker_on_drop` / `repair_in_flight_guard_clears_marker_on_panic`; live loop-not-parked via attended CRSH-ISC-12).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-23: A fetch outcome that goes stale because a FRESH ADVERT advanced the discovered-entry generation re-parks a browse retry at the outcome's generation (fires next cursor tick against the newer advert) and keeps the share Unresolved — never stranded Unresolved-without-a-retry; a stale outcome for a WITHDRAWN share does not re-park; and a stale outcome only re-parks a share that is STILL unresolved — a share a newer overlapping fetch already resolved is not reverted by a late stale outcome (#180 F2, R4) (probe: gui+tui fold_fetch_outcome unit tests — re-advert staleness re-parks / withdraw staleness does not / resolved-share not reverted).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-24: A queued repair is dispatched only if the record is still repair-due when the queue drains — a record whose session recovered after being queued is dropped from the queue, not re-established; a record dispatched immediately is removed from pending_repairs so it is never double-repaired (#180 F4/F5) (probe: SessionHealthTracker::is_repair_due recover/dispatch unit test; live drain behavior via attended CRSH-ISC-12).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-25: A successful download (confirm_fetch success) clears the share's Unresolved mark and its parked browse retry — symmetric with the download-failure mark and the browse-preview-success clear; no share stays "re-resolving" after a completed download (#180 F6) (probe: gui+tui unit test — download success over an Unresolved share clears the mark + drops the parked retry).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-26: A verified withdraw releases the share's imported route — immediately when idle, or deferred to in-flight-fetch completion — and drops its route_guard entry, so no imported route leaks past withdraw and route_guard does not grow unbounded; the release is idempotent (a re-withdraw hands out no route) (#180 F7, §RS-3) (probe: route_guard unit tests — idle-withdraw releases+drops, in-flight-withdraw defers to completion, re-withdraw returns None).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-27: A consumer re-imports a share's rotated route even when the catalog folds the re-advert `Unchanged`. A sharer restart / route-death re-advertises the SAME sealed advert (same `sent_unix_ms` + metadata) with only the `route_blob` rotated; the F3 identical-re-read arm folds `Unchanged` (`route_blob` lives in `discovered`, not the catalog), so the route-import path gated behind `change != CatalogChange::Unchanged` was skipped and the consumer stayed wedged on the dead route (share listed + `Unresolved`) — unrecoverable by the cadence repair (the record session was alive; only the imported route died), auto-reheal, manual re-download, or Refresh, until a consumer restart. The frontends now detect the blob change independently of the catalog verdict (`route_changed = discovered[id].route_blob != env.route_blob`) and re-import + stamp a fresh generation to arm the parked browse retry, preserving the share's `unresolved` flag; an identical re-read (no rotation) still folds with no generation churn (F3 / CRSH-ISC-21 preserved). Security (#152/#156): honoring the rotation does not reopen the hijack path — the announce reaches this point only after `share_binding_is_valid` (#156) and `verify_route_advert`, so `env.route_blob` is provably the id's verified owner's route (probe: gui + tui `crsh_isc_27_route_rotation_reimports_on_catalog_unchanged`; live wedge-heal via attended CRSH-ISC-12).
- [x] CRSH-ISC-28: The manual Refresh re-indexes each own published root and re-announces it, so a file added to a shared folder mid-session becomes fetchable by peers without a relaunch. The Veilid publish path hashes a root once at publish (`ShareContent::index_dir`), so a mid-session folder change is otherwise invisible until the next launch's auto-republish.
