On the user-initiated Refresh, the GUI re-sends `PublishShare` for every persisted own root (`state.persisted_published()`), which re-runs `index_dir` and re-announces under the SAME deterministic `share_id` (#156), so a fetcher folds the fresh manifest onto the existing catalog entry (no duplicate / dead second copy). The own-share list upserts by `share_id` (`upsert_own_share`) so repeated Refresh never duplicates an entry. Gated on Connected (no spurious "not connected" toast per root) and ridden only by the user-initiated Refresh (`on_refresh_shares` → `ResweepShares`), never the 3 s liveness poll (which sends the cheap local-only `RefreshShares`), so mid-session re-hashing is user-paced, not a background CPU storm (probe: gui `upsert_own_share_replaces_same_id_without_duplicating`; live add-file-then-Refresh via attended felt-test) (#195).

## Test Strategy

| surface | check | tool / probe |
|---------|-------|--------------|
| Per-ISC coverage | each MVP spec ISC has ≥1 registered integration test | `cargo xtask isc-coverage` (registry: `crates/daemonseed-integration-tests/src/isc_coverage.rs` — its `TOTAL` is the canonical ISC count; never hand-copied elsewhere) |
| Format | no unformatted code | `cargo fmt --all --check` |
| Lint | zero warnings | `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` |
| Tests | all green | `cargo test --workspace` |
| Wire codegen | committed snapshot matches | `cargo xtask check-proto` |
| MVP gate | 4-daemon end-to-end scenario passes | `cargo xtask mvp-gate` (portable-pty harness, 8-step transaction) |
| Anti-criteria | forbidden states fail closed | negative fixtures: replay, clock-skew, counter-rollback, handle-mismatch, uniform-close |
| Censorship survivability | first TLS record is generic-HTTPS-shaped on :443 | wire-shape fixtures (negative-allowlist model) |
| Resource floor | viable on Raspberry Pi 4 (4 GB) | Pi-4 bench |

The mechanized probe of record is `cargo xtask isc-coverage`, and the integration-test registry
(`isc_coverage::TOTAL` / `ISCS`) is the single source of truth for the ISC count and for what "covered"
means. No other file carries a hand-written ISC count. **Known coverage debt** (tracked follow-up): the
`covered` figure xtask reports is still a static lower-bound floor (`xtask::COVERED_ISCS`); a live tally
that walks the per-milestone `Coverage::register` calls is the deferred replacement. The denominator
(`TOTAL`) is no longer duplicated — xtask imports it from the registry crate.

## Features

Work decomposes into **milestones**: one milestone is one PR (multiple commits),
one SSH-signed release tag from M4a onward, and — for MVP-affecting work — a pass
of the 4-daemon end-to-end gate (`cargo xtask mvp-gate`; the Definition-of-Done
lives in `## Test Strategy`). The atomic unit of a feature is an ISC (`## Criteria`);
a milestone bundles the ISCs whose end-states it delivers.

This section is intentionally **not** a milestone roadmap or status table. That
history rots the moment it is hand-maintained in two places, so it lives only
where it cannot drift:

- **Shipped history** (which milestone delivered what, at which tag) →
  the SSH-signed **git tags** and **`CHANGELOG.md`** at the repository root.
- **Live ISC coverage** (how many ISCs are exercised) →
  `cargo xtask isc-coverage` (registry: `crates/daemonseed-integration-tests/src/isc_coverage.rs`).
- **In-progress / next-milestone** (planning) →
  the project lead's vault manifest (not committed to this repo).

The ISA is the frozen contract — Problem, Vision, Principles, Constraints,
Criteria, Out of Scope — that every milestone must honor. *What* shipped and
*when* is history; this file is about what must always hold.

## Decisions

- 2026-07-29: **The announcements pane orders newest-first by the operator's own `sent_unix_ms`, and that timestamp is deliberately NOT clamped (#237).** `OperatorSpace.posts` is a `BTreeMap` keyed by content-address hex, and the view was projected straight off `.values()` — so the pane rendered in *hash* order, which is arbitrary with respect to time and stable, hence wrong identically on every restart (the diagnostic in the report: it did not reorder on relaunch, ruling out a race). Fixed at the single construction site (`public_space_snapshot_event`) by sorting at render time; no change to the stored structure, no wire/proto/SemVer impact, MOTD untouched (it is a separate `Option<String>` field rendered in its own box, never a `posts` row). **Direction:** newest-first, not the chat transcript's oldest-first, because the pane is a top-anchored `Flickable` that never auto-scrolls — position 1 is what a reader sees without scrolling, and for a broadcast surface that should be the latest notice. **Tie-break:** the content-address slot key, ascending. Two announcements can genuinely share a `sent_unix_ms`, and an order that leaves them merely equal is not total — their relative position would be an artifact of the sort's internals and could differ between renders of the same set. The key is unique per post by construction, so appending it makes the order total and byte-identical every render. **The clamp question, decided against clamping.** The obvious move is to reuse `transcript::clamp_order_ms` (#131), which bounds an untrusted advisory `sent_unix_ms` into `[now − 24 h, now + 2 min]` so a forged stamp cannot pin the view. It is rejected here on two independent grounds. *(1) Different trust model.* A row exists in this pane only after `verify_served_post` accepted an ML-DSA-87 signature against the published operator whitelist (ISC-A-S3), so the value is not world-writable the way a lobby message's is: an unauthorized peer cannot place a post here at all, and a holder of the operator signing key can already write arbitrary content to this pane, so pinning its own post to the top is not an escalation over what it may already say. *(2) The window is the wrong shape for this content.* Announcements are long-lived **by design** — #141/#238 exist precisely to re-seed a standing announcement for as long as it stands — while the transcript window trusts only the last 24 h. Under a clamp, every announcement older than a day would collapse onto the same edge, tie, and fall back to content-hash order: the clamp would *re-introduce* the defect for exactly the posts that live longest. **Accepted residual:** an operator whose own clock is wrong mis-orders its own pane. Each row renders its UTC timestamp, so this is visible rather than silent, and it is not an adversarial case. If the write-gate ever widens beyond a single operator identity (the open fork in `docs/design/operator-space-management.md`), this decision must be re-taken — a multi-writer announce surface has the #131 threat model, not this one. **Unread dot is unaffected:** `state::item_content_hashes` sorts and dedups its output, so it is order-independent by construction and a re-ordered view cannot spuriously raise or clear the dot. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed · `AnnouncementsView` rustdoc (its "in served order" claim was stale) · this entry. No LAMA manifest change — nothing public changed signature.

- 2026-07-28: **The Veilid publish path serves from disk; ISC-A-C35 was claimed but not met on that path (#246).** ISC-C73 / ISC-A-C35 were closed at the M16 crown, and `hash_share` + `DiskShareContent` have existed since — but both front-ends' Veilid publish path called `ShareContent::index_dir`, so every published byte sat in a `MemoryChunkStore` for the session, `Arc`-held in the actor's served map. ISC-A-C35 forbids exactly that ("no publish- or serve-path operation may … hold an entire share's bytes in RAM"), so the criterion's `[x]` covered the CLI and the local path while the network path it most matters on did not satisfy it. Surfaced by a tester publishing a large music catalog and reporting the client's memory. **The fix is a source swap, not new serve logic:** `ServedShare` and `Command::ServeShare` now hold `Arc<dyn ChunkSource + Send + Sync>`, and both front-ends build a `ShareManifest` via `hash_share` and serve it through `DiskShareContent`. Dynamic dispatch over a type parameter because `ServedShare<C>` would force that parameter through `Command`, the actor loop, and the handle to keep one `HashMap<String, ServedShare>` uniform; one vtable hop against a disk read and an AEAD seal is immaterial. Each binary in fact serves only `DiskShareContent` now — the in-RAM source stays for tests and the CLI, which is why the boundary is a trait rather than a swap. `hash_share` rather than `indexer::cached_or_hash` because the Veilid path has no redb index to cache through. **Accepted consequence:** a published file that is moved, deleted, truncated, or grown after publish is refused at serve time (`ServeError::Io` / `ChunkModified`) where the in-RAM copy would have kept serving publish-time bytes — the fail-closed posture ISC-A-C35's own "cheap fail-closed checks" clause describes, and a re-publish is the way to serve changed content. A **same-length** in-place edit still passes the server's cheap checks and is rejected by the fetcher's per-chunk SHA-384 re-derivation (ISC-S28), exactly as `DiskShareContent::get_chunk` documents — the server never re-hashes at answer time. **Second half of the same criterion, caught in review:** ISC-A-C35 also requires every per-chunk disk read to run on a blocking thread, and `serve_loop` called `share::serve` inline under the registry lock — fine while the answer was an in-memory lookup, a runtime-worker stall once it became `File::open` + `seek` + `read_exact` on the hot path, holding the lock the actor's own `ServeShare`/`StopServe` and the advert watchdog's `last_served` also take. The serve step now runs in `spawn_blocking`, taking and releasing the lock inside it; a panicked blocking step replies `NOT_FOUND` (ISC-A-S21's offline-equivalent) rather than letting the fetcher burn its 5 s answer window. **ISC-A-C35 stays only partly met after this:** #154 tracks the remaining no-park gap — `PublishShare` / `SubscribeRendezvous` / `ResweepRendezvous` still run inline on the actor command loop — so the criterion's `[x]` should not be read as fully satisfied until that closes.

- 2026-07-28: **Operator announce keep-alive is operator-only, one slot per emission, on a jittered 45–75 min band (#238).** Three independent defects in one tick. **(1) Authority.** `collect_operator_keepalive_items` gated on `shares.operator.is_some()`, which means "subscribed to the announce record", not "is the operator" — `subscribe_operator_space` sets it for *every* client at connect. So every client re-published the operator's announcements. The record's owner seed is a well-known derivation every client computes, so possession of the write key never implied authority to use it; the keep-alive now takes the same `operator_write_enabled()` predicate that already gated the composer earlier in the same file (`upload_announcement`, `set_motd`). Integrity was never at risk (`apply_operator_item` verifies the F17 signature + content address before folding) — this is write volume and authority, not authenticity. **(2) Amplification.** Load landed on ONE record multiplied by fleet size × standing announcements, all byte-identical writes into the same content-addressed slots: at 3 announcements and 1000 clients, 1500 writes/min against WB-2's ~6/min empirical DHT-saturation anchor, crossing it at roughly 4 clients. Self-amplifying and inverted against success — worse precisely as adoption grows — and capable of destroying the availability the mechanism exists to protect. **(3) Cadence.** A fixed 120 s `tokio::time::interval` gives the record a recognisable period and phase-locks every holder into a thundering herd, which WB-1.2 / WB-3 I6 forbid for keepalives. **Decisions (caraka).** *Generous hosting by non-operators is rejected*: content evicting while no operator runs is a signal that an operator is not running, and a fleet-wide write subsidy makes that operational failure invisible. This forecloses nothing — the owner seed stays world-derivable, so fleet hosting can be reinstated later with no wire change. *The cadence is damped hard*, against measured evidence: announcement values survived **>24 h with nobody re-seeding**, so eviction pressure is orders of magnitude below what 120 s assumed. Band + one-slot-per-emission put the whole fleet at ~1 write/hour on the record, each slot refreshed every `N` hours (~8× margin over the observed floor at N=3, 4× at N=6, margin gone near N≈24 — a visible, tunable edge). The 24 h figure is a property of DHT load during that test, not a constant; the band is a named tunable in `daemonseed_core::public_space`. **Rejected alternatives:** slowing the cadence alone (divides but does not remove the fleet multiplier — 25× slower is still 10× over the saturation anchor at 1000 clients); probabilistic participation at `p≈1/M` from `PresenceTracker::len()` (works, but keeps a write subsidy caraka rejected on principle); operator-absence-gated hosting (**not implementable** — `MemberHeartbeat` carries no operator marker and no `.proto` names one, so "detect the operator on the lobby roster" needs a wire change of the same cost class as #136); Trickle-style suppression, RFC 6206 (the principled flat-load mechanism, but the sweep discards `ValueData.seq`, so "was this slot recently refreshed?" is not observable without new plumbing — recorded as the upgrade path if fleet hosting ever returns). **Also:** the cursor is key-based, not an index, because the announcement set changes between emissions and an index silently skips entries (the WB-ISC-14 trap); the write role is now stated in the subscribe trace, since under operator-only a launch that forgot `DAEMONSEED_OPERATOR=1` would otherwise silently stop re-seeding with nothing anywhere to say so; and the session's FIRST emission draws from a short jittered 2–5 min band, because a steady-band first emission would mean any operator session shorter than 45 min refreshed **nothing at all** — under operator-only that is the difference between the mechanism working and silently never running for an operator who works in short bursts (still jittered, so the first write is not a reliable session-start marker on the record). **Open, deferred to the operator-space design pass:** whether the gate is permanent or is subsumed by a cryptographic write-gate depends on an unratified fork between ISC-15's recorded offline-seed design and a root-signed whitelist of operator identity keys — see `docs/design/operator-space-management.md`. MOTD keep-alive remains excluded pending #136. **Review pass (3 independent lenses) — accepted and applied:** the session cursor started unseeded, so every launch re-walked the same key-order prefix and an operator with short sessions would refresh the head forever while the tail expired (now seeded from `random_announce_slot_cursor`); the actor is spawned at window construction, long before Connect, so the first deadline routinely landed with no record/posts/network and a wasted emission was charged a full steady band (an operator emission that finds nothing now retries on the short band); a failed keep-alive write was swallowed with no trace, which under one-slot-per-emission means a slot silently stops being refreshed for a whole rotation (now traced, and this write carries no WB-3 deadline so I6b does not rescue it — #166); `a_vanished_cursor_slot_resumes_at_the_next_key` used a cursor sorting below the whole set, where resume-at-next-key and restart-from-the-beginning coincide, so it could not fail for the trap it names (now uses a cursor strictly between two present slots); the jitter functions had no oracle at all (now band, ordering, cadence-rate and seed-variance tests, mirroring `presence::next_keepalive_interval` and `dm::keyrec`); and two rustdoc claims were false as written — the "whole fleet's write rate" figure holds only in RELEASE builds (debug makes every client an operator), and the first-emission jitter does NOT conceal the session-start marker, because the two bands are disjoint. **Accepted-and-recorded, not fixed here:** the MOTD exclusion is exact for the logical slot but NOT for the physical one — `current_state_subkey("motd")` is 21 and an announcement hashing there overwrites it (p≈1/64, pre-existing, mechanism filed as #134, scoped there to presence); `OperatorSpace.posts` is unbounded and world-writable, so rotation latency is `|posts| × band` with an attacker-influenced denominator; `posts` is rebuilt per session from the sweep with no persistence, so an adversarial overwrite while the operator is offline is now permanent where fleet re-publication previously restored it — the decision's eviction-only reasoning did not price adversarial overwrite; and WB-2 alone did not require the 30x damping (gate + one-slot-per-emission at the old 120 s already meets its 0.5/min operator allocation) — the >24 h retention measurement did. All four are carried in `docs/design/operator-space-management.md`. Test renamed `collect_operator_keepalive_items_excludes_the_mutable_motd` → `the_keepalive_excludes_the_mutable_motd`; the older Decisions/Verification entries naming the previous oracle are left as dated history. `daemonseed-veilid-net::schedule`'s WB-ISC-9 load model still enqueues this keepalive at 120 s — now conservative (it over-models real load), noted at the harness, not re-cut. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed · core LAMA manifest (`first_operator_keepalive_interval`, `next_operator_keepalive_interval`, `random_announce_slot_cursor` + the four band constants) · this entry · `docs/design/operator-space-management.md` + ROADMAP Designs.

- 2026-07-28: **Direct-messaging design FROZEN to DRAFT v6 (caraka ratified); DM ISC family re-cut in place to v6 (`docs/design/direct-messaging.md`).** Design + criteria only; no code/wire/proto change yet (the build slices carry the wire delta, strictly additive on the frozen v0.36.2 contract). The design was hardened over **8 adversarial panel rounds** (3 lenses each: crypto/replay, correlation/metadata, erasure/availability): R1–R2 refuted the original + revised *approaches*; R3–R4 refuted the v1/v2 *machinery* (broken single-chain ratchet, ring/epoch delivery); R5 was the first draft with no axis-refuting BLOCKER; R6 confirmed; R7 caught a first-contact-entry sizing BLOCKER + a decision-#4 arithmetic error; **R8 confirmed v6 clean — all three lenses "HOLDS, accepted residuals only."** **The frozen v6 construction:** per-contact pseudonym (in-seal bound to the long-term identity) over an ML-KEM-1024 encapsulation to the recipient's static published key; a **two-directional post-quantum double ratchet** (per-direction chains + bounded skipped-key cache + `(ratchet_gen,PN)` header + redundant `eph_ct`/generation) giving content forward secrecy after B's first reply; **per-page scattered addressing** `HKDF(AR‖dir‖page)` (K=16/`dflt(16)`) — paging fixed the write budget, and the AR-owner-gated records mean no third party can write/forge/erase the ongoing channel (the load-bearing win); a **sender-blind doorbell** (`dflt(32)`, recipient-keyed, sender-secret slot) holding a self-contained ~18 KB first-contact entry, admission A+B+C (PoW + grantee-bound one-time token + sender-blindness); `fc_epoch` wall-clock first-contact anti-replay; **fail-safe delivery** — three truthful states, an authenticated monotonic high-water + RLE gap-bitmap ack, never claims "delivered". **Decision #4 (budget) closes at ≈3.13/min** with a client-global aggregate ack cap. **caraka's ratified calls:** Option C over A; static-KEM; no DK-PoP (message-layer intended-recipient hash); addressing decoupled from the seal-key chain (content FS holds, address-linkability is not FS — accepted); wall-clock `fc_epoch`; **one-time PQ prekeys DROPPED** (opening burst has no FS — harvest-now recoverable — mitigated by user education: "the first DM is only hello"; a reserved `key_selector` field keeps a future prekey re-add wire-free); F1 lobby-rhythm, M1 rollback (+ world-writable key record), M11 recovery, the 32-slot concurrent-unknown-sender limit, MAX_SKIP bounded loss, ack-not-FS, universal address-plane harvest-now, opening-burst-no-FS all accepted as documented limits. **Build-contract items (folded into v6):** client-global aggregate ack cap (not per-channel); RLE monotonic gap-bitmap + prefix-advance-on-give-up; `msg_sig` as the sole PoP path symmetric for ACCEPT; top pad bucket ≤32768 + schema-derived write guard (fixes the latent `APP_MESSAGE_CAP` over-permit); jittered `fc_epoch`-boundary re-seal. **Forces #177.** Verified corrections vs the earlier direction re-cut: DFLT per-subkey cap = `min(32768, 1MiB/o_cnt)` (dflt(32)=32KiB, dflt(64)=16KiB, dflt(256)=4KiB); the local record store is count-UNCAPPED (`max_records: None`) — the earlier ~25-channel ceiling mis-cited `remote_max_records` and is withdrawn (bound = op-gate concurrency + #171 resweep latency). doc-sync: design doc FROZEN status + full v1–v6 lineage · ISA Criteria (DM family re-cut to v6) · CHANGELOG [Unreleased] · ROADMAP · this entry · build-slice issues spawned. **Supersedes the 2026-07-27 ADJUDICATED entry below** (which recorded the first-panel refutation and the direction re-cut).

- 2026-07-27: **Direct-messaging design ADJUDICATED but NOT frozen; DM ISC family re-cut to the Veilid *direction* (`docs/design/direct-messaging.md`; branch `docs/dm-design-of-record`).** Design-only; no code, no wire, no proto change. The forks were adjudicated, then a **3-lens adversarial panel (erasure/availability, correlation/metadata, crypto/replay) refuted the frozen candidate** — all three returned BLOCKER-class findings tracing to one root: the design priced *write authority* but never *retention* (eviction) or *read observability* (world-derivable addresses), and Fork 1C / stop-on-presence / the spam bound all rest on that conflation. The crypto lens additionally found the message seal construction does not decrypt (B1, circular: seal key needs `sender_pubkey`, placed inside the seal). **So the design-of-record is an adjudication record, not a ratified freeze; the reopened structural decisions (world-derivable-address oracle; availability erasure of first contact; the crypto construction) are caraka's call before any build.** What survives and is durable: the schema-sizing facts, the fork *verdicts* (C over A on the authorization axis, static-KEM over handshake, the WriteClass classification, offline store-and-forward as the shape), and the full enumerated findings any next iteration must answer — all in the design doc's § Adversarial-pass findings + § What must be resolved. The ISC family is re-cut to the DIRECTION only; the mechanism-specific criteria (C40–C43, A-C24) are marked provisional under the subheading. **The design in one sentence (the surviving shape):** a DM is a circle with one other person, keyed by encapsulating to the recipient's static published ML-KEM-1024 identity key instead of a shared phrase; delivery is sender-performed store-and-forward over three DHT records. **Fork 1 (write authority) → Option C (doorbell + sender-owned content)**, rejecting Option A (world-writable inbox): under A, Veilid's per-record all-or-nothing write authority means anyone can erase the inbox, and that erasure primitive *composes with the stop-on-presence rule* into silent targeted suppression at the attacker's optimal moment (recipient appears → sender stops re-seeding → attacker's sustained wipe wins the race, sender's UI honestly shows "delivered"). C splits the surfaces: a **key record** (`dflt(1)`, world-derivable owner from the recipient's ML-DSA pubkey, carries the signed monotonic-versioned EK), a **doorbell** (`dflt(256)`, world-writable, carries only fixed-size sealed pointers in a sender-secret-derived stable slot — not observer-computable, so no candidate-set who-DMs-whom attack), and a **sender-owned outbox** (`dflt(64)` append ring, owner seed from the sender's secret, no third party can write or erase). Option B (per-pair SMPL, whose member-range writes ARE erasure-proof — verified `veilid-core schema.rs:99-135`) recorded as the v2 consolidation target; not v1 because no SMPL path exists in the workspace. **Veilid DFLT `o_cnt` cap = 1024** (`schema/mod.rs:24`), but per-subkey value cap = `min(32768, 1MiB/o_cnt)` (`schema.rs:61-63`) — so `dflt(64)` caps at **16 KiB/subkey, not 32 KiB**; this answers #134's blocking unknown by formula (a dedicated presence schema takes `o_cnt ≤ 1MiB/padded_beacon`, ≈128 at the current ~7.4 KiB beacon) and surfaces a **latent guard defect**: `APP_MESSAGE_CAP=32768` over-permits the true 16 KiB cap on production `dflt(64)` records (`rendezvous.rs:258`) — file as its own fix. **Keep-alive ratified:** geometric backoff (~20 writes/week vs ~5000 flat), 7-day give-up → UI "undelivered". **Stop-on-presence split:** presence stops re-seeding for *established* contacts (recipient holds the erasure-proof outbox key) but NOT first contact (doorbell is erasable, so "seen present" ≠ "saw the pointer") — first contact re-seeds until inbound establishment or give-up. **WriteClass:** first user send = `Chat` (rank 1); every re-seed = `Keepalive` (rank 4), coalescing per-message; the retry loop lives in the persisted outbox ABOVE the scheduler. **WB-3 is NOT amended** — new writers in existing classes is *use* of the scheduler; scheduler-resident retries (which would be an amendment) are placed outside it. I6b does not apply (no hard DHT expiry in the DM path). **Wire delta is strictly additive** (3 new v1 messages, no existing record/field touched) — green-field on the frozen v0.36.2 wire; a DM to an old client ages to undelivered. **ISC re-cut in place, permanent IDs preserved** (C38–C46 / A-C20–A-C25); salvage honored — C44 contact cache, C45 explicit-accept (demoted from wire HELLO/ACCEPT to a local UI gate), C46 block list, A-C23 metadata scoping. **Forces #177** (client-ISC reconciliation, shared relay-era-residue cause). Adjudication hardened by a 3-lens adversarial panel (erasure/availability, correlation/metadata, crypto/replay) — findings dispositioned below at panel close. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Changed · ROADMAP Designs · ISA Criteria (DM family re-cut) · this entry. Build slices (key publication → outbox+doorbell → collection → UI) graduate into issues.

- 2026-07-21: **Download-subsystem redesign — step 9: max-review fixes (branch `feat/download-subsystem-redesign`).** The max-effort code review surfaced 14 findings; 11 fixed across five commits, 3 filed as issues (#210 GUI/TUI orchestration duplication, #211 folder-policy triplication, #212 per-chunk resume file-open). **Trust-surface fixes:** integrity dominance now holds across the whole fetch, not just the in-flight window — `download::drive_bounded` drives every file/chunk to a terminal result and short-circuits only on `Integrity` (DL-ISC-13); a manifest-fetch failure is classified by `FetchErrorClass` at the confirm seam so a hostile/malformed manifest poisons the share instead of parking a re-fetch (both frontends, DL-ISC-13); the per-route learned ceiling records once per route death via a `RouteWindow.collapsed` episode flag instead of ratcheting to floor under the death's concurrent failures (DL-ISC-2); `StagingArea::promote` recognizes a byte-identical pre-existing file as this download's own artifact and keeps it in place (never a `name-2` orphan) while refusing a symlink at the destination (DL-ISC-21); `ManifestDigestStore::open` serializes concurrent opens with an fs4 advisory flock (the redb `Database` field is dropped before the lock, so redb's exclusive lock frees first) — turning redb's fail-fast `DatabaseAlreadyOpen` into a serialized wait; `sweep_staging` is now invoked in production (download start, registry- and stored-manifest-gated) so unresumable `.dspart` debris is reclaimed (DL-ISC-22). **Placement:** the GUI carries the toggled folder's own manifest-relative path on `RootKind::Dir(String)` so a folder whose files nest deeper keeps its name (`common_prefix_dir` retired GUI-side; the TUI's cardinality-derived selection keeps it), and the GUI's managed-folder resolution fails closed on a `downloads.idx` read error (mirroring the TUI). **Cleanup:** the dead `rebase_to_selection_root` removed (superseded by `place_at_dest`); dangling `fetch_chunk` rustdoc links and four pre-existing core doc-link warnings cleared. **No wire/proto change** (`RootKind` is an in-process `NetCommand` field only). Approaches confirmed by an independent-Opus advisor pre-commit and two independent review lenses (correctness + adversarial) post-commit — all 11 CONFIRMED correct + regression-free, every adversarial attack REFUTED; the sole surviving observation (#213, cross-process pre-persist sweep window) is harmless best-effort and tracked. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] (Fixed/Removed) · core LAMA manifest · ISC-C68 · this entry.

- 2026-07-21: **Download-subsystem redesign — step 8b-2/9: TUI verified-resume parity (`daemonseed-tui`; branch `feat/download-subsystem-redesign`).** Mirrors the step-8b-1 GUI resume wiring onto the TUI download path (`veilid_net::run_confirm_download`), byte-for-byte behaviorally identical — no engine, core, or GUI change (all used as-is). **Profile-root handoff (the one TUI-specific piece):** rather than derive it from `DefineShare`'s `index_path` (which only arrives if the user defines a share, and never rides `Connect`), an explicit `profile_root: Option<PathBuf>` field is added to `NetCommand::Connect` — mirroring how 8b-1 added it to the GUI's `Connect` — populated in `main.rs` from the always-available unlocked `profile_root` (`Some(profile_root.clone())`; the TUI has no ephemeral path, but the field stays `Option` for GUI parity + fail-closed shape), held in `ShareState::profile_root` at the Connect handler, cloned into each `spawn_confirm_fetch` and threaded to `run_confirm_download`. The digest store lives at `profile_root/download-manifest-digests.redb` (the PRIVATE profile dir; new `const DIGEST_STORE_FILE`). **Fresh confirm (Part A):** record the confirmed manifest's SHA-384 in a per-op-opened-and-dropped `ManifestDigestStore` (redb exclusive lock — dropped before the long download), THEN `staging.persist_manifest(&manifest)` (the confirmed manifest with SHARE rel_paths) — record-then-persist, so a stored manifest never exists without its anchor; skipped when no profile (no anchor → a later resume re-downloads fresh). **Resume (Part B, fail-closed, DL-ISC-20):** a re-initiated ConfirmFetch whose `staging.read_stored_manifest().is_ok()` HALTS (→ `ConfirmOutcome::LocalFailed`) on any of, in order — no `profile_root`, store-open `Err`, `get_digest→Ok(None)` (missing anchor — never resume unanchored), `get_digest→Err`, `verify_stored_manifest→Err` (tampered/swapped), or the re-fetched LIVE `manifest != stored` (content changed — and this branch `staging.destroy()` + re-opens the store + `remove(share_id)` so a re-open is a clean fresh download, not an infinite re-halt). Else the placement source is the STORED manifest (re-pathed via the SAME `selection_roots`/`place_at_dest`/`resolve_share_folder`, `size`+`chunks` verbatim), `derive_resume_state` splits per file: `done_at_dest = fr.promoted_complete` (the CONTENT-checked flag, NOT a length check) → counted done; else `to_run` with `PlannedFile { already_verified: fr.verified, .. }`; the engine fetches only the missing chunks, seeded with the resumed-verified count into `FetchProgress`; an all-done resume `staging.destroy()`s and completes without touching the network. The managed-dir `downloads.idx` records EVERY selected file (done + to-run) via `idx_recs`. **Preserved TUI-specific behavior (NOT regressed to the GUI):** the managed-folder resolution keeps step-6 Fix #1 (a `FetchedStore` open/list failure → `LocalFailed`, never `unwrap_or_default()`), and a successful managed registration still emits a `FetchedShares` snapshot to auto-refresh the browse pane. **No new DL-ISC (DL-ISC-12/20 cover it); no wire/proto change** — `profile_root`/`RootKind` are in-process `NetCommand` fields only (binary crate → no LAMA manifest change); `daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL`/coverage unchanged at 110/239. **Coverage:** the resume flow is inline in the network-gated `run_confirm_download` (not an extractable pure fn — matching the GUI, whose 8b-1 added ZERO frontend unit tests), so its behavior is covered by the shared-engine tests (`download::tests::resume_fetches_only_missing_chunks_and_promotes` / `resume_with_all_chunks_verified_promotes_without_fetching`) and the core tests (`derive_resume_state` suite), which both frontends drive identically; the step-6 TUI seam tests (`selection_roots_map_node_kind_to_placement_roots`, `download_outcome_maps_to_confirm_outcome`, `fold_integrity_failed_poisons_without_mark_or_park`, `a_panicking_download_worker_yields_a_terminal_outcome`) remain green. DoD gate green: fmt · clippy `--workspace --all-targets` (+ `-p daemonseed-gui --features desktop`) · `test --workspace` (204 tui tests) · check-proto · isc-coverage 110/239. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Added · this entry.
- 2026-07-21: **Download-subsystem redesign — step 8b-1/9: resume wiring (engine + GUI) (`daemonseed-veilid-net` + `daemonseed-gui`).** Wires the 8a verified-resume core into the download path. **Engine:** `download::PlannedFile` gains `already_verified: Vec<usize>` (fresh = empty, behavior + tests unchanged); `run_download`/`fetch_one_file` SKIP fetching+writing those chunks (already staged from a prior attempt) — seeded into progress + counted toward completeness — and `preallocate` opens an existing partial WITHOUT truncating, so staged bytes survive into the promote. **GUI fresh confirm:** record the confirmed manifest's SHA-384 in a `ManifestDigestStore` at `profile_root/download-manifest-digests.redb` (the PRIVATE profile dir, opened per-op-and-dropped before the download — redb exclusive lock), THEN `persist_manifest` (record-then-persist → a stored manifest never exists without its anchor). **GUI resume (fail-closed, DL-ISC-20):** a re-initiated ConfirmFetch whose staging carries a stored manifest HALTS (LocalFailed) on any of — no unlocked profile, store-open error, `get_digest→None` (no anchor), `get_digest→Err`, `verify_stored_manifest→Err` (tampered/swapped), or the re-fetched LIVE `manifest != stored` (content changed) — else it re-paths the digest-verified STORED manifest into placement space (same `selection_roots`/`place_at_dest`, `size`+`chunks` verbatim), runs `derive_resume_state`, and fetches only the missing chunks against the STORED addresses; re-verified chunks count toward `FetchProgress`. No new DL-ISC (DL-ISC-12/20 cover it); no wire/proto change; `daemonseed-tui` gets a 1-field compile-default (`already_verified: Vec::new()`, identical fresh behavior; TUI resume is 8b-2). **Post-build xhigh review (2 independent lenses) — the security core held (every bypass hypothesis refuted: planted/tampered manifest → HALT, crash windows safe, per-op store, no mis-surfaced success); 3 fixes folded:** (1) **HIGH location-mismatch** — `derive_resume_state` had ORed the promoted-dest + staging locations into ONE `verified` set, but the engine promotes STAGING, so a promoted-only-verified chunk would be skipped and promoted as a SPARSE ZERO-HOLE (corrupt content as Complete). FIXED: `derive_resume_state` is now STAGING-authoritative for `verified`/`missing` (a promoted-only chunk is `missing`/re-fetched — no hole), with a separate CONTENT-checked `promoted_complete` flag driving the done-at-dest fast-path (also closing a length-only-trust gap on the dest file); GUI `done_at_dest = fr.promoted_complete`. (2) **content-changed DEAD-END** — the `manifest != stored` halt now `staging.destroy()` + `store.remove(share_id)` so a re-open is a clean fresh download (was an infinite re-halt with misleading guidance). (3) doc — `derive_resume_state`'s guarantee softened to "re-verifies AT USE" (the derive→promote window shares a fresh download's write→promote co-resident exposure — within the accepted threat model; F3 planted-staging DoS noted, self-healing). **Coverage:** engine `resume_fetches_only_missing_chunks_and_promotes` / `resume_with_all_chunks_verified_promotes_without_fetching`; core `derive_resume_does_not_skip_promoted_only_chunks` / `derive_resume_does_not_trust_a_garbage_dest_by_length` (+ `reads_a_promoted_file` updated for the location split). doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Added · this entry. **This amends the step-8a `derive_resume_state` two-location description below (that OR was the location-mismatch bug fixed here).** 8b-2 (TUI mirror) pending.
- 2026-07-21: **Download-subsystem redesign — step 8a/9: verified-resume CORE (`daemonseed-core::storage`; branch `feat/download-subsystem-redesign`).** Per the ratified design §Part 3, the fail-closed content-integrity-at-rest primitives that a resume re-derives against — pure fs + redb, no network, fully unit-testable. **Stored manifest:** `serialize_stored_manifest` gives the confirmed manifest a DETERMINISTIC canonical byte string (fixed field order, big-endian length-prefixed, slice-order iteration, no map — so identical input → identical bytes; a magic+version prefix fails an old file closed); `StagingArea::persist_manifest`/`read_stored_manifest` write/read it via a temp-then-rename inside the fetch's staging area at `<root>/.dspart/<share_id>/.dspart/manifest` — a **judgment call**: storing it under the reserved `.dspart` component (which `sanitize_rel_path` already refuses in any `final_rel`, DL-ISC-18) makes it collision-proof against any staged file with ZERO new content-placement collateral (a global `.manifest` reservation would have failed placement of a legit share file named `.manifest`), and `destroy()`/the sweep clean it with the area. A missing/unparseable stored manifest is a typed error, never a silent empty (fail-closed). **Digest anchor (DL-ISC-20):** `manifest_digest` = SHA-384 over the serialization (reusing `oxicrypt_sha::sha384`, not hand-rolled); `storage::manifest_digest::ManifestDigestStore` (new module, mirroring `share_index`'s redb `open`/`put`/`get`/`remove` + boxed-`redb::Error` discipline, new table `download-manifest-digests-v1`, key `share_id` → 48-byte value) keeps it in the profile's OWN trusted state — not the co-resident-writable downloads root — so a co-resident tamper of the staging copy plus a colluding sharer cannot bypass the confirm gate. No encryption on the value (a digest is not secret; the store anchors integrity, not confidentiality). `verify_stored_manifest` reads the staging copy, re-serializes+re-digests, constant-time-compares to the anchor, and returns the manifest ONLY on a match — a tampered/swapped/missing manifest halts. **Re-derivation (DL-ISC-12):** `derive_resume_state(manifest, dest_root, staging) -> ResumePlan{files: Vec<FileResume{rel_path,size,total_chunks,verified,missing}>}` re-hashes each chunk region — boundaries from `CHUNK_SIZE` + the stored `size`, never file-self-describing — at BOTH the promoted final file and the staged partial; a chunk is present iff either re-verifies against its content address (per-chunk SET semantics, no prefix assumption — a `{0,2}`-present `{1,3}`-missing subset re-derives exactly). Mismatch/short/sparse/unreadable → MISSING (discard+refetch); all-verified file → Complete. **Signature judgment call:** `derive_resume_state`'s entries must carry the file's placement-resolved on-disk `rel_path` (what was staged/promoted under) — the caller (step 8b) re-paths the digest-verified confirmed manifest into placement space before calling, because the STORED manifest holds share rel_paths (so DL-ISC-20's re-fetched-vs-stored compare works against the sharer's manifest) while re-derivation locates bytes at the FINAL layout. **NOT wired** into the confirm/fold/frontends (step 8b): no `run_download`, veilid-net, or GUI/TUI change here. Registers DL-ISC-12 pos + DL-ISC-20 neg (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 237→239; pos 157→158; neg 80→81; unit-covered in core, not integration-registered, so `COVERED` unchanged at 110 → coverage 110/239). No wire/proto change. **Coverage:** `storage::fetched::tests::{stored_manifest_serialization_is_deterministic_and_round_trips, stored_manifest_deserialize_is_fail_closed, persist_and_read_stored_manifest_round_trips, read_stored_manifest_missing_is_an_error, verify_stored_manifest_accepts_the_matching_digest, verify_stored_manifest_rejects_a_tampered_staging_manifest, _rejects_a_swapped_manifest, _rejects_a_missing_staging_manifest, derive_resume_returns_verified_and_missing_as_a_set, _classifies_a_tampered_partial_as_missing, _on_a_fully_staged_file_is_complete, _reads_a_promoted_file, derive_resume_empty_file_is_complete}` + `storage::manifest_digest::tests::*`. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Added · this entry · ISA Criteria (DL-ISC-12/20) · isc registry (count + ledger) · daemonseed-core LAMA manifest (`StagingArea` methods, `ManifestDigestStore`, `verify_stored_manifest`, `derive_resume_state`, `ResumePlan`/`FileResume`, `manifest_digest`). **Post-build xhigh review (2 independent lenses):** the CORE is fail-closed — serialization injectivity (digest binding), fail-closed verify, and the re-hash-only path to `verified` all CONFIRMED; the plaintext digest store leaks nothing beyond the already-plaintext `downloads.idx`/staging (privacy verdict acceptable, now cited in the module doc). **One real defect FOLDED (F1):** `derive_resume_state` did not check that the chunk list tiles `[0, size)`, so a hostile `{size: 2*CHUNK_SIZE, chunks: [one]}` (uncovered sparse tail) OR a co-resident APPEND past `size` on a promoted file could report Complete with unverified bytes — fixed with a coverage guard (`chunks.len() == size.div_ceil(CHUNK_SIZE)` → else all-missing, mirroring the engine's DL-ISC-11) + an exact-length gate (a promoted/staged file is trusted only if `file_len == size`) + `saturating_add` in `chunk_region`. Oracles added: `derive_resume_rejects_an_undersized_chunk_list` + `derive_resume_rejects_an_appended_promoted_file`. **Carried to step 8b (wiring preconditions — the 8b review MUST gate these):** (A) HALT on `ManifestDigestStore::get_digest → None` + place the store in the PRIVATE profile dir (not the co-resident-writable downloads root); (B) redb's exclusive lock means GUI+TUI can't concurrently open the store — decide open-per-op vs a `downloads.idx`-style advisory lock (#208); and the placement re-path must carry `size`+`chunks` VERBATIM from the digest-verified manifest while enforcing DL-ISC-20's re-fetched-vs-stored compare in 8b.
- 2026-07-20: **Download-subsystem redesign — step 7/9: retire the legacy fetch windows (`daemonseed-veilid-net`).** Both frontends now fetch through the budget-admitted seam (steps 5/6), so the legacy window-parameter chunk path is dead and removed: `share::fetch_chunk` + `VeilidNetHandle::fetch_chunk` + their two LAMA manifest entries + the two now-redundant window-path tests (`serve_then_fetch_round_trip_with_fragmentation` + `fragments_pipeline_concurrently_and_reassemble_in_order` #109 — their manifest + multi-fragment-chunk reassembly + bounded-fragment-concurrency coverage is retained by `share::tests::budgeted_fetch_reassembles_and_admits_through_the_route_budget` (~20 fragments, peak in-flight >1 and ≤ `W_CEIL`) + the `fetch_manifest` tests). **KEPT:** `share::fetch_sealed` (still shared by the non-budgeted `fetch_manifest`, which the browse PREVIEW uses in both frontends — exact parity), `FRAGMENT_FETCH_CONCURRENCY` (const `2`, feeds `fetch_manifest`), and the `AimdWindow` type in `aimd.rs` (per the design — unused by the fetch path now, retained). No wire/proto change; no ISC change (mechanical removal). Oracle: grep-clean (no surviving `fetch_chunk(` caller) + full workspace gate. **Tracked follow-up (NOT this step — a DoS-invariant capacity re-derivation deserving xhigh; the invariant is still SAFE as-is):** the #125 serve-lane bound (`actor.rs` `SINGLE_FETCHER_PEAK_FRAGMENTS = 8*8` → `SERVE_QUEUE_CAP >= 64`) still references the retired twin-window arithmetic; under the budget the single-fetcher peak (≤ `W_CEIL`/route, ≤ `G_GLOBAL` global ≈ 12) is LOWER, so `SERVE_QUEUE_CAP = 128` stays over-provisioned and the invariant holds — but the derivation should be re-expressed under the budget model. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Removed · this entry · veilid-net LAMA manifest (2 entries removed).
- 2026-07-20: **Download-subsystem redesign — step 6/9: TUI engine adoption / parity (`daemonseed-tui`; branch `feat/download-subsystem-redesign`).** Per the ratified design §step 6, the TUI download path (`veilid_net::run_confirm_download`) is rewritten onto the SAME shared engine as the GUI (step 5), so both frontends drive one `run_download` — no behaviour is re-implemented. **Parity, not new contract:** step 6 adds NO DL-ISC IDs (DL-ISC-8/13/14 are frontend-agnostic, registered at step 5); `daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` is unchanged (110/237 coverage held). **Selection roots (DL-ISC-8):** `NetCommand::ConfirmFetch` gains `root_kind: RootKind` (mirrors the GUI's `net::RootKind`), replacing the shape-guessing `rebase_to_selection_root`. The TUI preview is a FLAT manifest-row list (no folder tree), so — a judgment call recorded here — the node kind is a function of the selection cardinality, derived in `main.rs`: `selected: None` → `Share`, one row → `File`, several → `Dir`; the net side maps it to `SelectionRoot`s (`selection_roots` + `common_prefix_dir`, byte-identical to the GUI) and `place_at_dest` lands each per the DL-ISC-7 table. A whole-share download to a chosen dest now keeps full rel_path ancestry (the intended fix, matching the GUI — the old rebase stripped the common ancestor). **Engine drive:** the worker leases the per-route budget ONCE (keyed by the discovered route's verified `sender_pubkey`, now copied onto the TUI's `DiscoveredRoute` + populated at the advert fold), imports the route, `fetch_manifest_budgeted`, then `run_download` — verified chunks stage-then-promote (the per-chunk `sanitize_rel_path`/`File::create`/`write_all`/`cleanup_written` loop AND the static `FRAGMENT_FETCH_CONCURRENCY` window retired IN THE TUI; `aimd.rs`/veilid-net legacy-window retirement is step 7). **Outcome mapping (dictated):** `confirm_outcome_from` maps `DownloadOutcome::{Complete→Complete, TransientFailed→RouteFailed (mark+park), IntegrityFailed→IntegrityFailed, LocalFailed→LocalFailed}`; the new `ConfirmOutcome::IntegrityFailed` fold sets a durable `poisoned_shares` flag + emits `FetchError` with NO Unresolved mark and NO parked retry (DL-ISC-13). **Panic seam (#207 / DL-ISC-14):** `spawn_confirm_task` now takes a `FnOnce → Fut` + `on_panic` and drives the non-`Send` engine future on a dedicated current-thread runtime on a `spawn_blocking` thread under `catch_unwind` — still fully OFF the actor loop (#197); the browse seam `spawn_fetch_task` (a `Send` future) gains the JoinHandle→JoinError→`on_panic` form. **Managed dir + two fixes:** `FetchedStore::register_share` records the `downloads.idx` entry off-loop after promotion (folder via the `pub(crate)` `net::resolve_share_folder`, reused not copied); on success the worker still emits a `FetchedShares` snapshot so the browse pane auto-refreshes (retained TUI UX). **Fix #1 (TUI does better than the GUI):** a store open/list failure while resolving the managed folder now returns `LocalFailed` — never `unwrap_or_default()` (which would mis-resolve/orphan a re-fetch's folder). **Fix #2 (documented, deferred):** the concurrent-same-display-name folder race (two distinct shares with identical names both read `existing` before either registers → both pick the unsuffixed folder) is named in a `net::resolve_share_folder` code comment + tracked; it is NOT silent data loss (promote is no-clobber, DL-ISC-21 — colliding files collision-suffix, only the folder identity is shared), and a correct fix (atomic folder reservation under the idx lock) is deferred rather than shipped half-baked. **Retired dead TUI wiring:** `net::{sanitize_rel_path, render_downloads_idx, cleanup_written, hex_lower}` + their now-orphaned tests removed (superseded by the core engine's `StagingArea`/`place_at_dest`/`register_share`). **Coverage (mirrors the step-5 suite):** tui `selection_roots_map_node_kind_to_placement_roots` / `common_prefix_dir_is_the_parent_directory_prefix` / `download_outcome_maps_to_confirm_outcome` / `fold_integrity_failed_poisons_without_mark_or_park` / `a_panicking_download_worker_yields_a_terminal_outcome`; the actor `crsh_isc_29`/`crsh_isc_8`/`crsh_isc_7` seam tests updated for the new `on_panic` seams. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Changed · this entry (no ISC criterion/registry change — parity). **Not a public-signature change** (`daemonseed-tui` is a binary crate; the `NetCommand`/`RootKind` additions are in-process only) → no LAMA manifest update.
- 2026-07-20: **Download-subsystem redesign — step 5/9: GUI engine adoption (`daemonseed-gui`; branch `feat/download-subsystem-redesign`).** Per the ratified design §step 5, the GUI download path (`veilid_net::run_confirm_download`) is rewritten onto the shared engine. **Selection roots (DL-ISC-8):** `NetCommand::ConfirmFetch` gains `root_kind: RootKind` (`Share`/`File`/`Dir`), threaded from the browse tree's `FetchTarget` (a folder subtree stays `Dir` even when it holds one file); the net side maps it to `SelectionRoot`s (`Share` → whole-share root, `File` → the file, `Dir` → the selected files' common parent-dir prefix) and `place_at_dest` lands each per the DL-ISC-7 table. **Engine drive:** the worker leases the per-route budget ONCE (keyed by the discovered route's verified `sender_pubkey`, copied onto `DiscoveredRoute`), imports the route, fetches the manifest budgeted, then runs `run_download` — verified chunks stage-then-promote (whole-file RAM buffering retired, #207). **Outcome mapping (dictated):** `DownloadOutcome::{Complete→Complete, TransientFailed→RouteFailed (mark+park), IntegrityFailed→IntegrityFailed, LocalFailed→LocalFailed}`; the new `ConfirmOutcome::IntegrityFailed` fold sets a durable `poisoned_shares` flag and emits `FetchError` with NO Unresolved mark and NO parked retry (DL-ISC-13; the engine already destroyed the staged partials). **Panic seam (#207 / DL-ISC-14):** both spawn seams yield a terminal outcome on a worker panic. **Runtime note (judgment call):** the engine future is not `Send` (a cross-crate higher-ranked inference limitation, rustc #99492 — box-coercion did NOT rescue it), so `spawn_confirm_task` runs the download to completion on a dedicated current-thread runtime on a `spawn_blocking` thread — still fully OFF the actor loop (#197), the actor's workers serve the chunk `app_call`s concurrently. **Managed dir:** `FetchedStore::register_share` (new, idx-only, no byte re-buffer) records the `downloads.idx` entry off-loop after promotion; folder resolved by a GUI copy of `resolve_share_folder` (the tui helper is `pub(crate)`). The GUI's own `net::sanitize_rel_path` is retired (path hygiene now lives in the core engine). ISC-A-C31 reworded verbatim (same ID). Registers DL-ISC-8 pos + DL-ISC-14 pos + DL-ISC-13 neg (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 234→237; pos 155→157; neg 79→80). **Known deferrals:** stored-manifest persistence / resume (DL-ISC-20, steps 5c/8); the incomplete-downloads UI affordance (steps 5c/8); a `Dir` selection whose files share a deeper single sub-path lands at that sub-path (the clicked folder path is not carried — the common-prefix rule fixes the reproduced one-file-folder + scattered defects but is not the full folder identity); managed-dir re-fetch overwrite semantics (the engine's promote is no-clobber per DL-ISC-21 — the managed path is GUI-unreachable today since the GUI always sends `flat_dest: true`, exercised at step 6 TUI parity). **Coverage:** gui `selection_roots_map_node_kind_to_placement_roots` / `common_prefix_dir_is_the_parent_directory_prefix` / `download_outcome_maps_to_confirm_outcome` / `fold_integrity_failed_poisons_without_mark_or_park` / `a_panicking_download_worker_yields_a_terminal_outcome` + the updated `fetch_target_*` root-kind assertions; core `register_records_idx_without_writing_bytes`. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Added/Changed · this entry · ISA Criteria (ISC-A-C31 reword + DL-ISC-8/13/14) · isc registry (count + ledger) · daemonseed-core LAMA manifest (`register_share`). **Post-build xhigh review (2 independent lenses):** the mutex-poison→process-abort hypothesis was refuted by probe (the `RouteBudget` mutex is never held across an `.await`); three fixes folded — poison-tolerant `RouteLease`/`BudgetPermit` `Drop` (`unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner)`, hardening the DL-ISC-14 panic-survival guarantee against any future panic-under-lock), a managed-dir idx-registration failure now downgrades `Complete`→`LocalFailed` (no silent promoted-but-unindexed success), and the panic-seam fallback no longer mislabels a runtime-build failure as a panic.

- 2026-07-20: **Step 4c/4d review-fixes (`daemonseed-core::storage::fetched`).** A two-agent mini-review of `2be92e1` (registry/sweep) + `876c57e` (idx-lock) — a bug lens and an adversarial-security lens — found three issues and empirically refuted the scariest attacks. **Fixed:** (1) `LiveFetchRegistry` used a `HashSet` not a refcount — overlapping registrations of one `share_id` (a re-fetch overlap) would unregister early when either guard dropped, so a sweep could delete a still-in-flight fetch's staging; now a refcounted `HashMap<String, usize>` (conf 85). (2) `IdxLock::acquire` followed a symlink at `.idx.lock` — a co-resident attacker could redirect it to a different inode and defeat the cross-process serialization; now refuses a symlink at the lock path (a residual check-then-open window remains; atomic `O_NOFOLLOW` is tracked) (conf 60). (3) the sweep's "closes the TOCTOU" claim overstated the code (the registry check and delete are not atomic); softened to an honest caller-ordering contract in the code comment + this criterion — the step-5/6 wiring must run the sweep where no registration races it (conf 30–45, dead code). **Empirically REFUTED** (adversarial agent ran standalone programs on rustc 1.95): destructive-sweep-via-symlink (post-CVE-2022-21658 `remove_dir_all` never follows a symlink out of `.dspart`), hex-decode registry bypass (the component is a true bijection), and IdxLock crash-safety (OS releases on death) — all hold. Known accepted trade-off: a deliberately long-lived lock holder can DoS `record_share` (any advisory-lock design). No API/wire change, no ISC count change (strengthens DL-ISC-9/22). **Coverage:** `registry_refcounts_overlapping_registrations` + `idx_lock_refuses_a_symlink`. doc-sync: this entry + DL-ISC-22 criterion softened.

- 2026-07-20: **Download-subsystem redesign — step 4d/9: cross-process `downloads.idx` file lock (`daemonseed-core::storage::fetched`; branch `feat/download-subsystem-redesign`).** Per the ratified design §Part 2 (#208), the idx read-modify-write must serialize across the separate GUI and TUI binaries (an in-process mutex cannot). **New:** a private `IdxLock` guard takes an exclusive OS advisory lock (`flock`/`LockFileEx` via the `fs4` crate) on a `<root>/.idx.lock` sibling for the whole `record_share` RMW, re-reading the manifest UNDER the lock so a concurrent writer cannot lose an entry via a stale snapshot; the OS releases the lock on drop or process death (a crash cannot wedge it). **Dependency: `fs4` added** (caraka approved — daemonseed is not the dependency-conscious project, oxicrypt is; and a cross-platform advisory lock is likely reusable). Workspace dep `fs4 = { version = "0.13", features = ["sync"] }`; the std API is `fs4::fs_std::FileExt::lock_exclusive`. No public API change (`record_share` signature unchanged), no wire/proto change. Registers DL-ISC-9 anti (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 233→234; neg 78→79). **Coverage:** `storage::fetched::tests::concurrent_record_share_never_loses_an_entry` (8 concurrent `record_share`s via separate stores → all land). doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Added · this entry · ISA Criteria DL-ISC-9 · isc registry (count + ledger). **STEP 4 COMPLETE** (4a placement · 4b staging · 4b-fix · 4c registry/sweep · 4d idx-lock); next is step 5 (gui scheduler adoption — the ISC-A-C31 reword lands there).

- 2026-07-20: **Download-subsystem redesign — step 4c/9: live-fetch registry + staging sweep (`daemonseed-core::storage::fetched`; branch `feat/download-subsystem-redesign`).** Per the ratified design §Part 3, a startup/idle sweep reclaims UNRESUMABLE staging debris (e.g. a crash before the confirmed manifest persisted) without ever deleting a registered or resumable fetch's state. **New:** `LiveFetchRegistry` (a cheap-to-clone shared `share_id` set) with `register` → a drop-guard; `sweep_staging(root, &registry, is_resumable)` deletes each `<root>/.dspart/<hex-share_id>` dir that is NEITHER registered NOR resumable (the caller's `is_resumable` = a stored confirmed manifest exists). A resume registers before the sweep can run (closes the TOCTOU); a directory whose name is not a valid staging component (not hex) is foreign and left untouched (never deletes on a name pattern alone). The registry is IN-PROCESS: cross-process/other-client active staging is protected by its stored manifest (resumable), not the registry. NOT yet wired to the frontends (steps 5/6). No wire/proto change. Registers DL-ISC-22 anti (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 232→233; pos 155; neg 77→78). **Coverage:** `storage::fetched::tests::sweep_reclaims_only_unregistered_unresumable_debris` / `sweep_keeps_a_registered_resume` / `sweep_leaves_foreign_dirs` / `sweep_empty_root_is_noop` / `registry_guard_unregisters_on_drop`. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Added · this entry · ISA Criteria DL-ISC-22 · isc registry (count + ledger) · daemonseed-core LAMA manifest (`LiveFetchRegistry`/`sweep_staging`). The idx cross-process file-lock (DL-ISC-9) follows as 4d (flock per the ratified design — caraka approved the dependency).

- 2026-07-20: **Step 4b review-fixes: harden the staging guard, no-clobber promote, and share_id namespace (`daemonseed-core::storage::fetched`).** A per-commit two-agent mini-review of `f43192b` found four real issues (both an independent bug lens and an adversarial-security lens converged). **Fixed:** (1) the `.dspart` refusal was byte-exact/case-sensitive — a hostile manifest could alias the quarantine on Windows/macOS via `.DSPART` / `.dspart.` (Win32 strips trailing dots); now case-folded + trailing-dot/space-stripped (conf 70). (2) `promote`'s `exists()`-then-`rename` was TOCTOU and `std::fs::rename` REPLACES on both platforms, so a concurrent promote could clobber (defeating DL-ISC-21); now an atomic `create_new` reservation of the target slot closes the race — a losing racer advances to the next suffix (conf 75). (3) `safe_folder_name(share_id)` is many-to-one (`a/b` and `a_b` both fold to `a_b`), so two distinct share_ids could share one staging area and cross-contaminate; the staging component is now injective `hex(share_id)` (conf 55). (4) `write_verified_chunk` gained a bounds check — a chunk whose offset+len exceeds the pre-sized file is refused, not silently grown (conf 35). Traversal guards otherwise confirmed to HOLD (final_rel escape, `destroy` scope, promote-target, symlink). No API/wire change, no ISC count change (strengthens the already-registered DL-ISC-18/21). **Coverage:** `staging_guard_rejects_case_and_trailing_dot_variants` / `distinct_share_ids_get_distinct_staging` / `write_rejects_out_of_range_offset` / `promote_to_a_fresh_name_lands_directly`. doc-sync: this entry.

- 2026-07-20: **Download-subsystem redesign — step 4b/9: stage-then-promote staging writer (`daemonseed-core::storage::fetched`; branch `feat/download-subsystem-redesign`).** Per the ratified design §Part 3, add `StagingArea` — a fetch's reserved quarantine at `<root>/.dspart/<share_id>/<final-rel>`: `preallocate` pre-sizes a sparse file, `write_verified_chunk` writes a VERIFIED chunk at its manifest-derived byte offset (a positional `pwrite`, so concurrent chunk writes at distinct offsets are race-free; partial state is a SET of verified chunks, not a prefix), `promote` moves a completed file to `<root>/<final-rel>` and `destroy` erases the whole area (the integrity-abort disposition). The reserved `.dspart` component is now refused by `sanitize_rel_path` (a hostile manifest can neither reach into the quarantine nor collide with a partial). Promotion is **no-clobber** — it lands at the first free `name`/`name-N` slot (suffix before the extension), so a plain cross-platform `std::fs::rename` to a guaranteed-fresh target suffices; the overwrite-if-provably-ours replace path (Windows `ReplaceFileW`) is deferred to step 8 (resume), where a promote may legitimately replace its own prior partial. NOT yet called by the scheduler (steps 5/6). No wire/proto change. Registers DL-ISC-11 pos + DL-ISC-18/21 anti (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 229→232; pos 154→155; neg 75→77; DL-ISC-18 registers here with its full end-state — the placement half was guarded at 4a). **Coverage:** `storage::fetched::tests::staging_writes_at_offsets_then_promotes` / `staging_namespace_is_refused_in_manifest_paths` / `promote_never_clobbers_a_preexisting_file` / `destroy_erases_the_staging_area`. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Added · this entry · ISA Criteria DL-ISC-11/18/21 · isc registry (count + ledger) · daemonseed-core LAMA manifest (`StagingArea` + `STAGING_DIR`). 4c (idx file-lock + registry/sweep) follows.

- 2026-07-20: **Download-subsystem redesign — step 4a/9: placement as a stated total function (`daemonseed-core::storage::fetched`; branch `feat/download-subsystem-redesign`).** Per the ratified design §Part 2, the old `rebase_to_selection_root` GUESSES the user's intent from path shapes (longest common prefix), which discards the actual selection — a folder holding exactly one file collapsed to the bare filename, and a scattered selection recreated the full share-internal ancestry (both reproduced). **Fix at ingestion:** carry the user's *selection roots* (the tree nodes toggled, ISC-C72) as a new `SelectionRoot::{File,Dir}` (the empty `Dir` = the whole share) and make placement a total function of them — `place_at_dest(roots, selected_files) -> Vec<PlacedFile>`: roots are normalized (nested subsumed, duplicates dropped); each file maps to its deepest governing root; a `File` root drops to its basename, a `Dir` root keeps its own name + subpath (arrives whole, ratified item 2), the whole-share root keeps full rel_paths; between-roots basename collisions suffix the later root (`name-2`). Every computed destination-relative path passes `sanitize_rel_path` as ONE unit before return (the placement half of DL-ISC-18; the reserved-staging-component half lands at 4b). The managed downloads dir is unchanged (`record_share`, full `<share-folder>/<rel_path>`). `rebase_to_selection_root` stays for now (the frontends still call it until step 5 wires the roots through `ConfirmFetch`), so both compile. No wire/proto change (the `ConfirmFetch` selection-roots field is an in-process `NetCommand` field added at step 5). Registers DL-ISC-7 pos (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 228→229; pos 153→154; neg 75 unchanged). **Coverage:** `storage::fetched::tests::place_*` (single / one-file-folder-keeps-folder / scattered-each-top-level / whole-share / nested-normalized / collision-suffix / full-path-guard). Step 4b (staging writer + `.dspart/` + offset writes + promote) and 4c (idx file-lock + sweep) follow as their own gate-green sub-commits. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Added · this entry · ISA Criteria DL-ISC-7 · isc registry (count + ledger) · daemonseed-core LAMA manifest (`SelectionRoot`/`PlacedFile`/`place_at_dest`).

- 2026-07-20: **Download-subsystem redesign — step-3 review fix: collapse the fetch window ONCE per route death, not per fragment (`daemonseed-veilid-net::share`; branch `feat/download-subsystem-redesign`).** A per-commit review of `8d6f664` found `fetch_sealed_budgeted` observed `FragmentOutcome::Failed` per fragment — so a route death, which fails up to `W` concurrent in-flight fragments, collapsed the window many times and ratcheted the sharer's learned ceiling to floor instead of the design's `max(W_floor, W_kill/2)` (half the killing width). The step-2 controller was unit-tested with sequential single observations; the concurrent step-3 fetch path violated that assumption. **Fix:** the fragment futures now observe only `Completed` (the per-fragment latency-valve signal); the `Failed` collapse is observed EXACTLY ONCE at the fetch boundary — `fetch_sealed_budgeted` wraps `fetch_sealed_budgeted_inner` and, on a returned error, observes `Failed` only when `err.fetch_class() == FetchErrorClass::Transient` (the step-1 taxonomy), so content-integrity / not-served failures still never signal the controller (the route is fine). Safe either way (the prior behaviour was over-conservative, never unsafe), but restores the intended adaptive resume width. No API/wire change; no ISC count change. **Coverage:** `share::tests::a_route_death_collapses_the_window_once_not_per_fragment` (learned ceiling = `W_CEIL/2`, not floor). doc-sync: this entry (no new CHANGELOG line — refines unreleased branch code already covered by the step-3 [Unreleased] entry).

- 2026-07-20: **Download-subsystem redesign — step 3/9: the budget-backed fetch seam (`daemonseed-veilid-net::share`; branch `feat/download-subsystem-redesign`).** Per the ratified design §Implementation-plan step 3, added `fetch_manifest_budgeted` / `fetch_chunk_budgeted` ALONGSIDE the legacy `window`-parameter `fetch_manifest` / `fetch_chunk` (retired at step 7), so both frontends keep compiling at every commit. The budgeted path admits every fragment `app_call` through a shared `RouteLease` (the single per-route limiter — no per-fetch window) via a new `call_fragment_budgeted` that acquires a `BudgetPermit` per attempt and DROPS it before any retry backoff sleep (DL-ISC-6), and feeds each fragment's outcome to the window controller (`Completed{over_threshold = latency ≥ FRAGMENT_LATENCY_THRESHOLD}` / `Failed` on terminal transport failure; content-integrity errors abort the fetch WITHOUT signalling the controller — the route is fine, the content is hostile). The fragment pipeline still yields in request order (`buffered(W_CEIL)` is only a poll bound; the budget, not this bound, is the concurrency limiter, and fragments across concurrent chunks/files on one lease share that cap). Extracted `open_manifest_frame` / `open_and_verify_chunk` helpers shared by both the legacy and budgeted paths (behaviour-preserving refactor). The functions are generic over the route key `R` so the seam test drives them with a trivial key; production instantiates `RouteLease<RouteId>`. NOT yet called by the frontends — that is steps 5/6. No new ISC (DL-ISC-6 registered at step 2; the seam adds an integration probe), no registry count change, no wire/proto change. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Added · this entry · DL-ISC-6 seam probe · veilid-net LAMA manifest (2 budgeted fetch functions). **Coverage:** `share::tests::budgeted_fetch_reassembles_and_admits_through_the_route_budget` (byte-for-byte reassembly + peak ≤ `W_CEIL` + genuine concurrency + the controller climbed).

- 2026-07-20: **Download-subsystem redesign — step 2/9: the per-route concurrency budget + error-primary controller (`daemonseed-veilid-net::route_budget`; branch `feat/download-subsystem-redesign`).** Per the ratified design-of-record §Part 1, the quantity that kills a serving private route is the total concurrent in-flight fragment `app_call`s to that ONE route (#204: 8×2 died, 1–2 held), which the shipped code caps only indirectly as the product of two per-level windows. **New module `route_budget`:** a `RouteBudget<R>` registry (generic over the route key so the counting-fake suite drives it with trivial keys; production keys on `RouteId`) with counter-gated admission (`in_flight(route) < W(route)` AND `in_flight(global) < G_GLOBAL`, a `std::sync::Mutex` counter + `tokio::sync::Notify` rather than a `Semaphore` because `W` shrinks below outstanding on a breach), a `RouteLease`/`BudgetPermit` RAII pair (permit drop frees route + global slots so a retry-backoff release never pins the global pool), and an **error-primary** window controller: slow-start at `W_FLOOR`, window-clocked additive increase (+1 per full window of clean completions), error collapse to floor + a learned ceiling `max(W_FLOOR, W_kill/2)` keyed by SHARER pubkey (survives route rotation; ratchets down only; the live window also lowers its own ceiling so a surviving account cannot climb back), and latency demoted to a secondary 3-of-5 valve. Constants ratified: `W_FLOOR=2, W_CEIL=8, G_GLOBAL=12, F_FILES=4`, valve 3-of-5 @ the 2 s threshold; `W_CEIL < G_GLOBAL` is a compile-time invariant (DL-ISC-19). The controller is clock-free (injected `FragmentOutcome`s), so the whole suite is deterministic. **NOT yet wired into the fetch path** — that is step 3 (budget-backed fetch seam). A test caught a real bug pre-commit: the error collapse recorded the ceiling in the registry map but not in the live window, so a surviving account climbed back to `W_CEIL` — fixed by ratcheting the window's own ceiling on failure. No wire/proto change. Registers DL-ISC-1/2/3/4/5/15/16/17 pos + DL-ISC-6/19 anti (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 218→228; pos 145→153; neg 73→75; ledger updated). **Coverage:** the 12 `route_budget::tests::*` unit oracles (counting-fake admission + controller state-machine). doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Added · this entry · ISA Criteria DL-ISC-1..6/15..17/19 · isc registry (count + ledger) · veilid-net LAMA manifest (`route_budget` module + 5 types + 4 constants).

- 2026-07-20: **Download-subsystem redesign — step 1/9: the veilid-net fetch boundary gains a typed failure taxonomy (`FetchErrorClass`, `daemonseed-veilid-net::error`; branch `feat/download-subsystem-redesign`).** Per the ratified design-of-record (`docs/design/download-subsystem.md`), the #205 resume blocker was that a transient timeout and a content-integrity failure were indistinguishable — both `VeilidNetError::Send(String)` — so no downstream code could decide retain-and-resume vs abort-poison. **Change:** a new `VeilidNetError::Integrity(String)` variant, a `FetchErrorClass { Transient, Integrity, NotServed, Local }` enum, and `VeilidNetError::fetch_class()`; the `share.rs` fetch-path sites that detect hostile/malformed content — SHA-384 content-address mismatch, chunk-address mismatch, wrong/absent response frame, over-cap fragment, over-cap fragment total, over-cap reassembly — now return `Integrity` instead of `Send`, so they classify FATAL (never persisted, never resumed past). Transient transport (`Send`/routing/actor/not-ready), the authoritative `NotServed`, and the engine's own disk/path faults (`Local`, contributed downstream) stay distinct; a local identity/unimplemented fault classifies `Transient`, never `Integrity`, so it can never poison-abort a share. Two existing oversized-rejection tests updated their assertion to `Integrity` — the behavior they exercise is now correctly typed. No wire/proto change (veilid-net crate-internal error enum; MINOR for the crate). Registers DL-ISC-10 pos (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 217→218; pos 144→145; neg 73 unchanged; distribution ledger updated). **Coverage:** veilid-net `error::tests::fetch_class_maps_each_variant_to_its_class` + `fetch_class_never_returns_local_from_a_transport_error`. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Changed · this entry · ISA Criteria DL-ISC-10 · isc registry (count + ledger) · `docs/llm-api-manifest/daemonseed-veilid-net-api.yaml` (`Integrity` variant + `FetchErrorClass` enum + `fetch_class` note added to `error_types`). - 2026-07-20: **#197 — the downloader-side `ConfirmFetch` runs off the net-actor loop, mirroring the #180 §RS-2 `FetchShare` restructure (`daemonseed-{gui,tui}::veilid_net`; branch `fix/download-off-loop-197`).** The node downloading a share was starving its own chat: the chunk download (`confirm_fetch`/`confirm_fetch_inner`) was awaited **inline** on the command loop that also processes `SendLobby`/`SendCircle`/`SendRoom`, so a slow/large download blocked chat until it finished (the same #154 inline-park class #180 fixed for the *listing* fetch, CRSH-ISC-8; the served side was already off-loop via the #125 dedicated serve lane). **Fix (both frontends):** `spawn_confirm_fetch` copies the route blob + room key out of share state under an immutable borrow (immediate local failures — not-connected / lobby-not-subscribed / not-discovered — still fail inline, no spawn, no Unresolved mark, exactly as before), then dispatches `run_confirm_download` through a `spawn_confirm_task` seam (the single guarantee that no download is awaited on the loop) and returns to `select!`. The download worker holds **no `&mut ShareState`**: it imports the route, fetches + SHA-384-verifies + writes each file, streams `FetchProgress` via a cloned `evt_tx`, cleans partials on failure (ISC-A-C31), and returns a `ConfirmOutcome` (`Complete` / `RouteFailed` / `LocalFailed`). A new `confirm_outcome_rx` `select!` arm folds it via `fold_confirm_outcome` — the ONLY place the download's `&mut ShareState` mutation happens: `Complete` clears Unresolved + drops the parked retry (CRSH-ISC-25 F6) + emits `FetchComplete`; `RouteFailed` (import / manifest / chunk-fetch route-death) marks Unresolved + parks a one-shot retry (never a prune, #180 §RS-1.4/§RS-1.5) + emits `FetchError`; `LocalFailed` (path-sanitize / disk write / downloads-idx write) emits `FetchError` only. **Semantic parity, timing-only change** — mid-download the loop was fully blocked before, so nothing could observe the old inline timing; which failures mark Unresolved is preserved per-crate (the TUI's single marking path routes all in-loop failures through `RouteFailed`; the GUI's path/disk failures are `LocalFailed`). **One deliberate TUI micro-divergence:** the post-verify `downloads.idx` write failure now returns `LocalFailed` (no Unresolved clear) where the prior inline code cleared Unresolved just before it — leaving the mark untouched is the safer choice (the download is absent from the browse manifest so it did not fully land; any Unresolved state self-heals via the parked-retry / route-rotation paths), and it is a disk-full-class corner. **Deliberately out of scope:** no `route_guard` participation for the download (preserves the pre-change behavior — the inline download never registered its imported route on the guard); no download cancellation/pause UI (the spawn opens the door — a follow-up); no served-side residual-gap review (#197 §2 — separate). Registers CRSH-ISC-29 pos (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 216→217; pos 143→144; neg 73 unchanged; distribution ledger updated). **Coverage:** gui/tui `crsh_isc_29_actor_never_awaits_a_download_inline` (the gated-download-vs-chat probe over the `spawn_confirm_task` seam) + `crsh_isc_29_fold_applies_mark_clear_per_outcome` (the mark/clear-per-outcome semantic-parity guard); the live two-client download-while-chatting is the attended felt-test. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed · this entry · ISA Criteria CRSH-ISC-29 · isc registry (count + distribution ledger) · gui/tui inline comments. API manifest unchanged — no public signature changed (internal actor functions + a new `NetCommand` handler dispatch path).
- 2026-07-19: **#204 / #128 D-2 — the folder-download chunk window slow-starts and adapts to route health rather than a fixed 8-wide fanout (`daemonseed-gui::veilid_net::confirm_fetch_inner`; `daemonseed-veilid-net::aimd::AimdWindow::slow_start`; branch `chore/veilid-0.5.7`, v0.35.0).** A folder download fans out at two concurrency levels: the fragment window (`FRAGMENT_FETCH_CONCURRENCY`, per chunk) and the chunk window (`CHUNK_FETCH_CONCURRENCY` = 8, chunks-per-file — the dominant one for a folder). #204 lowered only the fragment window (8 → 2); the chunk window still cold-opened at 8, so a multi-file folder sustained an 8×2 fanout that killed the serving private route mid-folder on **Windows** (a single small file = 1 chunk × 2 = trivial, which is why single-file downloads passed and folders failed — file 1/2 completed, then a later file's chunk fetch hit route-death). The fragment AIMD cannot help: it floors total in-flight at 8×1 = 8, never below, because the chunk count is fixed. **Fix:** a second `AimdWindow` governs the chunk window via a new `AimdWindow::slow_start(start, floor, ceiling)` — it opens at `CHUNK_SLOW_START` = 2 and climbs by one per healthy chunk to the `CHUNK_FETCH_CONCURRENCY` ceiling, halving on a `FRAGMENT_LATENCY_THRESHOLD` breach (classic congestion-control slow-start); it is shared across the whole download and read once per file, so backoff learned early in a folder persists file-to-file. Both windows are fed the same per-chunk max-fragment latency. **Deliberately out of scope (security):** the error/recovery path is untouched — SHA-384 chunk content-address fail-closed (ISC-S28 / ISC-A-S20) and the all-or-nothing partial-wipe (ISC-A-C31) are unchanged; a route-death still aborts the folder and parks the #180 retry. Graceful reconnect-and-resume-without-wiping is a follow-up (surfaced to caraka, not yet filed) — it requires first classifying transient-transport errors apart from integrity failures, which currently share `VeilidNetError::Send(String)`, so that a re-drive can never retry past a failed integrity check; that belongs in its own reviewed PR, not bundled here. **Coverage:** `aimd::tests::slow_start_opens_below_the_ceiling_and_climbs`; the live Windows folder download is the attended felt-test. No new ISC (refines the #128 / #204 fetch behavior). doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed · this entry · aimd module doc + `slow_start` rustdoc (the stale "not yet live-wired" note corrected) + gui inline comments. API manifest unchanged — `AimdWindow::slow_start` is new public surface but `aimd` is internal fetch-control, not in a LAMA manifest.
- 2026-07-17: **#195 — the manual Refresh re-indexes own published shares (`daemonseed-gui::main` `on_refresh_shares`; `daemonseed-gui::veilid_net` `publish_share`/`upsert_own_share`; branch `feat/consumer-route-self-heal-180`, v0.34.0).** The reindex machinery already existed but was only wired to the connect-time auto-republish — no mid-session UI path re-hashed an own root, so a file dropped into a shared folder stayed invisible to peers until the next launch (caraka: "reindexing is already built, but was never plumbed in to the gui"). The Veilid publish path holds no live fs-watch (the core `Indexer`/`ShareWatcher`/`mtime_rescan` is the retired relay-path layer — deliberately NOT rewired). **Change (GUI only):** `on_refresh_shares`, after `ResweepShares`, iterates `state.persisted_published()` and re-sends `PublishShare` per root, which re-runs `ShareContent::index_dir` and re-announces under the SAME deterministic `share_id` (#156) so peers fold the fresh manifest onto the existing entry. `publish_share`'s own-list append became an upsert-by-`share_id` (`upsert_own_share`) so repeated Refresh never duplicates an own entry (also a latent connect-time double-republish guard).
