Gated on `ui.get_connected()` and ridden only by the user-initiated Refresh — never the 3 s liveness poll (`RefreshShares` stays cheap/local, per the #133 CPU-storm guard). **MVP scope (caraka):** manual re-index on Refresh, NOT a live fs-watch — caraka flagged #195 as the change most likely to cause UX issues, so the re-hash is user-paced; a live watch stays an explicit follow-up decision. **TUI parity deferred:** the issue and the v0.34.0 Windows felt-test are GUI-only; the TUI has no manual own-share reindex trigger and none is added here. Registers CRSH-ISC-28 pos (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 215→216; pos 142→143; neg 73 unchanged; distribution ledger updated). doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Added · this entry · ISA Criteria CRSH-ISC-28 · isc registry (count + ledger) · gui inline comments. API manifest unchanged — no public signature changed (a new private `upsert_own_share`; `NetCommand::PublishShare` is unchanged). Live add-file-then-Refresh is the attended morning felt-test.
- 2026-07-17: **#180 self-heal REFRAMED to route-freshness — the auto-re-download is retired; a route-refreshed share is marked fetchable-again and the user re-initiates (`daemonseed-{gui,tui}::veilid_net::process_parked_browse_retries` Fire arm; branch `feat/consumer-route-self-heal-180`).** The attended two-client orinoco felt-test, DIAG-instrumented, refuted the redesign's central premise (a consumer-side death mode) and reframed the goal (caraka). **Measured on the live repro:** the consumer's route-refresh path already works and is prompt — after a SIGKILLed sharer returns, the fresh route re-imports in ~54s of the dead-route mark, delivered via the veilid **watch push** (`watch DELIVERED` on the sharer's record → re-import fold ~37ms later), NOT the slow resweep; the watch does NOT die on a sharer hard-kill (no `watch DIED`) — it survives and reconnects, delivering re-adverts within ms of publication. The re-import fires on both `change=Updated route_changed=true` (normal path) and `change=Unchanged route_changed=true` (de85cca's guard, observed live). So the earlier ~5-min "wedge" was NOT a consumer bug — it was a watch window shorter than the heal envelope plus the sharer's slow veilid warmup (reliable-peer acquisition), which is a veilid attach property, not daemonseed's to tune. **§RS-0 fork Q1 RESOLVED — no Fable escalation, no emission-class change:** an instant-on-failure reactive re-read buys almost nothing (the watch already delivers promptly once the sharer is back), so no browse-timed op is needed. **Reframe (caraka):** the self-heal's job is ROUTE FRESHNESS, not download completion — a sharer may be gone minutes, days, or forever (most leave deliberately or crash and stay away), so optimizing for an instant sharer-bounce auto-re-download is the wrong scenario. **Change (both frontends, §RS-0-trivially-safe — it REMOVES an op) — corrected placement after the xhigh review.** The initial cut put the mark-fetchable on the parked-retry `Fire` arm (`spawn_fetch_share` → `clear_share_unresolved`). The **xhigh `/code-review` (workflow `wyui6ygm5`) CONFIRMED two correctness bugs in that placement:** (a) the parked retry lives at most `BROWSE_RETRY_WINDOW` (600s), so a sharer returning after 10 min — the reframe's explicitly-common "gone for days" case — leaves the share permanently `Unresolved` against a live re-imported route (the reframe's headline goal, silently defeated by the retained window); (b) `Fire` classifies on any discovered-**generation** bump, but a content-only re-advert (route blob unchanged, possibly dead) also bumps the generation → the share flips to fetchable-looking against a dead route (false-available/flip-flop). Both share one root: the reframe's signal is a **route ROTATION**, but the park keys on generation and a window. **Corrected fix:** clear `Unresolved` at the **route-rotation fold** (`apply_discovery`, when `route_replaced` — the route blob actually rotated), which also drops the park. This is a local no-network op (the review's own insight: with no re-fetch, no cursor-tick decorrelation is owed — CRSH-ISC-15 binds *network* dispatch, not a local flag), is unbounded in time (fixes (a)), and gates on a real rotation not a generation bump (fixes (b) — a content re-advert keeps the share re-resolving). `process_parked_browse_retries` becomes **housekeeping only**: a `Fire` tick drops a now-stale park (generation bumped without a rotation), expiry surfaces a give-up hint **naming the share** (which re-reads `ParkedBrowseRetry.name`, resolving the review's orphaned-field cleanup finding without a wide field removal), a withdraw drops it. The fn loses its now-unused `net`/`fetch_outcome_tx` params (and the `fire` vec drops the parked name); `spawn_fetch_share` STAYS for the normal-browse path (gui:1437/tui:658). Review PLAUSIBLE finding #2 (dropping mid-fetch-rotation auto-completion) is **working-as-designed per the reframe** — the rotation marks the share fetchable and the user re-initiates. CRSH-ISC-6 REWORDED in place (still `Positive` — no ID renumber, no `TOTAL`/distribution-ledger change: 215, pos 142, neg 73) to the route-rotation-fold semantics; CRSH-ISC-15 clarified (network-dispatch anti-criterion). Tests (both frontends): `crsh_isc_27_*` asserts the rotation clears `Unresolved` + drops the park; `crsh_isc_6_content_readvert_does_not_mark_fetchable` guards finding (b); `crsh_isc_6_fire_drops_stale_park_without_clearing` covers the housekeeping tick; `crsh_isc_6_window_expiry_*` unchanged. `browse_retry.rs` + design-doc §RS-2/line-106 rustdocs re-synced. **Deferred fork (surfaced to caraka):** the parked-retry subsystem is now largely vestigial; a follow-up could retire it entirely (reworking CRSH-ISC-6/15/19/23) — a design call, not taken here to bound the change. **de85cca (route-rotation guard, CRSH-ISC-27) is KEPT** — it's a necessary sub-case of route freshness (a same-timestamp rotated advert must still re-import); its open availability-only route-rollback DoS finding is unchanged by this reframe and remains caraka's acceptance call + a follow-up anti-rollback issue. **Residual reduced:** the design's "retry-existence bit" residual (a fired retry re-requesting the manifest signals retry-ness to an in-room sharer) is now moot — the fired retry issues no manifest request. The DIAG traces (`apply_discovery DIAG` in gui, `watch DELIVERED`/`watch DIED` in veilid-net actor) that produced this evidence are removed. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Changed · this entry · ISA Criteria CRSH-ISC-6 reword · isc registry comment · design doc §RS-2 amendment (line-106 ISC candidate + a dated reframe note). API manifest unchanged — no public signature changed (internal fn param removal). The live end-to-end reframe UX (share marks fetchable, no auto-download, user re-fetch succeeds, no consumer restart) is the attended orinoco CRSH-ISC-12 re-test.
- 2026-07-17: **#180 route-rotation self-heal — a consumer re-imports a rotated route on a catalog-`Unchanged` fold (the CRSH-ISC-12 wedge, root-caused; `daemonseed-{gui,tui}::veilid_net` `apply_discovery`; branch `feat/consumer-route-self-heal-180`).** The attended two-client orinoco felt-test — SIGKILL the sharer (no leave tombstone → the consumer keeps the share listed with a now-dead imported route), then relaunch it (new veilid private route) — reproduced the dead-consumer wedge and root-caused it. Route re-import + generation bump was gated behind `change != CatalogChange::Unchanged`, but the F3 identical-re-read arm (`share_catalog::apply`) matches on same-owner + same `sent_unix_ms` + same metadata and EXCLUDES `route_blob` (which lives in `discovered`, not the catalog). A restart-republish re-advertises the SAME advert (the advert timestamp tracks content, not route rotation) with only the route blob rotated → folds `Unchanged` → the re-import is skipped → the consumer is wedged on the dead route: the share stays listed + `Unresolved`, and NOTHING recovers it — not the cadence repair (the rendezvous record session was still alive; only the imported route died), not the auto-reheal, not a manual re-download, not Refresh — until a consumer restart. The old comment's premise "a genuine re-announce/route rotation always bumps the timestamp" is false for a restart-republish. **Fix (both frontends, approach B):** compute `route_changed = discovered[id].route_blob != env.route_blob` before the gate and widen it to `change != Unchanged || route_changed`, reusing the existing `route_replaced` re-import/release path — a rotation re-imports, stamps a fresh generation (arming the parked retry), and preserves `unresolved`; F3 no-churn holds because only a real blob change triggers it. **Security (#152/#156):** honoring a route change on `Unchanged` does not reopen the hijack/redirect path — the `change != Unchanged` gate is NOT that protection. An announce reaches this line only after `share_binding_is_valid` (#156: `share_id == derive_share_id_v2(sender_pubkey, root_commitment)`, so a foreign key derives a different id and is dropped at ingest) AND `verify_route_advert` (blob/sig cryptographically bound to the id's verified owner); a binding-valid announce's `sender_pubkey` necessarily equals the stored owner (both derive the same id), so any `Unchanged` reaching here is the verified owner's rotated route. Registers CRSH-ISC-27 pos (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 214→215; pos 141→142; neg 73 unchanged; distribution-ledger updated). The Rule-2 Advisor call timed out (CLAUDECODE nested-session block) — proceeded on the traced security analysis; the xhigh reviewer pass is the independent check. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed · this entry · ISA Criteria CRSH-ISC-27 · isc registry (count + distribution ledger) · gui/tui inline comments. API manifest unchanged — no public signature changed (internal `apply_discovery` + a local `route_changed` binding). Live end-to-end wedge-heal remains the attended orinoco CRSH-ISC-12 re-test.
- 2026-07-15: **#180 R1/R4 — two more regressions from the earlier #180 hardening, caught by the re-review (branch `feat/consumer-route-self-heal-180`).** Both extend an existing ISC (no new ID; `daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` stays 214). **R1 (regression from the CRSH-ISC-13 ↔ CRSH-ISC-24 interaction / the F4/F5 drain hardening):** the §RS-4 manual Refresh arms a record (`refresh_armed`) that is NOT latched repair-due — the fold arm's single-failure override (`refresh_or_cadence_due` = `RepairDue OR (armed && is_failed_pass && Calm)`) dispatches it. But the gui fold arm CONSUMED the arm (`refresh_armed.remove`) at the top, then — if busy/warming — enqueued the key into `pending_repairs`; the F4 drain re-check (`is_repair_due(&key)`) is FALSE for the never-latched armed record, so the queued repair was DROPPED as "recovered" — a manual Refresh of a dead record during busy/warmup silently failed to heal it, breaking CRSH-ISC-13's "one action, no restart". **Fix (gui only — the tui has no `refresh_armed` mechanism; its `RefreshShares` merely re-emits a snapshot, so R1 does not exist there):** change the arm lifecycle — the fold arm now PEEKs the arm (`contains`, not `remove`); consumes it on immediate dispatch and on a not-due pass (a healthy/suppressed armed record clears its arm and re-establishes nothing, preserving CRSH-ISC-16), but LEAVES it in place when the repair is enqueued busy/warming; the drain consumes the arm and dispatches iff `is_repair_due(&key) || armed` (new `drain_should_dispatch` seam, mirroring the fold arm's `refresh_or_cadence_due`). Net invariants: armed-enqueued dead record heals at drain (R1 restored); a recovered record (latch cleared AND arm consumed by the not-due fold) is dropped (F4 preserved); a healthy armed record clears its arm with no dispatch (CRSH-ISC-16); a latched cadence record dispatches via `is_repair_due` (unchanged). Covered by `crsh_isc_13_armed_refresh_survives_a_busy_enqueue_and_the_drain_honors_it` over the composed pure seams (`refresh_or_cadence_due` + the `SessionHealthTracker` latch + `drain_should_dispatch` + the arm lifecycle); live end-to-end is CRSH-ISC-12/13. **R4 (regression from CRSH-ISC-23 / the F2 fresh-advert re-park):** `fold_fetch_outcome`'s stale branch unconditionally set `disc.unresolved = true` and re-parked whenever the entry still existed — but two overlapping different-generation browse fetches are reachable by ordinary user action (Browse at G spawns slow fetch A; a fresh advert advances to G+1; Browse again → fetch B at G+1 succeeds, clears Unresolved, renders the manifest; fetch A then folds stale → the branch flipped the RESOLVED share back to Unresolved and fired a redundant fetch). **Fix (both frontends):** re-park only if the share is currently still `unresolved` (`matches!(discovered.get(&share_id), Some(disc) if disc.unresolved)`); a resolved share is left resolved and the stale outcome is dropped; the `note_fetch_finished(None)` route hygiene on the stale path is unchanged (CRSH-ISC-19). Covered by `crsh_isc_23_resolved_share_is_not_reverted_by_a_late_stale_outcome` (both frontends); the F2 re-park test and the withdraw-staleness test stay green. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed (two lines) · this entry · ISA Criteria CRSH-ISC-13 + CRSH-ISC-23 text extensions · NO isc registry count change (both extend existing ISCs). API manifest unchanged — no public signature changed (internal fold arm + a private `drain_should_dispatch` helper).
- 2026-07-15: **#180 R2/R3 — two regressions from the earlier #180 hardening, caught by the re-review (branch `feat/consumer-route-self-heal-180`).** Both extend an existing ISC (no new ID; `daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` stays 214). **R2 (regression from CRSH-ISC-21 / commit c156982):** the F3 identical-re-read arm (`daemonseed-core::share_catalog::apply`) folded `Unchanged` WITHOUT refreshing `cur.received_at`. But `received_at` is the liveness clock the catalog TTL prune (600s) ages against, and a sharer's watchdog republishes the SAME sealed advert (only the route blob rotates) every ~150s — so the consumer's steady resweep now always folds `Unchanged`, `received_at` freezes at discovery time, and ~600s later the prune removes a LIVE, actively-reswept share, which re-Adds on the next resweep: a periodic flicker + generation/route churn — the very thing F3 removed, now periodic. Pre-F3 the re-read folded `Updated` and refreshed `received_at`, so a reheard share never pruned. **Fix:** refresh `cur.received_at = now` in the identical-re-read arm BEFORE returning `Unchanged` — separating liveness re-hearing (refresh the clock) from content-change signalling (skip the generation bump). The `identical_reread_folds_unchanged` test (which asserted `received_at` was UNCHANGED — encoding the bug) is flipped to `identical_reread_refreshes_received_at_but_folds_unchanged`, and a `reheard_share_survives_ttl_prune` test covers the TTL liveness. **R3 (regression from CRSH-ISC-22 / commit afde5de):** the spawned `RepairRendezvous` task (`daemonseed-veilid-net::actor`) removed the `repair_in_flight` marker only on the post-await line, so a panic in `repair_rendezvous` (or the veilid code it awaits) unwound BEFORE the `.remove(&owner_seed)` — leaving `owner_seed` stuck in the set forever, so every future `RepairRendezvous` for that record hit `!set.insert(..)` and was skipped (replied Ok without repairing), permanently disabling that record's self-heal until app restart. **Fix:** RAII — a `RepairInFlightGuard` whose `Drop` removes the marker, constructed at the top of the spawned task; the explicit post-await `.remove(..)` is deleted, so the marker is cleared on BOTH normal completion and panic-unwind. Covered by `repair_in_flight_guard_clears_marker_on_drop` + `repair_in_flight_guard_clears_marker_on_panic`. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed (two lines) · this entry · ISA Criteria CRSH-ISC-21 + CRSH-ISC-22 text extensions · NO isc registry count change (both extend existing ISCs). API manifest unchanged — no public signature changed (an internal arm mutation + a private RAII guard).
- 2026-07-15: **#180 F7 — a verified withdraw releases the share's imported route + drops its route_guard entry (`daemonseed-core::route_guard::ImportedRouteGuard`; `daemonseed-{gui,tui}::veilid_net` `CatalogChange::Removed` withdraw arm; branch `feat/consumer-route-self-heal-180`).** The verified-withdraw branch removed the `discovered` + `parked_retries` entries but made NO `route_guard` call — no route release, no guard-entry drop. After a completed idle fetch, `note_fetch_finished` leaves the guard entry `{route: Some(R), in_flight: 0}`, and `gc()` only reclaims a fully-empty entry — so on withdraw R was never released through the §RS-3 path (only the veilid LRU eventually evicts it) and `route_guard.entries` grew without bound across a long session of share-discovery churn. **Fix (core):** add `ImportedRouteGuard::note_share_withdrawn(&share) -> Option<R>`, mirroring `note_advert_replaced`'s in-flight discipline — when idle (`in_flight == 0`) it `take`s the imported route, removes the entry, and returns the route for the caller to release; when a fetch is in flight it marks the entry `withdrawn` and returns `None`, deferring the release to `note_fetch_finished`, which — on reaching `in_flight == 0` on a withdrawn entry — releases whatever route the entry holds and lets `gc` drop it (so no `{route: Some, in_flight: 0}` can linger). **Idempotency / double-release safety (Advisor):** the released route is always `take`n from the entry (idle branch removes the entry; completion branch empties `route`), so the same `RouteId` is never handed out twice; a re-withdraw of an already-withdrawn/absent share returns `None`. A withdraw racing a concurrent re-import is handled by `note_fetch_started` clearing the `withdrawn` mark (a fresh fetch means the share is live again), so a re-imported route is retained, not released out from under the new fetch; and an actual double-release at the transport is a benign no-op (`release_tolerant` maps veilid `InvalidArgument` to a trace). **Fix (both frontends):** in the `CatalogChange::Removed` arm, after removing `discovered`/`parked_retries`, push any route returned by `note_share_withdrawn` onto `pending_route_releases` (drained through `release_route`/`release_tolerant`, exactly like the existing `note_advert_replaced` path). **Coverage:** the tightest transport-free seam is the core `route_guard` unit tests — idle-withdraw releases the route + drops the entry, in-flight-withdraw defers to `note_fetch_finished` completion (releases + drops), a re-withdraw returns `None` (no double hand-out), and a withdraw-then-re-import retains the newly imported route; the frontend wiring is one-line and code-inspected. Registers CRSH-ISC-26 pos (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 213→214; pos 140→141; neg 73 unchanged). doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed · this entry · ISA Criteria CRSH-ISC-26 · isc registry · core/gui/tui inline comments. API manifest: `daemonseed-core`'s public surface gained `ImportedRouteGuard::note_share_withdrawn` — but `route_guard` is not in a LAMA manifest (internal consumer-side, unlisted), so no manifest entry is added.
- 2026-07-15: **#180 F6 — a completed download clears the share's Unresolved mark + parked browse retry (`daemonseed-{gui,tui}::veilid_net::confirm_fetch`/`confirm_fetch_inner` success tail; branch `feat/consumer-route-self-heal-180`).** The download path already marked a share `Unresolved` (+ parked a generation-tagged retry) on every download-stage failure (import / manifest / chunk-fetch fail), but on SUCCESS it emitted `NetEvent::FetchComplete` and **never cleared** the mark or the parked retry — a mark/clear setup-teardown asymmetry. So a failed-then-retried download that then succeeded left the share rendered "re-resolving" indefinitely, and the stale parked retry fired a redundant browse fetch on the next advert fold. Only a browse-PREVIEW success (`fold_fetch_outcome`'s `Manifest` arm), a verified withdraw, or catalog TTL ever cleared it. **Fix (both frontends):** on download success, call `clear_share_unresolved(shares, evt_tx, share_id)` immediately before the `FetchComplete` emission — exactly as the browse-preview success path already does. GUI has one `FetchComplete` site (`confirm_fetch_inner` tail); TUI has two (`confirm_fetch` flat-dest and managed-dir), so the clear is placed once after the download-loop success match, before both. No failure path changed (they correctly `mark_share_unresolved`); the fix only ADDS the clear on success, restoring the `mark ⟷ clear` symmetry. Neither frontend needed a new helper — both already had `clear_share_unresolved` (from the browse-preview path). **Coverage:** the full `confirm_fetch`/`confirm_fetch_inner` needs a live veilid handle + real chunk I/O to reach the success tail, so the unit test exercises the tightest seam — `clear_share_unresolved` over a share left `Unresolved` with a parked retry (the exact post-download-failure state) asserts the mark clears + the retry drops; the byte-transfer portion is live-only. Registers CRSH-ISC-25 pos (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 212→213; pos 139→140; neg 73 unchanged). doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed · this entry · ISA Criteria CRSH-ISC-25 · isc registry · gui/tui inline comments. API manifest unchanged — no public signature changed (internal fetch success tail).
- 2026-07-15: **#180 F4/F5 — a queued repair is re-checked at drain and an immediately-dispatched record is removed from the queue (`daemonseed-{gui,tui}::veilid_net` steady-resweep drain + fold arm; `daemonseed-core::session_health`; branch `feat/consumer-route-self-heal-180`).** Two defects in the `pending_repairs` path let a *healthy* record be needlessly re-established. **F4 (spurious re-establishment):** a `RepairDue` record queued while a resweep/repair was in flight kept its `repair_due_latched` set (the busy branch does not call `note_repair_dispatched`); if its session then RECOVERED before the queue drained (a successful sweep resets the streak + clears the latch via `observe`), the orphaned queue entry was still drained and *unconditionally* dispatched — tearing down and re-establishing a live record (with `REPAIR_CLOSE_FIRST=true` this closes the record, and a message arriving in the close→re-watch gap can be lost; the backoff multiplier is also bumped). The drain re-resolved the owner but never re-consulted the tracker. **F5 (double repair):** the fold-arm immediate-dispatch branch called `note_repair_dispatched` + `spawn_repair` but did NOT remove the key from `pending_repairs` (the `!pending_repairs.contains(&key)` guard only protected the *else* enqueue), so a key already enqueued (busy) that then reached `RepairDue` while `resweep_busy` momentarily read false was dispatched immediately AND popped-and-dispatched again by the next drain. **Fix (both frontends):** (1) `SessionHealthTracker::is_repair_due` is promoted from `#[cfg(test)]` to `pub` and rustdoc'd as the drain-time re-check — it returns `false` exactly when the record recovered (`observe` cleared the latch) or was already dispatched (`note_repair_dispatched` cleared it). (2) F4 — the steady-resweep drain now dispatches a popped key ONLY if `session_health.is_repair_due(&key)`; a stale entry is dropped (pop_front already removed it), preserving the owner-resolution and the `resweep_busy` one-repair-in-flight serialization. (3) F5 — the fold-arm immediate dispatch now also `pending_repairs.retain(|k| k != &key)` so a stale queued copy is never double-popped; the F4 drain re-check ALSO catches this (latch clear after dispatch), so the two are defense-in-depth. **No pure drain-decision helper was extracted** — the decision reduces to `is_repair_due` alone (a `fn(bool)->bool` wrapper would be vacuous), so coverage is the `is_repair_due` recover/dispatch unit test (`crsh_isc_24_is_repair_due_tracks_recover_and_dispatch`) plus the live drain behavior under the attended orinoco CRSH-ISC-12 (a recovered record is not re-established; a dead record heals once). Registers CRSH-ISC-24 pos (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 211→212; pos 138→139; neg 73 unchanged). doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed · this entry · ISA Criteria CRSH-ISC-24 · isc registry · gui/tui inline comments + `session_health::is_repair_due` rustdoc. API manifest: `daemonseed-core`'s public surface gained `SessionHealthTracker::is_repair_due` — but `session_health` is not yet in a LAMA manifest (the tracker is internal-consumer-side and unlisted), so no manifest entry is added.
- 2026-07-15: **#180 F2 — a fresh-advert-stale fetch outcome re-parks the browse retry (`daemonseed-{gui,tui}::veilid_net::fold_fetch_outcome`; branch `feat/consumer-route-self-heal-180`).** The stale-generation guard (`discovered[share_id].generation != Some(outcome_generation)`) dropped a fetch outcome (`note_fetch_finished(None)` + `return`) without re-parking. This is correct for a WITHDRAWN share (entry gone) but wrong for the other staleness cause: when a parked retry FIRES (removing its parked entry) and its in-flight fetch then goes stale because ANOTHER fresh advert advanced the generation mid-fetch, the share was left `unresolved=true` with an EMPTY `parked_retries` → `process_parked_browse_retries` never fired it again → the share stranded permanently re-resolving even though the newest advert carried a live route — defeating the self-heal's headline re-announcing-sharer case. **Invariant restored: `unresolved ⟹ a parked retry exists`** (except transient windows the cursor tick closes). Fix, in the stale branch of both frontends, after the route-release: if the entry is still in `discovered`, set `unresolved` and re-park a one-shot retry, else drop (the withdraw case). **Crux — re-park at the OUTCOME's generation, not the current.** `parked_retry_action` Fires iff `current_generation > parked_generation`; the stale outcome was fetched at generation N (say) while `discovered.generation` is now N+1, so parking at the outcome's N gives `current(N+1) > parked(N)` ⇒ Fire on the very next cursor tick against the newer advert's route. Re-parking at the current generation would instead make it Wait for yet another advert — wrong. All three `FetchOutcome` variants carry `name`, so the top destructure was extended to extract it (no fallback needed). The route is still released on the stale path (CRSH-ISC-19 route hygiene unchanged). The existing CRSH-ISC-19 fold test — which modelled staleness by advancing the generation while the share stayed discovered — encoded the pre-fix (buggy) drop; it was repurposed to model the genuine CRSH-ISC-19 case (withdraw → drop, no re-park) and a new CRSH-ISC-23 test covers the fresh-advert re-park. Registers CRSH-ISC-23 pos (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 210→211; pos 137→138; neg 73 unchanged). doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed · this entry · ISA Criteria CRSH-ISC-23 · isc registry · gui/tui rustdoc (the `fold_fetch_outcome` header + inline comments describe both staleness causes). API manifest unchanged — no public signature changed (internal fold arm).
- 2026-07-15: **#180 F1 — RepairRendezvous dispatched off the transport actor loop (`daemonseed-veilid-net::actor`; branch `feat/consumer-route-self-heal-180`).** The actor loop is single-tasked (`while let Some(cmd) = cmd_rx.recv().await { match … }`); the `Command::RepairRendezvous` arm awaited `repair_rendezvous(…)` — close/open/watch + a full 0..64 re-sweep — INLINE. On a dead record each GET hits the veilid timeout, so the sweep (and the whole loop) blocked for seconds→tens of seconds, stalling every other inline command (`SendSealed`, `SubscribeRendezvous`) — re-introducing the exact chat-starvation class #180 exists to kill. **Crux — atomicity is the lock's, not the await's.** The re-establishment's atomicity vs a concurrent same-record write comes from the `record_lock` acquired inside `repair_gated` and held across the entire close/open/watch/sweep span (CRSH-ISC-3/18), NOT from awaiting on the loop; so the arm now clones the (all Arc-backed) handles and `tokio::spawn`s the repair, replying from the spawned task — the loop returns to `recv()` immediately while the lock still serializes writers. Mirrors the existing `AppCall`/`PublishShare`/fetch off-loop spawns (`ResweepRendezvous`, which spawns its sweep via `spawn_gated_sweep`, was already correct and is untouched). Adds a per-record in-flight guard (`Arc<std::sync::Mutex<HashSet<[u8;32]>>>`, loop-local): the transport can legitimately receive two RepairRendezvous for the same record (a re-dispatch the frontend dedup cannot reach), so a repair already in flight is skipped (reply `Ok(())`, no spawn) rather than double-run; the std Mutex is only held briefly (insert on dispatch, remove on completion), never across an await. Registers CRSH-ISC-22 pos (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 209→210; pos 136→137). doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed · this entry · ISA Criteria CRSH-ISC-22 · isc registry · veilid-net rustdoc unchanged (the arm + guard are self-documenting; `repair_rendezvous`' own doc still describes the under-lock re-establishment, unchanged). API manifest unchanged — no public signature changed (internal arm + a loop-local guard).
- 2026-07-15: **#180 F3 — identical advert re-read folds Unchanged (`daemonseed-core::share_catalog::apply`; branch `feat/consumer-route-self-heal-180`).** `apply`'s announce branch caught only *older* re-reads (`sent_unix_ms < announced_unix_ms`); an **equal-timestamp identical re-read** fell through to the `Updated` arm, so the consumer's steady resweep (~15s), re-reading the same stored advert every cycle, returned `Updated` each pass → `apply_discovery` bumped `next_generation` with no real change, dropping a successful in-flight browse's captured generation and helping strand a share `Unresolved`. Fix: a new arm **before** the `Updated` arm folds `Unchanged` iff owner (already proven equal by the pubkey arm above) AND `sent_unix_ms` AND every stored metadata field (`name`, `rating`, `sender_handle`) are equal. **Crux — the gate requires timestamp equality**: a genuine re-announce/route rotation carries the same metadata with a **fresher** timestamp and MUST still fold `Updated` (the self-heal's re-advertise trigger), and it does — a newer-ts re-read falls through to `Updated`. The gate is field-byte equality, NOT a payload hash. This is catalog-only-sufficient because the route blob is published **atomically with the metadata + timestamp**, so a genuine route/content change always carries a fresher timestamp; a timestamp+metadata-identity gate can never swallow a real change. `apply_verified` delegates to `apply`, so `apply` is the single content-decision site — no second edit needed. Registers CRSH-ISC-21 pos (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 208→209; pos 135→136). doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed · this entry · ISA Criteria CRSH-ISC-21 · isc registry · core rustdoc unchanged (arm is self-documenting).
- 2026-07-15: **#180 §RS-4 gui manual Refresh — BUILT (sweep-first + evidence-gated re-establishment; branch `feat/consumer-route-self-heal-180`).** The Refresh button (`NetCommand::ResweepShares`, sent only from `main.rs` `on_refresh_shares`) previously re-rendered and re-swept ONLY the lobby share record inline in `handle_command` (awaiting the GET on the actor loop). Realized as the full §RS-4 contract, moved into the actor loop (`handle_refresh_shares`) since it needs loop-local state the stateless dispatcher can't reach: (1) immediate `SharesSnapshot` re-render; (2) a sweep-first wave over EVERY share-bearing subscribed record (operator announce + lobby chat/share + every circle — the same seed set the steady resweep round-robins), traffic-shaped identically to cadence (`resweep_rendezvous`, GETs only, NO open/watch), spawned off the loop so a multi-record wave never starves chat and serialized behind `resweep_busy` (CAS-acquired) so it never overlaps a cadence sweep; (3) evidence-gated re-establishment via a `refresh_armed: HashSet<RecordKey>` in loop state — every swept record's key is armed, and the `SweepHealth` fold arm re-establishes on `refresh_or_cadence_due(decision, armed, input)` = `RepairDue OR (armed && is_failed_pass() && Calm)`, lowering the K threshold to 1 for an armed record ONLY (un-armed cadence keeps the K-consecutive path). A healthy armed record clears its arm and re-establishes nothing (CRSH-ISC-16), so a Refresh over a healthy record emits only sweep GETs; the existing re-establishment machinery (warmup guard, `resweep_busy` free-check, `record_key_owners` owner lookup, `note_repair_dispatched` + `spawn_repair` else `pending_repairs`) is reused verbatim, keeping the tracker latch/streak consistent. `SweepHealthInput::is_failed_pass` was made `pub` (daemonseed-core surface change) so the gate reuses the exact predicate rather than duplicating it. Parked-retry decorrelation is unchanged (§RS-4 does not bypass it). Registers CRSH-ISC-13 pos + CRSH-ISC-16/20 anti (`daemonseed-isc` `TOTAL` 205→208). **TUI manual-Refresh deferred** — the TUI's `RefreshShares` trigger is overloaded (it drives both the liveness auto-poll and the manual button on that frontend) and needs a separate command before the §RS-4 contract can attach without re-sweeping on every poll; the TUI already carries the cadence self-heal (steps 3a/3b), so coverage parity is not lost, and design Open-question #128 ("sequencing is the build's") permits the split. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Added · this entry · ISA Criteria CRSH-ISC-13/16/20 · isc registry · core/gui rustdoc.
- 2026-07-15: **#180 consumer route self-heal — design FROZEN + RATIFIED (`docs/design/consumer-route-self-heal.md`; design-only, the v0.33.2 build implements).** Boundary-level rulings: (1) **emission classes under WB-0** — discovery-surface DHT ops (GET/open/watch) are cadence-only; browse-consent does not transfer across observer surfaces (a browse-timed route re-resolve GET is forbidden); errors do not launder provenance (an error may influence local computation only); repair emissions are legal iff evidence is network-state-only AND dispatch rides a cadence tick (informationally sterile by construction). (2) **Fetch-path pruning retired** — both gui prune sites (import-fail + manifest-fail) were transport-failure prunes deleting live shares (Fixes-That-Fail); prune keys only on verified withdraw + catalog TTL. (3) **Un-gated-op margin limiter** — the §I5″.1 margin invariant becomes enforced (capacity-2 semaphore at every `open_dht_record`/`watch_dht_values` site) since repair/Refresh add un-gated call sites in spawned contexts. (4) **Refresh is sweep-first** — re-establishment only for repair-due or just-failed records; a healthy record never shows an open/watch fingerprint at refresh time (the draft's blanket re-establishment was panel-refuted as a "user pressed Refresh" oracle to storage hosts). (5) Three brief premises corrected against veilid-core 0.5.4 source: watches self-reconcile+renew (re-watch needed only after re-open); remote-route import dedups + LRU-expires (leak bounded); release `InvalidArgument` is benign already-evicted. Hardened by a 3-lens adversarial panel (3 BLOCKERs, 8 MAJORs integrated — incl. a 2× healing-envelope arithmetic error: 15s tick, K-th-pass dispatch, single-record heal ≤ ~8 min) + advisor pass (added: no-programmatic-Refresh anti-ISC, decaying repair backoff). Spawns CRSH-ISC-1..20 (registered at the build commits, not here); CRSH-ISC-12 is the attended two-client `[DEFERRED-VERIFY]` oracle behind the repro-first gate (the veilid-level death mode is honestly unconfirmed). doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Added · this entry.
- 2026-07-14: **v0.33.1 GUI UX patch — Windows console, roster-on-switch, name-only roster, connection status (branch `fix/gui-ux-v0.33.1`).** Five GUI fixes: (1) `windows_subsystem` under the `desktop` feature so the windowed Windows build spawns no console (the offscreen build keeps stdout for `--self-check`/PNG verification); (2) per-room roster cached on `CircleState` and repainted in `on_switch_circle`, fixing the HERE NOW pane not following a rail switch (#138) — a beacon repaint only fired on a roster content change for the active room, so a switch alone left the prior room's roster; (3) roster inline row shows the display name only, `#12hex` fingerprint hover-only, uniform lobby+circle (#173); (4) connection status is transport-level `connected · veilid`, not the joined room name (#182) — `NetEvent::Connected`/`RoomJoined` made fieldless as the room/handle string became unused. **`high` code-review triage (workflow `wf_cc9c46bf`):** name-collision inline disambiguation — ACCEPTED name-only per the documented "no hash unless hover" design; collision-aware `name#hex` deferred to #186 (hover reveals the fingerprint). Windows `--self-check`/`--show-*` stdout loss on the desktop build — ACCEPTED (those are Linux-host offscreen-verification flags, not a Windows-bundle workflow; the bundle is GUI-only). Empty people-column on a cold-cache switch — ACCEPTED by design (honest-empty replaces the stale-wrong #138 behaviour; self-heals on the next beacon). APPLIED: the hot-path roster clone removed; 3 unit tests added for `set_room_roster`/`active_roster`; a comment on `strip_handle_hash`'s intentional leniency vs `Handle::format`. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed/Changed · AGENTS.md release-version row (adds `APP_VERSION`) · version stamps (lama.yaml/README/APP_VERSION → v0.33.1).
- 2026-07-13: **WB-5.1 read-lane cure BUILT (branch-only, gate GREEN VM-side; executes the §WB-5.1 nine-step contract).** As-built against `docs/design/veilid-write-budget.md` §WB-5.1: `dht_gate::DhtGate` is now four dedicated tokio-`Semaphore` pools (`CHAT_PERMITS=2` · `FLOOR_PERMITS=1` · `W_MAX=2` · `READ_PERMITS=9`, `DHT_BUDGET=14`, `R_MIN=6`), a compile-time `const _` assert pins the `W_max=2` partition, and there are **no cross-pool fallback paths** (`acquire_priority` gone; `acquire_chat`/`acquire_floor`/`acquire_write`/`acquire_read`); `acquire_wait` is trace-only telemetry. `rendezvous::sweep` acquires a read permit **per `get_dht_value`** via the testable `sweep_gated` seam (the whole-sweep permit is deleted from `spawn_gated_sweep`). `schedule.rs`: the `NonchatWindow` acquire-wait controller is deleted (window = `min(distinct pending non-chat records, W_max)`, static); a `DispatchLane {Chat,Floor,Window}` routes the sink to the matching pool; split `window_in_flight`/`floor_in_flight` counters with a `Done.lane` tag; `Pending.starved_since` (inherited across I3 coalescing) drives both I8 escalation and the floor predicate `FLOOR_AGE = 2 × age_bounds[class]`; `pick_floor` picks idle records deadline-first then `(class, starved-age)`, dual-eligible with the window; the estimator is a median-of-5 (`LATENCY_WINDOW`), EMA removed; `next_wakeup` measures from `starved_since` and adds the floor crossings. Core `presence::ReapGate` (new, transport-free) folds the published median through an 8s/4s hysteresis band + `REAP_RESUME_GRACE=220s`, owned per-frontend; both gui+tui reapers use it (replacing the stateless `write_congested`) and emit `NetEvent::PresenceStale`. `#168`: dispatch-future construction moved inside the supervised inner spawn (sync+async panic both recover the lane counter); `record_locks`/`OpenCache` adopt the `unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner())` poison-recovery idiom. **Margin audit (step 2) resolved: `allow_offline` background flush bypasses the operation gate → `DHT_GATE_MARGIN` stays 2** (design build-note). Registers WB-ISC-16/17/18/20/21–28 in `daemonseed-isc` (`TOTAL` 201→213); WB-ISC-15/19 + the frontend stale-render stay `[DEFERRED-VERIFY]` (orinoco felt-test). Advisor (Rule 2, commitment boundary) ran; three sharpeners integrated (write-path issues no gated GET; panic test asserts `floor_in_flight`→0 on both paths; ReapGate stale-median bounded by keepalive cadence). Task ISA `PAI/MEMORY/WORK/20260713-wb5-read-lane-build/ISA.md`. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Added · this Decisions + Verification · design build-note · isc registry.
- 2026-07-13: **WB-5.1 read-lane permit amendment FROZEN + RATIFIED (Fable; `docs/design/veilid-write-budget.md` §WB-5.1), superseding §I5′.1–.2 — the WB-5 controller build (`wip/wb5-controller-2026-07-13`) reworks against this contract.** The build refuted the frozen sequential-reads premise: per-record sweeps each pinned a general permit for a whole ~64-GET multi-minute run, so ≥13 cold-start sweeps drained the 13-permit pool and starved every non-chat write (the 2026-07-13 xhigh cluster; #157 re-opened). Verdict: **per-GET read gating composed with a four-pool partitioned accountant** (chat 2 · I8-floor 1 · write `W_max`=2 · read 9; margin 2 reserved for the un-gated open/watch classes under a single-permit no-hold-and-wait rule, with a fixed `allow_offline`-flush audit decision rule: margin→3/read→8 iff the background re-fanout draws veilid's gate). Neither half suffices alone — per-GET bounds *waits* but not *occupancy* (a sequential sweep re-acquires instantly); the partition is the occupancy cap and makes write protection independent of GET-duration. **The §I5′.2 acquire-wait controller is retired**: window = write partition ⇒ the signal is structurally zero and a decrease returns permits to no one; the freeze's own accountant-only fallback becomes the design (`acquire_wait` demoted to telemetry, WB-ISC-27 anti). Explicit invariant amendments: **I4** re-scoped (≤2s for up to 2 concurrent cross-record chats, no spill — unconditional never-behind-non-chat); **I8** gains a direct starved-age floor predicate (`FLOOR_AGE = 2 × age_bounds[class]`, `starved_since` survives I3 coalescing) on the dedicated floor permit, dual-eligible with the window, picker over idle records ordered deadline-first then (class, starved-age); **I6b** rides the floor lane deadline-first under a `slack ≥ 660s` bound (the prior build blocked deadline writes unboundedly on the drained pool). Estimator: median-of-5 + 8s/4s hysteresis band + `REAP_RESUME_GRACE` 220s (the estimator's two consumers must not flip on the same edge). `stale_suspected` gets production wiring (both frontends). #168 residuals closed (dispatch-construction moved inside the supervised spawn; `record_locks`/`OpenCache` poison-recovery). R_min=6 derived from the warmup-inflation bound (≤2.2× at max step-up; WB-ISC-19 rider extended to measure warmup first-content). Dispositions for WB-ISC-1/10/16..20 + new WB-ISC-21..28 + the nine-step build contract are in the doc. Hardened pre-ratification: two adversarial lenses (24 findings, incl. the I6b-demotion BLOCKER and the I4/I6b/I8 supersession contradictions) + advisor (5 findings, read-side common-mode). Task ISA `PAI/MEMORY/WORK/20260713-wb5-read-lane-cure-freeze/ISA.md`. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Added; §WB-5 header flipped to its 2026-07-12 ratified status.
- 2026-07-13: **#163 share-withdraw re-linger — resolved by rejecting the tombstone approach, NOT by tombstone-dominance (attended, supersedes the deferred `wip/163-withdraw-tombstone` branch).** The night-run `wip/163` build made a share withdraw a `WriteKind::Tombstone` so it would dominate; the `high` review found that — now that #156 makes `share_id` deterministic — a withdraw's dominance **drops a same-id reshare** (data-loss regression). Root analysis (attended): **shares already resolve correctly by recency**. `ShareCatalog::apply` orders announce AND withdraw by the provenance-bound `sent_unix_ms` (receive-time is TTL-only), so a reshare with a newer timestamp already wins on mainline (`None → insert` after a withdraw removed the entry). Presence uses tombstone-dominance safely because a leave is never followed by a same-id current-state in the confusion window and presence re-asserts on a heartbeat; a reshare is a deliberately-newer same-id current-state that is one-shot — so kind-dominance is right for presence and wrong for shares. **Fix (this commit): withdraw is a one-shot write, not a persisted advert.** `VeilidNetHandle::publish_share` gains `persist: bool`; a withdraw (`false`) skips the `share_adverts` re-publish set and, inside `publish_one_advert` (which owns `advert_routes`), releases its own transient route via a **guarded compare-and-release** — free the route only if `advert_routes[share_id]` is still the id we installed, so a concurrent reshare's live route is never torn down (the `high`-review [0] route-race in the first cut of this fix; a bare remove-by-key would break the reshare). Presence's tombstone-dominance is untouched. **NOT fixed (pre-existing, follow-up):** the withdraw's single CurrentState write can still race a reshare on the shared subkey (review [1]) — a receiver reading fresh in the window sees the tombstone until the reshare's watchdog re-publishes (~150s) / recency saves a receiver who saw both; unchanged from mainline, self-healing, file as an issue. Behavioral oracle (no re-linger + guarded release under a real concurrent withdraw/reshare) is integration-only — the actor loop has no `VeilidAPI` harness — so it is **`[DEFERRED-VERIFY]` = the orinoco felt-test**; the guard itself is inspection-provable + gate-green (veilid-net clippy + 40 lib tests). Advisor (Rule 2) flagged the delivery-reliability question → resolved: a lost one-shot withdraw self-heals via the catalog prune TTL, the explicitly-designed backstop. `high` review re-run this session (`reviews/163-high-review-2026-07-13.json` was the deferred cut; this session's workflow re-review triaged [0] applied, [1]+[3] follow-up, [2] doc-sync applied). doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed · veilid-net LAMA manifest (`publish_share` +`persist`).
- 2026-07-13: **#135 xhigh review triage (workflow we34grfl3) — no correctness bugs, no hygiene regression; 4 cleanup/completeness follow-ups, none blocking.** [0] `derive_boxed_seed`'s `Box::new(seed)` leaves un-zeroized stack transit — the review confirms this is **byte-identical to the pre-existing pattern, carried forward, NOT a #135 regression**; the heap-direct expand (`hkdf.expand(info, boxed.as_mut_slice())`) is a hygiene *improvement* follow-up. [1] `CircleKey`/`PublicRoomKey` (content keys) match the `boxed` arm but are intentionally out of #135 scope (extra `from_bytes`/`AeadKey256` API) — a further-consolidation follow-up. [2] the macro's `boxed`/`inline` arms duplicate `as_bytes()`/`Debug` — a macro-internal DRY nit (an inner `@hygiene` sub-rule would factor it); correct as-is. [3] `storage::seeds` `SealingKey`/`IndexKey` use `Zeroizing<>` (neither arm fits) — a `zeroizing` arm could cover them. Committed as-is; the four noted for a later hygiene-completeness pass (`reviews/135-xhigh-review-2026-07-13.json`).
- 2026-07-13: **Redacted-zeroizing secret-seed hygiene consolidated to one macro + one boxed-seed derivation helper (#135, behavior-preserving refactor of a secret-material trust surface).** The 32-byte redacted-zeroizing newtype (`ZeroizeOnDrop` + a redacted `Debug` + one `as_bytes()`) was copied verbatim across six `*VeilidOwnerSeed` types (`circle::key`, `public_room` ×3 after #153, `public_space`) plus the two identity-rooted inline secrets (`ShareRootIkm`, `VeilidNodeSeed`), and the `HKDF-expand→stack-zeroize(both paths)→Box` derivation body was copied across the six owner seeds — one missed site silently regresses a hygiene invariant (a dropped zeroize, a leaking `Debug`, a one-sided stack zeroize). Extracted to a new `pub(crate)` `secret_seed` module: (1) a `redacted_secret_newtype!` macro with `boxed` (`Box<[u8; N]>`, `ZeroizeOnDrop`) and `inline` (`[u8; N]`, `Clone + Zeroize + ZeroizeOnDrop`) arms threading each call site's rustdoc, generating the newtype + `as_bytes()` + a tuple-form redacted `Debug`; (2) `derive_boxed_seed<const N>(&HkdfSha384, info) -> Result<Box<[u8; N]>, KdfError>` carrying the both-path stack-zeroize once. **Deliberately NOT shared:** the per-context HKDF-**extract** stays at each call site (circle canonicalizes secret entropy under `CIRCLE_KEY_SALT`; room/announce extract public IKM under their own salts — the salt/IKM/canonicalization must not be unified); the distinct key-class newtypes are retained (a compile error still bars substituting one secret for another); the three derivation error enums keep their own variant sets (each maps `KdfError` via its existing `Hkdf` variant — no public error-surface change). `ShareRootIkm`/`VeilidNodeSeed` are folded for **newtype hygiene only** — their derivations (the 3rd/4th expansions of the identity PRK in `derive_identity_keys`) stay distinct, per the task's "leave the derivation distinct" allowance. **One observable change, flagged:** those two types' `Debug` normalizes from the `debug_struct` form (`ShareRootIkm { ikm: "<redacted>" }` / `VeilidNodeSeed { seed: "<redacted>" }`) to the tuple form `Name(<redacted>)` the six owner seeds already use — both remain fully redacted (the manifest's "Debug redacts with the literal `<redacted>`" invariant holds), no test previously locked the old format, and new exact-format tests now lock the tuple form. No proto/wire change; the six newtypes + `ShareRootIkm`/`VeilidNodeSeed` public signatures and all three error enums are byte-identical, so no LAMA change (the macro/helper are `pub(crate)`). Oracle: per-type byte-identity KATs (fixed input → hardcoded pre-refactor hex) prove no derivation drifted; see Verification. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Changed.
- 2026-07-13: **AES-256-GCM envelope helper extracted — one `nonce ‖ ct ‖ tag` primitive for the three sealed-frame modules (#149, behavior-preserving refactor).** The seal/open envelope invariant was inlined byte-for-byte in `room_message`, `heartbeat`, and `share_rollcall`; a fix (or a bug) had to be made three times and could drift. Extracted to one crate-internal `aead_envelope::{seal_envelope, open_envelope}` (`pub(crate)`, new module) taking a concrete `&Aes256Key` + the caller's `aad` + plaintext/sealed bytes, plus a `pub(crate) EnvelopeError { EntropySource, Encrypt, TooShort, Decrypt }`. **Three invariants kept caller-side, deliberately, so the wire and the public surface are unchanged:** (1) each site passes its OWN AAD (`ROOM_MESSAGE_AAD`/`MESSAGE_AAD` · `HEARTBEAT_AAD` · `SHARE_ROLLCALL_AAD`) — the helper never unifies one, preserving cross-kind isolation (ISC-A-S2: a heartbeat must never open as a room message); (2) the heartbeat's fixed-length padding (WB-ISC-6, `HEARTBEAT_SEALED_LEN`) stays before `seal_envelope` / after `open_envelope` — the helper is length-preserving and knows nothing of framing; (3) each module maps `EnvelopeError` onto its existing error type via a `From` impl, so the `TooShort→Truncated` and `Decrypt(TagMismatch)→Authentication` mappings the inlined code carried are preserved exactly and no module's public error enum or seal/open signature changes. Plaintext zeroization stays at the call site (the caller owns the buffer and zeroes it after the helper returns). No proto/wire change; no LAMA change (the helper is crate-internal; the three public signatures are byte-identical). Oracle: the full existing seal/open/tamper/cross-surface suite in the three modules stays green (it exercises the public paths through the new helper), plus 6 new helper-level oracles (round-trip, `nonce ‖ ct ‖ tag` layout offsets, historic-fixed-nonce-frame-opens for format compat, wrong-AAD→TagMismatch, TooShort, per-seal random nonce). The `8601ce8`-review "per-frame pubkey clone→borrow" micro-opt was found already-applied: `open_signed_room_message` has borrowed `&message.sender_pubkey` since 8601ce8 (the `.to_vec()` calls are seal-side, building owned wire fields) — nothing to fold. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Changed.
- 2026-07-13: **#156 xhigh code-review triage (10 findings; workflow werqc9sws).** APPLIED: [0] `two_node_room.rs` felt-test built `AnnouncementFields` without the new required `root_commitment` → migrated to a v2-valid binding. **Gate gap exposed:** `daemonseed-veilid-net` is `exclude`d from the workspace, so `cargo test --workspace` / `clippy --all-targets` never compiled it — a false-green; caught only by `cd crates/daemonseed-veilid-net && cargo test`. [4]/[5] core LAMA manifest documented `apply` (not `apply_verified`) and omitted the `root_commitment` field → fixed (it pointed implementers at the unchecked ingest method). [6] veilid-net manifest `serve_share` cited retired v1 `derive_share_id` → `derive_share_id_v2`. [8] v1 `derive_share_id` (dead in production) given a superseded-retention note. DEFERRED to the attended `max`/`ultra` pass (file as issues): [1] `share_id` from `Path::to_string_lossy()` collapses two folders differing only in invalid-UTF-8 bytes to one id — availability-only, same-publisher, extraordinarily rare; the fix (raw OS-path bytes) changes the frozen derivation input and is cross-platform-sensitive (Windows OsStr ≠ bytes) → a design call; v2 only newly exposes it on the being-retired relay path. [2]/[3] empty-`root_commitment` withdraw fallback (own-record lost) is dropped by the v2 gate → share lingers to the 600s catalog TTL — deliberate + commented, own-record-lost only; a real fix needs `shares.own` rebuilt on reconnect or a live-share digest on the Veilid beacon. [7] `stable_share_root_ikm()` re-runs a full ML-DSA+ML-KEM keygen per connect (doubles PQ keygen) — efficiency, connect-time; fold into a one-derivation `stable_identity_keys()`. [9] 4-site copy-paste of the derive triple → extract `derive_share_binding(ikm, pk, root)` (pairs with the [1] fix). Re-gate after fixes: fmt · core clippy · `daemonseed-veilid-net` `cargo test --no-run` compiles.
- 2026-07-12: **#156 receiver-verifiable `share_id`↔pubkey binding v2 — BUILT (branch-only, gate GREEN VM-side; executes the freeze below).** As-built against `docs/design/share-id-binding.md`: the nonce IKM is `ShareRootIkm`, a FOURTH HKDF-SHA-384 expansion of the identity PRK (label `daemonseed/identity/{presentation}/share-root-ikm/v2`, 32 bytes) — the identity entropy, deliberately NOT the ML-DSA-87 SK (an SK-vs-entropy or GUI-vs-TUI split would fork the nonce and re-mint the id). Frozen normative constants: `SHARE_NONCE_SALT = daemonseed/share-root-nonce-salt/v2`, nonce domain `daemonseed/share-root-nonce/v2`, commitment domain `daemonseed/share-root-commitment/v2`, id domain `daemonseed/share-id/v2`; nonce 32 B, `root_commitment` 48 B, `share_id` 128-bit. Ingest gated at all three points — `ShareCatalog::apply_verified` (announce AND withdraw), a standalone `share_binding_is_valid` route-import gate ahead of the route map (closes the #152 route-theater), and the circle path. All four publish sites derive; `mint_share_id` retired from the publish path. `root_commitment` bound into the provenance signature; AAD/provenance domains split to `.../aad/v2` and `.../provenance/v2`. Registered ISC-S32/S33/S34 + ISC-A-S24/A-S25/A-S26 (isc `TOTAL` 194→200); ISC-S21 rewritten off the stale CSPRNG-mint text. Breaking `daemonseed-proto` v1 (SemVer MAJOR — new `bytes root_commitment`; `check-proto` snapshot regenerated). Built by Engineer (truncated at the maxTurns cap mid-test-run); the orchestrator independently re-ran every gate, inspected the diff, and completed the doc-sync tail. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Changed/Fixed · ISA Criteria (S21 + 6 ISCs) + Verification · design doc Status→BUILT · core/proto LAMA manifests.

- 2026-07-12: **#156 share_id↔pubkey binding FROZEN + RATIFIED (Fable design-freeze; design-of-record `docs/design/share-id-binding.md`).** Verdict: **Option A-refined** — the announcement carries a `root_commitment` whose nonce is HKDF'd from one pinned identity IKM, so `share_id = trunc128(SHA-384(domain ‖ pk ‖ root_commitment))` is receiver-recomputable without the folder path; a receiver folds only if `share_id == derive_share_id_v2(sender_pubkey, root_commitment)`. Two independent crypto lenses (opus) confirmed the **core binding is sound — occupying a specific victim id is a fixed-target second preimage, 2^128, no birthday shortcut; the deterministic secret nonce makes the commitment hiding** (v1's nonce-free derivation was itself a folder-path confirmation oracle, closed by v2). Ratified with these lens fixes folded into the frozen text: proto3 has no `required`, so an absent/non-48-byte commitment is **hard-rejected with no legacy arm** (the binding rests entirely on the ingest check); the derive-check gates the catalog fold **and** the withdraw branch **and** route import **and** the circle path (all build ISCs — #152 shipped route-theater once); all four publish sites migrate off `mint_share_id` (three currently emit random ids a v2 receiver would reject); `root_commitment` binds into the provenance signature; the A-over-B "more structure" rationale is withdrawn as false (`rc` is receiver-unverifiable at ingest — all resistance is the pk-committed id's second-preimage hardness); the cross-tier same-folder linkage that determinism grants a dual-membership peer is **accepted** to keep cross-relay id portability (tier-in-nonce is the lever if that trade ever inverts). Breaking `daemonseed-proto` v1 change, clean cutover break (no live pre-cutover shares). ISC-S21 revision (it still says "OS CSPRNG minted" — stale since #112) lands with the build. Task ISA `PAI/MEMORY/WORK/20260712-veilid-fable-freeze/ISA.md`. Advisor + 3-lens panel pattern per the Fable campaign. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Added (the design doc graduates straight to build against #156; no ROADMAP entry).
- 2026-07-12: **WB-5 write-lane amendment FROZEN + RATIFIED (Fable; `docs/design/veilid-write-budget.md` §WB-5), reopening WB-3's I5 controller only.** The 2026-07-12 orinoco felt-test measured `set_dht_value` completions of 54–601s at minimal offered rate; veilid-core 0.5.4's set fanout deadline is **progress-extending** (each accept re-arms the 10s timeout — minutes-long completions are legitimate on a churny net, `fanout_call.rs:642-657`), so the latency is an **exogenous regime, not our congestion**. WB-3's AIMD-on-latency therefore collapses the window to floor=1 and serializes non-chat writes against a floor it cannot lower. Established facts: DHT **reads and writes share ONE process-global 16-permit semaphore** (`storage_manager/mod.rs:177`), and **content fetch (`app_call`) is OFF that gate** (`rpc_app_call.rs` never touches `StorageManager`) — so the budget is `writes + read-lane ≤ 16`, fetch independent. Verdict (ratified): freeze the **diagnosis + corrected controller structure + sequencing, NOT the numeric**. Structure = a record-scaled non-chat window; a **permit-acquire-wait** congestion guard replacing the slope test (shared-gate contention is a uniform offset a bucket-slope guard is blind to — the deepest adversarial finding); an enforced cross-lane accountant keeping write+read in-flight under the gate; chat (I4) + the I8 floor reserved against the **semaphore**, not just the window. Sequencing = **`W_max` starts at the frozen 2** and steps up only after an attended single-client control run confirms offered-rate-independence (WB-ISC-19). Adds a WB-4.L2 discovery floor to the #172 resweep backoff (an elevated regime must not stop discovery → #157 regression) and a presence-staleness UI signal under reap-in-calm. Withdraw durability is best-effort (`Ok(None)` may only queue an offline flush) — the catalog TTL is the real guarantee. Preserves every WB-3 rate bound + I2/I4/I8. Transport-internal, no proto/wire change; night-buildable against the structure, numeric gated on the orinoco rider. Advisor + ordering/consistency adversarial lens (F1–F7). doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Added.
- 2026-07-11: **WB-2 read-budget half is frozen-in-name-only — a design-freeze candidate (surfaced closing out the #157 follow-ups).** The 2026-07-09 write-budget freeze nailed WB-0/WB-1/WB-3/WB-4 and WB-2's *write* ceiling+oracle (≤4 non-chat writes/min; WB-ISC-9 paused-time oracle passing; WB-2 Oracle #2 — the `DAEMONSEED_VEILID_TRACE` live-trace write-rate/p95 — is DEFERRED-VERIFY → the orinoco felt-test). But WB-2's *read* lane (`docs/design/veilid-write-budget.md` §Read budget, line 85) was declared — "reads paced in their own lane… the oracle counts read ops against a declared read ceiling" — and never actually frozen: **no numeric read ceiling exists and no read-count oracle exists** (WB-ISC-9 counts writes only; there is no read-lane WB-ISC), and the steady-resweep read cost scales **linearly with room count** (the #157 known limitation), so the read lane has no ceiling that holds as N grows. **#171 (tail-sweep) and #172 (stop-on-quiet backoff) are therefore the WB-2 read-lane's unbuilt governors, not standalone efficiency items.** A WB-2 read-budget close-out — freeze a numeric read ceiling + a read-count oracle + situate #171/#172 as the mechanisms under it — is a legitimate Fable design-freeze; cutover-scope, not alpha-blocking. Priority: the higher-stakes Fable freeze is **#156** (security boundary); the WB-2 read-budget close-out is the natural second. Brief for #156: vault `Projects/Daemonseed/156-share-id-binding-fable-brief-2026-07-11.md`.
- 2026-07-11: **#157 follow-up triage — #3 (dedup) landed; #1/#2 reclassified design-first.** Cleared the mechanical one and, on reading the code, found the other two are NOT mechanical cleanups — each re-opens the #157 loss if rushed. **#3 (shared `next_resweep_seed`) DONE:** hoisted the byte-identical GUI/TUI cursor selector into a new `daemonseed-veilid-net::resweep` module (`pub fn next_resweep_seed`) as its single home + canonical oracle; both net actors import it and their local fn + duplicate tests are removed. The per-crate `STEADY_RESWEEP_TICK` / warmup hand-off consts stay put (crate-specific docs; hand-offs differ 70s vs 20s — the follow-up named only the fn). Gate GREEN (VM-side, `$HOME/.cache/daemonseed-gate-target`): fmt clean; clippy `-D warnings` on veilid-net (crate dir), `daemonseed-gui --features "desktop veilid"`, `daemonseed-tui --features veilid`; nextest veilid-net 40 (oracle moved here, 39→40), gui 129, tui 213 (each −1: the duplicate test removed) — coverage preserved, one canonical oracle instead of two copies. LAMA manifest deliberately unchanged: `next_resweep_seed` is an internal cross-crate helper with no cross-implementation contract (unlike `member_slot_id`), not consumer surface. Reviewer: a verbatim move with a passing oracle + green gate → verified by an eyeballed `git diff` (only `next_resweep_seed`-related removals + the 2-line `lib.rs` export), not the 16-agent `/code-review` (proportionality on a zero-logic-change hoist). **#1 (tail-sweep) → DESIGN-FIRST, not a lever:** the 2026-07-10 framing ("resweep only beyond each record's high-water") does not map to the subkey layout — the append-ring is **spatial**, not temporal (`rendezvous::{MEMBER_REGIONS=32, RING_DEPTH=2}`; each member hashes by `node_pub` bytes to a FIXED region, `region*RING_DEPTH + seq%RING_DEPTH`), so there is no monotonic per-record high-water subkey to sweep beyond; skipping subkeys strands a message in a skipped region — the exact #157 non-delivery. A correct "sweep only what changed" needs per-subkey DHT-`seq` version tracking (read cheap, refresh only advanced slots), a real design item, not a high-water index. **#2 (stop-on-quiet backoff) → DESIGN-FIRST:** the actor observes only raw `VeilidNetEvent::Inbound`, which INCLUDES deduped re-deliveries (dedup lives downstream in `push_message`), so "back off when no NEW content arrives" has no signal at the actor layer — a correct backoff needs a newly-accepted signal plumbed back from the dedup layer (else every resweep that re-reads old backlog resets the timer and backoff never engages). Both stay the right levers for their known limitations but are design-first; filed as #171 (tail-sweep) / #172 (stop-on-quiet backoff), cross-linked as a coupled pair (a cheap tail-sweep changes the backoff cost model). doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Changed.
- 2026-07-10: **#157 review triage (`/code-review high`, 6 findings).** APPLIED four: (1) the TUI copied the GUI's 70s warmup hand-off but has **no** stepped warmup schedule (only the connect-time login sweep), so 70s was a dead window with no re-surfacing — the TUI hand-off is now 20s (clears the connect burst, not a long gap); (3) the sweep is a continuous background read load (one 64-GET/tick indefinitely) that partly re-introduces the #140 fan-spin cost — the tick is raised 10s→15s and the sustained-load cost is documented with a stop-on-quiet-backoff follow-up; (4) a resweep can outlast the tick on a slow DHT and overlap the next, breaking the "one sweep at a time" WB-2 bound — an in-flight `AtomicBool` guard now skips a tick while a prior sweep is still running (gui + tui); (6) the TUI hand-off test asserted only `>0` (vacuous) — strengthened to a sensible band. DECLINED two, with rationale: (2) per-record latency grows linearly with room count — this is the already-documented known limitation whose fix is the tail-sweep follow-up, not a new defect; (5) `next_resweep_seed` + constants are duplicated in the gui/tui actors — the cursor copies are byte-identical and each unit-tested (no current defect), so sharing the helper into `daemonseed-veilid-net` is a maintainability follow-up, deferred to keep this fix focused and avoid re-gating the workspace-excluded crate this session. **Follow-ups (not in this change):** tail-sweep (per-record latency), stop-on-quiet resweep backoff (sustained read load), shared `next_resweep_seed` in `daemonseed-veilid-net` (dedup).
- 2026-07-10: **Steady-state re-surfacing — WB-4 built, generalized past the frozen #157 scope (#157).** The 2026-07-10 orinoco felt-test reframed the priority: share *discovery* worked (downloads landed), but lobby/circle *chat* written after the warmup window never reached a settled peer — matching record keys, DHT writes 36–246s, and warmup resweeps that end at +60s while the passive DHT watch delivers ValueChanges only intermittently. WB-4's L2 (temporal leg) diagnosis therefore applies to chat too, refuting the freeze's premise that "chat traffic itself generates ValueChanges" is a *reliable* re-surfacing mechanism. **caraka chose (AskUserQuestion) to generalize the reader-resweep to ALL subscribed chat/discovery records**, not just the share-advert record — a felt-test-justified extension of the frozen design (the design's own reproduce-before-fix clause invites returning findings that contradict the diagnosis). Mechanism (gui + tui net actors): a post-warmup `steady_resweep` select arm re-sweeps ONE record per ~10s tick, round-robin, over the current subscribed set (lobby chat + share-advert + operator + each circle chat; **presence excluded** — it self-heals via keepalive re-writes). One 64-GET sweep per tick bounds the instantaneous read burst (WB-2 read lane); per-record cadence = tick × record-count, scaling with room count. Advisor (Rule 2) corrections integrated: the round-robin cursor is **key-based, not index-based** (an index cursor over a set that changes on join/leave would skip a record and reintroduce the exact non-delivery bug), with an empty-set no-op guard and a +70s warmup hand-off so the two resweep sources never double the read burst. Re-delivery stays deduped (`push_message`), presence re-freshen from re-read old chat is blocked by WB-1.5 leave-tombstone dominance, and unread is gated by the per-room high-water. **Known limitation:** per-record latency grows linearly with room count; the follow-up lever is a tail-sweep (resweep only beyond each record's high-water) so more records fit per tick. ISC: WB-ISC-14 registered (`daemonseed-isc` TOTAL 193→194); WB-ISC-15 (a late-converging client still discovers/receives) DEFERRED to its two-node orinoco oracle. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed + design-doc WB-4 build note.
- 2026-07-10: **Chat→presence fold replay-freshness bound (#165).** The read-side chat fold (`apply_member_write`, gui lobby + circle, tui lobby) refreshed roster liveness on any verified same-room chat write, unlike the beacon fold which gates on `beacon_is_fresh` (±window). An untrusted relay replaying one captured, provenance-valid old chat write could thus sustain a ghost "online" indefinitely (replay < TTL), defeating both crash-ages-at-TTL and graceful-leave-disappears. The chat fold now gates the presence refresh on `beacon_is_fresh(msg.sent_unix_ms, now)` — message delivery and dedup are untouched (WB-ISC-4 still folds a *fresh* chat write). Oracle: `issue_165_stale_chat_write_does_not_refresh_presence`. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed.
- 2026-07-10: **Graceful-close leave delivery — gui (#161, gui half).** The WB-1.3 leave tombstone was spawned fire-and-forget in the gui `WithdrawAllOwned` handler and the process exited before its DHT set completed, so a graceful quit left the member on peers' rosters for the full ~600s TTL — the exact linger WB-1.3 exists to eliminate. The gui close now *awaits* each per-room leave (`publish_public_leave` / `publish_circle_leave`, mirroring the already-awaited withdraws; `publish_presence` resolves after the scheduler's `set_dht_value` returns), and the close budget is raised 8s→12s to cover withdraws + leaves. The tui has no graceful-close command surface and still relies on the TTL backstop (tui half of #161 open). Live delivery is a felt-test/DEFERRED-VERIFY item (orinoco: quit a gui client → peers drop it within the close budget, not at the TTL). doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed.
- 2026-07-10: **WB-3 scheduler hardening — busy-spin fix (#162) + in-flight tombstone dominance (#164).** Two post-freeze correctness fixes to `daemonseed_veilid_net::schedule` surfaced by an independent `/code-review max` over the WB-1/WB-3 diff; neither changes a boundary, both close gaps the paused-time oracles structurally missed. (1) **#162:** `next_wakeup` clamped an already-passed I8 starvation-escalation crossing to `now` (`.max(now)`), so under sustained congestion (full AIMD window) the driver hot-looped `sleep_until(now)` → 100% CPU. Escalation only reorders the non-chat pool — it does not bypass the I5 cap — so a past-due crossing has nothing to wake for; it is now armed only while still future (deadlines, which dispatch-and-clear via `pick_deadline_due`, are unchanged). (2) **#164:** the WB-ISC-12 tombstone-dominance check scanned only the per-record queue; an in-flight tombstone (popped, record in `in_flight` as a bare `RecordId`) was invisible, so a same-id keepalive enqueued mid-leave-write resurrected the departed member on completion. The scheduler now tracks the in-flight tombstone's logical id per record (`in_flight_tombstone`) and dominates a same-id current-state against it exactly like a queued tombstone — extending WB-ISC-12 to the in-flight window. Oracles: `issue_162_past_due_escalation_does_not_busy_spin`, `issue_164_in_flight_tombstone_dominates_same_id_current_state`. doc-sync: CHANGELOG [Unreleased] Fixed.
- 2026-07-10: **WB-1 presence model built (Slice B of the write-budget freeze) — constant-cadence fixed-length keepalive + read-side inference.** Replaces the [50,55]s open-loop beacon + 60s reaper (the #159 Tier-1 stopgap) across `daemonseed-core::{heartbeat,presence}` and the gui/tui veilid net actors. **Write side (through the WB-3 funnel):** a JOIN beacon on subscribe (class-2 session-boundary), a KEEPALIVE every [180,220]s jittered (class-4, `next_keepalive_interval`, activity-independent), and a LEAVE tombstone on graceful close (class-2 `WriteKind::Tombstone`, so I3 dominance stops a queued keepalive resurrecting a departed member). **Fixed length (WB-ISC-6):** all presence writes pad to one constant `HEARTBEAT_SEALED_LEN` before AES-256-GCM (`len ‖ protobuf ‖ zero-pad`), so ciphertext length is not a share-count / handle-length / leave-vs-keepalive oracle; the digest (`live_share_ids`) is moved OUT of the veilid beacon (share liveness rides the share record post-#153) and an over-budget payload is rejected (`HeartbeatError::TooLarge`), never sealed longer. **Leave marker:** a new provenance-bound `MemberHeartbeat.is_leave` (proto field 7) — bound into the ML-DSA signature so a relay can neither forge nor strip a leave; byte-identical to a keepalive on the wire (only in-room members decrypt it). **Read side (emission-free, WB-ISC-8):** freshness = `max(last beacon, last verified same-room member write)` with leave-dominance (WB-ISC-7 — a same-room write `≤ leave_ms` never re-freshens a departed member); a same-room chat write advances freshness (WB-ISC-4, wired into the gui/tui inbound chat paths, delivered after the message); the tracker is room-scoped so cross-room activity can't refresh it (WB-ISC-2); TTL 600s (WB-1.9); reaping suspended while the local enqueue-to-ack latency is elevated (WB-ISC-5, `REAP_CONGESTION_THRESHOLD`, signal exposed via `VeilidNetHandle::last_write_latency_ms`). **Scope:** presence slot geometry is UNCHANGED (`current_state_subkey(hex(pubkey))`) — the disjoint-region collision fix stays a #77 requirement (WB-1.11), tolerated at lobby scale with ephemeral ids. Leave tombstones are gui-only (the tui has no graceful-close command surface; it relies on the TTL backstop). WB-ISC-1–8 register here (`daemonseed-isc` TOTAL 185→193: 5 pos + 3 neg). Proto change: `MemberHeartbeat.is_leave` (additive scalar field 7, PATCH-level wire — old decoders default false).
- 2026-07-10: **WB-1 presence model built (Slice B of the write-budget freeze) — `daemonseed_core::{heartbeat,presence}` + the gui/tui net actors, on Slice A's funnel.** Replaces the [50,55]s open-loop beacon + 60s reaper. **Write side (activity-independent):** a constant-cadence [180,220]s jittered keepalive (`presence::next_keepalive_interval` — an activity-free CSPRNG draw taking no input from sends/reads/congestion) enqueued class-4 through the WB-3 funnel; a class-2 join beacon on subscribe and a class-2 leave tombstone in the close budget; **all presence-class writes seal to ONE constant byte length** (`HEARTBEAT_PADDED_PLAINTEXT_LEN = 8192` → `HEARTBEAT_SEALED_LEN`, framed `len(4,LE)‖protobuf‖zero-pad`; the variable `live_share_ids`/digest is moved OFF the beacon so a key-free storage node cannot read share counts or change-times from ciphertext length, and an oversized payload is rejected rather than sealed longer — WB-ISC-6). **Read side (emission-free by contract — WB-ISC-8):** freshness = `max(last beacon, last verified same-room member write)` dominated by the leave tombstone (a provenance-bound `is_leave` field a relay can neither forge nor strip; no same-room write with `sent_unix_ms ≤ leave_ms` re-freshens a departed member, while a genuinely newer write rejoins — WB-ISC-7); freshness input is same-room-bound (WB-ISC-2, a public surface never timestamps private-circle activity); TTL 600s (= 2×keepalive-max + 160s slack); reap suspended while the scheduler's enqueue-to-ack latency (`VeilidNetHandle::last_write_latency_ms`) is elevated (WB-ISC-5, bias-to-forgiveness). Presence slots stay `current_state_subkey(member pubkey)` — the dedicated larger schema / disjoint per-member regions is a #77 requirement (WB-1.11), NOT this build; the alpha tolerates the residual at lobby scale with ephemeral ids. `cot.proto` MemberHeartbeat gains the provenance-bound leave marker and drops the variable metadata (regenerated; `check-proto` matches). WB-ISC-1–8 register in `## Criteria` (`daemonseed-isc` TOTAL 185→193).**
- 2026-07-10: **WB-3 write scheduler built (Slice A of the write-budget freeze) — `daemonseed_veilid_net::schedule`.** The one prioritized, rate-limited funnel every `set_dht_value` passes through (I1): chat > session-boundary > advert-refresh > keepalive > republish, per-record FIFO, last-writer-wins current-state coalescing keyed on the LOGICAL id (member/share/"motd", never the physical subkey), with non-coalescible dominant tombstones/withdraws (the #121/#118 resurrection guard — a queued withdraw is never superseded by a same-id current-state write; a same-id current-state enqueued while a withdraw is pending is dropped). Chat holds a reserved dispatch slot, is never coalesced/dropped, and dispatches ≤2s to an idle record at any queue depth (I4); the non-chat in-flight set is bounded by the dormant `AimdWindow` fed enqueue-to-ack latency (I5, initial cap 2); deadline override for operator MOTD/announcement expiry (I6b, #158); graceful-close flush of chat+tombstones with class-3/4/5 shed (I7); one-class starvation escalation (I8); and the enqueue surface reachable only from write intents — no read/render/reap path can enqueue (I9, WB-0's derived rule). **Seam:** a `WriteSink` trait abstracts the physical write (the WB-2 oracle requirement) — production dispatches through the UNCHANGED per-record `publish_rendezvous`/`publish_current_state` (so #131 clamp-at-insert + ring-seq-inside-`record_lock` are untouched, I2/I13); a counting mock + paused virtual time drives the oracles. The actor's three write commands + the advert-refresh path enqueue and return, so a slow DHT set never parks the command loop (#154 retired as designed). WB-ISC-9/10/11/12/13 register here (`daemonseed-isc` TOTAL 180→185); the presence model (WB-1, WB-ISC-1–8) and the two-leg discovery fix (WB-4, WB-ISC-14/15) are separate later slices.
- 2026-07-09: **Veilid write-budget design FROZEN + ratified — design-of-record `docs/design/veilid-write-budget.md` (#159 Tier-2; reframes #157).** Boundary-level calls, decided against ISC-A-S2 and hardened by a 3-lens adversarial review: (1) **presence model** = constant-cadence **fixed-length** sealed keepalive ([180,220]s initial, tunable [120,300]s; TTL 600s; join/keepalive/leave byte-identical so ciphertext length carries no share/handle metadata) + **read-side** freshness inference (same-room verified writes refresh liveness; leave-tombstone dominates; reap suspended while enqueue-to-ack latency is elevated). The frozen invariant: *user activity may influence only local computation — never the timing, size, or existence of a presence/discovery-class network emission, write or read-fetch*; read-triggered writes (receipts, acks, last-active bumps) are forbidden by contract. Activity-derived presence is admissible ONLY receiver-side — write-side coupling is the metadata channel ISC-A-S2 forbids. (2) **Write scheduler**: every `set_dht_value` through one prioritized rate-limited funnel (chat > session-boundary > advert > keepalive > republish; per-record FIFO with seq-at-dispatch preserves #131/D-0b; logical-id coalescing, tombstones non-coalescible; chat flushes at close; subsumes #154). Ceiling: 4 non-chat writes/min steady (scheduler-seam paused-time oracle — the build MUST add the write-sink seam). (3) **Two-leg discovery rule**: a discovery write needs a swept-whole record AND steady-state re-surfacing; **#157's violation is the temporal leg** (warmup resweeps end +60s < 68–112s foreign-content convergence; write-once adverts raise no post-settle ValueChange) — fix by re-surfacing, not re-placement; reproduce-before-fix. #77 build MUST resolve presence slot collisions (~37% pair at 8 members). WB-ISC-1..15 register at the build commits.
- 2026-07-09: **Veilid DHT write saturation — Tier-1 presence-cadence mitigation (#159); structural fix deferred to the veilid-write-budget design freeze.** A live 2-client felt-test (3 circles + active shares + presence, `feat/veilid-migration`) measured `set_dht_value` latency at 90s–314s (`refresh_share_adverts: done in 290945ms`) — one root cause, three faces: chat arrives minutes late, presence heartbeats miss the reap TTL (roster flicker-then-reap), and share-advert route refresh lags so fetchers import dead routes (`chunk fetch failed`).
