Root cause: daemonseed imposes a high volume of periodic writes on Veilid's seconds-per-write discovery primitive, dominated by the per-member lobby presence beacon (~[15,20]s; #77 circle presence would multiply it). **Tier-1 (this commit):** widen the beacon cadence to [50,55]s (gui+tui) — a ~3× write cut; re-test dropped latency to ≤5.8s and restored presence/chat/warmup. **Tier-2 (deferred):** presence is fundamentally a *read* built as a per-member *write* (the amplification); the structural fix (presence-as-reads + a priority write scheduler, chat > presence > adverts) is decided against the ISC-A-S2 anti-dox threat model in a Fable design-freeze producing `docs/design/veilid-write-budget.md`. Couples with #157 (share-advert record layout is the same rendezvous-record-placement question). Cadence value is re-test tunable; not a wire change.

- 2026-07-09: **Pre-alpha trust-fix batch — #152 share ownership + #153 share-record split (fix both properly; caraka's call).** Goal: a Veilid alpha for idle testers off `feat/veilid-migration` WITHOUT the full v0.33.0 main-cutover (an alpha ≠ the cutover; and caraka confirmed breaking the relay gates nothing — no testers remain on the relay and relay/Veilid testers never meet). A whole-branch Fable pre-cutover review filed #152 (share censorship/hijack), #153 (chat↔share silent collision), #154 (actor no-park latency), #155 (nameless double-render). Triaged by *alpha* impact: #153 is the one that bites (silent message loss in the exact chat+share path testers exercise) → fix; #152 is adversary-gated but the lobby record is on the PUBLIC DHT so a trusted group doesn't bound the attacker → fix; #154/#155 defer to the cutover. **#153:** share adverts get their OWN world-derivable Veilid rendezvous record (`derive_room_share_veilid_owner_seed` / `RoomShareVeilidOwnerSeed` / `public_room_share_veilid_owner` — a fourth sibling, disjoint from room-key/chat/presence), exactly mirroring how presence was separated (P1). Chat's append-ring and shares' current-state scheme no longer overlap the 64-slot subkey space. gui+tui subscribe + re-sweep the share record and publish/withdraw adverts on it; inbound demux is by-parse (no record tag) so it needed no change. **#152:** `ShareCatalog::apply` binds authority to ownership — reject a withdraw or owner-changing refresh whose `sender_pubkey` ≠ the stored owner (first-writer-wins on identity). **A `/code-review high` (13 agents) then caught #152 as INCOMPLETE — 3 CONFIRMED gaps:** (1)+(3) `apply_discovery` mutated the discovered-**route** map unconditionally (the fetch path reads that map, NOT the catalog), so a rejected forged withdraw still evicted the owner's route and a rejected hijack refresh still replaced it — #152 was security-theater without gating those on the `CatalogChange`. FIXED: gate `discovered.remove` on `Removed` and `discovered.insert` on `!= Unchanged`. (2) ingest never verifies `share_id == derive_share_id(sender_pubkey, root)`, so first-seed squatting of a not-yet-announced `share_id` survives — the receiver lacks `root` so it can't recompute; a receiver-verifiable binding is a design change → DEFERRED to **#156** (cutover-gate, adversary-gated, not alpha-blocking; once bound, first-writer-wins is unnecessary for security). Two cleanups also FIXED: the share write path fails closed on a derivation error (no all-zeros world-writable write record — presence's benign fallback was wrong for a write path); and the three room owner-seed derivations share one `expand_room_owner_seed` body (no divergence of trust-critical KDF plumbing). **`Closes #153` at the cutover; #152 stays open pending #156.**

- 2026-07-08: **Room↔circle convergence — one signed `RoomMessage` (Option A; design-of-record `docs/design/room-circle-unification.md` § Design (A); #145/#146/#147).** A public room and a private circle now behave identically except how their key is derived. `daemonseed-proto` merges `CircleMessage` + `PublicRoomMessage` into one signed `RoomMessage` (MAJOR wire break; no cutover gate — the relay is unused, so it breaks no users; no migration — ephemeral content). `daemonseed-core` gains `room_message`, the SINGLE signed seal/open path parameterized by `(key, AEAD AAD, provenance domain, expected_room_id)`; `circle::message` + `public_room` are thin wrappers supplying their key + DISTINCT domains (`daemonseed/circle/message/v2` — the signed shape — vs `daemonseed/public-room/message/v1`). **Two ratified hardening calls (commit-boundary advisor):** (a) the open helper verifies the signature against a caller-supplied `expected_room_id` — NEVER the carried field — so "never trust the carried room_id" is a property of the type signature, not a runtime branch (public passes the subscribed room name; circle passes `circle_room_id(cot_key)` = 6-byte→12-hex of `SHA-384(cot_key)`); (b) `sender_pubkey`/`signature` are exact-length-gated before any ML-DSA call (empty pubkey ≠ anyone). App: gui+tui route circle send/open through it; `mine`/dedup key on the stable `sender_pubkey` (fixes the #143 rename-dedup root — handle is mutable, pubkey stable); the GUI transmits the canonical `name#<12hex>` handle (`net::canonical_wire_handle`) so `display_bound` honors the display name (the TUI already did via `own_handle()`). New ISC-C99 (circle authorship provenance); registry addition (`daemonseed-isc`) is a tracked follow-up (unit-covered now, integration `register()` pending, per the M15 known-gap pattern). **Two independent reviews before commit:** a Silas adversarial pass (all six trust properties hold, no break) and a `/code-review max` (18 agents) — the latter caught real display-layer regressions the security review + unit tests missed (GUI transmitted a bare handle → floored every peer to `#hex` + broke own-loopback dedup), all fixed; one carried-`room_id` propagation (LOW-1) fixed; TUI cold-start own-half + a triplicated AEAD envelope deferred to follow-up issues.
- 2026-07-08: **Reviewer triage — round-2 fix batch (`/code-review high`, 2 confirmed / 1 refuted).** (1) CONFIRMED "the MOTD DHT value can expire with no re-publish path" → ACCEPTED / DEFERRED: this is the documented #141 tradeoff — excluding the mutable MOTD from keep-alive trades a recoverable expiry for eliminating the network-wide reversion; safe MOTD keep-alive is tracked to #136 (monotonic version). Not a new regression: pre-#141 the MOTD had no keep-alive either. Downstream note (reviewer, plausible): a MOTD aging out while posts persist flips `combined_content_hash` → a one-time spurious Announcements unread dot — minor, resolves with #136 MOTD durability (candidate follow-up). (2) CONFIRMED CHANGELOG #141 rationale/mechanism prose vs the AGENTS.md reference-doc rule → FIXED (trimmed to a bare fact). (3) REFUTED `announcements_unread(view, current_hash)` coupled-param — the verifier cleared it (the caller computes the hash once; that is #142's intent).

- 2026-07-08: **Removed the vestigial #93 connect-time landing plumbing (keeps base-desktop clippy green).** #142 replaced the #93 unread-gated auto-landing with a content-hash unread dot, leaving `connect_time` (on `NetEvent::PublicSpaceSnapshot`), `OperatorSpace::landing_pending`, `OPERATOR_CONNECT_LANDING_DELAY`, and the connect-time self-refresh spawn as dead code — the field was written by both actors but read only by tests, tripping `dead_code` under base `desktop`. `cargo xtask release-gate` runs `clippy daemonseed-gui --features desktop -D warnings`, so it would have blocked the v0.33.0 cutover. Removed the whole cluster. Operator content still drives the dot: each verified inbound fold emits a `PublicSpaceSnapshot`, and the #140 warmup re-sweep force-refreshes the operator record early — so dropping the vestigial settle-refresh loses nothing. The veilid actor keeps its `_cmd_tx` param for spawn-signature uniformity with the relay actor. GUI only.

- 2026-07-08: **refined: #143 scroll-yank — a deduped own loopback no longer re-scrolls (code-review fix, GUI).** The #143 own-backlog render (below) left the Lobby/Circle handlers running `set_scroll(STICK_BOTTOM)` + `apply_view` whenever the room was active, keyed only on `active == idx`, not on whether a line was inserted. An own message echoes locally then loops back as the same `(who, text, sent_unix_ms)`; `push_message` dedups it (no new line) but the handler still ran with `stick = mine = true`, yanking the reader's scroll to the bottom. RootCauseAnalysis located the root at `push_message`'s return contract, which collapsed "inserted a line" and "raised the unread dot" into one boolean meaning the latter — so the triage's literal "gate on the existing return" would have REGRESSED (that boolean is false for EVERY message into the active room, so an own echo AND an incoming-at-bottom message would both stop rendering). Fix: `push_message` returns `PushOutcome { inserted, unread_raised }`; the active-room scroll/render gates on `inserted`, the background rail rebuild stays on `unread_raised` (which still implies `inserted`). Oracle: `own_echo_loopback_is_deduped_and_reports_not_inserted`. GUI only.

- 2026-07-08: **refined: #141 keep-alive excludes the mutable MOTD (code-review fix, GUI).** The initial #141 keep-alive (below, same day) re-published MOTD + announcements uniformly. A batch code-review found this reverts the MOTD network-wide: the `"motd"` slot is MUTABLE and the fold (`apply_operator_item`) has no newer-wins, so re-publishing a stale MOTD lets peers adopt + rebroadcast it over a newer/cleared one — silent data corruption. Content-addressed announcement slots are idempotent under re-publish (key = content hash), so they carry no such risk. Fix: `collect_operator_keepalive_items` collects announcements ONLY; MOTD keep-alive waits for the #136 monotonic version that makes the mutable slot safe. The symptom caraka actually hit was an announcement expiring, so the durability win is preserved. Oracle: `collect_operator_keepalive_items_excludes_the_mutable_motd`. GUI only.

- 2026-07-08: **#144 startup "assembling network" mask — first cut (GUI).** The cold-start DHT convergence floor is unavoidable (#140: ~1.5–2 min warm / up to ~7 min first-ever), so the app opened to an empty-but-filling Lobby that read as broken. Cover it with a full-cover startup splash framed as the serverless feature ("Assembling your peer network — no servers, just peers"). **Event-driven, not timer-driven** (the warmup is long + variable, so a fixed timer would reveal an empty app too early or overstay): raised in `connect_now` on Connect, dismissed on the first real content (`NetEvent::Message` / `CircleMessage`, or a non-empty `Roster`) or by the user ("Enter anyway"). Shows the live `connection-status`. **First cut** — deferred follow-ups: plumb the attach peer-count (the trace has 44→72) into the overlay, per-surface "discovering…" states behind it, an actual animation. GUI Slint (`startup-overlay-open` + a full-cover `Rectangle`); the render + dismiss timing are felt-test-gated (no new testable logic — the dismiss triggers are one-line event-handler calls). GUI only.

- 2026-07-08: **#140 warmup re-sweep — dialled down after felt-test (GUI).** The orinoco felt-test (2026-07-08) showed the 4-round 12/25/45/75 s schedule was net-negative: it spun the host fans up (~900 force-refresh gets/client — 64 subkeys × ~14 re-sweeps, mostly empty slots) while giving NO benefit for the priority target (operator content converged ~120 s, past the +90 s window → the passive watch delivered it), and #142 removed the #93 landing the schedule used to feed. Dialled to a LIGHT 2-round schedule `WARMUP_RESWEEP_SCHEDULE` = [20, 60] s, `WARMUP_CIRCLE_RESWEEP_ROUNDS` = 1 (circles only round 1) — a best-effort early-catch that helps only fast-converging content; slower content rides the passive watch. Decoupled from `OPERATOR_CONNECT_LANDING_DELAY` (the round-2 ≤ landing invariant dropped — #142 killed the landing). **The proper load fix — stop-on-content backoff (stop re-sweeping a record once it delivers) — remains deferred** (needs the sweep to report a found-count back; today `resweep_rendezvous` returns before its spawned sweep runs). **Whether to keep this light schedule, dial further, or revert to the single one-shot is caraka's call after felt-test** — the resweep is the lowest-value item of the session and may not earn even this load. Felt-tunable.

- 2026-07-08: **#142 announcements unread-dot, never force-open (GUI).** caraka's decision: replace the #93 connect-time auto-landing (`landing_decision → Landing::Announcements → set_active_tab(3)`) with a universal Announcements-tab unread dot. The auto-land (a) landed on a BLANK pane when operator content had not DHT-converged by the +35 s deadline (felt-test 2026-07-08), and (b) yanked the user mid-flow for rare operator content. The dot is non-intrusive, behaves identically on connect and mid-session, and mirrors the room dot (#64). New `state::announcements_unread(view, stored_hash)` — non-empty view AND (hash != stored, or never seen); the **empty-view guard** (a not-yet-converged / blank view is never unread) subsumes the blank-land defect. `main.rs` computes unread on EVERY `PublicSpaceSnapshot` (connect + mid-session; `connect_time` ignored), sets the `announce-unread` Slint prop, and clears + persists the seen-hash when the pane is the active tab or is opened (`on_announcements_tab_opened`). Slint: `TabLabel` gains an `unread` dot (never on the active tab). `landing_decision`/`Landing` removed. **Deferred (coupled to #140):** `OPERATOR_CONNECT_LANDING_DELAY` + the connect-landing spawn + `landing_pending`/`connect_time` are now vestigial (the +35 s spawn still fires a harmless refresh); their removal touches the resweep's landing-delay references, so it rides the #140 keep/dial-down decision. Oracles: `announcements_unread_covers_all_cases`, `viewing_updates_stored_hash_then_clears_unread`. The dot render is felt-test-gated. GUI only.

- 2026-07-08: **#141 operator content keep-alive (GUI).** Operator MOTD/announcements were written once on post (`set_motd`/`upload_announcement` → `publish_current_state`) and never refreshed; Veilid DHT values expire without owner refresh, so an announcement not recently re-written silently aged out (felt-test 2026-07-08: a prior session's announcement was gone on both fresh clients while the recently-edited MOTD survived; a fresh announcement propagated fine → expiry, not a #134 collision). Fix: a slow `OPERATOR_KEEPALIVE_INTERVAL` (120 s) timer in the net actor re-publishes the client's KNOWN `OperatorSpace` content (MOTD + each post) to the SAME slots (`"motd"` / content-address hex — identical to the compose paths), re-using the stored already-F17-signed bytes (idempotent, last-writer-wins). Gated on holding the announce owner seed (the write-gate) so only a writer keeps content alive (dev: every client; prod: the operator). The tick collects items synchronously via the pure `collect_operator_keepalive_items` then SPAWNS the DHT writes off the actor loop, so a many-item record never stalls chat/commands (#128 class). **Accepted limitation:** keep-alive holds only while a seed-holder is online — with no always-on node (serverless), content still expires if every writer is offline long enough (inherent; the operator being online is the production assumption). Interval is felt-tunable + should be confirmed under Veilid's default DHT value TTL. Oracle: `collect_operator_keepalive_items_bundles_known_content`. GUI only; live durability DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco.

- 2026-07-08: **#143 own-message cold-start backlog — dedup, not handle-suppress (GUI).** `handle_inbound` suppressed own inbound by `sender_handle == my_handle` (correct for a live re-surface, since the optimistic echo already rendered it — #101), but on cold start there is no prior echo, so the ring-sweep backlog dropped the user's own half → only the other party's messages rendered ("half the conversation"). Fix: emit own inbound `mine:true` (lobby `NetEvent::Message` + circle `NetEvent::CircleMessage`) and rely on the existing `push_message` exact-match dedup on `(sent_unix_ms, mine, who, text)` — the send path stamps ONE `sent_unix_ms` on both the wire message and the local echo (`send_room`/`send_circle`), so the live re-surface dedups exactly; a cold-start backlog own message has no echo and inserts once. Same content-dedup posture as the circle #105/#107 path, applied to own messages instead of blanket handle-suppression. Oracle: `handle_inbound_emits_our_own_looped_back_lobby_message_as_mine`. GUI only; live behaviour DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco.

- 2026-07-08: **#140 warmup re-sweep — stepped + priority-ordered (GUI).** The cold-start warmup investigation established the floor: attach → `public_internet_ready` ~8 s, all records open+watched ~13 ms, first PEER content ~68 s (lobby) / ~112 s (circle) — so the ~1.5–2 min restart warmup is **Veilid DHT value-convergence**, not local setup, and the sweep already force-refreshes (`rendezvous::sweep` → `get_dht_value(force_refresh=true)`). App-level headroom is thin (persistence already maxed — the on-disk table store is why restart beats first-ever; timeouts are a trap — raising `rpc.timeout_ms` reprices every RPC), so the one real lever is **read cadence**. The single post-connect re-sweep at +25 s (lobby + operator + circle chat records) becomes a **stepped** schedule `WARMUP_RESWEEP_SCHEDULE` = [12, 25, 45, 75] s dispatched on **absolute `sleep_until` deadlines** (a round's own await never drifts later rounds), re-sweeping in priority order **operator MOTD/announce → lobby → circles** (`warmup_priority_records`, unit-tested). **Load-bounded:** each sweep is `SUBKEY_COUNT`=64 force-refresh gets/record, so operator+lobby re-sweep every round but **circles only the first `WARMUP_CIRCLE_RESWEEP_ROUNDS`=2 rounds** — a heavily-joined user never fans out to `rounds × circles` concurrent sweeps. The first two rounds land at/under `OPERATOR_CONNECT_LANDING_DELAY` (35 s) so operator content is refreshed before the #93 landing (unit-guarded). **Scope:** GUI only; presence records excluded (self-heal via the heartbeat cycle). **Deferred:** stop-on-content backoff (needs `resweep_rendezvous`/`sweep` to report a found-count — today it returns before the spawned sweep runs); subkey-level ordering (chat ring before share slots within the lobby record). **Review triage (workflow code-review, high, 13 agents, 3 findings — all applied):** (1) **schedule drift** — advancing bookkeeping to the nominal offset (not measured time) let a round's await push later rounds past the 35 s landing → `sleep_until(start + at)` absolute deadlines. (2) **4× connect fan-out** — the circle taper (`WARMUP_CIRCLE_RESWEEP_ROUNDS`) bounds the many-circles blow-up the review flagged as warmup-window congestion (#128/#113 class). (3) **doc** — rewrote the "run concurrently" comment to state re-sweeps are issued sequentially in priority order (operator dispatched first). Two candidates were **refuted** by the verify pass (reconnect-overlap stacking; operator-open-ahead-of-lobby). Part of the #140 umbrella; the event-driven "assembling network" mask is the parallel UX half. Best-effort, felt-tunable — the live latency win is DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco.

- 2026-07-07: **Veilid Phase 4 #77 — circle member-presence over Veilid (GUI).** Extends the P-a lobby presence (#74, shipped + reviewed) to circles, per-circle — a clean mirror using the `derive_circle_presence_veilid_owner_seed` primitive already built in A-a. Each `VeilidCircle` gains a `presence_owner_seed` (its presence sibling record — P1: presence rides its OWN record, never the chat rendezvous) + a `PresenceTracker`; `join_circle` subscribes the presence record + inits the tracker; the heartbeat timer (`emit_and_reap_lobby_presence` → renamed `emit_and_reap_presence`, now also taking `&mut [VeilidCircle]`) emits one sealed beacon per circle (`seal_circle_heartbeat` under the circle's `cot_key`, published off-loop to the circle's presence record) and reaps each circle's tracker → `NetEvent::Roster{circle_id: Some(id)}`; `handle_inbound` (now `&mut [VeilidCircle]`) tries `open_heartbeat` under each circle's `cot_key` after the lobby heartbeat attempt (the seal scopes a beacon to exactly its circle — a distinct `cot_key` per circle means no cross-circle mis-open; a circle chat message already returned earlier via `open_message`, a lobby beacon via the lobby key, so only a real circle beacon reaches here), own-filters by pubkey + `beacon_is_fresh` (#78), applies to that circle's tracker, and emits `Roster{Some(id)}` on a render change. `main.rs` already routes `Roster{circle_id: Some(id)}` to the active circle (`if active_circle_id() == circle_id`), so no UI change. The circle heartbeat `room` = `default_circle_label(&circle_fingerprint(&cot_key))` is provenance-only (each beacon is self-verifying; routing is by which circle's key opened it, not the label). **Scope:** GUI only. **TUI circle presence DEFERRED** (the TUI does lobby presence emit/ingest from P-a but has no roster render; circle presence there mirrors that deferral — cutover-gate: TUI parity does not gate, GUI primary). Shares the operator/presence `current_state_subkey` 64-slot ceiling (#134). **Oracle:** `circle_heartbeat_folds_into_the_owning_circles_roster` (a verified fresh non-own circle beacon folds into that circle's roster + emits `Roster{Some(id)}`); P-a's two-node oracle already proves the mechanism-level convergence. Reviewed at **high**. **DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco:** two GUI clients in the same circle each see the other on the circle's roster; one quits → drops within the TTL. **Review triage (code-review, high, 6 findings):** (1) **applied — the roster was never RENDERED for circles** (a dead feature): the Slint roster pane (`app.slint`) was hard-gated to `active == 0` (Lobby only), so circle `Roster` events were routed by `main.rs` but the pane collapsed to 0px off-Lobby; ungated to `active-tab == 0` (the roster now shows the ACTIVE room's `HERE NOW` — Lobby or circle — on the chat tab, `main.rs` already sets `root.roster` to the active room's roster). **This is #77's render half — necessary for the feature to be visible + felt-testable.** (2) **applied — `apply_inbound_beacon` helper** collapses the near-triplicated own-filter/#78-freshness/apply/roster-emit logic (lobby + circle inbound) into one home, closing the divergence risk the review flagged on this trust-adjacent path. (3) **applied — skip the per-tick `SignKeypair` clone when no circles are joined** (guarded the circle emit on `!circles.is_empty()`). (4) **DEFERRED → #138 — stale/empty roster on switch-into a background room** with already-tracked members (the actor only pushes `Roster` on a render-CHANGE, and `main.rs` renders only the active room's; switching in doesn't re-render from the tracker). Not felt-test-blocking for the core two-clients-in-one-circle case (both active → live render); needs a switch→net roster-push, filed #138. (5) **declined — the circle CHAT own-suppression by handle** (not pubkey): pre-existing (not a #77 change — #77 only reborrowed `circles` as `&mut`), improbable with `#hash`-suffixed handles; out of scope. (6) **declined (negligible) — recomputing the circle label per tick** (a cheap `default_circle_label` every ~15–20 s); not worth a cached field + the join-path churn. (7) **noted — unbounded per-tick circle publish spawns** (one detached `publish_presence` per circle each tick): bounded by circle count, shares the presence off-loop-spawn pattern; the #134-class concern, not fixed here. **Diff now also touches `app.slint`** (the roster ungate) — the visual render is felt-test-gated. Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/phase4-veilid-presence-announcements.md`](docs/design/phase4-veilid-presence-announcements.md) (Part 1, #77).
- 2026-07-07: **Veilid Phase 4 A-d (#93) — the unread-gated announcements landing over Veilid (GUI).** The #93 primitives are all transport-agnostic and already built + tested: `state::{combined_content_hash, landing_decision}`, the `announce_seen`/`persist_announce_seen` marker, and the `main.rs` landing on a `PublicSpaceSnapshot{connect_time:true}` (hash the view, `landing_decision` vs the stored marker, auto-open the announcements tab iff changed, then persist). A-c emitted `connect_time:false` (deferred the landing). A-d makes the Veilid actor emit exactly ONE `connect_time:true` snapshot per connect at the right moment. **The marker-key was a non-issue:** `main.rs:1662` keys the marker on `relay_target().0` consistently for both store and read, and it returns a stable default in Veilid mode — so the existing landing logic works unchanged; no Veilid-specific channel id was needed. **The real nuance — async backlog (the relay's synchronous gRPC fetch didn't have it):** the operator record's backlog arrives ASYNC via the post-connect sweep, so an eager `connect_time:true` would run the landing over an empty/partial view — and since the stored marker usually matches the *full* content on a reconnect, a partial-view hash would differ and **false-positive-land on every reconnect**. **Resolution — a delayed settle-then-land:** the Connect handler re-arms an `OperatorSpace.landing_pending` flag (set on subscribe, re-armed each Connect for relay parity — the landing fires on every connect if content changed, not only the first) and spawns a one-shot task that, after `OPERATOR_CONNECT_LANDING_DELAY` (28 s, just past the #132 25 s re-sweep so the backlog + re-swept items have folded — a felt-test tunable), self-sends `NetCommand::RefreshPublicSpace` via the actor's self-send channel (the previously-unused `_cmd_tx`, now threaded as `cmd_tx` through `handle_command`). `refresh_public_space` consumes `landing_pending` (`std::mem::replace → false`) and passes it as `connect_time` to `public_space_snapshot_event`, so the landing fires exactly once over the SETTLED view and every other snapshot (a manual refresh, an inbound fold, a post-publish refresh) is `connect_time:false` — never yanking the user. A manual open before the delay simply consumes the flag first (landing there is a no-op — the user navigated there) and `main.rs` self-corrects the persisted marker to the full hash as the backlog folds. The delayed task's `cmd_tx.send` is a no-op if the actor has shut down (`let _`); no view is captured at spawn time (the settled view is read when the refresh runs). **Scope:** GUI only (`veilid_net.rs`); no core/proto/wire change (the `cmd_tx` threading is internal actor plumbing). **TUI untouched** (cutover-gate: GUI primary). **Oracle:** `refresh_fires_the_connect_landing_once_then_clears` (first refresh `connect_time:true`, second `false`) + the 5 `handle_command` test call-sites updated for the new param. Reviewed at **high**. **DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco:** a maintainer posts new announce content; a second client, on connect, auto-lands on the announcements pane; reconnecting with unchanged content stays on the Lobby. **Review triage (code-review, high, 5 findings):** (1) **applied — the operator owner_seed was MISSING from the #132 post-connect re-sweep** (which re-swept lobby + circles only), so a MOTD/announcement published during the warmup window was never re-fetched before the landing fired → the landing ran over stale content and missed the new item; the re-sweep seed list now includes the operator record. (2) **applied (partial) + DEFERRED (#137)** — the fixed-delay timer is a heuristic, not a settle confirmation, so on a slow DHT / a manual open during warmup / a rapid reconnect the landing can run over a PARTIAL view (land early/spuriously or miss a late item). Hardened: the delay bumped 28 s → 35 s (past the re-sweep + fold latency) and the const doc now states the best-effort limit honestly; the robust fix (hash-stable settle detection + a loop-owned timer replacing the detached self-refresh, which also removes the reconnect-overlap edge) is filed as **#137**. These residuals are UX auto-open *timing* only — the pane, the verified content, and the marker stay correct (a manual open mostly self-corrects: `main.rs` re-persists the marker on each fold while the tab is active; the reconnect-overlap edge is latent — the Veilid path is currently connect-once). (3) **applied — CHANGELOG entry cut to bare facts** (the mechanism/rationale prose it had violated the reference-artifact rule; the why now lives here + in the commit). **Scope stays GUI-only; no core/proto/wire change.** Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/phase4-veilid-presence-announcements.md`](docs/design/phase4-veilid-presence-announcements.md) (Part 2, A1 gaps-closed / #93).
- 2026-07-07: **Veilid Phase 4 A-c (#92) — the announcements/MOTD composer over Veilid (GUI), on the A0 F17-project-channel model.** A-b landed the operator-record transport (`publish_current_state`); A-c wires the GUI net actor's `SetMotd`/`UploadAnnouncement`/`RefreshPublicSpace` (were "not yet on Veilid") onto it. **The A0 model resolves what looked like an A2 whitelist block:** the MVP is ONE project-owned channel whose content is signed with the **F17 project-release key**, and `Whitelist::authorizes` treats `project_release_pubkey()` as UNCONDITIONALLY authorized — so an **empty `Whitelist::default()` verifies a project-channel MOTD/post**, and NO signer-whitelist distribution (the deferred A2 concern) is needed for this channel. So the composer signs with F17 (`dev_project_release_keypair()`, a new dev-only core accessor from the in-source `PROJECT_RELEASE_SEED`, the signing sibling of `dev_project_announce_veilid_owner_seed`'s write-gate), NOT the local stable identity (that's the relay per-signer model, deferred as multi-author A3). **The read-side demux — a 1-byte KIND tag.** Inbound `VeilidNetEvent::Inbound` carries no slot id, and `MotdPayload`/`PostPayload` are prost-ambiguous, so each operator-record value is `[kind] ++ prost(artifact)` — `0x00` MOTD (`SignedArtifact`), `0x01` announcement (`Post`). `apply_operator_item` (placed AFTER the circle/lobby-chat/presence/discovery attempts, so a legitimate blob of those is already consumed) splits the tag, decodes, and `verify_served_motd`/`verify_served_post`s against the empty whitelist — only an F17-signed item passes; a bad signature, a content-address mismatch (`verify_served_post` re-derives + compares, so a forged `content_address` can't poison the posts map), or a non-authorized signer is dropped. Verified items fold into an `OperatorSpace` (`motd` + `posts` keyed by the verified content-address slot); `RefreshPublicSpace` projects them via the reused transport-agnostic `build_announcements_view` (empty whitelist + `project_release_pubkey()` as the MOTD server key) into a `PublicSpaceSnapshot { view, can_compose: true, connect_time: false }`. The Slint composer/pane (relay #91/#92) render it unchanged — like P-b, the UI is transport-agnostic. **`can_compose = true` is the dev-possession gate** (in dev everyone derives the in-source project key, so everyone is "the maintainer"); the production gate (only the offline-seed holder composes) is deferred. **STOP-AND-LEAVE (deferred, not defects):** the production composer gate + the A2 whitelist documentary-roster, and the rollback-freshness version field (#136). **Scope:** GUI + one core dev accessor; no proto/wire change (the KIND tag is an app envelope over the operator record's opaque bytes; `prost`+`hex` become optional `veilid`-gated gui deps). **TUI untouched** — cutover-gate: TUI parity does not gate (GUI primary). **Delegated build (Engineer), fully re-verified** (gates re-run + the security paths — the demux's F17-only acceptance, the verified-address slot keying, no-panic on untrusted bytes — inspected in the diff per never-trust-subagent-completion) + reviewed at **high**. **Follow-up (Engineer-flagged):** a now-inert `main.rs:1531` guard filtering the retired "not yet on Veilid" string — harmless, a trivial cleanup, left untouched. **Oracle:** `cargo test -p daemonseed-gui --features "desktop veilid" veilid_net` (envelope round-trip, verified-F17-MOTD/announcement fold, unsubscribed-ignore) + `cargo test -p daemonseed-core public_space` (`dev_project_release_keypair` matches the pubkey). **DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco:** a maintainer posts a MOTD + announcement, a second client sees them land (the on-wire operator-record round-trip; VM NAT blocks attach). **Review triage (code-review, high, 4 findings — the demux was confirmed SECURE: no malicious/non-F17 blob acceptance, `verify_served_post` re-derives the content-address so a forged one can't poison the map):** (1) **DEFERRED → #134** — the MOTD (`"motd"`) and announcements (content-address slots) share the operator record's `current_state_subkey` 64-slot space, so items collide past a handful (last-writer-wins on-wire) and an announcement can even evict the MOTD (~1/64 each). This is the SAME architectural ceiling as #134 (presence); the operator channel is low-volume so it bites far later, and the fix is the same dedicated-schema boundary call — deferred, #134 broadened to cover it, code-commented on `OperatorSpace`. (2) **applied — lazy re-subscribe:** `subscribe_operator_space` was Connect-only, so a transient subscribe failure disabled announcements for the session behind a misleading "not connected"; the three handlers now (re-)subscribe on demand (self-healing) and surface an honest message distinguishing not-connected from subscribe-pending. (3) **applied — O(N²) PQ re-verify:** `public_space_snapshot_event` re-ran `build_announcements_view` (which re-verifies every ML-DSA signature) per event; since `OperatorSpace` holds only already-verified content, the view is now built DIRECTLY from it (`render_motd`/`post_render_fields`, no re-verification). (4) **applied — dispatch tests:** added `SetMotd`/`UploadAnnouncement` guard-path tests so a click-before-subscribe can never `unwrap`-panic the actor task. Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/phase4-veilid-presence-announcements.md`](docs/design/phase4-veilid-presence-announcements.md) (Part 2, A0/A1).
- 2026-07-07: **Veilid Phase 4 A-b — the operator-owned announce record: the write-gate transport primitive (STOP-AND-LEAVE on the substantive semantics).** A-a built the core (the write-gate owner-seed + the `AnnounceFreshness` guard); A-b lands the veilid-net **transport**: `VeilidNetHandle::publish_current_state(owner_seed, slot_id, bytes)` writes opaque bytes to a NAMED current-state slot on the project-announce owner record (MOTD = a fixed `"motd"` slot; announcements = content-addressed slots), owner-signed so **only a holder of the A-a project-announce owner seed can write** (the single-owner DFLT constraint IS the A1 write-gate); clients `subscribe_room` on the owner and read. It is a thin public re-export of the SAME owner-signed `Command::PublishCurrentState` current-state write already carrying presence (reviewed at P-a) — no new trust-surface *logic*, only a new caller-facing slot API. **The content layer needs no re-targeting:** the cli authoring/verify helpers (`sign_motd`/`sign_post`/`verify_served_motd`/`verify_served_post`) already produce/verify a transport-agnostic `wire::SignedArtifact`, and `core::verify_artifact` takes raw `&[u8]` — so a client packs a signed public artifact into these bytes and verifies it on read exactly as on the relay (A1: write-authorization is the owner key; content-provenance is the ML-DSA-87 key + whitelist, client-verified — two independent layers). Content is public + signed, NOT AEAD-sealed (unlike chat/presence). **STOP-AND-LEAVE — three substantive design boundaries deferred (not guessed past), per the slice's higher-risk rule:** (1) **the rollback-freshness version source (#136)** — `AnnounceFreshness` requires a strictly-monotonic operator counter, but the wire payloads carry only `signed_timestamp_ms` (wall clock, which the A-a review proved unsafe as a rollback version); wiring the guard needs a monotonic `version` field = a **proto change on the frozen contract** — a boundary call for caraka. So A-b ships the write-gate + provenance (both sound) but NOT the rollback guard (named gap, #136). (2) **the whitelist-on-record distribution (A2)** — on Veilid the operator IS the writer, so no independent party enforces the whitelist against the writer; it degrades from an enforcement boundary to a client-verified *documentary roster* the operator publishes on the record. HOW the roster rides the record (a dedicated slot? its own signed artifact?) is undecided — deferred with the composer work. (3) **the app publish/subscribe wiring** (`NetCommand::UploadAnnouncement`/`SetMotd`/`RefreshPublicSpace`, still "not yet on Veilid" in gui/tui) is **A-c #92** (the signer-gated composer) — the transport primitive this slice adds is what A-c wires onto. **Review scope note (honest):** the xhigh directive for A-a/A-b targets trust-surface *logic*; A-b's landed code is a logic-free pass-through to an already-xhigh-adjacent (P-a-reviewed) owner-signed write, so a fresh xhigh over it adds little — the substantive operator-record logic (freshness wiring, whitelist roster, composer gate) is DEFERRED and will get its xhigh when it lands (A-c + #136). **Scope:** veilid-net only (one public method + an `#[ignore]` oracle); no core/proto/wire change. **Wire/ISC impact: none yet** — announce ISC IDs mint at the v0.33.0 cutover. **Oracle:** `crates/daemonseed-veilid-net/tests/two_node_operator_record.rs` (`#[ignore]`, orinoco) — A writes the MOTD + an announcement slot on the dev-owner record, B reads both back byte-identical; the write-gate exclusivity is a Veilid single-owner property (not exercisable in-process with the shared in-source dev seed) and the provenance is core's already-tested `verify_artifact`, so this proves the operator-record TRANSPORT. **DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco** (VM NAT). Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/phase4-veilid-presence-announcements.md`](docs/design/phase4-veilid-presence-announcements.md) (Part 2, A1/A2).
- 2026-07-07: **Veilid Phase 4 A-a — announce-channel core: the write-gate owner-seed derivation + the rollback-freshness guard (project channel, A0/A1).** Announcements/MOTD were relay-shaped (a server holds a whitelist file, verifies each `UploadPost`/`UploadMotd`, and IS the write authority); Veilid deletes the server, so the write authority needs a new home. Per the design-of-record (A0, caraka 2026-07-07: **MVP ships one PROJECT-owned channel**, per-community Fork B deferred), the write-gate is a **single non-derivable operator/maintainer Veilid owner keypair** on an operator-owned DHT rendezvous record (the single-owner DHT constraint IS the gate). This slice adds the CORE primitive (veilid-net record wiring is A-b). **The decoupling the relay collapsed (A1):** write-authorization (who may place an owner-signed subkey — a Veilid owner key) and content-provenance (who signed the content — the F17 ML-DSA-87 key, verified client-side by the carried-over `verify_artifact`/`Whitelist`) are now two independent layers. `derive_project_announce_veilid_owner_seed(project_seed)` = `HKDF-SHA-384(salt=PROJECT_ANNOUNCE_OWNER_SALT, ikm=project_seed)` expand under `info=PROJECT_ANNOUNCE_VEILID_OWNER` — a **sibling** of the F17 content-signing key (both from the one maintainer-held project-release seed, but the content key uses that seed as an ML-DSA seed *directly* while this HKDF-expands it), so possessing the transport owner never yields the content-signing key or vice versa. **Owner-key custody boundary (A1/A2, named accepted cost):** in production the project seed stays OFFLINE (maintainer-held) and only the derived owner PUBKEY is baked into clients (the exact F17 model — clients compute the record address, read/watch/verify, cannot write); the in-source `PROJECT_RELEASE_SEED` is a dev placeholder, so `dev_project_announce_veilid_owner_seed()` exposes the dev write-gate for the composer + felt-tests and is RETIRED when the seed becomes a baked pubkey. A2's honest cost carries: on Veilid the operator IS the writer, so no independent party enforces the whitelist against the writer — the published whitelist degrades from an enforcement boundary to a client-verified documentary roster (deferred to A-b's wiring where it's consumed). **Rollback guard (#78 applied to the operator record, "gaps closed"):** live-only DHT + no store-and-forward means a client reading a stale subkey sees an old roster/MOTD, and an untrusted transport could serve a stale slot to roll back a revocation; an `AnnounceFreshness` guard holds `last_seen` as STATE (`accepts`/`accept`, `>=` so an idempotent re-fetch is fine while a strictly-older slot is rejected; `accept` advances to the max on a fresh record). The **version MUST be a strictly-monotonic operator-incremented counter, NEVER a wall clock** — the record's actual version field is A-b's schema. **Scope:** core-only (public_space.rs + kdf/info.rs); no veilid-net wiring, no proto/wire change (all additive `pub` API + one new HKDF salt/label). **Wire/ISC impact: none yet** — announce ISC IDs mint at the v0.33.0 cutover (frozen-contract). **Oracle:** `cargo test -p daemonseed-core` — determinism/per-seed, disjointness from the content-signing seed AND from every circle/room/presence rendezvous owner, `Debug` redaction, and the freshness guard's accept-forward/accept-equal/reject-rollback + advance-on-accept matrix; `project_announce_owner_constants_are_pinned`. **Review triage (code-review, xhigh, 4 findings — no domain-separation defect: the write-gate disjointness held):** (1+2) **applied — the freshness guard was redesigned from a free `announce_is_fresh(incoming, last_seen)` fn into the `AnnounceFreshness` type.** The reviewer flagged two PLAUSIBLE correctness risks the free fn invited: a `sent_unix_ms` version source (which my doc had sanctioned) is NON-monotonic — an operator clock step-back makes a legitimate newer record carry a lower version and be silently rejected fleet-wide (a rollback guard, unlike the presence beacon's self-healing window, cannot tolerate skew) → the doc now MANDATES a strictly-monotonic counter and forbids wall-clock; and the `(u64, u64)` same-typed shape let the not-yet-written A-b caller transpose args and INVERT the guard (accept rollbacks) → holding `last_seen` as state removes the transposable second argument entirely, and `accept()` bundles the advance-to-max the caller previously had to remember. (3+4) **declined (convention + scope) → filed #135.** The `ProjectAnnounceVeilidOwnerSeed` newtype is the 5th verbatim copy of the redacted-zeroizing 32-byte owner-seed boilerplate, and `AnnounceOwnerError` the 3rd single-variant HKDF error enum; both MATCH the established circle/room/presence convention, and consolidating all sites into a shared helper/macro is itself a trust-surface refactor (secret-hygiene across 5 sites) that needs its own review — out of scope for A-a, tracked as #135 (tier:backlog). Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/phase4-veilid-presence-announcements.md`](docs/design/phase4-veilid-presence-announcements.md) (Part 2, A0/A1/A2).
- 2026-07-07: **Veilid Phase 4 P-a (#74) — member presence over Veilid: a SEPARATE presence rendezvous record + a current-state transport write, wired into the gui/tui net actors (Lobby-first).** The presence *core* (`heartbeat.rs` seal/open, `presence.rs` `PresenceTracker`, #78 freshness) was already transport-agnostic and built on the relay path; this slice grounds it on the Veilid DHT rendezvous engine. **Boundary call P1 — presence gets its OWN record, never the chat rendezvous.** New core siblings `derive_room_presence_veilid_owner_seed` / `derive_circle_presence_veilid_owner_seed` (+ `RoomPresenceVeilidOwnerSeed` / `CirclePresenceVeilidOwnerSeed` types, + the `public_room_presence_veilid_owner` / `circle_presence_veilid_owner` HKDF labels) are a THIRD sibling of the room key / `cot_key` — domain-disjoint from BOTH the content key AND the chat rendezvous owner (`derive_room/circle_veilid_owner_seed`). Rationale (design-of-record `docs/design/phase4-veilid-presence-announcements.md` P1): the room record's per-member slots are a 2-slot append-ring (`RING_DEPTH=2`) sized for chat *history*; routing a ~15–20 s liveness beacon there evicts chat backlog within ~2 intervals (a Tragedy-of-the-Commons over shared slots). A distinct world-derivable record decouples the two stocks — a beacon can never touch the chat ring. **Transport/app split (kept faithful to the chat path):** veilid-net stays transport-only — it gains ONE new primitive, `VeilidNetHandle::publish_presence(owner_seed, member_id, sealed)` (a `Command::PublishCurrentState` → `publish_current_state`, spawned off the actor loop, per-record-lock-serialized), which writes to a per-member `current_state_subkey(member_id)` slot (last-writer-wins, one slot/member — a re-beacon overwrites in place). Subscription reuses `subscribe_room` on the presence owner seed; the misleading `presence() -> Unimplemented` stub is replaced. The seal/open (`seal_public_heartbeat`/`open_heartbeat`) + the emit/ingest/reap loop live in the gui/tui net actor, which holds the `PublicRoomKey` + the stable `SignKeypair` (veilid-net never holds keys). This is a reasonable reading of the brief's "implement `VeilidNetHandle::presence()`" — the *feature* is realized across veilid-net (transport) + app (loop), exactly as chat is. **Cadence (P2):** a jittered **[15, 20] s** emit interval (`next_heartbeat_interval()` + 5 s — reuses the core CSPRNG jitter, so beacons de-sync and stay off a fixed period, ISC-A-S2), sitting at/above the ~14.7 s cross-node DHT watch floor so beaconing never outruns propagation; `PresenceTracker::with_cadence(20 s, HEARTBEAT_MISS_COUNT)` → 60 s TTL (the ~45–60 s bias-to-forgiveness window). Values are felt-test tunables. The timer is a self-rescheduling pinned `Sleep` (a fresh jittered deadline each fire), added as a `tokio::select!` arm alongside `prune_timer`; it emits one beacon (carrying the served-share digest, #76) and reaps the roster on the same tick. **Inbound:** `handle_inbound` opens each blob with `open_heartbeat` (distinct heartbeat AAD ⇒ only a real beacon opens; chat/announcement blobs fall through), self-filters our own beacon by pubkey, drops a non-`beacon_is_fresh` (#78) replay/future-dated beacon, then `PresenceTracker::apply`s it. **GUI vs TUI:** the GUI emits `NetEvent::Roster{circle_id:None, entries}` on a render-visible change (a member appeared, or a refresh changed a displayed handle) and on a reap that removed ≥1 — so P-b (#75) is a pure Slint render; the three relay roster helpers (`beacon_is_own`/`roster_render_changed`/`roster_from_members`) became `pub(crate)` for reuse. The TUI has no roster event/render (matching its relay actor), so it maintains the tracker (apply/reap) purely so a TUI client is VISIBLE on GUI rosters and #77/TUI-roster is unblocked — its render is deferred. **Scope:** Lobby-only (circle presence is the tail #77, on the same mechanism). **Wire/ISC impact: none yet** — veilid-net is `[workspace] exclude`d + pre-cutover; the core additions are additive `pub` API + three new (relay-unused) HKDF labels; relay/default build byte-unchanged. New presence ISC IDs mint at the v0.33.0 cutover (frozen-contract posture, Phase-2/3 precedent). **Alternatives rejected:** a compile-time subkey split inside the one room record (chat band + presence band) — shrinks chat ring capacity and bakes the split into the record semantics; kept only as a documented fallback if the extra record/watch cost ever bites. **Review triage (code-review, high, 5 verified findings):** (1) **applied — off-loop emit.** The beacon publish was awaited INLINE in the gui/tui actor select arm, re-introducing the #128 D-0b stall (a multi-second DHT `set` every ~15–20 s would freeze inbound chat + command service); now the seal happens on the loop and the DHT write is `tokio::spawn`ed (matching `send_room`/`send_circle`), with the fast in-memory reap staying inline. (2) **applied — folded `member_slot_id` into `publish_presence`** (now takes `member_pubkey: &[u8]`, hex-encodes internally): removes the byte-identical `member_id_hex` duplicated across gui/tui and the risk the two clients derive DIFFERENT slot ids. (3) **DEFERRED (boundary call) — the `current_state_subkey` 64-slot ceiling.** `SUBKEY_COUNT=64` was sized for a handful of shares; lobby presence is UNBOUNDED, so members whose pubkeys FNV-collide slot-share (last-writer-wins → the loser is transiently off other clients' rosters; ~54% birthday odds at n≈10). Real at community scale, but the fix — a larger *dedicated* presence DHT schema — changes the deterministic record-key schema and reopens the "truly unbounded roster" question, exactly the design-boundary call this slice must not guess past. The design-of-record already names the remedy ("a larger dedicated schema lifts the ceiling if it ever bites"); the overclaiming "one fixed slot per member" doc is corrected to state the degradation, and the ceiling is filed as **#134** for caraka's schema-size decision. Felt-test at 2 clients is unaffected. (4) **declined — the per-beacon `members()` clone+sort** in the inbound handle-lookup: it mirrors the relay path (consistency), runs on the infrequent per-beacon path, and a keyed `PresenceTracker` accessor would be a core change touching both transports — out of scope for P-a. Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/phase4-veilid-presence-announcements.md`](docs/design/phase4-veilid-presence-announcements.md) (Part 1, P1/P2).
- 2026-06-29: **#113 (partial) — GUI Veilid downloads fetch a file's chunks with bounded concurrency.** `confirm_fetch_inner`'s inner chunk loop was one-`fetch_chunk`-at-a-time; now a `fetch_chunks_ordered(addrs, cap, fetch, on_chunk)` helper runs up to `CHUNK_FETCH_CONCURRENCY` (8) chunk fetches at once via `futures_util::stream::buffered`, which preserves input order so the file reassembles byte-for-byte while the link stays busy; `on_chunk` keeps per-chunk progress. This is the level above #109 (which pipelined the *fragments within* one chunk). Each chunk is still SHA-384-verified inside `fetch_chunk` (ISC-S28); the first error prunes the stale share and aborts (the in-flight fetches cancel when the stream drops). New dep `futures-util` (gui, optional, `veilid`-feature-gated — the download path is `#[cfg(feature="veilid")]`; default build unperturbed); same spec the #109 pipeline uses, already in the lock. **Scope / bare `#113` (NOT Closes):** only the *chunk* dimension is parallel — files are still fetched sequentially (the outer per-file loop awaits each file) and a second concurrent download still queues because `confirm_fetch` blocks the actor's select loop. Both residual dimensions need the download spawned OFF the actor loop (it holds `&mut shares` for prune-on-fail, so off-loop needs an ownership refactor) — a separate follow-on; left for caraka to decide whether chunk-parallelism suffices to close #113. No core/proto/wire change; no new ISC (transport perf). **Oracle:** `veilid_net::tests::fetch_chunks_ordered_runs_bounded_concurrent_and_reassembles_in_order` — a concurrency-counting fake fetcher proves peak in-flight `1 < peak ≤ cap` with in-order reassembly. **DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco:** a multi-chunk download completes visibly faster.
- 2026-06-29: **Veilid lobby (public-room) chat wired into the GUI net actor.** Closes the gap where the GUI Veilid actor returned `NetEvent::Error("not yet on Veilid")` for `SendRoom` / `JoinRoom` — public-room chat now rides Veilid exactly as circles do (#98/#101), the existing public-room end-state (ISC-S22 server-readable room key, ISC-S24 self-signed provenance, ISC-C56/C57) now holding over the Veilid transport rather than only the relay. Mechanism: `SendRoom` seals a `PublicRoomMessage` via `seal_room_message` under the world-derivable lobby `PublicRoomKey` and publishes it onto the lobby rendezvous (`publish_room`, the same record share-discovery already subscribes); the sender optimistically local-echoes (`NetEvent::Message{mine:true}`) before the DHT round-trip; `subscribe_lobby` now emits `RoomJoined{lobby}` on connect so the UI enables the chat box (relay-path parity); `handle_inbound` opens inbound bytes with `open_room_message` (own-suppressed by handle, #101 lesson) ahead of `apply_discovery` — the distinct per-kind AEAD AAD means only the matching open succeeds, so chat vs share-announcement vs (future) heartbeat never cross-decode. **Scope:** GUI veilid_net actor only; no core/proto/wire change (all core seal/open + `derive_room_veilid_owner_seed` pre-existed). TUI parity is a follow-on (#110-class). **No new ISC** — transport port of an existing end-state (isc-coverage unchanged at 110/180). **Stale-brief note:** tonight's queue opened on "#74 presence primitive (build core)", but #74/#75/#76 are already CLOSED (core `heartbeat.rs`/`presence.rs` built on the relay path, `f97a45d`/`2fd1e1f`) and #78's freshness window is built (`a30ae5c`, issue open only for the clock-free anti-replay design fork); the genuine unbuilt gap was lobby chat over Veilid, so the run pivoted there. **Oracle:** `veilid_net::tests::{handle_inbound_surfaces_a_verified_lobby_chat_message, handle_inbound_suppresses_our_own_looped_back_lobby_message, send_room_without_a_lobby_reports_a_clean_error}` + fmt/clippy(`-D warnings`, desktop & desktop+veilid). **DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco:** two clients exchange Lobby messages over real public Veilid (the thing that did not work tonight).
- 2026-06-29: **#117 refinement (felt-test) — fire the restore banner on Veilid relaunch + relabel the in-flight tag.** Felt-test (caraka): (1) the per-share Manage-shares tag read "republishing…" oddly on a FIRST publish; (2) on relaunch he saw no reassurance because the Manage dialog is closed during the auto-republish — "I wouldn't go looking in Manage shares for that." **Decisions:** (a) thread `restored: bool` into the Veilid `publish_share`; the connect-time republish passes `true`, so the live emit drives the EXISTING always-visible "Restored N shares from last session" connect-notice banner (auto-dismiss) — a sharer sees their shares come back on relaunch without opening any dialog (the relay path already did this; the Veilid path had hardcoded `restored: false`). (b) relabel the in-flight tag "publishing… · establishing your share" (drop the "re-") so a first publish reads correctly. **Open (caraka to confirm):** whether the restore banner suffices, or the own share should ALSO echo onto the Public Shares pane during the republish window (a browser-tree change — the literal "appears in the public shares pane like a chat echo" ask) — deferred pending his call. **Scope:** GUI only; no wire change. **Oracle:** gates green (clippy base + desktop, `my_shares_add_replace_remove`); banner + label are felt-test-gated → orinoco.
- 2026-06-29: **#107 follow-up — persist the circle read high-water across relaunch.** The RAM-only high-water (below) fixed reconnect *within* a session but reset to 0 on relaunch, so the first backlog load after a RESTART re-tripped the unread dot — caraka hit this on a felt-test restart (the original complaint). **Decision:** persist a per-circle high-water in the at-rest blob, mirroring the `announce_seen` read-state pattern. Core: `Seeds.circle_seen: BTreeMap<entropy, i64>` + `set_circle_seen` (monotonic) / `circle_seen`, encoded as an additive `circle-seen <hex(entropy)> <ms>` directive (entropy is hex-encoded — it is a phrase with spaces; an absent line parses to an empty map → backward-compatible, no blob-version bump). GUI: `Profile::{circle_seen, persist_circle_seen}` (reseal-if-advanced) + `GuiState::persist_all_circle_seen` (called from the desktop close handler — the common restart path) + `set_profile` seeds each restored circle's `high_water_ms` from the blob. **Persist timing:** on graceful close (one batched write — no per-message / per-switch I/O); a crash-without-close falls back to the RAM behavior (re-trips once). **Scope:** core blob + GUI; no wire/proto change (client-local read-state, ISC-A-C3); no new ISC pre-cutover. **Oracle:** core `circle_seen_round_trips_and_is_monotonic` + `absent_circle_seen_directive_parses_empty`; GUI `circle_high_water_persists_across_relaunch_and_suppresses_backlog_retrip` (read to ts=100 → close → reload → backlog at 100 stays quiet, ts=101 trips). **DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco:** read a circle, restart the client, confirm the already-seen backlog does NOT re-trip the dot.
- 2026-06-29: **#117 per-share "republishing…" tag — set expectations during the slow Veilid (re)publish window.** On a reconnect the GUI Veilid actor replays `republish_roots`, but `publish_share` emitted `PublishStarted` only AFTER the slow serve+advert window (route allocation + DHT write), so the share was absent from "Your live shares" while it re-established — a sharer read that as "my share died" and re-published it (the #112 duplicate, now also prevented by the deterministic share_id). **Decision (caraka — per-share tag, not a global status):** thread an in-flight flag through the existing own-share path rather than a parallel channel — `NetEvent::PublishStarted` gains `republishing: bool`; the Veilid `publish_share` emits it `true` BEFORE the serve+advert (the row appears immediately as "republishing… · re-establishing your share"), and `false` after the advert lands (the row flips to "N file(s) · serving"); on a publish failure a `PublishStopped` drops the in-flight row. `MyShare`/`MyShareRow` carry the flag; the status line is computed (never an `if`, item-tree safety). The `PublishStarted` handler runs M16 persistence + the status banner only on the live (`republishing: false`) emit, so the in-flight emit purely surfaces the tag. **Scope:** GUI only — no core `ShareListing` change (the tag rides own-shares, not discovered listings), no wire/proto change; the relay emit sets `republishing: false` (the relay publishes synchronously + retires at cutover). No new ISC pre-cutover. **Oracle:** `state::tests::my_shares_add_replace_remove` (id-a in-flight → `republishing` true; the live re-push flips it false). **DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco:** reconnect with a published share → the row shows "republishing…" then flips to "serving" once live (and a sharer no longer thinks it died).
- 2026-06-29: **#115 leave-circle + evil-maid-gated phrase reveal.** Two missing circle-management affordances: no way to leave a circle, no way to get its join phrase back out. **Decisions (caraka-ratified):** (1) **Leaving is easy** — a single confirm overlay reminding "you'll need the phrase to rejoin; there's no recovery", then forget the circle (drop it from the rail AND the profile blob via `Seeds::remove_circle`, so it does not silently re-join next launch). No passphrase required to leave. (2) **Revealing the phrase is gated on the unlock passphrase** — an evil-maid guard: on an *unlocked but unattended* client the circle's secret must not be one click away. `Profile::verify_passphrase` re-opens the on-disk at-rest blob with the entered passphrase (same crypto as Unlock, fails closed via `seeds::open`); only on success is the phrase revealed for copy. The passphrase input is masked (`input-type: password`) — the unlock passphrase is more sensitive than the phrase it gates. A revealed phrase clears on sheet-close and on any active-circle change, so it never lingers. **Honest boundary:** this defeats the opportunistic "open sheet → copy" grab, NOT memory forensics — the phrase lives decrypted in RAM while unlocked; the UI gate raises the bar for the realistic evil-maid, it is not a guarantee (caraka's threat model: evil-maid, explicitly NOT over-the-shoulder). **Scope:** GUI only (`state.rs` forget/phrase/verify + `profile.rs` forget_circle/verify_passphrase reusing the core unlock crypto; `app.slint` details-sheet buttons + passphrase-prompt + leave-confirm overlays mirroring the #66 rename overlay; `main.rs` callbacks). No wire/proto change; no new ISC pre-cutover. **Known limitation (follow-up):** leaving does not yet send a net command to drop the circle's live subscription — the actor keeps the orphaned subscription until reconnect (inbound for the gone `circle_id` routes to `None` and is dropped — no crash; it won't re-join since it's out of the rejoin set). **Copy caveat:** clipboard write is best-effort (arboard X11 ownership); the on-screen reveal always works, so copy degrades to "read it off the screen". **Oracle:** `state::tests::forget_circle_drops_it_from_disk_and_reveal_is_passphrase_gated` (full profile round-trip: persist a circle → reveal gated by correct/wrong passphrase → leave → reload from disk shows it did NOT rejoin); `Seeds::remove_circle` already core-tested.
- 2026-06-29: **#107 backlog unread — a per-circle read high-water mark gates the dot.** On a reconnect/login the ring sweep re-delivers a circle's recent backlog; into a NON-active circle, `push_message` inserted each (off-screen — "not in the visible chat") and tripped the #64 unread dot. The exact-match dedup can't suppress it when the RAM transcript is fresh (relaunch) or already-pruned, so circles the user had already caught up on lit up again ("ghosts from yesterday tripped the unread in all the rooms"). **Decision:** add a per-circle `high_water_ms` (the newest `sent_unix_ms` the user has seen) and trip unread only for a message STRICTLY newer than it — backlog at/below the mark folds into the transcript silently. The mark advances when the room is active (live messages are seen) and on focus (`switch_to` catches up to the transcript's latest). RAM-only like the transcript (the reported bug is intra-session reconnect; a first-ever load this session may still trip once — genuinely new — but a reconnect after catch-up will not). **Scope:** GUI `state.rs` only — no wire/proto/UI change (the dot already renders; only WHEN it trips changed); refines the existing #64 unread behavior, no new ISC. **Alternatives rejected:** persisting the high-water into the profile blob (would also fix relaunch-first-load, but a bigger at-rest change for the milder case — deferred); widening the dedup window (can't tell backlog from a genuine resend, and still trips on a pruned transcript). **Oracle:** `state::tests::{backlog_at_or_below_high_water_does_not_retrip_unread, message_newer_than_high_water_still_trips_unread, focusing_a_circle_advances_high_water_to_latest}`; the existing #64 + #105 tests unchanged.
- 2026-06-29: **#116 self-ghost — self-filter our own announcements in `apply_discovery`, before the withdraw branch.** Root cause (felt-confirmed on orinoco): a sharer's own lobby announcement loops back through the same lobby record it sweeps, and `apply_discovery` had no self-check — so it folded the own share into `shares.discovered`, where it shows in Public shares (`listings()` = `own` ++ catalog) yet is absent from the unpublish dialog (`own`-only) = an un-deletable self-ghost. **Decision:** drop any announcement whose provenance-verified `sender_pubkey` equals our held `shares.signing.public_key()`, placed **before** the withdraw branch — so both an own-republish AND an own-withdraw short-circuit, and the discovered catalog only ever holds **foreign** shares (ours live in `shares.own`). Placement-before-withdraw is deliberate (FirstPrinciples): it blocks fold-in at the source so no self-ghost can form, and on restart a stale own-announcement from an old node-keyed slot is dropped on sweep rather than re-folded — so #118 pre-fix residue can't reform as a self-ghost. **Scope:** both Veilid actors (`crates/daemonseed-{gui,tui}/src/veilid_net.rs`); receive-side only, no wire/proto change; no ISC change pre-cutover. **Alternatives rejected:** display-time filtering only (that *is* the bug — the ghost still occupies the catalog and trips the count/snapshot); a non-early insert-time skip (leaves an own withdraw needlessly mutating the catalog). Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/unified-room-model.md`](docs/design/unified-room-model.md) (the discovered catalog is foreign-only by construction).
- 2026-06-28: **Veilid Phase 3 — prune-on-fetch-fail (#112 companion).** The deterministic `share_id` (above) *prevents* the duplicate going forward, but a stale copy already in a recipient's catalog (from before the fix, or a mid-session route rotation, or an un-TTL'd withdraw) still needs clearing. **Decision:** when a fetch (`fetch_share` preview or `confirm_fetch` download) fails at the network stage — route won't import, manifest/chunk fetch errors, or `NotServed` — the recipient prunes that `share_id` from its `ShareCatalog` + discovered-route map and emits a fresh `SharesSnapshot` (`prune_unreachable_share`). This realizes ISC-S30's named "prune-on-fetch-fail" liveness mechanism, previously a relay-path concept not wired on the Veilid path. **Self-correcting:** a still-live share re-announces (on-change + the heartbeat digest, ISC-C86) and reappears, so pruning a transiently-unreachable live share is recovered automatically — and the per-fragment retry (`call_fragment`, 3×) already ran before the failure, so a prune means the route is genuinely unreachable, not a single blip. **Precondition failures don't prune** (not connected / lobby not subscribed / not discovered are about local state, not the share's liveness). The fetch helpers took `&ShareState`; they now take `&mut` (route + room-key copied out before the network calls so the prune borrow is free). **ISC impact: none new** (realizes existing ISC-S30). **Wire impact: none.** **Oracle:** `prune_unreachable_share_drops_a_folded_item_and_snapshots` (fold → prune → catalog+discovered empty + empty snapshot; no-op prune emits nothing). **DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco:** click/download a stale copy → it disappears from the list. Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/veilid-migration.md`](docs/design/veilid-migration.md) § *Slice 2b status* (duplicate-share fix).
- 2026-06-28: **Veilid Phase 3 — deterministic `share_id` (fixes the duplicate-share / dead-route bug #112).** Live felt-test surfaced a published share appearing **twice** on the sharer's reconnect — the second copy un-fetchable (`could not get remote private route`). Root cause: `publish_share` minted a fresh random `share_id` on every call, so #108's reconnect-republish created a NEW share identity each session; with the **ephemeral per-launch node identity**, the prior session's id lingered in recipients' catalogs pointing at the now-dead node's route. The `ShareCatalog` already dedups by `share_id`, so the duplicate existed *only* because the id changed. **Decision (caraka — Demonsaw lineage):** make the `share_id` **deterministic** — `daemonseed_core::share_announce::derive_share_id(sender_pubkey, root)` = SHA-384 over a domain-separated (`daemonseed/share-id/v1`) length-prefixed `(pubkey ‖ root)`, truncated to 128 bits, lowercase-hex (same shape as `mint_share_id`). A republish re-asserts the SAME id → the fetcher folds it onto the existing catalog entry in place (live route, no duplicate). **Chosen over the originally-requested persist+reuse** (caraka ratified the switch): persist+reuse needed a blob-format change to the custom `publish` line + ~10 connect-plumbing sites + a backfill migration, GUI-only; deterministic is one core fn + one GUI call site, **also covers the TUI**, and touches no at-rest format. **No new linkability:** the announcement already carries `sender_pubkey` as its provenance + verification anchor, so an observer can already tie publisher↔share — Demonsaw used a derived id for the same reason ("the pubkey is already the public metadata AND the verification, so use it for more"). **Trade-off (accepted):** the same folder under one identity always maps to one share id (can't publish a folder as two distinct shares; the id doesn't rotate per session) — fine/desired for public shares. `mint_share_id` is retained (unchanged) for any caller wanting an opaque random id. **Scope:** GUI publish path now; the TUI publish path can adopt `derive_share_id` when its publish/persist story lands (#110). **ISC impact: none yet** (pre-cutover; ISC-S21 "opaque id" still holds — derived ids are still opaque to a non-pubkey-holder). **Wire impact: none** — same 32-hex `share_id` on the wire; relay/default build byte-unchanged. **Oracle:** `daemonseed_core::share_announce::tests::derive_share_id_is_deterministic_and_shaped` (stable across calls, 32 lowercase-hex, varies by root + by identity); gui clippy `--features "desktop veilid" --all-targets -D warnings` clean; `cargo check --workspace` clean. **DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco:** publish → reconnect → the recipient shows ONE entry (updated route), fetch succeeds. Companion: prune-on-fetch-fail (next). Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/veilid-migration.md`](docs/design/veilid-migration.md) § *Slice 2b status* (duplicate-share fix).
- 2026-06-28: **Veilid Phase 3 — land the Slice-2b red-team residuals (serve/advert hardening).** The 2026-06-27 red-team flagged residuals to land *with* the serve/fetch wiring; now that the GUI+TUI wiring exists, four are fixed in `daemonseed-veilid-net` (`actor.rs` + `share.rs`): **(a) route leak** — `publish_one_advert` allocated a fresh private route on every refresh but never released the previous one; a per-share `advert_routes: Arc<Mutex<HashMap<String,RouteId>>>` now holds the current `RouteId` and the prior is `release_private_route`d on re-publish and on `StopServe`. **(b) refresh head-of-line blocking** — `Command::RouteMaintenance` ran the whole route-reallocation wave inline on the actor loop, stalling inbound serve `app_call`s and fetches; it is now **spawned**, coalesced by an `AtomicBool` in-flight guard + a pure `refresh_due` time-gate, with the coalesce window stamped from **completion** (not start). To let the off-loop refresh publish safely, the append-ring cursor `ring_seq` became `Arc<Mutex<HashMap<RecordKey,u32>>>` (locked only for the synchronous bump, never across an await) so an off-loop advert write and an on-loop room-message write to the lobby record don't collide on a slot. **(c) inbound app_call silent drop** — the update callback `try_send`'d `InboundAppCall` and dropped it on a full channel (→ fetcher timeout); it now falls back to a spawned awaited `send`. **(e) unbounded seal cache** — `ServedShare.cache` grew to a share's whole chunk count; it is now LRU-bounded (`SEAL_CACHE_CAPACITY=16`). **Eviction safety:** the cache exists for fragment seal-consistency (all fragments of one response from one AES-GCM nonce); a mid-fetch eviction (only under concurrency beyond capacity) is **fail-closed** — the fetcher reassembles mixed-seal fragments, the AEAD open fails, the fetch errors; no corrupt bytes are accepted. Capacity 16 covers many concurrent fetchers (each holds one target's fragments in flight). **Deferred (with reasoning):** **(d)** a fetch-side open+verify+import helper — the security property already holds, routes are anti-swap-`verify_route_advert`'d at discovery (`apply_discovery`) before they ever enter `shares.discovered`, so an import-time re-verify is redundant; **(f)** route-advert freshness (`sent_unix_ms`) — optional, wire-affecting, pairs with the Phase-4 roster; and the core `SHARE_ANNOUNCE_AAD`==`SHARE_ANNOUNCE_PROVENANCE_DOMAIN` defense-in-depth split — a core/compat-affecting change deferred to the cutover. **ISC impact: none yet** (pre-cutover). **Wire impact: none** — veilid-net-internal, `[workspace] exclude`d; relay/default build byte-unchanged. **`tests/two_node_share.rs` wrapper-rewrite** (carry the signed `DiscoveryEnvelope` end-to-end) deferred to the orinoco round — it is `#[ignore]` (live-attach only), and the discovery→verify→import→fetch path is already live-proven by the 2026-06-28 GUI felt-test. **Oracle:** veilid-net fmt / clippy `--all-targets -D warnings` / 17 lib tests incl. new `actor::tests::refresh_due_gates_on_adverts_and_interval` (coalesce gate) and `share::tests::seal_cache_is_lru_bounded` (LRU bound + touch-survives). Concurrency/route-lifecycle behaviors are not unit-testable without a live `VeilidAPI` → **DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco** (the thorough re-verify round; a RedTeam re-attack on the fixes can run there). Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/veilid-migration.md`](docs/design/veilid-migration.md) § *Slice 2b status* (red-team residuals).
- 2026-06-28: **Veilid Phase 3 — auto-republish persisted shares on connect (#108), GUI; TUI scoped out.** The GUI Veilid `Connect` handler destructured `display_handle, rejoin_circles, stable_signing_key, ..` — silently dropping `republish_roots`, the same field the relay path replays on connect. So a sharer's previously-published shares were not re-served or re-announced after a reconnect/restart (the share went dark, no error). **Fix (GUI):** after the lobby subscribe + the #102 `rejoin_circles` loop, iterate `republish_roots` and re-publish each (index → `serve_share` → `publish_share` onto the lobby) — the exact `rejoin_circles` pattern, one axis over. The node identity is ephemeral per launch, so each re-serves under a fresh private route and discovery re-announces the new advert (correct, not a regression). Names come from `crate::net::republish_name` (promoted `fn`→`pub(crate)`) so the relay and Veilid republish loops name shares identically — one source, no drift. **TUI scoped OUT of #108 (finding, caraka to ratify):** the issue says "GUI + TUI", but the TUI's `NetCommand::Connect` carries no `republish_roots`/`rejoin_circles` and the relay TUI has *no* connect-republish at all — so there is no relay-parity gap to mirror. A TUI republish would be a NEW feature gated on the TUI Veilid publish/persist story (today `DefineShare`→`ShareDefineFailed("not yet")`), not parity. Implementing it under #108 would over-reach the issue's "relay parity" framing. **Also (gate cleanup, fits here):** a pre-existing `clippy::overly_complex_bool_expr` in `state.rs` (`assert!(push_message(..) || true)`) tripped under clippy 1.96 and blocked the `--all-targets` gate; the `push_message` return is the unread-dot signal (irrelevant at that call site — the count assertion is the real check), so the `|| true` hack became `let _ =`. **ISC impact: none yet** (pre-cutover). **Wire impact: none** — GUI-internal; relay/default build byte-unchanged. **Oracle:** gui `cargo check` (no veilid) clean; clippy `--features "desktop veilid" --all-targets -D warnings` clean; `veilid_net::` 6 + the coalesce test pass; fmt clean. No offline unit test for the connect-replay itself (it needs a live transport) — same as #102; **DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco:** publish a share, reconnect, confirm it re-appears in a second client's catalog and fetches. Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/veilid-migration.md`](docs/design/veilid-migration.md) § *Slice 2b status* (Auto-republish on connect).
- 2026-06-28: **Veilid Phase 3 — pipeline the public-share fetch fragments (#109).** A completed download was correct but SLOW: `share::fetch_sealed` pulled a chunk's ~34 sealed fragments one `app_call` at a time (`for i in 1..total { call().await }`), so wall-clock was the sum of ~34 serial private-route round-trips plus every fragment's retry/timeout wait. **Decision:** keep fragment 0 sequential (it carries `total`), then pull fragments `1..total` through a bounded-concurrency pipeline — `futures_util::StreamExt::buffered(FRAGMENT_FETCH_CONCURRENCY=8)`, which polls up to 8 round-trips concurrently **within the one fetch task and yields them in request order**, so reassembly stays a simple in-order concat. **Why `buffered`, not `tokio::spawn`/`JoinSet`:** spawning needs `Send + 'static` futures, which would force those bounds onto the generic `call` closure and break the in-process test transports (their closures borrow a `Mutex<HashMap>` — not `'static`); `buffered` cooperatively polls without spawning, so no `Send`/`'static` is required and the I/O-bound `app_call`s still overlap. **Dependency:** `futures-util` added as a direct dep of `daemonseed-veilid-net` — already in this (workspace-excluded) crate's `Cargo.lock` via veilid-core, so a direct dep adds **zero new supply-chain surface**, and it is confined to the veilid-only crate. **Security — per-fragment size cap added:** the serial loop bounded reassembly memory by aborting mid-stream once the running cumulative total exceeded `MAX_REASSEMBLED_LEN`; a pipelined fetch cannot abort mid-stream, so each fragment is now capped at `FRAGMENT_SIZE` (`check_fragment_size`). With `total ≤ MAX_FRAGMENTS` already enforced, `MAX_FRAGMENTS × FRAGMENT_SIZE = MAX_REASSEMBLED_LEN` — a **strictly stronger** bound than before, and legitimate fragments are always ≤ `FRAGMENT_SIZE` (the serve side slices to that), so the cap rejects only malformed/hostile replies. Fragment 0 size and the final cumulative `MAX_REASSEMBLED_LEN` backstop are both retained. **Scope:** fragment-level only — chunk-level pipelining at the GUI/TUI download driver (chunks are still fetched one `fetch_chunk` await per chunk) is a separate, larger axis (touches the disk-write ordering + progress reporting in both consumers) and is left as a follow-up; #109 named the 34 sequential *fragments*, the per-chunk bottleneck. **ISC impact: none yet** (transport ISCs minted at cutover, Phase-2/3 precedent). **Wire impact: none** — `fetch_manifest`/`fetch_chunk` signatures unchanged; veilid-net is `[workspace] exclude`d + pre-cutover, relay/default build byte-unchanged. **Oracle:** veilid-net fmt / clippy (`-D warnings`, `--all-targets`) / 15 lib tests, incl. the new `fragments_pipeline_concurrently_and_reassemble_in_order` (an instrumented transport records peak in-flight `app_call`s during a real ~21-fragment chunk fetch and asserts `1 < peak ≤ 8` with byte-for-byte recovery — a serial fetch could never exceed 1) and `oversized_fragment_is_rejected`. **Live throughput re-verify of a faster completed download → orinoco (DEFERRED-VERIFY).** Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/veilid-migration.md`](docs/design/veilid-migration.md) § *Slice 2b status*.
- 2026-06-28: **First live GUI 2-node share felt-test (orinoco) — what worked + three findings.** Two `daemonseed-gui --features "desktop veilid"` clients on the real public Veilid network. **Confirmed live:** attach to `public_internet_ready` (AttachedFull, 100+ peers), lobby + circle rendezvous subscribe, circle send/receive both directions, and share discovery (a ~12 KB `DiscoveryEnvelope` reached the fetcher). **Findings:** (1) **Connection-status display bug — FIXED.** The GUI read "offline · not yet on Veilid" while fully attached; root cause: `main.rs` conflated a surface-level `NetEvent::Error` with `ConnectFailed`, flipping `connected=false`, so a stubbed Phase-4 surface poll ("not yet on Veilid") read as a connection drop. Connection state now owned solely by `Connected`/`ConnectFailed`/`Disconnected`; generic `Error` shows a transient notice, the known stub is silent. (2) **Circle message duplication — FIXED.** The login backlog **sweep** and the live **watch** both delivered the same circle message, so it appeared twice; `push_message` now skips an exact `(sender, body, sent_unix_ms)` already present (alongside the #105 ordered-insert). Separate from the stale-replay issue under discussion (yesterday's messages reappearing via the sweep is a freshness problem, not a dedup one). (3) **Chunk fetch failed — DIAGNOSED + FIXED.** The trace (with the new full-error logging) showed `chunk fetch failed: send failed: Timeout` — a transient `app_call` round-trip timeout.
