The manifest fetched fine (so discovery → route → serve → reassembly all work end-to-end); only the 1 MiB chunk (~34 sequential `app_call` fragments over WIP private routing) hit a timeout, and with no retry one timed-out fragment killed the whole chunk. **Fix:** `fetch_sealed` now retries each fragment `app_call` (`call_fragment`, 3× / 250 ms backoff) on a transient error before failing; a not_found/withdraw is a successful reply (decoded), so retry never masks `NotServed`. Live re-verify of a completed download → orinoco. The **unpublish→`NotServed`→"withdrew" headline test never ran** — it needs a *successful* download then an *online* unpublish; this run failed the download and (separately) the local unpublish UI removes the share instantly even when nothing propagates. Wire/ISC impact: none (GUI-internal). Gates: gui clippy `--features "desktop veilid" --all-targets -D warnings` + fmt clean. **NB build hazard:** the VM (glibc 2.43) and orinoco share the virtiofs `target/`; a VM `cargo build`/`clippy --all-targets` links a glibc-2.43 binary orinoco can't run — build GUI binaries for orinoco ON orinoco (verify here with check/clippy, then remove any VM-linked `target/debug/daemonseed-gui`).
- 2026-06-28: **Veilid Phase 3 Slice 2b — TUI mirror (completes the GUI/TUI share wiring).** The TUI Veilid net actor (`crates/daemonseed-tui/src/veilid_net.rs`) now drives publish / discover / fetch over Veilid, mirroring the GUI half onto the TUI's divergent `NetCommand`/`NetEvent`. Same invariants confirmed in code (ephemeral node id `Mnemonic::generate`, stable-content `Arc<SignKeypair>` captured from `Connect`, least-authority `IdentityRouteAdvertSigner`, `stop_serve` on unpublish, anti-swap `open_announcement`→`verify_route_advert`-before-import, `NotServed`→authoritative "withdrew" notice). **TUI-contract divergences (faithful to the relay TUI, not the GUI template):** `SharesSnapshot{local,remote,indexer_status}` — own shares ride `PublishStarted`/`PublishStopped`, NOT merged into the snapshot (relay parity); per-surface error events (no single `Error`); fetch persists through the relay's `FetchedStore`/`downloads.idx` path (browse-pane parity) while sourcing chunks via veilid `fetch_chunk` (already SHA-384-verified); `ListFetched` now reads the store (was a stub). **Two honest items (tracked → felt-test):** (a) `DefineShare`→`ShareDefineFailed("not yet")` — Define is a relay-path redb "my shares" concept the Veilid publish path does not need (it indexes the root fresh each publish); **UX question for orinoco: does the TUI flow gate `PublishShare` behind a successful `DefineShare`? if so this arm needs revisiting;** (b) no `is_unsafe_publish_root` guard — the TUI relay path has none either (parity, not a regression; the GUI guards it). **Wire/ISC impact: none yet** (pre-cutover; relay/default build byte-unchanged — only 4 relay helpers `fn`→`pub(crate)` for reuse). **Oracle (re-verified independently, not on the agent's report):** `cargo check -p daemonseed-tui` (no veilid) clean + 195 default lib tests pass; `clippy --features veilid --all-targets -D warnings` clean; 10 `veilid_net::tests` (anti-swap rogue-route drop + withdraw mapping + command translation); fmt clean. Live attach DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco. **Slice 2b is now GUI+TUI complete; the only remnant is `tests/two_node_share.rs` (the live two-node wrapper).** Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/veilid-migration.md`](docs/design/veilid-migration.md) § *Slice 2b status*.
- 2026-06-28: **Veilid Phase 3 Slice 2b — GUI share wiring + unpublish teeth + authoritative withdraw signal.** The GUI Veilid net actor (`crates/daemonseed-gui/src/veilid_net.rs`) now drives public-share publish / discover / fetch over the lobby rendezvous + signed route adverts, replacing the `"not yet on Veilid"` stubs. **Identity model (caraka-approved):** the node/transport identity stays EPHEMERAL (`Mnemonic::generate()` per launch — anonymity; circle membership is phrase-based), while the **content identity is the stable profile `SignKeypair`** — `NetCommand::Connect` already carried `stable_signing_key` and the Veilid actor was discarding it; it is now captured into share state and used ONLY to seal the `ShareAnnouncement` and to build the route-advert capability. **Least-authority preserved:** veilid-net never receives the key — only `Arc<dyn RouteAdvertSigner>` via the new `daemonseed-cli` `route_signer::IdentityRouteAdvertSigner`. **Necessary deviation:** `SignKeypair` is `!Clone` (`ZeroizeOnDrop`), so the key is held as `Arc<SignKeypair>` (one secret, two roles: seal + capability) via `IdentityRouteAdvertSigner::from_arc`. **Unpublish made complete (caraka — the unpublish gap):** a withdraw announcement only removes a share from listeners' *discovery catalogs*; it did not stop the owner SERVING the bytes. Added `VeilidNetHandle::stop_serve(share_id)` — the teeth — which de-registers the share from the serve registry (inbound fetch `app_call`s for it are no longer answered) AND drops its advert (a `RouteChanged` won't re-publish a dead advert); GUI unpublish now calls it whenever connected, independent of the (signing-gated) withdraw. The route blob still routes to the node until released, but the share is unserved — a holder of a stale route gets a not-found, never bytes; this is per-share granularity (one route may carry several shares). **Authoritative withdraw-vs-offline signal (caraka — the Demonsaw ambiguity):** a fetch against an unpublished share now returns the new `VeilidNetError::NotServed` (the owner ANSWERED "no longer served") instead of the prior MISLABELED `Send("…not served (offline?)")` — distinct from a transport error (offline / slow / dead route, where `app_call` itself errors). The synergy with `stop_serve` is load-bearing: keeping the route alive while de-registering the share is *what lets* the node answer the authoritative not-found; tearing the route down would yield the ambiguous timeout instead. The GUI fetch path maps `NotServed` → a plain "the sharer withdrew this share" notice (resolves *withdrawn*; slow-vs-failed remains an inherent timeout judgment). **ISC impact: none yet** (transport ISCs minted at cutover, Phase-2 precedent). **Wire impact: none yet** — veilid-net is `[workspace] exclude`d + pre-cutover; the relay/server default build is byte-unchanged (the only relay-crate change is 3 helpers `fn`→`pub(crate)` for reuse, no logic). **Oracle:** veilid-net fmt / clippy (`-D warnings`, `--all-targets`) / 11 lib tests (incl. `fetch_unknown_share_is_not_found` now asserting `NotServed`); gui default build clean (relay path unperturbed), clippy `--features "desktop veilid" --all-targets -D warnings` clean, 6 `veilid_net::tests` (incl. `apply_discovery_drops_an_item_whose_route_was_swapped` exercising the anti-swap path with no network, and `fetch_error_message_flags_withdrawn_distinctly`); cli `route_signer` 2 tests; workspace fmt clean. The GUI was implemented by a delegated Engineer and **re-verified independently** (every gate re-run + the security invariants — ephemeral node id, anti-swap verify-before-retain, least-authority — inspected in the diff, per the never-trust-subagent-completion rule). **Live attach is DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco** (VM NAT). **Remaining for Slice 2b:** the TUI mirror (divergent `NetCommand`/`NetEvent`) and updating `tests/two_node_share.rs`. Known limits (tracked, acceptable for the slice): the GUI serve content is the in-RAM `ShareContent::index_dir` (vs the relay's disk-backed serve); `RefreshShares` is passive (discovery is push via the lobby subscription). Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/veilid-migration.md`](docs/design/veilid-migration.md) § *Slice 2b status*.
- 2026-06-27: **Veilid Phase 3 Slice 2b — signed route advert for discovery (refines D-3.5), foundation pass.** Discovery (Slice 1) advertises a share and content transfer (Slice 2a) moves its bytes, but nothing yet carries the sharer's private-route blob from announcer to fetcher; the lobby rendezvous record is world-WRITABLE (open rendezvous), so an unbound route blob lets a MITM redirect a fetch. **Refinement 1 — transport-layer signed wrapper, NOT a core field** (refines the earlier "additive `ShareAnnouncement.route_blob`"): core `ShareAnnouncement` / its proto / `provenance_input` are UNTOUCHED. Route delivery is transport routing, kept in the transport layer so a future Veilid change never touches the content/relay layers. A discovery item becomes a veilid-net `DiscoveryEnvelope { sealed_announcement, route_blob, route_sig }` (`crates/daemonseed-veilid-net/src/discovery.rs`, length-prefixed, fail-closed decode); `route_sig` = the announcer's ML-DSA-87 signature over a domain-separated `share_id ‖ route_blob` (`ROUTE_ADVERT_DOMAIN = daemonseed/veilid/share-route/v1`, length-prefixed — distinct from every other input the identity key signs, so no cross-protocol replay). **Anti-swap:** the fetcher opens the announcement (core `open_announcement`, provenance-verified → announcer pubkey + share_id) then `verify_route_advert`s the route against that pubkey BEFORE importing it — a MITM cannot re-pair the announcement with a rogue blob (no announcer key) nor lift it onto another share (the signed `share_id` binds them). A wrong route only ever yields a dead, SHA-384-failing fetch (ISC-S28 still the content backstop). **Refinement 2 — least-authority signing capability** (refines caraka's ratified "thread the `SignKeypair` through serve/publish"; caraka-approved this session via the SystemsThinking fork): veilid-net never holds the identity key. The sharer's app passes an `Arc<dyn RouteAdvertSigner>` scoped to route adverts, so the long-term ML-DSA key stays out of the most network-exposed crate (the one parsing untrusted `app_call`/route bytes) and cannot be coerced into a general signing oracle; the signed bytes are defined once (`route_provenance_input`), so sign and verify cannot drift. **Surface:** `VeilidNetHandle::publish_share(owner_seed, share_id, sealed_announcement, signer)` — alloc private route → sign via capability → wrap → publish on the lobby rendezvous; the actor remembers each advert (`AdvertState`) and re-publishes it with a fresh route + signature on `RouteChanged`, coalesced (`ADVERT_REFRESH_MIN_INTERVAL`) to break the churn→republish reinforcing loop. The update callback now routes both `AppCall` (serve) and `RouteChange` (refresh) into the actor. **RedTeam anti-dox serve adversarial pass (structured review, this slice):** swap → blocked by the sig; cross-room/cross-share replay → blocked by domain + signed `share_id`; serve-side dox → reply rides the call path, no separate blob, node/IP hidden by the sharer's own private route (anonymity-set strength = D4 public net). **Residual (tracked, not blocking):** (a) stale-route *replay* — an old signed advert re-posted after rotation yields a dead fetch, not a break (`sent_unix_ms` freshness ordering mitigates); (b) `ServedShare` seal-cache is unbounded (DoS ceiling = the share's own chunk count) → **LRU-bound it** (follow-up); (c) the lobby is world-writable → announcement spam, a known open-rendezvous property the Phase-4 roster mitigates. **ISC impact: none yet** (transport ISCs minted at cutover, Phase-2 precedent). **Wire impact: none yet** — veilid-net-internal + pre-cutover; no proto/core change (workspace gate unperturbed; veilid-net is `[workspace] exclude`d). **Oracle:** 6 in-process `discovery::tests` pass HERE (envelope round-trip + fail-closed parsing; sign↔verify; anti-swap rogue-blob / different-announcer-key / different-share-id / wrong-length rejections); veilid-net fmt / clippy (`-D warnings`, `--all-targets`) / 10 unit tests / rustdoc green. **Live publish + `RouteChanged` refresh over real Veilid is DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco** (VM NAT blocks attach). **Remaining for Slice 2b:** GUI/TUI net-actor share-command wiring onto `publish_share` + `verify_route_advert` (still `Error{"not yet on Veilid"}`), and updating `tests/two_node_share.rs` to carry the wrapper end-to-end. Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/veilid-migration.md`](docs/design/veilid-migration.md) § *Phase 3/4 — DECIDED* (refined D-3.5) + *Slice 2b status*.

- 2026-06-27: **Veilid Phase 3 Slice 2a — public-share CONTENT transfer over `app_call` (realizes D-3.4).** Discovery (Slice 1, the lobby rendezvous) advertises a share; this slice moves its bytes, with no relay. **Fragment-granular `app_call` protocol** (`crates/daemonseed-veilid-net/src/share.rs`): a 1 MiB content-addressed chunk far exceeds Veilid's 32 KiB `app_call` cap, so the fetcher pulls the sealed content frame one ≤`FRAGMENT_SIZE` (30 KiB) fragment per `app_call` and reassembles — request `[tag][share_id(32)]([chunk_addr(48)])[fragment u32]`, reply `[status][total u32][fragment]`. **D-3.4 invariant kept:** core's 1 MiB `CHUNK_SIZE` + SHA-384 content-addressing are untouched; the fetcher reassembles a chunk, opens it under the `PublicRoomKey`, and **re-derives SHA-384 to verify it against the requested address** (ISC-S28 / ISC-A-S20) — a faithful sharer passes by construction, a tampered chunk fails closed. **Fragment consistency:** AES-256-GCM draws a fresh nonce per seal, so the serve side seals each answer ONCE and caches it (`ServedShare`), slicing every fragment from the one cached blob — else fragments from different seals could never reassemble. **Serve-loop location:** the actor owns the served-share registry (`share_id → ServedShare`) and the `VeilidAPI`, so inbound `app_call`s are routed from the update pump into the actor (a new internal `Command::InboundAppCall`) where `share::serve` answers and `app_call_reply` replies; outbound fetch `app_call`s are spawned (`Command::AppCall`) so a multi-fragment fetch never blocks the loop. Surface: `VeilidNetHandle::{serve_share, fetch_manifest, fetch_chunk}` (the prior `serve_share` Slice-2 signpost is replaced). **Anti-dox (D-3.5):** the sharer serves over a private inbound route (`new_inbound_route`, blob hides its node/IP); the fetcher addresses `Target::RouteId` from its default safe context. Content stays sealed under the `PublicRoomKey` — the route sees ciphertext (ISC-A-S22). **Deferred to Slice 2b:** delivering the sharer's route blob *in* the `ShareAnnouncement` (the additive `route_blob` field, D-3.5 — so discovery auto-wires to fetch; today the route is passed directly) + signing it into the announcement provenance, and the GUI/TUI net-actor share-command wiring (they still route share commands to `Error{"not yet on Veilid"}`). **ISC impact: none yet** (transport ISCs minted at cutover, Phase-2 precedent). **Wire impact: none yet** — the fragment framing is veilid-net-internal + pre-cutover; no proto/core change (so the workspace gate is unperturbed — Slice 2a is veilid-net-only). **Oracle:** 4 in-process `share::tests` pass HERE incl. `serve_then_fetch_round_trip_with_fragmentation` (a >2-fragment file served + fetched + reassembled + SHA-384-verified end-to-end, no network) + `fetch_unknown_share_is_not_found`; veilid-net fmt / clippy (`-D warnings`, `--all-targets --all-features`) / build green. **Live `app_call` over real Veilid is DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco:** `crates/daemonseed-veilid-net/tests/two_node_share.rs` (sharer serves over a private route, fetcher reassembles + verifies a multi-fragment chunk, outsider room key fails) compiles here; the VM's NAT blocks attach. RedTeam (anti-dox serve adversarial pass) still owed at Slice 2b once route_blob delivery exists. Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/veilid-migration.md`](docs/design/veilid-migration.md) § *Phase 3/4 — DECIDED*.

- 2026-06-27: **Veilid Phase 3/4 Slice 1 — extract the shared rendezvous engine, add the public-room owner seed, build the lobby/public-room rendezvous + share discovery (D-3.1–D-3.6 ratified, caraka).** The "Phase 3 needs Phase 4's lobby" coupling resolved structurally: `crates/daemonseed-veilid-net/src/circle.rs` was already a generic shared-owner DFLT rendezvous engine mis-filed as a circle feature (every function takes an owner `KeyPair` + opaque sealed bytes; "circle" entered only via the owner-seed source). **D-3.1 — extracted it to `rendezvous.rs`** (behaviour-preserving rename: `open_or_create`/`rendezvous_key`/`member_base_subkey`/`publish`/`sweep` + watch wiring unchanged; `identity::circle_owner_keypair`→`rendezvous_owner_keypair`; the private actor command `PublishCircle`/`SubscribeCircle`→`PublishRendezvous`/`SubscribeRendezvous`). The public `VeilidNetHandle::publish_circle`/`subscribe_circle` contract is unchanged; `two_node_circle.rs` is the inertness oracle. Locked as a rule (engine reuse): every Veilid rendezvous = `(owner-seed derivation, key-class, payload codec)` over the one module; no feature re-implements record/ring/sweep. **D-3.2 — one net-new core crypto sibling:** `daemonseed_core::public_room::derive_room_veilid_owner_seed(room, suite)` + `RoomVeilidOwnerSeed`, under `kdf::info::public_room_veilid_owner` (`daemonseed/veilid/room-owner/{family}/{room}`). **Signature refinement (flagged, not silent):** taken as `(room, suite)` — a SIBLING of the room key (re-extracts the public-room PRK under the owner label) — NOT literally `(&PublicRoomKey)` as first sketched, because chaining the transport owner off the *content* key is precisely the coupling the sibling pattern exists to prevent; this is the faithful mirror of `derive_circle_veilid_owner_seed(entropy, suite)` (which likewise takes the inputs, not `cot_key`). World-derivable (room name + family are public) so the lobby/public room is an open rendezvous by construction; an operator channel (MOTD/announcements, Phase 4) instead uses a non-derivable owner keypair as its write-gate. Domain-separated from both the room key (distinct `info`) and a like-named circle owner seed (distinct salt + `info`) — ISC-A-S18 / ISC-A-S2 hold. **D-3.3 — build order inverts the published phase numbers** (the lobby is a Phase-4 object but a Phase-3 prerequisite, since share discovery announces *into* it): rendezvous extraction → core sibling → lobby/public-room rendezvous → share **discovery**, shipped as Slice 1; share **content** transfer + GUI/TUI wiring as Slice 2. `VeilidNetHandle::{publish_room,subscribe_room}` are thin wrappers over the same generic rendezvous command as circles — only the caller's owner-seed + seal key differ; a public-share `ShareAnnouncement` (sealed under the `PublicRoomKey`) is just an item published on the lobby record, so discovery is a parameterization, not new transport. **D-3.4 — content transfer = transport-layer fragmentation (the cleaner invariant, caraka):** keep core's 1 MiB SHA-384 chunk content-addressing (ISC-S28 / ISC-A-S20 stay meaningful) and fragment any oversized `ShareFrame` into ≤32 KiB `app_call` frames; the fetcher reassembles then SHA-384-verifies. (Reducing core `CHUNK_SIZE` to ≤32 KiB was rejected — large-share manifests overflow the cap too, so fragmentation is unavoidable either way; keeping 1 MiB addressing is the cleaner invariant.) Slice 2. **D-3.5 — anti-dox serve (Slice 2):** sharer `new_private_route` → publish blob; fetcher addresses `Target::RouteId` from its default safe context; the route blob is delivered as an additive `ShareAnnouncement.route_blob` field (MINOR), refreshed on `RouteChanged`; `map_update` starts handling `AppCall`. **D-3.6 — availability:** content-serving owner-online is an invariant (= the relay's owner-served-on-demand, NOT a regression); DHT discovery is an improvement (survives owner-offline; no always-on relay); once a share's freshness and the owner's presence share the room record (Phase 4) an offline owner's share self-removes from discovery before a fetch can hang. **ISC impact: none yet** — per the migration's frozen-contract posture, new transport-layer `ISC-*` are minted at cutover (Phase 2 precedent); Slice 1 records the decisions here + in the design doc. **Wire impact: none yet** — veilid-net is workspace-excluded + pre-cutover; the core additions are additive pub API + a new (relay-unused) info string; the `route_blob` proto field lands in Slice 2. **Oracle:** daemonseed-core + veilid-net fmt / clippy (`-D warnings`, `--all-targets --all-features`) / build green; new core tests pass (`public_room::tests::{room_owner_seed_is_deterministic, room_owner_seed_distinct_rooms, room_owner_seed_disjoint_from_room_key_and_circle_owner}` + `kdf::info::tests::public_room_veilid_owner_info_string_is_pinned`); full workspace `cargo test` / clippy / fmt / check-proto green; both root and crate `Cargo.lock` untouched. **Live DHT discovery is DEFERRED-VERIFY → orinoco:** `crates/daemonseed-veilid-net/tests/two_node_room.rs` (a sealed `ShareAnnouncement` reaches a second node via the lobby rendezvous and opens under the room key; an outsider room key fails) compiles here; the VM's SLIRP NAT blocks public attach. **Process note:** the commitment-boundary advisor was caraka's direct ratification of D-3.1–D-3.6 (the inline `Inference.ts --mode advisor` is host-blocked by the CLAUDECODE rule, as in Phase 2). RedTeam (the anti-dox serve adversarial pass) is deferred to Slice 2, where its target — the private-route `app_call` serve flow — actually exists. Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/veilid-migration.md`](docs/design/veilid-migration.md) § *Phase 3/4 — DECIDED*.

- 2026-06-27: **Order the circle transcript by `sent_unix_ms` (#105).** DHT propagation latency means a circle message's arrival order at a receiver can differ from its send order (observed in the #98 S4 felt-test). `wire::CircleMessage.sent_unix_ms` (best-effort sender wall-clock) is now threaded through `NetEvent::CircleMessage` + `state::Msg`, and `push_message` does an ordered insert (`partition_point` by `sent_unix_ms`) instead of append — so a late, older message slots into its chronological place; for in-order arrivals (Lobby, the common circle case) it is a no-op append. The Lobby path (`NetEvent::Message`, no wire stamp) passes its receive time, keeping its behavior an append. **Caveat (carried to #100/#78):** `sent_unix_ms` is the *sender's* clock and daemonseed assumes no shared time source, so ordering is only as good as members' clock sync; a per-sender monotonic sequence would be more robust — timestamp ordering is the pragmatic first step. GUI-internal (`NetEvent`/`Msg` are `daemonseed-gui`-internal) — no wire/proto/LAMA surface changed. Oracle: `state::tests::circle_messages_insert_in_sent_unix_ms_order` (out-of-arrival-order inserts land in timestamp order) + clippy `-D warnings` (default + `desktop veilid`, `--all-targets`) + the 34 `state::tests` unchanged.

- 2026-06-27: **#98 S4 PASSED live (orinoco) + two felt-test fixes (#101 echo, #102 rejoin).** The Veilid circle path works GUI↔GUI on the real network: two independent nodes derived the same rendezvous and exchanged sealed messages over the DHT, no relay. (2) Two local instances both defaulting to `my_handle="guest"` SILENTLY suppress each other's messages (own-handle de-dup) even if the whole path works — so the felt-test launches each instance with a DISTINCT display name. (3) `send_circle` publishes once vs `#99`'s re-publish loop — a candidate delivery gap on a freshly-created record (deferred; not fixed blind). Gates GREEN: veilid-net fmt + clippy `-D warnings` standalone; `daemonseed-gui --features "desktop veilid"` clippy `-D warnings`. **Live trace output is DEFERRED-VERIFY → S4 on orinoco** (this VM's NAT blocks attach); the next felt-test produces a decisive trace, not another inference. Trace + env-knobs are debug/test-only, behind the `veilid` feature; no wire/proto/LAMA surface changed. - 2026-06-26: **#98 S2 (GUI half) done — parallel Veilid net actor behind the `veilid` feature; UI untouched.** New module `crates/daemonseed-gui/src/veilid_net.rs` (`veilid_net_actor`) implements the SAME `NetCommand`/`NetEvent` contract the relay actor does, backed by `daemonseed_veilid_net::VeilidNetHandle`. **Backend selection lives INSIDE `NetHandle::new()`** (net.rs): a `#[cfg(feature = "veilid")]` branch spawns `veilid_net_actor` on a multi-thread runtime (veilid-core spawns its own tasks); the relay path keeps its current-thread+`LocalSet` runtime. So `main.rs` (the call site at `build_ui`) and the whole UI are unchanged — the cleanest possible seam, since `NetHandle` is the same struct/channel shape either way. **Live in Veilid mode:** `Connect` → fresh D3 identity (`derive_identity_keys(Mnemonic::generate(), Primary).veilid_node_seed`, per-launch — circle membership is phrase-derived, not node-derived) → `VeilidNet::start` → `attach_and_wait(180)` → `Connected`; `JoinCircle{circle_id,phrase}` → `derive_cot_key` + `derive_circle_veilid_owner_seed` → `subscribe_circle(owner_seed)`; `SendCircle` → `seal_message(cot_key)` → `publish_circle` + local echo; inbound `VeilidNetEvent::Inbound{bytes}` → try `open_message` against each joined circle's key (AEAD authenticates the match), own messages suppressed by handle (the local-echo de-dup). `CircleJoined.asset_addr` is synthesized via `asset_address(cot_key, b"veilid-circle")` (deterministic per circle) purely for the UI's existing fingerprint/dedup slot — the real rendezvous is the DHT record key. **Not yet on Veilid (Phases 3/4):** SendRoom/JoinRoom/PublishShare/UnpublishShare/RefreshShares/RefreshPublicSpace/UploadAnnouncement/SetMotd/FetchShare/ConfirmFetch → `NetEvent::Error("not yet on Veilid")`; internal/timer commands are silent no-ops. Under the feature the relay actor is dormant (deleted at cutover), so net.rs carries a feature-scoped `#![cfg_attr(feature = "veilid", allow(dead_code, unused_imports))]`. **REMAINING for #98:** the TUI mirror (its `NetCommand` differs — `JoinCircle{phrase}` no circle_id, `SendChat{circle_id,body,sender_handle}`, `drain_events` not `try_recv`) + unit tests for the command-translation/`not-yet` arms + S4 live two-client felt-test on orinoco (S1's gate, this VM's SLIRP NAT blocks attach) + S5 doc-sync. (Delegation floor relaxed — self-implemented at full seam fidelity to avoid an agent mirroring a 5.4k-line module from a brief, the documented truncation risk; thinking floor met: SystemsThinking/FirstPrinciples/IterativeDepth/ReReadCheck.)

- 2026-06-26: **#98 S2 (TUI half) done — parallel Veilid net actor in the TUI; the seam built with it; UI untouched.** New module `crates/daemonseed-tui/src/veilid_net.rs` (`veilid_net_actor`) implements the TUI's `NetCommand`/`NetEvent` contract over `daemonseed_veilid_net::VeilidNetHandle`; `lib.rs` gains `#[cfg(feature="veilid")] mod veilid_net;` and `net.rs` the feature-scoped `#![cfg_attr(feature="veilid", allow(dead_code, unused_imports))]`. **Backend selection in `NetHandle::new()`**: a `#[cfg(feature="veilid")]` branch builds a multi-thread runtime and `block_on`s `veilid_net_actor`; the relay branch keeps current-thread+`LocalSet`+`net_actor` unchanged, so `main.rs`/the ratatui UI are byte-unchanged. **TUI surface divergences handled:** `JoinCircle{phrase}` (no circle_id over the wire) — the actor assigns `next_circle_id`, derives the client-local label via `default_circle_label` over a synthesized content-key fingerprint, is idempotent by `owner_seed`, and emits `CircleJoined{circle_id,label,entropy:phrase}`; `SendChat{circle_id,body,sender_handle}` seals + `publish_circle`; the not-yet-on-Veilid surfaces map to each one's matching error event (`RefreshShares→SharesError`, `PublishShare→PublishError{root:Some}`, etc.) carrying `"not yet on Veilid"` (the TUI has no single generic `Error` variant the GUI's contract does); flagged for caraka). **KEY DIVERGENCE FROM THE GUI MIRROR — no actor-level local echo.** The TUI `App` Enter handler already pushes the composed line locally (`app.rs:3010`, "the relay never reflects a frame to its sender"), and the `ChatMessage` event handler appends with no dedup (`app.rs:1541`) — so echoing in the actor (as the GUI does, where the actor is the *only* render path) would render every own message twice. The actor therefore does NOT echo; it only suppresses the DELAYED DHT re-surface of the own write, by sender-handle match, with `my_handle: Option<String>` (`None` until the first `SendChat`) so a real inbound from another member is never over-suppressed before a `name#hash` is known. (Bug caught by an Engineer fidelity review; confirmed by directly reading `app.rs:3003-3022` + `:1535-1548`.) **Oracle:** `cargo clippy -p daemonseed-tui --all-targets -D warnings` (default, relay path) clean; `cargo clippy -p daemonseed-tui --features veilid --all-targets -D warnings` clean; 6 new `#[cfg(all(test,feature="veilid"))]` unit tests pass (the not-connected / not-yet-on-Veilid / empty-snapshot arms — the only behaviour driveable without a live DHT); the relay's 195 default-feature tests unperturbed. The live two-client felt-test is **DEFERRED-VERIFY → S4 on orinoco** (this VM's SLIRP NAT blocks public attach). **REMAINING for #98:** S4 live felt-test, then Phases 3/4 (lobby/shares/presence/MOTD) + the v0.33.0 cutover flag-flip. (Delegation floor relaxed — single bounded mirror, self-implemented at full surface fidelity with an independent Engineer review as the re-verify; thinking floor met: ContextSearch/FeedbackMemoryConsult/Advisor/ReReadCheck.)

- 2026-06-26: **Veilid Phase 2 — circles = shared-owner DFLT DHT rendezvous + per-member append-ring (refines D2 away from `SMPL`).** Fan-out shape decided (caraka): a circle's owner keypair is derived deterministically from the shared circle entropy (`daemonseed-core::circle::key::derive_circle_veilid_owner_seed` — a SIBLING expansion of the same circle PRK that yields `cot_key`, under `kdf::info::circle_veilid_owner`, so the rendezvous address is NOT a function of the content key and neither derives from the other). Every member therefore computes the SAME `DHTSchema::DFLT` record key via `get_dht_record_key` = the relay-free rendezvous address (the DHT analog of the relay-era `SHA-384(cot_key‖server_id)`), and every member holds the owner secret so all write owner-signed subkeys with **no pre-known member list** — which is precisely what an `SMPL` baked-in, owner-signed member set could NOT provide for open membership (and `create_dht_record` even rejects self-as-member under SMPL). This refines the Phase-0 "D2 = SMPL" finding: SMPL fits closed/known membership; cot_key-derived OPEN rendezvous wants shared-owner DFLT. **Persistence = per-member append-ring** (each member writes a small ring within its own region, region = hash of its node pubkey): a connecting member sweeps the record for a **bounded recent backlog** and watches it for new writes. **Durable scrollback is explicitly OUT OF SCOPE** (caraka) — the ring is a bounded, best-effort recent window (DHT TTL/refresh), not history; full history would need a pinning/availability node (post-MVP, D1). **Subscribe = watch + one-shot login sweep**; inbound circle messages surface as `VeilidNetEvent::Inbound` (eventual — watch latency ~tens of seconds). Content stays sealed under the circle key (ISC-C8 unchanged); this layer moves opaque bytes only (ISC-A-S2). **Single encryption layer (caraka, 2026-06-26):** the rendezvous record key is derived with NO Veilid encryption key, so Veilid stores values **verbatim** — the cot seal (AES-256-GCM, post-quantum) is the ONLY content encryption and the DHT sees ciphertext exactly as the relay did. (`create_dht_record` force-assigns a *random* per-record encryption key — `create_record.rs:87` — that members can't derive, breaking cross-member reads; `open_or_create` reopens with the no-encryption-key record key, which resets the handle to verbatim storage — `open_record.rs`. This also surfaced the first live oracle failure: B read A's bytes but they wouldn't open, because A's writes were encrypted under a key B lacked.) Keeping content crypto decoupled from Veilid's *classical* transport crypto also keeps a future Veilid-PQC suite a free, content-independent adoption. Tunables (`crates/daemonseed-veilid-net/src/circle.rs`): `SUBKEY_COUNT=64`, `MEMBER_REGIONS=32`, `RING_DEPTH=2` — sized for small circles so the backlog sweep is cheap; region hash-collision degrades to slot-sharing (a roster, Phase 4, removes the blind hash). Verified HERE: daemonseed-core + veilid-net fmt/clippy(`-D warnings`)/build green, 588 core tests pass (incl. owner-seed determinism / canonicalization / entropy-divergence / content-key-separation), workspace clippy + check-proto green, both root and crate `Cargo.lock` untouched. **Live DHT behavior is DEFERRED-VERIFY (orinoco):** `crates/daemonseed-veilid-net/tests/two_node_circle.rs` (#99, `#[ignore]`) compiles here; the VM's SLIRP NAT blocks public attach, so it runs on a real-network host. **Process note:** the Algorithm commitment-boundary advisor was caraka's direct adjudication of the four design forks (the inline `Inference.ts --mode advisor` second opinion is blocked on this host by the no-nested-`claude`/CLAUDECODE operational rule). Filed: #100 (message-age display — `CircleMessage.sent_unix_ms` already on the wire; a backlog message reads stale only if its age is shown). Rationale-of-record: [`docs/design/veilid-migration.md`](docs/design/veilid-migration.md) Phase 2.

- 2026-06-26: **Transport pivot — daemonseed moves off its own relay onto Veilid (wire v2.0); Phase 1 lands the `veilid-net` core.** The always-on `daemonseed-server` relay (rendezvous + blind forward + NAT traversal) is replaced by the Veilid stack, which provides all three as a decentralized substrate. This is a **substrate swap, not a rewrite**: the v0.29.0 relay-blind refactor already cut the codebase along this seam, and the 2026-06-26 spike proved a real circle message crosses the public Veilid wire with the PQ envelope byte-identical (oracle green). Because the proto payloads, identity, and AES-256-GCM content envelopes are transport-agnostic, the swap is a clean **wire v2.0** (MAJOR transport bump, ISC-S14) the content/identity layers ride unchanged — the payoff of the transport-purity discipline. Design-of-record: [`docs/design/veilid-migration.md`](docs/design/veilid-migration.md), which defines the locked decisions **D1–D5** (fra1 = bootstrap-only seed; circle/lobby fan-out = DHT `SMPL`; node key derived from the identity seed; ride the PUBLIC Veilid network; hop-count a user setting > 1). **Invariants preserved (why the swap is cheap):** ISC-A-S2 (transport sees ciphertext only — Veilid carries opaque `app_message`s and holds no key) and ISC-C8 (content keys derive ONLY from the circle entropy, NEVER from Veilid's classical x25519 material — content stays post-quantum regardless of substrate).
  **D3 — Veilid node identity from the daemonseed seed.** `daemonseed-core::identity::keys::derive_identity_keys` adds a 4th HKDF expansion of the same identity PRK under a domain-separated label (`kdf::info::DOMAIN_VEILID_NODE`) → a 32-byte Ed25519 seed (`VeilidNodeSeed`; VLD0 = Ed25519, so the bytes ARE the node secret), so one 24-word phrase yields one identity across content + transport while sharing no key material with the ML-DSA / ML-KEM keys.
  **Phase-1 design calls (caraka, 2026-06-26):** ① `veilid-net` is a **command-channel actor** — `VeilidNet::start` spawns a task owning `VeilidAPI` + `RoutingContext`, driven via a cloneable `VeilidNetHandle` (the `AppSession` replacement the GUI/TUI plug into). ② Phase 1 uses Veilid's **default routing context** (already a 1-hop safety route); raising the hop count via `with_safety(Safe { hop_count })` is the D5 dial-up (carried in `VeilidNetConfig::hop_count`). *(Refined 2026-06-26 from "Unsafe context" — live on orinoco, an explicit Unsafe context requires veilid-core's `footgun-nodeid-target` feature, the anti-dox `Target::NodeId` path we avoid; the private-route `Target::RouteId` send the spike's public mode proved uses the default context.)* ③ **rendezvous is Phase 2** (DHT `SMPL`, owner-from-`cot_key`); Phase 1 hands the route blob in-process/test (KISS). ④ `veilid-net` is **transport-pure** — opaque bytes only, never the content key/plaintext (seal/open stays in `daemonseed-core`); this also keeps the future veilid-PQC (VLD1-class) contribution clean. ⑤ route-failure is **surface-only** for now (`VeilidNetEvent::RouteChanged`); re-allocation is later work.
  **Repo strategy:** the migration accumulates on the long-lived `feat/veilid-migration` branch; with `main` frozen at v0.32.0 the cutover is a near-trivial merge (superseding the design's "tree-replacement of unrelated histories"). `daemonseed-veilid-net` lives in `crates/` but is held in `[workspace] exclude` (own Cargo.lock) so veilid-core's dep tree never perturbs the frozen relay resolution; it joins `members` when `daemonseed-server` is disconnected at cutover. Relay crates are **disconnected, not purged** — hard-pruned only after v0.33.0 settles.
  **ISC impact:** none yet — new transport-layer `ISC-*` are minted at cutover, when the relay is actually replaced and the frozen contract changes (ID-stability + frozen-contract posture). The D3 derivation is covered by `daemonseed_core::identity::keys::tests::veilid_node_seed_{is_deterministic,is_derived_not_zero,diverges_by_identity,diverges_by_mnemonic}`. **Wire impact:** none yet — the wire v2.0 bump lands at cutover; this branch is additive + workspace-excluded. **Oracle:** the 2026-06-26 spike (`== SPIKE PASSED ==` on the public Veilid net) + the 4 D3 unit tests + the excluded crate's fmt / clippy (`-D warnings`) / build GREEN; the live 2-node `send_sealed` over a private route **PASSED on orinoco 2026-06-26 via `crates/daemonseed-veilid-net/tests/two_node_sealed.rs`** (real public Veilid network, 33.4s; ciphertext on the wire, member recovers byte-for-byte, outsider key fails) — the first productized end-to-end proof of the Veilid transport path through the `VeilidNetHandle` API.

- 2026-06-26: **Make the circle-details sheet reachable — per-circle "Details" open control + "Close" (#35).** #36 shipped the sheet's content (the three name vectors) but nothing *opened* it: the live open-trigger was stranded on the unmerged tail of `fix/gui-overlay-polish` (the bulk of that branch merged earlier; its 2-commit tail did not). **Decision — re-apply the trigger fresh rather than cherry-pick** the 71-behind commit (`3d937de`): the current `app.slint` already carries the sheet, the `sheet-open` property, and the #36 vectors, so only the opener was missing. (1) `sheet-open` flipped `in` → `in-out` so a `clicked` handler can set it. (2) A per-circle header `if root.active != 0 : PillButton { label: "Details" }` sets `sheet-open = true` — hidden on the Lobby (no phrase/fingerprint), `if` (not `visible:`) so it leaves no Lobby layout gap. (3) A "Close" pill in the sheet header sets `sheet-open = false`, and the sheet is `visible:`-gated on `sheet-open` (no longer permanently mounted off-screen — the resize-flash fix). **ISC impact:** none — the sheet's content coverage is ISC-C96 (#36); reachability makes that felt-test performable (it was previously unreachable). **Wire impact:** none — UI-only, no proto/LAMA change (no SemVer impact). **Oracle:** the gate-green slint compile + the `--show-circle-detail` offscreen render (the sheet renders with the new Close header + the three vectors, no panic); the live Details→open / Close→dismiss click flow is the attended felt-test.

- 2026-06-26: **GUI share-name persistence — a named share auto-republishes under its chosen name (#41).** The auto-republish path already threaded an optional name end to end (`PublishedShare { root, name }` round-trips the blob; `republish_name` does the basename fallback on `None`), but `persist_published` hard-coded `add_published(root, None)`, so a custom name was NEVER stored and every republish fell back to the basename — the one remaining gap. **The name-a-share UI already existed** (the publish overlay's `publish_name_input` `MenuTextInput` flows `publish-name` → `submit-publish` → `pick_dir_and_publish` → `PublishShare { name }` → `PublishStarted { name }`, blank → basename in `pick_dir_and_publish`); #41 only needed the persistence threaded. **Decision — thread the name into persistence:** `Profile::persist_published` / `GuiState::persist_published` take `name: Option<&str>`; the `PublishStarted` handler persists it. **Decision — what to persist (custom vs basename):** persist `Some(custom)` for a user-typed name and `None` for the basename default, so `republish_name` keeps deriving the basename for un-named shares. The custom-vs-basename signal is **derived at the persist point by comparing the effective `name` to the root's own basename** rather than threading a `was_custom` flag through `PublishShare`/`PublishStarted` and ~10 test sites: the one bit is losslessly recoverable from data already in the event, keeping the fire-and-forget command/event surface unchanged. The failure mode is inert — a custom name that equals the basename persists as `None`, which republishes to that same basename (identical result), and any future divergence between the handler's basename logic and `republish_name` can only store `Some(basename)` vs `None`, which `republish_name` treats identically. **No clobber on republish:** `Seeds::add_published` is keyed on the root and ignores the name for an already-stored root, so the connect-time republish (which re-calls `persist_published`) never overwrites a stored custom name. **ISC impact:** new ISC-C98 (client-positive). Unlike C97 (pure declarative Slint, uncovered), #41 adds real persistence logic, so it is registered COVERED on the core seal/open name round-trip (`published_share_name_persists_across_seal_open`, mirroring C96's core-logic registration) with the GUI write-through unit-tested in-crate and the visible peer-sees-the-name half felt-gated: registry TOTAL 179→180, pos 118→119, client-side 122→123, COVERED 109→110, MILESTONE_COVERED alpha3 9→10 (the stale header `178 total` and the missing C97 header line were corrected in passing per doc-sync). **Wire impact:** none — `persist_published` is `daemonseed-gui`-internal, no proto/LAMA change (no SemVer impact). **Oracle:** `published_share_name_persists_across_seal_open` + `state::tests::profile_round_trips_published_shares_across_reload` (custom name `Some` survives reseal/reopen un-clobbered by a re-publish, basename default `None`); the live "a peer sees the custom name" round-trip is the attended felt-test (ISC-C98 left `[ ]`).
- 2026-06-26: **Right-click clipboard context menu on the GUI text fields via a reusable wrapper, using Slint's built-in TextInput clipboard functions (#67).** The GUI text fields were raw `TextInput`s with no right-click menu; the only clipboard affordance was #63's behind-the-field right-click→paste on the Join phrase. **Decision — approach:** wire the menu to each field's OWN built-in `TextInput` functions (`cut`/`copy`/`paste`/`select-all`) rather than hand-rolling clipboard writes via `arboard`. Those built-ins use Slint's backend (winit) clipboard, which manages X11 selection ownership correctly — a transient `arboard::Clipboard::new()?.set_text()` drops selection ownership the moment the `Clipboard` is dropped, so a Copy/Cut would not survive to be pasted into another X11 app (the documented #63 caveat applies to writes). Using the backend clipboard sidesteps that entirely, so no `write_clipboard_text` helper was added. (1) A single reusable `MenuTextInput` component (`Rectangle` → builtin `ContextMenuArea` → `TextInput`, mirroring Slint's own `LineEditBase`) carries the Cut/Copy/Paste/Select-all `Menu`; it exposes `text`/`single-line`/`font-size`/`wrap`/`input-type` props, `accepted`/`edited` callbacks, and `focus-input`/`select-all`/`copy` public functions (named `focus-input` because `focus` is a builtin member). **NB the wrapper must inherit `Rectangle` and contain a *builtin* `ContextMenuArea` — inheriting `ContextMenuArea` directly panics the slint compiler ("This should not happen because of inlining", langtype.rs `as_builtin`), since the menu-lowering pass requires a builtin there, not a user component.** (2) 15 text-entry fields were converted; `fs_mnemonic_box` (read-only recovery-phrase display, which already has a Copy button) was left a bare `TextInput`. (3) The Join field sets `custom-paste: true` and routes the menu's Paste to the existing `paste-into-join` (`read_clipboard_text`) path via `paste-requested`, preserving its clipboard-read + strength-refresh + refocus and keeping `read_clipboard_text` in the live path; the now-redundant behind-field paste `TouchArea` was removed (#63 unregressed, now surfaced through the proper menu). **ISC impact:** new ISC-C97 (client-positive). The menu is pure declarative Slint with NO new Rust logic to honestly unit-test, so — unlike C94/C95/C96 — **no automated coverage was registered** (registering against a fabricated probe would be dishonest): registry TOTAL 178→179, pos 117→118, client-side 121→122; **COVERED and MILESTONE_COVERED unchanged** (C97 joins the built-but-uncovered set, like C59–C65). **Wire impact:** none — UI-only, no proto/LAMA change (no SemVer impact). **Oracle:** the gate-green build (the slint compile of the menu lowering + the windowed `--features desktop` build); the visible right-click menu and Cut/Copy/Paste/Select-all behavior across the fields are the attended felt-test (ISC-C97 left `[ ]`).
- 2026-06-26: **Render the three circle name vectors in the context sheet over the existing accessors (#36).** The relay-scoped adj-noun label (`GuiState::circle_deterministic_label_of`) and the relay-independent `#<12hex>` fingerprint (`GuiState::circle_fingerprint_of`) were already in place + unit-tested (ISC-C62 "data shipped"), both carrying `#[allow(dead_code)]` pending the UI affordance; the context sheet (`sheet`, the #35 "Circle details" slide-in) already existed but showed no name vectors. **Decision:** render the three vectors in that sheet over the existing accessors — no new data path. (1) The sheet gains three `in property <string>` props (`circle-name` / `circle-relay-label` / `circle-fingerprint`), each rendered as a captioned row gated `visible` on a non-empty value (so the Lobby, all-blank, shows just the header copy). The captions deliberately distinguish the vectors: `Name`; "Relay name (matches only on this relay)" — making the relay-scoping explicit and NOT implying a cross-relay identity; "Fingerprint (verify out-of-band — same everywhere)" — marking the universal vector. (2) `main::apply_circle_detail(ui, st, idx)` sets the props from the two accessors + `circle.name` (chosen name shown only for a real circle, so the Lobby stays blank); it is called wherever the active circle changes or its label updates (switch-circle, materialize, initial view, the `CircleJoined` rendezvous swap). The `#[allow(dead_code)]` on both accessors is removed now that the binary drives them. (3) Values are display-only — no mutation. **ISC impact:** new ISC-C96 (client-positive, the visible 3-vector affordance, distinct from C62's data path); registry TOTAL 177→178, pos 116→117, COVERED 108→109, MILESTONE_COVERED alpha3 8→9. **Wire impact:** none — UI-only, no proto change (no SemVer impact). **Oracle:** `state::tests::circle_detail_label_derives_from_net_contract_not_chosen_name` (the accessor logic: pre-join label = fingerprint, post-join label = `default_circle_label` diverging from the relay-independent fingerprint, Lobby = `None`) + the integration `circle_detail_isc_coverage::{circle_detail_vectors_diverge_label_relay_scoped_fingerprint_universal, isc_c96_covered}` (registers ISC-C96 against the core label/fingerprint divergence substrate); `--show-circle-detail` offscreen fixture for the screenshot. The visible placement of the three rows in the sheet is the attended felt-test (ISC-C96 left `[ ]`).
- 2026-06-26: **Palette-driven rename + live handle update over the existing rename core (#66).** ISC-C82 (#66 core) shipped the display-name re-seal (`GuiState::rename_identity` → `Profile::rename`) but left the Ctrl-K palette entry and the live-wire-handle update felt-deferred — so the only way to set a name was first-start, and a nameless pre-#65 profile stayed nameless with no in-app recovery. **Decision:** build the UI affordance + live update on top of the existing core, no new persistence logic. (1) A new `"Rename identity"` action in the palette registry (index 6) opens a rename overlay (`rename-open`, mirroring the About/Join overlays); `on_open_rename` prefills `rename-name` with the current `display_handle` (blank for a nameless profile — the #65 recovery path) and clears any prior error. (2) `on_submit_rename` validates with `is_valid_display_name` (invalid → inline `rename-error`, overlay stays open), then calls `GuiState::rename_identity`; the `#[allow(dead_code)]` on `rename_identity` is removed now that the binary drives it. (3) **Live update** — on success the rail identity line (`my-handle`, seeded from `display_handle()` on enter-main) is refreshed in place AND a new `NetCommand::SetMyHandle { handle }` updates the net actor's `my_handle` (`handle_set_my_handle`, the live counterpart of the `Connect{display_handle}` assignment) so local echoes / heartbeats / `mine` detection present the new name without a reconnect — the connection proof stays the ephemeral one (D8, only the display name changes). The Actor struct construction was extracted to `Actor::new` so the live-update is unit-testable. **ISC impact:** new ISC-C95 (client-positive, distinct affordance over C82's core); registry TOTAL 176→177, pos 115→116, COVERED 107→108, MILESTONE_COVERED alpha3 7→8. **Wire impact:** none — `SetMyHandle` is a UI→actor command, no proto change (no SemVer impact). **Oracle:** `state::tests::rename_identity_*` (the core re-seal round-trip, incl. nameless recovery) + `net::tests::set_my_handle_updates_presented_handle` (the live handle mutation) + the integration `rename_identity_isc_coverage::{rename_named_identity_persists_across_unlock, rename_recovers_a_nameless_profile, isc_c95_covered}` (registers ISC-C95 against the core re-seal substrate); `--show-rename` offscreen fixture for the screenshot. The visible palette flow (open → type → Save → the rail line flips, no reload) is the attended felt-test (ISC-C95 left `[ ]`).
- 2026-06-26: **Port the TUI's per-circle presence into the GUI net actor (#77).** ISC-C88 (#75) wired GUI presence for the lobby only; the TUI already had full per-circle presence, but the GUI `CircleSub` had no tracker, its `EmitHeartbeat` beaconed the lobby only, its `ApplyHeartbeat` folded only into the lobby tracker, and `NetEvent::Roster` was lobby-scoped. **Decision:** mirror the TUI's per-circle emit/ingest/reap into the GUI net actor — the core heartbeat/presence primitives (#74) and the TUI reference were both already complete, so this is pure GUI net wiring. (1) `CircleSub` gains a `presence: PresenceTracker` built fresh on join (cadence-matched, like the lobby). (2) `handle_emit_heartbeat` emits a beacon per joined circle, sealed under that circle's `cot_key` via `seal_circle_heartbeat`, with the sealed `HeartbeatFields.room` set to the circle's deterministic `default_circle_label(&asset_addr)` — the SAME value the TUI seals and every member derives from the rendezvous, so provenance binds consistently across clients (the sealed room is provenance-only; the receive side never matches on it). The reap runs per circle on the same tick. (3) The circle inbound reader opens an inbound frame under that circle's key (`open_heartbeat`, tried after `open_message`) and posts `ApplyHeartbeat { room }` where the routing key is the **GUI `circle_id` as a decimal string** (the GUI circle has no label to key on; circle_id is its native identity) — `handle_apply_heartbeat` parses it back to find the circle, while the lobby still routes by room name. The own-beacon self-filter (extracted to `beacon_is_own`) and the ISC-A-C41 freshness gate run before routing, so they hold for the lobby and every circle alike. (4) `NetEvent::Roster` gains `circle_id: Option<u64>` (None = lobby, Some = circle); the binary updates the people column only when the event's room equals `GuiState::active_circle_id()`, so a background circle's roster updates its tracker net-side without changing the visible column. The roster-push predicate is extracted to the shared `roster_render_changed`. Relay-blind (ISC-A-S2): the relay forwards only the opaque sealed beacon and holds no circle key. **ISC impact:** new ISC-C94 (client-positive); registry TOTAL 175→176, pos 114→115, COVERED 106→107, MILESTONE_COVERED alpha3 6→7. **Wire impact:** none — reuses the existing `MemberHeartbeat` over the existing circle subscribe stream; no proto change (no SemVer impact). **Oracle:** `daemonseed-gui::net::tests::presence_roster::{circle_beacon_opens_under_its_key_only_then_rosters, self_filter_drops_own_beacon_only, roster_render_changed_matrix}` (the real seal_circle_heartbeat→open_heartbeat→PresenceTracker path + wrong-circle isolation + self-filter, GUI helpers) + the integration `presence_circle_isc_coverage::{circle_presence_seal_open_track_round_trip, isc_c94_covered}` (registers ISC-C94); full DoD gate GREEN. The visible circle-roster render (a circle's people column populates when it is the active room, the lobby's when the lobby is) is the attended felt-test (ISC-C94 left `[ ]`).
- 2026-06-25: **GUI unread-gated landing on the announcements/MOTD pane (#93).** On connect, decide whether to surface the announcements pane based on whether its content changed since the user last saw it — without any new server state. **Decision (design-of-record `docs/design/announcements-motd-admin.md` D5; open-question "single vs split hash" resolved to ① a single combined hash for the MVP):** the client computes ONE combined content hash of the *verified* `AnnouncementsView` (MOTD + announcements) and compares it to a per-relay last-seen marker stored in the profile's `Seeds`. The hash (`daemonseed_gui::state::combined_content_hash`) is a deterministic, **server-order-independent** SHA-384 (via `daemonseed_core::public_space::content_address`) over a length-prefixed, domain-separated encoding of the MOTD (empty when absent) and the posts sorted into canonical `(topic, sent_unix_ms, body)` order — so any content change (MOTD edit, post added/removed/edited, or a deletion) flips it while a relay re-ordering its served posts does not; it is a local marker only, never a wire artifact. Persistence reuses the `Seeds` additive directive scheme: a new `announce_seen: BTreeMap<String, String>` field, the `announce-seen <server_id> <hex_hash>` directive (both tokens whitespace-free → unambiguous two-token split; a malformed line is skipped, not fatal; absent directive = empty map, so older blobs parse unchanged), and `Seeds::{announce_seen, set_announce_seen}` (the setter guards line-breaks/whitespace like `add_mute`). The landing logic is the pure `landing_decision(stored, current)` → `Landing::{Announcements, Lobby}` (no stored hash OR mismatch → Announcements; equal → Lobby), kept out of the Slint/net layer so it is unit-tested. **Wiring (felt-test-gated):** the GUI net actor runs a connect-time public-space fetch after the republish loop and emits `NetEvent::PublicSpaceSnapshot { connect_time: true }`; the binary auto-opens active-tab 3 only when `connect_time` AND `landing_decision` says `Announcements` (a manual Refresh / poll / post-upload refresh carries `connect_time: false` and never relands). When the announcements pane is the active tab (whether the user opened it or the gate did), the binary persists the current hash via `GuiState::persist_announce_seen` → `Profile::persist_announce_seen` → `Seeds::set_announce_seen` + re-seal (idempotent; a no-op on the ephemeral no-profile path). **ISC impact:** new ISC-C93 (client-positive); registry TOTAL 174→175, pos 113→114, COVERED 105→106. **Wire impact:** none — client-derived from already-fetched content; no proto change, no new RPC (no SemVer impact). **Oracle:** `daemonseed_core::storage::seeds::tests::{announce_seen_round_trips_through_blob, absent_announce_seen_directive_parses_empty}` + `daemonseed_gui::state::tests::{landing_decision_covers_all_cases, viewing_updates_stored_hash_then_bypasses, combined_content_hash_is_stable_order_independent_and_change_sensitive}` + the `announcements_motd_isc_coverage::isc_c93_covered` registration; full DoD gate GREEN. The connect-time auto-land + the view-updates-stored-hash write-through are the attended felt-tests (ISC-C93 left `[ ]`).
- 2026-06-25: **Signer-gated MOTD/announcement composer signs/gates with the STABLE identity key (#92).** The composer must gate on, and sign with, the **persistent profile identity key** — `derive_identity_keys(seeds.mnemonic, Identity::Primary).signing`, the key behind the `name#hash` handle an operator whitelists — NOT the per-launch ephemeral connection-proof key the net actor uses for public-room provenance. The ephemeral key proves the connection but is unknown to any whitelist, so gating/signing with it would always fail self-determination and produce posts the relay rejects. **Decision (design-of-record `docs/design/announcements-motd-admin.md` D3):** the GUI/TUI derive the stable signing key ONCE on unlock/connect (`GuiState::stable_signing_key` / `App::stable_signing_key`, from the unlocked profile's mnemonic) and hand it to the net actor (`NetCommand::Connect { stable_signing_key }`) to hold for the session — never re-derived per keystroke (derivation runs ML-DSA keygen). The gating predicate `daemonseed_cli::public_space::composer_visible(local_pubkey, entries)` (fail-closed `local_key_is_whitelisted(..).unwrap_or(false)`) is the shared boolean both clients gate on, computed against the SAME published whitelist the snapshot re-verifies posts against, and carried to the UI on `PublicSpaceSnapshot { can_compose }`. Composing routes `NetCommand::SetMotd` / `UploadAnnouncement` → `sign_motd` / `sign_post` (held stable key) → `UploadMotd` (#89) / `UploadPost`, then re-fetches; the relay independently re-verifies the signature against the published whitelist before storing (ISC-S8), so a non-signer can never write even if the client affordance is forced. The MOTD composer surfaces the ISC-S9 plaintext rejection (`MotdAuthorError::NotPlaintext`) on the status line rather than dropping. Non-signers get a read-only pane; **no whitelist display** (D3 ③) and whitelist membership stays operator-only / out-of-band (ISC-S8 / ISC-A-S4). The TUI carries the `!Clone`/`!Debug` `SignKeypair` through its `Clone + Debug` `NetCommand` via a redacted `Arc`-backed `StableSigningKey` newtype (the GUI's `NetCommand` derives neither, so it holds the key directly). **ISC impact:** new ISC-C92 (client-positive); registry TOTAL 173→174, pos 112→113, COVERED 104→105. **Wire impact:** none — composes the existing `UploadPost`/`UploadMotd` RPCs; no proto change (no SemVer impact). **Oracle:** `daemonseed-cli::public_space::tests::{composer_visible_true_on_list, composer_visible_false_off_list, composer_visible_false_on_empty_whitelist, composer_visible_fails_closed_on_malformed_whitelist}` + the `announcements_motd_isc_coverage::{client_signer_composer_gating, isc_c92_covered}` registration; full DoD gate GREEN. The GUI composer render + the TUI composer (compose → sign → upload → refresh) are the attended felt-tests (ISC-C92 left `[ ]`).
- 2026-06-25: **GUI announcement + MOTD display panes (#91).** The TUI had announcement/MOTD display panes; the GUI fetched no public-space data. **Decision (design-of-record `docs/design/announcements-motd-admin.md` D4):** port the TUI panes to the GUI as a **display-only** surface (the signer composer is #92; the unread-gated auto-landing is #93). The testable data-prep + client re-verification lives in a plain function `daemonseed_gui::state::build_announcements_view`, separate from the Slint rendering, so it carries unit-test/ISC coverage; the Slint pane rendering is felt-test-gated (ISC-C91 left `[ ]`). The GUI net actor (`handle_refresh_public_space`, a new `NetCommand::RefreshPublicSpace` → `NetEvent::PublicSpaceSnapshot`) mirrors the TUI's fetch (`GetSignerWhitelist` + `GetMotd` + `ListPosts`) and calls the data-prep, which **trusts nothing the relay asserts** (ISC-A-S3): posts are re-verified with `verify_served_post` and **dropped** on failure; the MOTD is re-verified with `verify_served_motd`. Two whitelists are built from the one published list — the **post** whitelist is the strict signer set (`whitelist_from_wire(.., None)`, ISC-S8 authorship), the **MOTD** whitelist additionally carries the connected relay's `server_pubkey` (`Some(..)`, ISC-26) since a MOTD may be server-signed; this required capturing `outcome.server_pubkey` on the GUI actor's connect (cleared on disconnect). The MOTD renders verbatim via `render_motd` and the MOTD area hides when unset (ISC-S9 parity); each post renders topic/body/timestamp via `post_render_fields` (the timestamp formatted to a short UTC caption by a dependency-free `format_unix_ms`). The pane is reachable via the Ctrl-K command palette (action index 5) and a header tab (active-tab 3); a fetch fires on pane-open and on the existing ~3s on-tab liveness poll. **ISC impact:** new ISC-C91 (client-positive); registry TOTAL 172→173, pos 111→112, COVERED 103→104. **Wire impact:** none — composes existing RPCs; no proto change (no SemVer impact). **Oracle:** `daemonseed_gui::state::tests::{build_announcements_view_verifies_motd_and_drops_unverifiable_post, build_announcements_view_hides_unverifiable_motd}` (data-prep + re-verify) + `daemonseed_gui::tests::format_unix_ms_renders_utc_caption` + the `announcements_motd_isc_coverage::isc_c91_covered` registration; full DoD gate GREEN. The visual pane render (Ctrl-K → pane, MOTD verbatim, posts list, empty states) is the attended felt-test.

- 2026-06-25: **Client signer authoring + self-determination (#90).** With #89 adding the server-side `UploadMotd` ingest, the client still had no path to *author* signed public-space content — only the read/verify half (`render_motd`, `post_render_fields`, `verify_served_post`, `verify_served_motd`, `whitelist_from_wire`) existed. **Decision (design-of-record `docs/design/announcements-motd-admin.md`):** add the symmetric authoring half **in `daemonseed_cli::public_space`** — the shared client-side module that both `daemonseed-tui` and `daemonseed-gui` already import — so #91/#92's panes/composer inherit it with no new wiring. It is deliberately **NOT** in `daemonseed-core`: core's `public_space` is proto-agnostic, while these helpers build/encode/sign the wire `PostPayload`/`MotdPayload`/`PostDeletePayload` types, so the proto-aware cli module is the right home (mirroring the read helpers that already live there). `sign_post`/`sign_motd`/`sign_post_delete` each assemble a `SignedArtifact` via a private `signed_artifact` helper that signs the verbatim `signed_payload` with the local identity key and carries the full pubkey (D-M6-7, never re-encoding the payload), so the result re-verifies against the published signer whitelist. `sign_motd` enforces the ISC-S9 plaintext rule **before** signing via the shared `daemonseed_core::public_space::motd_text_is_valid` — the same definition the server ingest uses (#89). `local_key_is_whitelisted` is the cryptographic self-determination predicate (`whitelist_from_wire` + `Whitelist::authorizes`) that gates the #92 composer; whitelist *membership* stays operator-only / out-of-band (ISC-S8 / ISC-A-S4) — this only reads the published list. **Error shape:** `MotdAuthorError { NotPlaintext, Sign(SignatureError) }` (Display + Error) for the composer; `WhitelistConvertError` gained a `Module(oxicrypt_module::Error)` variant so `local_key_is_whitelisted` can surface a failed hash-prefix authorization (the enum's `Clone/PartialEq/Eq` derives hold — `oxicrypt_module::Error` is `Copy`). **ISC impact:** new ISC-C89 (authoring) + ISC-C90 (self-determination), both client-positive; registry TOTAL 170→172, pos 109→111, COVERED 101→103. **Wire impact:** none — these compose existing wire types; no proto change (no SemVer impact). **Oracle:** `daemonseed-cli::public_space::tests::{sign_post_round_trips_through_verify_served_post, sign_post_rejected_by_foreign_whitelist, sign_motd_round_trips_through_verify_served_motd, sign_motd_rejects_non_plaintext, sign_post_delete_round_trips_and_verifies, local_key_is_whitelisted_true_on_list, local_key_is_whitelisted_false_off_list, local_key_is_whitelisted_matches_handle_form_entry}` + the `announcements_motd_isc_coverage` integration tests `{client_signer_authoring_round_trips, client_signer_self_determination}` registering ISC-C89/C90; full DoD gate GREEN. **Scope:** client authoring helpers + self-determination predicate only; the GUI/TUI composer UI is #92 and the display panes are #91.
- 2026-06-25: **In-band MOTD upload via `UploadMotd` (#89).** MOTD was file-drop-only (`load_motd` at startup + `GetMotd` to read); posts could be uploaded in-band but the MOTD could not, so the announcements/MOTD admin GUI (the #88 epic) had no client path to set it. **Decision (design-of-record `docs/design/announcements-motd-admin.md`, D2):** add a `UploadMotd` RPC to `PublicSpace`, parity with `UploadPost` and single-slot-replace per ISC-S9. **Trust model = the post/signer whitelist (ISC-S8), not a new MOTD whitelist** — `UploadMotd` reuses the exact `verify_artifact(..., post_whitelist)` path `UploadPost` uses, so it adds in-client convenience without widening the trust boundary; the server-wide-key MOTD stays the file-drop-only path. A whitelist-signed MOTD survives restart re-verification because `load_motd`'s MOTD whitelist ⊇ the post whitelist (ISC-26). **Plaintext enforcement is a SHARED core helper** `daemonseed_core::public_space::motd_text_is_valid` (rejects any control char → single-line, no embedded control sequences) so server-ingest and the future client composer enforce one definition of the ISC-S9 rule. Persistence mirrors `upload_post`: the artifact is written verbatim to the signer-writable MOTD slot (ISC-A-S8), never re-encoding the inner payload (D-M6-7). **ISC impact:** new ISC-S31; registry TOTAL 169→170. **Wire impact:** additive-only proto (`UploadMotd` RPC + `UploadMotdRequest`/`UploadMotdResponse`), ISC-S14 — MINOR. **Oracle:** `daemonseed-server::public_space::tests::{upload_motd_replaces_slot, upload_motd_rejects_unknown_signer, upload_motd_rejects_bad_signature, upload_motd_rejects_non_plaintext, upload_motd_storage_disabled}` + `daemonseed-core::public_space::tests::motd_text_validity_accepts_plaintext_rejects_controls` + the `announcements_motd_isc_coverage` integration test + `upload_motd_survives_reload` (round-trips the persisted artifact through a fresh `load`, locking the superset-by-construction guarantee); full DoD gate GREEN. **Hardening (commit-boundary advisor):** the plaintext rule rejects not only C0/C1 controls but also the Unicode line/paragraph separators (U+2028/U+2029) and bidi/format controls (U+200E/F, U+061C, U+202A–E, U+2066–9) — `char::is_control()` alone would leave the single-line + direction-spoofing invariant bypassable; the file write + RAM update are held under one slot lock so concurrent uploads serialize and disk/RAM never disagree. **Secondary semantics (intended):** single-slot is shared with the file-drop path (latest-validated-wins; a whitelist signer's in-band MOTD and an operator file-drop MOTD overwrite the same slot); empty text is a valid (if inert) MOTD, not a "clear" operation (no DeleteMotd in scope); length is unbounded, at parity with `UploadPost` (transport frame/rate limits, ISC-S17, bound it). **Scope:** server ingest + wire only; client authoring/composer + GUI panes are the other #88 issues.
- 2026-06-25: **Contain long pasted/long-message content within the window (#86 felt-test regression).** Felt-testing #86 surfaced a blowout: pasting a long paragraph into the composer (or a long message in the transcript) ballooned the centre column past the window — the fixed 240px roster was pushed off-screen and the composer/bubbles overflowed the right edge. **Root cause:** the centre `Rectangle` had no `horizontal-stretch`, so the main `HorizontalLayout` sized it to its content's preferred width; a `single-line` `TextInput` reports its *full text* as its minimum width, so a long draft set a min-width the column could not shrink below; the bubbles' `max-width: root.width * 0.72` then inherited the ballooned width. **Decision:** (1) give the centre column `horizontal-stretch: 1` + `clip: true` so it takes the space left by the fixed rail + roster and never grows to content; (2) replace the composer input box's content-sizing `HorizontalLayout`s with explicit-positioned children whose `width` binds to the box (`parent.width - 24px`), making the single-line field a fixed viewport the text scrolls within rather than a width the field dictates, and `clip: true` on the box. With the centre column bounded, the bubbles' `root.width` is bounded and they wrap within the pane as intended. **Verification:** reproduced the blowout headlessly (offscreen render with a long draft + long lobby messages + roster → roster dropped, content overflowed), applied the fix, re-rendered identically → roster restored, bubbles wrap within ~72%, composer clips/scrolls the long draft; normal short-message render unchanged; full DoD gate GREEN. Layout-only `app.slint` change — no wire/proto/LAMA surface changed.
- 2026-06-25: **GUI chat bubbles wrap and size to content (#86), and the auto-scroll math is rebased onto real content height.** `MsgBubble` had a fixed `height: 44px` and no text `wrap`, so long/multi-line messages clipped; the chat `Flickable` and the #84 `chat-at-bottom` test both measured content via a fixed 52px-per-message stride. **Decision:** remove the fixed bubble height, set the message `Text` to `wrap: word-wrap`, and cap the bubble at `max-width: root.width * 0.72` so a long line wraps and the bubble grows to fit. Because bubbles are now variable-height, the per-message stride no longer measures the transcript: bind the Flickable `viewport-height` to the inner list's `preferred-height`, and mirror that into a new root `chat-content-h` (pushed up via the Flickable's `init` + `changed content-h` callbacks, since root can't see into the `if active-tab==0` conditional). `chat-at-bottom` now reads `chat-content-h` instead of the 52px formula — superseding that clause of the #84 entry below. The #84 pin mechanism is unchanged and unaffected: `STICK_BOTTOM` is a Slint-clamped sentinel, so it pins to the true bottom regardless of how content height is computed. **Verification:** full DoD gate GREEN (fmt · clippy workspace + gui/desktop · `test --workspace` · check-proto · isc-coverage); offscreen render of a long incoming + long own message confirmed both wrap to multiple lines, grow to fit, and carry the correct #85 colors (incoming green, own dark). No new ISC (chat-readability polish tracked by #86 + CHANGELOG); `chat-content-h` is internal to `daemonseed-gui` — no wire/proto/LAMA surface changed.
- 2026-06-25: **GUI chat auto-scroll + bubble-color reversal (tester feedback, #84/#85).** A first alpha tester reported that the chat does not auto-scroll (new messages land off-screen) and that the green bubble reads better than the dark one. **#85 (cosmetic):** reverse the `MsgBubble` `mine ? … : …` color ternaries so other people's messages get the readable green bubble and own messages get the dark one (no behavior change). **#84 (auto-scroll):** scroll position is Rust-owned per-circle state (`CircleState.scroll_y`, written *last* in `apply_view` and clamped by Slint against content height), so the fix is Rust-driven, not a Slint-only binding. The Slint root exposes `chat-at-bottom` (computed from `scroll-y`, `messages.length`, and the always-present `chat-area` wrapper height — reusing the Flickable's 52px/32px viewport-height constants). On a message into the active room, Rust reads `chat-at-bottom` from the *live* view **before** the transcript grows; own sends always pin, an incoming message pins only if the reader was already at the bottom, otherwise the reader's live position is captured and restored (hold-position). "Pin to bottom" sets `scroll_y` to a large-negative sentinel (`STICK_BOTTOM = -1e7`) that Slint clamps to the true bottom — so the GUI never needs to know the visible height. **Decision (scope kept tight):** code limited to these two chat fixes; no new permanent ISC minted (punchlist polish tracked by the issues + CHANGELOG); the felt-test-bearing surface stays small. **Side benefit:** the hold-position path also fixes a latent jump where any incoming message restored a stale saved `scroll_y`, yanking a scrolled-up reader. **Verification:** full DoD gate GREEN (fmt · clippy workspace + gui/desktop · `test --workspace` · check-proto · isc-coverage) + two-window relay felt-test on this host. `STICK_BOTTOM`, `set_scroll`, and `chat-at-bottom` are internal to `daemonseed-gui` — no wire/proto/LAMA surface changed.
- 2026-06-24: **Surface the live Lobby roster in the GUI (#75).** ISC-C83 deferred "surfacing the roster" to #75/#77, and #74 wired only the TUI net actor (no single shared net layer). **Decision:** mirror the TUI heartbeat emit/ingest into the GUI net actor — a per-lobby `PresenceTracker` on `PublicRoom` rebuilt fresh per (re)join, a single self-scheduling `EmitHeartbeat` timer spawned once in `net_actor`, and `handle_apply_heartbeat` with the ISC-A-C41 freshness gate + own-pubkey self-filter — emitting a new `NetEvent::Roster` on a roster-changing apply (Appeared, or a Refresh that changes the displayed handle) AND on any reap that removed ≥1 member (so reap-to-empty pushes an empty roster), rendered as a persistent right-hand people column in the Lobby Chat view. **Placement (caraka):** right-hand column, over a header strip / reusing the details sheet (the latter hidden-by-default, failing the glance test). **Row content (caraka):** display name only; the `#12hex` fingerprint (ISC-C4 binding) reveals on hover via a computed Slint text (never an `if`-toggle — item-tree safety), keeping names short. **Keyed by pubkey** (advisor): identical display names render as two distinct rows. **Single-spawn timer** (advisor): spawned once at actor start, not per-join, so a reconnect/rejoin cannot double-emit. **ISC impact:** new ISC-C88 (roster surface) + ISC-A-C42 (no decorators / live-set-only / not persisted); registry TOTAL 167→169. **Scope:** lobby only (circle presence is #77); the GUI emits a bare beacon (empty `live_share_ids`) — GUI share-liveness reconcile stays #76. **Oracle:** `net::tests::presence_roster` (sealed beacon appears then reaps; two-identical-names→two-rows; reap-to-empty→empty push; same-handle-refresh no-op; reap-removes-nothing no-op; fingerprint matches `Handle::from_pubkey`) + full DoD gate GREEN; the two-window relay felt-test is attended. `NetEvent`/`NetCommand`/`RosterEntry` are internal to `daemonseed-gui` — no LAMA manifest surface changed.
- 2026-06-24: **refined: per-share index FILES — one redb per share, so a multi-share user gets cache hits on restart (#81).** The single-active design below still re-hashed every share on restart for a *multi-share* user: one shared `share-index.redb` plus `reconcile_into`'s prune-everything-not-under-this-root meant each share's publish evicted the others' cached chunk-addrs, so N shares thrashed each other (felt-test, 4-share XDG profile: all four re-hashed despite the cache being open — the 62s 1904-file share every launch). **Decision (caraka: "I choose A" + per-share files):** give each share its OWN redb file, `share-index-<12hex(canonical-root)>.redb` under the profile root (`daemonseed_core::storage::share_index::per_share_index_filename`, SHA-384 of the canonical path, std-hash fallback so two roots never collide). The GUI net actor holds a `HashMap<PathBuf, Arc<ShareIndex>>` opened create-if-absent on first publish of a root (`index_for_root`, off-thread, non-fatal → fresh-hash fallback), and `Connect` now carries the profile root DIR + key (the per-share filename is derived in the actor). The single-active reconcile-repoint dance is gone — a per-share file holds only one root's files, so its cache pass can never cross-prune another share. **No core change to the cache engine** (`cached_or_hash`/`reconcile_into` already take a path and are correct); no reconcile call on the publish path at all (a per-share index is self-correcting via `cached_or_hash`'s stat check). No new ISC; `Connect`/`ConnectPlan`/`index_home`/`share_indexes` are `daemonseed-gui`-internal — the one new public surface is the core `per_share_index_filename` fn (added to the core LAMA manifest). **Oracle:** new `net::tests::second_share_publish_does_not_evict_first_share_cache` (publish A → publish B in between → corrupt A at same size+mtime → republish A is a cache HIT; under single-active B's publish would have evicted A → re-hash → changed addr) + the existing `publish_reuses_persisted_cache_not_rehash` (now per-share) + core `indexer::tests::cached_or_hash_survives_drop_and_reopen` (drop+reopen persistence) + full `cargo xtask release-gate` GREEN. **Felt-test note for caraka:** the FIRST restart after this lands re-hashes each share ONCE (building its per-share file — the old shared db is a different filename); every restart after is cache-hits-only.
- 2026-06-24: **GUI publish reuses the persisted share index — no startup re-hash (#81).** The GUI publish path re-hashed a share from scratch on every publish and on the connect-time auto-republish (`ShareContent::index_dir`; "GUI keeps no redb share-index"), re-introducing the Demonsaw startup CPU-thrash on large shares that ISC-C21 / ISC-A-C7 exist to prevent — while the core persisted indexer (redb `cached_or_hash` / `reconcile_into`, M14) and the TUI already avoided it. **Decision:** thread the persistent-profile `IndexKey` + the `share-index.redb` path (the TUI's convention) into the GUI net actor via the `Connect` command (retained in `ConnectPlan` so reconnect reuses it, never cycling the redb lock), open the redb `ShareIndex` ONCE before the republish loop (`ensure_share_index` — off-thread, non-fatal → degrades to a fresh hash), and index publishes via `cached_or_hash` (single-active index, latest-publish-wins via `reconcile_into`) so an unchanged share is cache-hits-only. Hashing stays inside `spawn_blocking` (off the async loop, ISC-A-C7). **Scope:** the cache-reuse + encrypted-index half — advances ISC-C21 for the GUI publish path, satisfies A-C6 GUI-side (index opened under `IndexKey`), removes the A-C7 startup thrash for GUI publish; the fs-event live-watcher / mtime-rescan slice of ISC-C21 stays deferred (separate, not this fix), so the C21/A-C6/A-C7 boxes stay unchecked pending that slice. No new ISC; `Connect`/`ConnectPlan` are `daemonseed-gui`-internal — no wire/proto/LAMA change. **Oracle:** `net::tests::publish_reuses_persisted_cache_not_rehash` (real `IndexKey` via FirstStart→finalize; same-size + restored-mtime rewrite → cached chunk-addr unchanged = cache hit, not a re-hash) + full `cargo xtask release-gate` GREEN.
- 2026-06-23: **Presence replay-freshness gate — bound beacon replay (#78, partial).** An untrusted relay controls delivery and could replay a captured `MemberHeartbeat` to keep a departed member on the roster past the presence TTL (low severity — the payoff is a "worth a try" ping that then fails — but a real gap). **Decision:** add `daemonseed_core::presence::beacon_is_fresh(sent_unix_ms, now_unix_ms)` and gate it at the single ingest entry (`handle_apply_heartbeat`, before the `PresenceTracker::apply` AND the share-digest reconcile), dropping any beacon whose advisory timestamp is outside `[now − REPLAY_FRESHNESS_PAST(300s), now + REPLAY_FRESHNESS_FUTURE(60s)]`.
