This bounds replay to the window instead of indefinitely. **⚠️ FLAGGED FOR caraka (why this is `#78` partial, not `Closes`):** (1) the window VALUE is unratified — it is deliberately generous because daemonseed assumes NO shared time source, so the gate trades replay-tightness for tolerance of unsynchronised-clock skew; too tight and a skewed peer's legitimate beacons get dropped (presence breaks), too loose and replay is barely bounded. (2) A wall-clock freshness gate makes presence depend on loosely-synced clocks at all — the alternative (a nonce/sequence-number anti-replay that needs no clocks, and which would also defeat replay of a *recent* beacon, which this gate cannot) is the deeper design. Both are caraka's calls. **Scope:** wired in the live TUI ingest (the GUI heartbeat ingest is #75; it will inherit the same gate when wired). **ISC impact:** new ISC-A-C41. **Oracle:** `presence::tests::beacon_freshness_accepts_recent_rejects_replayed_or_future` (in-window accepted incl. edges; hour-old replay + beyond-skew-future rejected); full `cargo xtask release-gate` GREEN.
- 2026-06-23: **Fix the v0.30.0 send regression — graceful EOS re-subscribes, only a failed re-subscribe tears down (#80).** v0.30.0 (#72) made BOTH inbound reader exit arms collapse `Ok(None)` (graceful end-of-stream) and `Err` into one teardown that cleared the lobby + every circle + all serve tasks, so a single stream blip blocked all sends while the relay still showed connected. **Approach (Advisor-reviewed):** the brief's "distinguish `Ok(None)` from `Err`" split is factually wrong — a dead connection's GOAWAY also surfaces as `Ok(None)` (proven by the killable-relay test harness), so that split would have broken the #72 reconnect test. Instead, **resubscribe-then-fallback**: each stream-exit posts `ResubscribeRoom` / `ResubscribeCircle`, and the re-subscribe ATTEMPT is the active probe — it succeeds only while the authenticated connection is alive (the tonic channel rides one `take()`n stream, `AppSession::open`; tonic cannot transparently re-dial, so success genuinely proves the connection up — the Advisor's session-masking risk does not apply here), leaving other streams intact; a failed re-subscribe (or a flap) falls through to `handle_disconnected` (#72/#71 teardown + backoff reconnect). Also raised the shared `KEEPALIVE_TIMEOUT` 10s→30s (GUI+TUI+CLI via `AppSession::open`) so a slow PONG on a congested WAN no longer trips a spurious dead-connection — belt-and-braces, since the re-subscribe path recovers regardless of why a stream died (keepalive is now a perf knob, not a correctness dependency). A per-stream flap guard (`BurstGuard`: ≥5 rapid re-subscribes within 3s) escalates to teardown so a flapping link converges to backoff. The dead `NetCommand::Disconnected` command variant (no longer posted — readers post Resubscribe; teardown is invoked directly) was removed. **ISC impact:** ISC-C79 trigger revised (teardown now via failed re-subscribe, not direct stream-exit); new ISC-C87 (graceful-EOS re-subscribe keeps session) + ISC-A-C40 (anti: graceful EOS must not tear down). **Scope:** the name-a-share UI is unrelated; the real-WAN keepalive behavior is a felt-test (morning — idle ~60s then send in Lobby + a circle; capture any GOAWAY reason code). **Oracle:** `net::tests::graceful_eos_resubscribes_without_tearing_down_session` (live relay: ResubscribeRoom + ResubscribeCircle keep `connected` and the circle still round-trips) + `net::tests::resubscribe_on_dead_connection_tears_down` (killable relay: a failed re-subscribe surfaces `Disconnected` + `connected==false`) + the existing `disconnect_detected_then_reconnect_restores_circle` stays green (now flows EOS→Resubscribe→fail→teardown→reconnect); full `cargo xtask release-gate` GREEN. `NetEvent`/`NetCommand` are internal to `daemonseed-gui` — no LAMA manifest surface changed.
- 2026-06-23: **Unify share announcements onto the presence heartbeat (#76).** Share discovery ran a jittered reconcile-timer roll-call (every client → every sharer re-announces all shares ~every 30 s) for ongoing liveness — redundant churn once a heartbeat already proves a sharer is alive. **Decision:** the `MemberHeartbeat` (ISC-C83) carries a `live_share_ids` digest (provenance-bound); a receiver `ShareCatalog::reconcile_sharer`s off it (refresh in-digest shares, drop a sharer's shares absent from it) and `prune_sharer`s on heartbeat lapse (presence reap now returns the reaped members). The periodic reconcile-timer roll-call is removed — a full `ShareAnnouncement` fires only on change (publish/unpublish) plus the startup + manual-refresh roll-calls (late-join + on-demand self-heal preserved). The digest never fabricates a share (it lacks name/rendezvous); a digest id the catalog lacks is filled by the next on-change announcement or a roll-call. **ISC impact:** new ISC-C86; ISC-S30 two-speed-liveness clause revised in place. **Oracle:** `heartbeat::tests::{digest_round_trips, tampered_digest_rejected}` + `share_catalog::tests::{reconcile_refreshes_in_digest_and_removes_absent, reconcile_only_touches_the_named_sharer, prune_sharer_drops_all_of_one_sharer}` + `presence::tests` reap-returns-members; full `cargo xtask release-gate` GREEN. **Scope:** lobby/public shares (the digest + reconcile are lobby-catalog-scoped; circle beacons carry an empty digest — no circle-share concept yet).
- 2026-06-23: **Member-plane connected-presence heartbeat — core primitive (#74).** Members had no way to see who is online before reaching out (the relay-plane refcount presence of ISC-S20 is identity-free by design). **Decision:** add a tier-split, AES-256-GCM-sealed, ML-DSA-87-self-signed `MemberHeartbeat` (`daemonseed_core::heartbeat`) posted into each subscribed room over the existing `CircleOfTrust.Subscribe` stream on a jittered ~10–15 s interval, with a per-room `PresenceTracker` (`daemonseed_core::presence`) that reaps a member after a multi-miss TTL (interval × miss-count, measured from monotonic receive time, bias-to-forgiveness). The frame is wire-shape-identical to other sealed frames (distinct AAD), so the relay forwards it blind. **ISC impact:** new ISC-C83/C84/C85 + ISC-A-C38/A-C39 + ISC-A-S23. **Oracle:** `heartbeat::tests` (both-tier seal/open round-trip, forged-room + wrong-key + tamper + truncation rejections, handle-never-cleartext, circle-not-public) + `presence::tests` (appear/refresh/stale, multi-miss reap with synthetic clock, ≤2-miss forgiveness, jitter band/variation, fresh-tracker-empty); full `cargo xtask release-gate` GREEN.
  - **Scope cut — net wiring is TUI-only; GUI emit/ingest rides #75.** There is no single shared net layer (emit/ingest loops are per-frontend: tui `net.rs`, gui net actor, cli `session.rs`), so #74 wires the TUI net actor (the complete, headless-testable path) and the GUI net actor does NOT yet emit or ingest heartbeats. This is a deliberate boundary, not an oversight: #75 (lobby roster) owns wiring the GUI emit+ingest alongside its rendering, since they felt-test together. Recorded on #75 so the asymmetry is documented, not discovered.
  - **Deferred (recorded, not omitted) — replay-freshness window.** `PresenceTracker` keys liveness on receiver-local monotonic time and treats a beacon with an equal-or-newer `sent_unix_ms` as a refresh, so an untrusted relay that *replays* a captured genuine heartbeat can keep a departed member on the roster past the TTL. This is **bounded** — the relay already controls all delivery, and a falsely-present member only costs a sender a "worth a try" ping that fails (live-only; the design ethos in `docs/design/presence-superstructure.md`). A `sent_unix_ms` freshness window (reject beacons older than ~a few TTLs against the receiver wall-clock, with skew tolerance) would cap it, but it introduces a wall-clock dependency + a tunable window that belongs with the cadence/TTL tuning the design doc lists as open. Deferred to the presence tuning pass / #76; flagged for caraka.
- 2026-06-23: **Rename identity — re-seal the display name on the at-rest blob (#66, core half).** There was no way to change an identity's display name after first-start, and a profile created nameless before #65 stayed nameless. **Decision:** `Profile::rename` validates the name (`is_valid_display_name`), calls `Seeds::set_display_name`, updates the live `display_handle`, and re-seals via the existing M13 write-through (`SealingKey::seal` → `write_seeds_blob`) — the same path `persist_circle` uses — so the new name lands on the at-rest blob and persists across unlock; `GuiState::rename_identity` delegates to it. The cryptographic identity (the handle hash) is unchanged — rename only changes the presented name. **Scope (night):** the core re-seal + recovery of nameless pre-#65 profiles; the **Ctrl-K command-palette entry** and the **live-wire-handle update** (the net actor's `my_handle` / `name#hash` question + self-@mention + share-ownership) are **felt-deferred** open questions → bare `#66`. **ISC impact:** new ISC-C82. **Oracle:** `state::tests::rename_identity_persists_the_new_name_across_reload` + `…_names_a_nameless_pre_fix_profile` (enroll → rename → reload the blob from disk → the new name persists; invalid name rejected).
- 2026-06-23: **Single-instance guard on the resolved profile root (#60).** A second non-portable client resolving the same XDG profile root opened a second window unguarded, risking concurrent open of the single-writer storage layer (redb + seeds + CAS) and on-disk corruption. **Decision:** guard the **resolved root** (not the binary), so `--portable` instances in different roots stay independent. `route_startup` takes an advisory PID lockfile (`daemonseed.lock`) in the root via `create_new` (`O_EXCL`); a live same-root holder (PID alive per `/proc` on Linux) exits the process — **refuse-to-start is the safe minimum; focus-existing needs cross-process IPC and is out of scope**. A stale lockfile from a crashed holder is reclaimed by the PID-liveness check; the held `InstanceLock` removes the file on clean exit (Drop). A lockfile I/O hiccup degrades to unguarded rather than refusing a legitimate launch. Dep-free (std only). **ISC impact:** new ISC-C81. **Oracle:** `single_instance::tests` (same-root second acquire refused; stale dead-PID lock reclaimed; Drop releases; different roots allowed concurrently). The real second-GUI-launch refusal is a felt-test (morning).
- 2026-06-22: **GUI connection resilience — drop detection + auto-reconnect (#72, #71).** The GUI network actor's inbound subscribe readers exited silently on `Ok(None) | Err(_)`, so a dropped or half-open connection (e.g. a VPN namespace swap black-holing the TCP connection) left the UI showing "connected" over a dead session forever, with no recovery. **Decision:** (1) the shared `AppSession` tonic channel sets h2 keepalive (`http2_keep_alive_interval` 15s + `keep_alive_timeout` 10s + `keep_alive_while_idle`), so a half-open socket surfaces as a stream error within a bounded window instead of hanging — this also benefits the TUI/CLI, which share `AppSession::open`. (2) Both GUI inbound readers, on stream exit, post an internal `Disconnected` command to the actor; the actor clears its live state (session/server-id/identity/room/circles/served-shares) so no half-open session is reused, emits `NetEvent::Disconnected`, and (3) if a prior successful connect plan exists, arms a capped-exponential-backoff timer (2s base, 30s cap) that re-issues the connect carrying the persisted handle + `rejoin_circles` + `republish_roots`, restoring circles and shares like a fresh launch. Teardown is idempotent (one drop, one teardown) so sibling readers of one dead connection collapse. **ISC impact:** new ISC-C79 (drop detection + stale-state teardown) and ISC-C80 (capped-backoff auto-reconnect-restores-state). The keepalive (ISC-4 mechanical) is Read-confirmed; the half-open-socket recovery is a VPN-toggle felt-test deferred to morning. **Oracle:** `net::tests::disconnect_detected_then_reconnect_restores_circle` — an in-process relay test severs A's transport (a killable IO wrapper modelling the dead socket), asserts `Disconnected` fires + `connected == false`, then drives reconnect and asserts the circle re-subscribes and a post-reconnect send/receive round-trips. `NetEvent`/`NetCommand` are internal to `daemonseed-gui` — no LAMA manifest surface changed.
- 2026-06-21: **Unified share model — shares move to relay-blind in-band discovery; the relay holds no share directory.** Shares were the lone exception to "everything sealed, relay reads nothing": public-share *content* rode `CotFrame.payload` cleartext, and discovery used a relay-held RAM registry (`SharePublishRegistry`) queried by a `ListPublicShares` RPC — the one role in which the relay was not a pure blind forwarder. Design-of-record: `docs/design/unified-share-model.md`. **Decision (caraka-ratified):** adopt the `public_room` model wholesale for shares so public and circle shares are byte-identical except which key seals them and which room they announce into. Four sub-calls: **(1) Discovery is in-band.** A sharer posts a sealed, ML-DSA-self-signed `ShareAnnouncement` into the room (lobby for public, circle for private) over the same `CircleOfTrust.Subscribe` stream chat uses; discovery = listening to the stream. The relay holds no share state (extends ISC-A-S2 with a new "no share directory" bar; ISC-A-S1). A `ShareCatalog` folds announcements and ages them out by a local receive-time TTL — two-speed liveness: live publish/withdraw broadcasts (fast push) + a jittered reconcile-timer roll-call (slow self-heal) + prune-on-fetch-fail. **(2) Roll-call is a DISTINCT sealed kind, not implicit-on-subscribe** (caraka-ratified): the relay emits no subscribe events, so fast late-join must be pull-based — a joining/refreshing client posts a sealed `ShareRollCall` and live sharers re-announce. One late-join hook (startup, the Refresh action, and the reconcile timer all post a roll-call). **(3) `share_id` minting moves client-side** (128-bit OS CSPRNG, `share_announce::mint_share_id`) — the relay no longer assigns it, which makes it relay-portable (a future cross-relay path) and removes the last relay-held share state. This **revises ISC-S21's "server-assigned"**; the unpredictable-128-bit-id intent is unchanged. **(4) Content is sealed; key classes are non-substitutable.** A public-share `ShareFrame` is AES-256-GCM-sealed under the public room key (server-*readable* via that public key, never wire-cleartext — aligns shares with ISC-S22/A-S16), a circle share under `cot_key`. The seal key types (`PublicRoomKey` / `CircleKey`) are non-substitutable at the type level (wrong tier = compile error), so uniform sealing can never silently seal a circle under the public key. This **revises ISC-C19's "server-visible listing"** stance: the listing is now a sealed announcement (server-readable, not wire-cleartext). **Honest labeling:** sealing public content buys *structural indistinguishability* (closes the public-vs-circle traffic-shape leak), not confidentiality — the UI still labels public shares "anyone can read." **Supersedes the 2026-06-13 publish-persistence decision's "discovery rides `ListPublicShares`" claim** (below): discovery now rides in-band announcements, not the relay registry; rotating per-publish ids remain (the more-private behavior), now minted client-side. **Shipped:** the engine + TUI (#52) and the GUI (#53) — both clients discover in-band, verified by `share_discovery_e2e` (announce/withdraw/roll-call through a real in-process relay, ciphertext-on-wire, AAD domain separation). **Landed (#51):** the relay share registry (`SharePublishRegistry`), the `PublishShare` / `UnpublishShare` / `ListPublicShares` RPCs, the `PublicShareListing` message, and the CLI's registry-backed share subcommands are removed; clients render the in-band `ShareCatalog` through a client-side `ShareListing`. Felt-confirmed on real desktops (publish → discover → fetch → play). **ISC impact (landed):** new ISC-S30 (in-band discovery), ISC-A-S22 (relay holds no share directory; public-share content never wire-cleartext), ISC-C77 (key-class non-substitutability); ISC-S21 / ISC-C19 revised in place. Federation note: announcements carry `share_id` not `server_id`, so they are already relay-agnostic — a future cross-relay path needs no message change; the privacy-preserving shape is client-side multi-relay fan-out, not relay-to-relay gossip.
- 2026-06-18: **GUI first-start — passphrase confirm re-entry, so a typo can't silently lock out a new identity.** Branch `fix/gui-overlay-polish` (felt-test finding, caraka), cherry-picked onto the unified-share-model branch for v0.29.0. First-start step 0 took the passphrase ONCE with no confirmation; with no recovery flow built yet (ISC-C51 Unlock is the only door), an unnoticed typo would seal an identity under a passphrase the user can never reproduce — permanent lockout. Added a second masked "re-enter it to confirm" field (`fs-passphrase-confirm`, two-way) under the passphrase field; `on_fs_passphrase_next` now compares `pass != confirm` and, on a mismatch, sets `auth-error` ("Those don't match — type the same passphrase in both boxes.") and returns BEFORE `FirstStart::initialize` — no sealing, no advance. Enter on the passphrase field now focuses the confirm field; Enter on confirm submits. Verified behaviorally headless: the offscreen `--first-start 0` path seeds a deliberately mismatched confirm + drives the real `fs-passphrase-next` callback, and the PNG (Read) shows both masked boxes, the red mismatch error, and the wizard **still on step 0** (no advance). Catches inconsistent typos (the standard set-a-password guarantee); a consistently-repeated typo is not caught — a future reveal/show-toggle would also address "is this what I planned" but is a separate security-UX call (deferred). No proto / library-API change; gate green (fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, test, check-proto), coverage steady 100/149. **Candidate ISC flagged for caraka:** "first-start must confirm the passphrase before sealing" is a real data-integrity property and may deserve its own `ISC-C*` — not added unilaterally to the contract.
- 2026-06-18: **GUI first-start felt-test round 2b — fix the type-back overflow regression + Enter on the "I've saved it" step.** (1) **Regression fix (self-introduced):** the earlier #4 change flipped `fs_confirm_input` to `single-line: true`, which removed wrapping — so a long paste (e.g. the full recovery phrase into the 3-word type-back box) ran one line straight past the box, the card, and out to the window edge (Slint single-line inlines `\n`→space, `i-slint-core text.rs:1763`; no clip on the container). Fixed by adding `clip: true` to the container Rectangle — and to the **other three** single-line auth-input containers (unlock, fs-passphrase, fs-name) since they share the identical latent overflow. Verified headless: `--first-start 2` seeded with an over-long value now clips at the box edge (PNG Read). (2) **Enter advances the "I've saved it" recovery-phrase step (step 1):** that step has no input field to carry an `accepted`, so nothing was focused and Enter did nothing (mouse-only). Added a `Key.Return` arm to the global key-host `FocusScope` gated on `screen=="first-start" && fs-step==1` → `fs-saved-next()` (the keypress bubbles up to the key-host since no field holds focus). Live Enter is the felt-test. **Captured but NOT fixed (cause unidentified):** a third find — typing a trailing space in the type-back field, focusing away, then back, drops the trailing space (words run together). There is NO trailing-whitespace trim in Slint's `TextInput` source, so it is not a single-line side effect; cause is elsewhere (Slint focus/selection quirk or a `fs-confirm` re-set) and needs a live reproduction before any fix — logged to the punchlist, will not guess.
- 2026-06-18: **GUI global font pass — vendor DejaVu Sans as the software-renderer default, retiring the missing-glyph debt (invisible password mask #5 + blank disclosure carets).** Root cause, confirmed at Slint-source level: the software renderer embeds glyph *textures* at build time (`EmbedForSoftwareRenderer` → `EmbedTextures`) for a fixed coverage set = `characters_seen` (the literal chars in the `.slint` + a default set of a–z/A–Z/0–9/punctuation/`●`/`…`, per `i-slint-compiler/passes/embed_glyphs.rs:53`). The password mask character is `●` U+25CF (`i-slint-core/items/text.rs:1364`, `map_or('●', …)`) — already *in* the default coverage, but the previously-bundled default font lacked that geometric-shape glyph, so the mask rendered blank (the same gap noted for the ▶/▾ carets). **Fix:** vendor `DejaVuSans.ttf` (unmodified) at `crates/daemonseed-gui/ui/fonts/`, `import "fonts/DejaVuSans.ttf"` it (populates `custom_fonts`, registered into the compiler font collection), and set `AppWindow.default-font-family: "DejaVu Sans"` so the embedded glyphs come from DejaVu — which has full coverage of `●`/▶/▾/`…`/em-dash (verified via fontTools cmap). DejaVu is reproducible (vendored, not host-dependent) and its glyph set is what makes the whole UI's coverage complete. **Chevron debt retired in the same pass:** the disclosure caret switched from the ASCII placeholder `v`/`>` (`ShareTreeRow`) to the proper chevrons `▾`/`▶` — these are now source-literal so `embed_glyphs` embeds them from DejaVu. **Verified headless (3 PNGs Read):** `--unlock` (seeded passphrase) now shows a row of ● bullets (was blank); `--show-shares` shows ▾/▶ carets (was `v`/`>`); `--first-start 0` confirms general text + em-dash render clean with no layout breakage. An offscreen `--unlock` passphrase seed was added (mirrors `SAMPLE_MNEMONIC`) so the mask render is headless-regression-checkable. **License:** Bitstream Vera (DejaVu) — permissive, AGPL-compatible, allows redistribution/embedding/bundling-as-part-of-a-larger-package; vendored unmodified (no rename trigger) with `fonts/DejaVuSans-LICENSE.txt` co-located per the "include this notice in all copies" term.
- 2026-06-18: **GUI auth-input felt-fixes (round 2): first-start type-back submits on Enter + unlock clears the passphrase on a wrong attempt.** Two felt-test finds from the 2026-06-17 batch. (1) `fs_confirm_input` (the ISC-C34 3-word type-back field) was `single-line: false` with no `accepted` handler, so Enter inserted a newline instead of confirming — the user had to click. Now `single-line: true` + `accepted => { root.fs-confirm-next(); }`, matching `fs_pass_input`/`fs_name_input` which already submit on Enter; `--first-start 2` PNG re-verified (layout intact). (2) The unlock handler left the (masked) typed text in place on `BlobError::AuthenticationFailed`, stranding the cursor at the end of invisible text — it now `set_unlock_passphrase("")` before refocus so the user retypes from empty. The first-start passphrase path is deliberately NOT cleared on its "too weak" error (the user is refining, not retrying — keeping the text is correct UX). **Two adjacent punchlist items resolved without code:** (a) the "auth-field autofocus on first-start step changes" item (carried in the maintainer-held manifest) is **already shipped** — `41a55c3` (DS3) wired `defer_set_fs_step → invoke_focus_auth` and `focus-auth` focuses every entry step (0/2/3) + unlock on arrival; the manifest note was stale. (b) The "passphrase field renders invisibly" felt-test (#5) was **reproduced + root-caused** but NOT fixed here: the offscreen `--unlock` PNG shows a seeded passphrase rendering blank because the `EmbedForSoftwareRenderer` bundled font lacks the mask glyph U+2022 (•) — the same missing-glyph class already documented for the blank chevrons (▶/▾) at `app.slint:146`. Fix is a font-embedding change (extend the embedded glyph coverage / vendor a glyph-complete face); scoped separately because "mask-glyph-only vs global font pass (also fixes chevrons, a brand call)" is caraka's fork.
- 2026-06-17: **C34 3-word type-back ratified as the SOLE skip-backup confirmation — ISC-C47 amended, the C33 secondary opt-in dropped.** caraka ratified that the 3-word random type-back (ISC-C34) standing alone is the accepted skip-backup gate at first-start; the full 24-word re-type (ISC-C33) is **not** required as a secondary opt-in. This resolves the "OPEN vs ISC-C47" flag raised in the DS4 commit (`8926f91`) entry below: the GUI's lack of a C33 secondary path is now **by design**, not a gap. ISC-C47 text amended in place (ID stable, no renumber). ISC-C33 itself (the recovery-FILE round-trip) is untouched and still required for that path.
- 2026-06-16: **GUI publish-persistence — published shares survive restart + auto-republish on Unlock (M16 wired into the GUI profile).** Mirrors the GUI's silent circle-rejoin. `Profile` gains `persist_published`/`unpersist_published`/`published` (write-through to the seeds blob via `Seeds::add_published`/`remove_published`, exactly like `persist_circle`). The persistence key is the share's **directory path** (`Seeds::published: Vec<String>`, hex-encoded so any path survives) — enough to re-serve. To bridge the share_id↔dir gap: `NetEvent::PublishStarted` now carries the `root` dir (the actor always knows it — it indexes the publish command's dir), so the GUI persists on confirmed publish and `GuiState::MyShare` carries the `root` (mirroring the TUI's `PublishedShare{root,share_id}`) for Unpublish to forget. Auto-republish: `NetCommand::Connect` gains `republish_roots` (read from `profile.published()` by `connect_now`); the net actor re-publishes each after the lobby auto-join, exactly like `rejoin_circles`, using the dir basename as the name + the presented handle. **`PublishStopped` does NOT unpersist** (it also fires on session-end/reap — those must survive to republish); only the explicit user Unpublish forgets. Verified: an enroll→persist→reload-blob→`persisted_published()` round-trip test (the kept root survives, the unpersisted one is gone) + the `MyShare.root`/`share_root`/`remove_my_share` unit assertions. **Internal types only** (`NetCommand`/`NetEvent` are the internal actor surface — the crate has no published library API; `check-proto` unaffected — these are not the gRPC proto).
- 2026-06-16: **GUI first-start backup-verify switched from the C33 full-phrase round-trip to the C34 3-word type-back (ISC-C47 default).** The round-6 GUI (entry below) used the streamlined C33 re-type; ISC-C47 makes the **C34** 3-word random type-back the default confirmation. The GUI confirm step now issues `Sealed::issue_type_back_challenge(OsRng)` on entry (3 cryptographically-chosen word positions, `daemonseed_core::handle::display_name::OsRng`), prompts "Type words #X, #Y and #Z of your recovery phrase.", and on submit **pre-checks** the answers against the held mnemonic + challenge positions (case-insensitive, trimmed) before invoking the consuming `verify_type_back` — same destroy-proofing as the old `verify_round_trip` pre-check (a typo must never consume the Sealed state, which would regenerate a different mnemonic). A wrong answer **re-issues a fresh challenge** (the core's per-attempt contract) and keeps the Sealed state. Mirrors `daemonseed-tui::screens::first_start::on_verify_type_back`. The C33 live-match indicator + the `fs-confirm-edited`/`fs-confirm-match` Slint surface + the GUI `phrases_match` replica are removed. **OPEN vs ISC-C47:** C47 also keeps the C33 full re-type "available as an explicit secondary opt-in" — this commit makes C34 the default but does **not** yet add a C33 secondary path, so **ISC-C47 stays `[ ]`** (C29 step-4 / C33 / C34 / A-C13 remain open). New GUI unit tests cover the pre-check (correct / case-insensitive+trim / wrong-word / wrong-count) + the prompt formatting; `--first-start 2` PNG Read-verified.

- 2026-06-16: **Windowed GUI startup panic fixed — `rfd`'s `tokio` feature poisoned Slint's zbus.** Adding `rfd` for the Shares-tab folder picker (commit 2) pulls `ashpd` → `zbus` with its `tokio` default; Cargo feature-unification applied that to Slint's winit-backend `zbus` too. Slint's `spawn_xdg_settings_watcher` opens a zbus connection on the **main** thread, whose tokio-executor `Transport::connect` calls `spawn_blocking` → `Handle::current()` → **panics ("there is no reactor running")** because the main thread had no tokio runtime → the windowed app died at event-loop startup. Latent since commit 2; never caught (commit 2 was never windowed-smoke-tested — the portal wasn't up). **Couldn't move zbus to async-io:** rfd pulls ashpd *without* `default-features = false`, so ashpd's `tokio` default is unconditional under unification. **Fix:** enter a multi-thread tokio runtime on the main thread for the event loop's lifetime (`rt.enter()` before `ui.run()`), so its worker + blocking pools service Slint's settings-watcher connection — a current-thread runtime won't do (the event loop blocks main and never drives tokio). **Root-caused** via a standalone rfd/ashpd/zbus repro (spawned-thread picker = no panic; main-thread no-runtime connect = the exact panic; main-thread + entered runtime = dialog opens) + the live backtrace (`spawn_xdg_settings_watcher` → `Transport::connect`). Verified: the real binary now survives startup. The pickers were never the bug (their own spawned threads carry runtimes); left unchanged.
- 2026-06-16: **GUI public-share NET path built (net-only; Shares-tab UI is the attended STOP-AND-LEAVE).** The GUI net actor (`daemonseed_gui::net`) gains the full share machinery, mirroring `daemonseed-tui::net`: commands `PublishShare`/`UnpublishShare`/`RefreshShares`/`FetchShare`(A1 manifest preview)/`ConfirmFetch`(A2 selective + `flat_dest` download); events `PublishStarted`/`PublishStopped`/`PublishError`, `SharesSnapshot`/`SharesError`, `FetchManifest`/`FetchProgress`/`FetchComplete`/`FetchError` + a `ShareManifestEntry`. Publish = `ShareContent::index_dir` (1 MiB sub-file chunking) → `publish_share` RPC (server-assigned opaque `share_id`) → `serve_share` from disk (spawn_local, tracked for `UnpublishShare` abort). Fetch = derive `public_share_asset_address(share_id, server_id)` → subscribe → `ManifestRequest`→manifest → per-chunk `ChunkRequest`→`ChunkResponse` with **SHA-384 re-derivation fail-closed** (ISC-S28) → write under `fetched_root` (path-traversal-guarded, ISC-A-C32; partial files deleted on failure, ISC-A-C31). **Deliberate simplification vs the TUI:** the GUI keeps no redb share-index, so publish always hashes fresh (no `cached_or_hash`/`IndexKey`) and drops the cancel/progress events — the manifest and served bytes are byte-identical; these were TUI UI affordances. The manifest-too-large publish guard is also omitted (publish-side safety for huge shares; add when the GUI gets a publish UI). **Oracle (three ways):** a canonical share-address pin (determinism + load-bearing argument order); an in-process publish→fetch round-trip through TWO GUI actors on a shared `CotRegistry` (real `publish_share`/`serve_share`/fetch handlers, both files recovered byte-for-byte) + a refresh/unpublish coverage test; and a **`#[ignore]` live-fra1 publish→fetch round-trip that PASSED on the real relay (4.9s, byte-for-byte)**. **STOP-AND-LEAVE:** the Shares-TAB UI wiring (buttons→commands, manifest/progress rendering) is the tester-feedback surface and needs caraka's eyes — the command/event fields are bound (not `..`) in the main.rs handler so the data path is a compile-checked contract for that wiring; the unconstructed-in-bin command variants carry `#[cfg_attr(not(test), allow(dead_code))]` (the `JoinRoom` convention).
- 2026-06-16: **GUI public-share Shares-tab UI completed (the Publish overlay).** The net layer (`e1df566`) already shipped every command/event/serve-task, so this is **UI + wiring only**: (1) the **Publish overlay** (`app.slint`, mirrors the New/Join-circle overlay; opened from a new Shares-header 'Publish' button) — an optional name field + a 'Choose folder & publish' CTA + a 'Your live shares' list with per-row Unpublish; (2) `main.rs` wiring — `on_open_publish` / `on_submit_publish` (off-thread folder picker `pick_dir_and_publish`, name defaults to the folder basename) / `on_unpublish_share`; the three previously-stubbed `apply_net_event` publish arms now drive `GuiState::my_shares` (`add`/`remove`) + a status line via `apply_my_shares`; **`SharesSnapshot` now passes the unlocked `display_handle` as `my_handle`** so one's own shares render tagged "you" on the next ~3s catalog poll; (3) `state.rs` — a Slint-free `MyShare` + `my_shares()`/`add_my_share`/`remove_my_share`, unit-tested (`my_shares_add_replace_remove`). **`sharer_handle` is self-asserted** (the relay never enforces it, A-S5b) and matched against `display_handle` for the "you" tag — internally consistent by construction. **Render-bug caught + fixed during headless verify:** the overlay's outer `VerticalLayout` had `alignment: start`, which zeroed the live-shares list's `vertical-stretch` (the exact trap the Shares-tab code warns about) — the list rendered blank until `alignment: start` was dropped; re-confirmed via the `--show-publish` PNG (two fixture shares + Unpublish buttons visible).
- 2026-06-15: **GUI materialized circles show the relay-derived adj-noun label, not the `#<hex>` placeholder (ISC-C62), via a canonical core home.** Round 4 left a materialized circle named by its `#<12hex>` fingerprint until "Round 5's relay-derived label (which needs a server_id)"; the TUI already derives that label in `net.rs` from the joined circle's rendezvous address. Rather than copy the TUI's derivation into the GUI, the function is lifted to the canonical home `daemonseed_core::circle::default_circle_label(&AssetAddr)` (next to `circle::key`/`circle::message`) — **both** the TUI and the GUI now delegate (the TUI's private copy + its test removed). The GUI's net actor `CircleJoined` event gains the `asset_addr` it already computes, and a headless-testable `GuiState::set_circle_rendezvous(circle_id, asset_addr)` fills the net contract's rendezvous slot + swaps the display name to the friendly label on the `CircleJoined` handler; `GuiState::circle_fingerprint_of(idx)` keeps the fingerprint reachable on demand (Demonsaw handle-UX precedent — hash hidden, surfaced on demand), derived from the retained phrase. The hover/detail UI affordance that *surfaces* the fingerprint is attended felt-polish (the accessor carries `#[allow(dead_code)]`, data path tested). Verified headless: core determinism test + 2 GUI state tests + an offscreen PNG (`--joined-label`) showing the relay-derived label ("jolly-sneezeweed") in the rail.
- 2026-06-15: **TUI circle-phrase generator flake fixed by lifting `generate_circle_phrase` to a canonical core home (ISC-C71/C9).** The round-4 decision (below) flagged that the TUI's Ctrl-G ran a bare `generate_diceware(12)`, which draws WITH replacement so ~3% of phrases repeat a word and `estimate_circle` (distinct-word credit) scores them 121b < 128 — so Ctrl-G could emit a "generated" phrase its own ISC-C9 join gate then rejects (~3% latent flake, masked by the single-draw `ctrl_g_generates_a_phrase_that_clears_the_circle_floor` test). Rather than copy the GUI's rejection-sampling loop into the TUI (a *third* copy — exactly the duplication that let the GUI fix the bug while the TUI's copy stayed broken), the loop is lifted to `daemonseed_core::passphrase::strength::generate_circle_phrase` (+ `CIRCLE_DICEWARE_WORDS`), the canonical home next to `generate_diceware`/`estimate_circle`; **both** the GUI's `state::generate_circle_phrase` and the TUI's Ctrl-G now delegate to it (zero reimplementation). The core fn has its own 64-sample guarantee test, and the TUI floor test now runs a 64× batch so a regression to the bare generator (which would fail ~86% of a 64-batch) cannot hide behind a lucky draw.
- 2026-06-15: **GUI round-6 persistent identity — first-start + Unlock + silent circle rejoin.** `daemonseed-gui` gains an auth gate: a `screen` property selects `first-start` (enrollment wizard) / `unlock` (daily login) / `main` (shell). **Load-bearing design finding:** daemonseed's "persistent identity" is NOT a persisted connection-signing key — the TUI connects with a fresh `ClientIdentity::ephemeral()` every time (`daemonseed-tui` net.rs, D8). Persistence is a **stable display handle** (derived from the persisted mnemonic, decoupled from the ephemeral connection key) + **silent circle rejoin** from the at-rest blob. So the net change is small: `NetCommand::Connect` now carries `display_handle: Option<String>` (sets the actor's presented `my_handle`) + `rejoin_circles: Vec<(u64,String)>` (re-joined via the existing `handle_join_circle` after the lobby auto-join); connect stays ephemeral. New Slint-free `profile.rs` owns the unlocked `Seeds`+`SealingKey`+root with M13 write-through (`Seeds::add_circle` → `seal_key.seal` → `write_seeds_blob`); `GuiState` gains `set_profile`/`display_handle`/`persisted_rejoins`/`persist_circle`. The wizard reuses the core `FirstStart` state machine (`initialize` gates passphrase strength via `estimate().is_session_green()` + generates the 24-word mnemonic → `verify_round_trip` → `finalize` → `write_first_start`); **streamlined round-trip backup** (caraka's call) — the mnemonic is shown then re-typed to confirm. **Crypto-safety subtlety:** `verify_round_trip(self,…)` CONSUMES the Sealed state, so the GUI pre-checks the round-trip with a byte-identical replica of the core's `phrases_match` and only calls `verify_round_trip` when it WILL succeed — a mistyped confirm never destroys the enrollment (which would regenerate a different mnemonic). Unlock = `load_for_unlock` → `seeds::open` (wrong passphrase → `BlobError::AuthenticationFailed` → "Wrong passphrase") → `session_materials_from_unlock` (same identity hash) → restore handle + circles. Networking is deferred behind the gate: the drain timer runs at startup but `Connect` fires only on auth success (`connect_now`). Bootstrap relay is defaulted (fra1) + hidden for alpha. Auth-field autofocus deferred — `init`-focus re-enters the property graph and panics (offscreen + risks windowed); fields are click-to-focus for now. Verified headless: 25 gui tests incl. a full enroll→join→reseal→reopen→restore round-trip (same key byte-for-byte, handle survives) + a silent-rejoin-presents-stable-handle in-process relay test, 5 Read-verified auth-screen PNGs.
- 2026-06-15: **GUI round-5 circle network path — circles chat on the relay.** The round-4 materialized circles are now live. The GUI net actor gains `NetCommand::JoinCircle{circle_id, phrase}`/`SendCircle{circle_id, text}` + `NetEvent::CircleJoined`/`CircleMessage`/`CircleError` and a multi-subscription `Vec<CircleSub>` (mirroring `daemonseed_tui::net`'s `Vec<Circle>`): `handle_join_circle` verbatim-mirrors the TUI — `derive_cot_key(phrase, &CNSA_2_0)` → `cot::asset_address(cot_key, server_id.as_bytes())` (the CIRCLE path, NOT `room_asset_address`) → naming-frame → subscribe → per-circle inbound reader; `handle_send_circle` seals via `circle::message::seal_message(cot_key, &CircleMessage{…})` (AEAD-only — membership is the auth, no signature) + local echo (the relay never reflects to sender). Each circle's reader owns its own key + `circle_id`, so a frame is attributed only to the circle whose key opened it (ISC-A-C30). The GUI assigns `circle_id` at materialize (it owns circle identity, unlike the TUI where the actor hands it out) and routes inbound `CircleMessage{circle_id}` → rail index. The composer Send routes Lobby→`SendRoom` / materialized-circle→`SendCircle`; the Lobby path is otherwise unchanged. Subscriptions are session-only RAM (no persistence / re-join-on-reconnect — a later milestone; the create-before-connect edge surfaces a non-fatal CircleError). **Interop (round-3 lesson applied):** the derivation is pinned by a canonical-equality + room-distinctness test, an in-process round-trip, and a **live fra1 throwaway-circle round-trip** (two ephemeral clients, unique random phrase, sealed canary — PASS); GUI↔TUI real-relay interop + the 2-GUI human felt-test remain the pre-tag confirmation.
- 2026-06-15: **GUI round-4 circle plumbing — join / new / materialize (UI-first, materialized-but-MUTE).** `daemonseed-gui` gains the Join-a-circle + New-circle overlays (action-registry → palette + rail empty-state) and a runtime materialize path: `GuiState::materialize_from_phrase` derives the circle-of-trust key (`derive_cot_key`, `CNSA_2_0`) and stores the **Round-5 net contract** on the circle — the originating phrase + the derived `CotKey` + an `Option<AssetAddr>` rendezvous slot (`None` pre-net) — so Round 5's circle networking plugs in with no rework. `CircleState` dropped `Clone` (it now holds a zeroizing `CotKey`); `CircleNet`'s `Debug` redacts the phrase. The binary seeds **Lobby-only** (the fake demo circles retired to a `#[cfg(test)]` fixture); the composer Send on a materialized circle routes to a clearly-marked Round-5 `SendCircle` seam (local-echo stub — **no circle networking this round**). Join gates on the live `estimate_circle(...).is_circle_green()` ≥128-bit floor (ISC-C9); the materialized circle's display name is the relay-independent `circle_fingerprint` (`#<12hex>`, ISC-C62) as a placeholder until Round 5's relay-derived label. Pre-commit confirmation-card crypto copy is PLACEHOLDER-marked for caraka's security review (D6). **ISC-C71 inaccuracy found + fixed (affects the TUI too):** ISC-C71 asserts a generated phrase "passes the same on-`Enter` join gate as a hand-typed one" — but `generate_diceware(12)` draws WITH replacement, so ~3% of 12-word phrases repeat a word and `estimate_circle` (distinct-word credit) scores them 121b < 128, so the gate would reject them (real entropy is 132b regardless). The GUI's `state::generate_circle_phrase()` rejection-samples until green; **the TUI's `App::on_key_join` Ctrl-G + its `ctrl_g_generates_a_phrase_that_clears_the_circle_floor` test carry the same latent ~3% flake** and should adopt the same fix. Verified headless (16 gui tests, 3 Read-verified PNGs, `--self-check`).
- 2026-06-15: **GUI-alpha foundation smoke passed + two felt-test fixes.** A 2-client live felt-test on the fra1 relay confirmed real public-Lobby chat (send + receive) end-to-end through the GUI. Two fixes landed from it: (1) **tab clicks** — `TabLabel` inherited `VerticalLayout`, so its `TouchArea` became a zero-height layout child that never covered the label and tab clicks silently missed (the rail worked because `RailRow` inherits `Rectangle`, where a child `TouchArea` fills the parent); `TabLabel` now inherits `TouchArea`. (2) **`DAEMONSEED_X11=1` / `--x11` opt-in** unsets `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` before backend init so winit uses X11/XWayland — winit 0.30's Wayland pointer path drops button events under VM software rendering; **native Wayland stays the default** (opt-in only), no-op on non-Linux hosts.
- 2026-06-15: **GUI core wiring — public Lobby room on the live relay (Slint↔tokio net-actor).** The attended round-2 half. A Slint-free `daemonseed_gui::net` actor (a dedicated thread running a `current_thread` tokio runtime + `LocalSet` holding the live `AppSession`, mirroring `daemonseed-tui::net`) connects via `connect_session` with an ephemeral `ClientIdentity` (exactly as the TUI connects), auto-joins `public_room::DEFAULT_ROOM`, and runs real public-room chat: inbound frames `open_room_message`→decrypt→render; the composer `seal_room_message`→publish, plus a local echo (the relay never reflects a sender's own frame). UI↔net is `tokio::mpsc` `NetCommand`/`NetEvent`; the UI thread NEVER blocks — events drain on a capped `slint::Timer` `try_recv`, commands are fire-and-forget. `daemonseed-gui` now depends on core/cli/proto/tokio. **Interop insurance:** the GUI's lobby rendezvous derivation is verbatim-mirrored from TUI `net.rs:1277` (`room_asset_address(&room_key, server_id.as_bytes())` over `DEFAULT_ROOM`) with a cross-derivation equality test, because both round-trip tests are GUI-to-GUI and a wrong-but-symmetric derivation would pass them. Verified: equality + an in-process-relay round-trip through the actor (duplex + `CotRegistry`) + a **live fra1 round-trip** (two ephemeral clients, a unique throwaway room, sealed canary, ~3s). Connection identity is ephemeral (regenerated per launch). **Honest gap:** GUI↔TUI interop on the *real* public lobby is proven only by a human felt-test (both automated tests are GUI-to-GUI).
- 2026-06-15: **GUI round-2 mechanical slice — interactive rail + RAM-only per-circle state.** The round-1 static shell became interactive at the mechanical level: clicking a rail row switches the active circle through a Rust callback (`switch-circle(int)`), backed by a Slint-free, unit-tested `state` module (`daemonseed_gui::state`) holding per-circle RAM-only `{messages, draft, scroll_y}` — persisted-on-leave, restored-on-return, reset on relaunch (the brief's Telegram-grade state-preserving switch). The composer is now a real `TextInput` and the transcript a `Flickable`, both feeding that state; tab clicks and a mouse-opened command palette ("Go to Lobby" drives a real switch) wire the action registry to a second surface. **Rust is the sole writer of `active`** (the rail click only invokes the callback), so the out-of-range guard is real rather than theater. Verified headless: 5 `state` unit tests (retention / independence / range-guard) + a perf-budget test + a `--self-check` live retention round-trip through the real callback + two offscreen PNGs that visibly swap circle. **Still mechanics-only: NO `daemonseed-core` / network / async** — the live net-actor wiring (the Slint↔tokio interop risk) and felt polish are the *attended* round-2 half, deferred to a maintainer session. No new repo ISCs (the GUI surface is still churny; detailed GUI criteria stay out of this frozen contract per the round-1 decision).
 - 2026-06-14: **GUI round-1 scaffold landed — `daemonseed-gui` crate (Slint, software-renderer shell).** The perf gate cleared (throwaway spike: state-preserving switch ~1.6ms, 220ms easing ~1.4ms/frame on the software renderer), so the proven spike was promoted into a real AGPL workspace crate: the three-zone shell as structured components + the alpha tab nav (Chat / Shares / a placeholder "coming soon" circle-shares tab) + a command-palette overlay from a Rust-side action registry; stub in-memory data, no `daemonseed-core` wiring yet. Base build is software-renderer-only so it compiles + offscreen-renders (`--screenshot`) from a headless terminal; the `desktop` feature forwards `slint/backend-winit` for a real window (felt-tested OK on the Wayland head). **slint pinned ~1.13 (resolves 1.14.1), not 1.15** — ratatui 0.29 (the frozen TUI) exact-pins `unicode-width =0.2.0`, which slint 1.15's compiler (annotate-snippets 0.12, needs ≥0.2.2) cannot satisfy; slint ≤1.14 uses the older annotate-snippets and coexists (chosen over bumping the frozen TUI). Fonts are embedded at compile time (`EmbedForSoftwareRenderer`) since the software renderer has no system-font access.
- 2026-06-13: **GUI repo topology — one workspace now, split-on-trigger (companion to the ISC-C6 Slint lock).** Question: separate the GUI(s) into their own repo(s) to keep the base API clean and make room for community GUIs. Finding: the separation already exists at the *crate* boundary — `daemonseed-proto` (the versioned gRPC/protobuf contract, `daemonseed/v1/`) + `daemonseed-core` (framework-agnostic client logic) ARE the base API; `daemonseed-tui` is already just a view binary, and Slint joins as a sibling `daemonseed-gui` binary. **What enables community GUIs is the contract, not the repo split:** `daemonseed-proto` is gRPC → language-agnostic (a web/Flutter/Electron GUI in any language can bind it), and it is the deliberately permissive (Apache/MIT) surface while `daemonseed-core` is AGPL — so a non-Rust or differently-licensed community GUI binds the permissive protocol, never the AGPL internals. **Decision:** keep the single workspace for now (atomic protocol+core+GUI changes in one CI/version bump, no cross-repo version-pinning tax while solo); the clean crate boundaries make a later split a `git filter-repo` + crates.io dependency, never a refactor. **Split a GUI to its own repo only on a concrete trigger:** (1) a real community GUI wants its own home/governance; (2) Slint's heavy Android/iOS CI starts dragging core's CI; (3) a GUI needs a divergent license or release cadence. **Enabling move now:** treat `daemonseed-proto` + `daemonseed-core` as a published, versioned, documented public contract (crates.io + the LAMA API manifest as resolver) and have the first-party Slint GUI consume the same published surface a community dev would — dogfooding the external API proves it is buildable-against.
- 2026-06-13: **GUI framework LOCKED — Slint (ISC-C6), pending an on-device spike gate.** A freshness review of the Rust GUI landscape narrowed the field to Slint, Dioxus, and Makepad/Robius. **Dioxus eliminated:** webview-default mobile ("quite experimental") plus an admitted gap — no mobile permission API (open issue #3870) — exactly where the eventual in-app VoIP feature needs one. **Slint chosen over Makepad/Robius** on maturity and risk: officially the only Rust toolkit with Android support (iOS tech-preview), real docs/funding, clean license. The reasons we had repeatedly backed off Slint were both stale — its royalty applies **only to embedded** (daemonseed is desktop+mobile → royalty-free; GPLv3 also available, AGPLv3-compatible), and its mobile story is now the strongest of the three, not the weakest. **VoIP plumbing de-risked:** runtime mic-permission / native-service access works via `ndk-context` (Slint's `android-activity` backend sets the JavaVM+Activity, `robius-android-env` or a direct JNI call reads it) — no Slint-specific glue, and Robius's `robius-location`/`robius-authentication` are working permission-gated precedents, so Robius crates are nice-to-have ergonomics rather than required. **This is a docs/API-level green light, not an on-device run.** Going all-in is gated on a compiled spike that prompts for and obtains mic permission on a physical device. **Android** is reachable from the Linux dev host once the SDK/NDK is installed (an Android device is on hand); **iOS cannot be built on Linux** (needs macOS + Xcode), so the iOS leg is delegated to a Mac-equipped contributor before any iOS release — it does not block the desktop or Android path. The terminal client is frozen as a protocol/test harness from here, not grown as a product surface (the TUI→Slint-desktop crossover: any milestone whose core deliverable is interaction/visual, VoIP, or mobile-bound is built in Slint, since that UI code transfers to mobile and TUI UI code transfers to nothing).
- 2026-06-13: **M16 post-smoke — chosen-dest download layout = "the selection becomes the dest root" + tilde expansion (ISC-C68).** First live smoke of selective fetch to a user-chosen dest showed the file buried: a single picked song landed at `<dest>/<share>/Artist/Album/song`, recreating the entire share-internal tree under a dest the user had explicitly chosen. **Decision (caraka, confirmed on smoke):** a chosen dest receives the selection rebased to its own root — one file → its basename directly under the dest; a folder/multi-file selection keeps the *deepest directory common to the selection* as the top entry (drop ancestors, preserve structure below), so `Artist/Album` → `<dest>/Album/...`. Implemented as a pure `core::storage::fetched::rebase_to_selection_root` (5 unit tests: single/folder/disjoint/no-prefix/empty) consumed by `net::handle_confirm_fetch` under a new `ConfirmFetch.flat_dest` flag; a chosen dest writes flat (no per-share folder) and gets no `downloads.idx` littered into the user's directory (the browse pane tracks only the managed downloads dir). The per-file traversal guard still validates the rebased path (rebasing only drops leading components). **Default (unchosen) downloads dir is unchanged** — still namespaced `<share>/<rel_path>` with the browse manifest. Folded in: a leading `~`/`~/` in the typed dest now expands to `$HOME` (`main::expand_tilde`) — the dest box is a text field, not a shell, so `~/x` had been creating a literal `~` directory. Verified live (caraka, 2026-06-13): single-file and album-folder both land as expected; tilde resolves.
- 2026-06-13: **M16 post-smoke — manifest-wait inactivity timeout (dead-share fetch no longer hangs).** Fetching a share whose sharer is offline hung indefinitely: the M16 30s frame-inactivity timeout covered only the chunk phase, not the **manifest-wait** phase (`open_share_stream`), so a reaped/offline share — which never answers the `ManifestRequest` — left the fetch waiting on a manual `Esc`. Wrapped the manifest read in the same `remaining_inactivity_budget` timeout the chunk loop uses, with a manifest-specific message (`FETCH_MANIFEST_TIMEOUT_MESSAGE` = "timed out waiting for the share — it may be offline; press r to refresh the list"). Found live during the v0.20.0 smoke (caraka). **Design follow-ups deferred to M17** (NOT done here): reap-on-*fetcher*-timeout is rejected as gameable (a flaky/malicious fetcher could DoS a live share) — zombie reaping belongs on a *sharer* heartbeat / relay idle-timeout, since clean disconnect already reaps (ShareReapGuard); and listing auto-refresh (re-validate-on-fetch + focus-scoped poll, relay-push long-term) so a stale client view self-heals. The blind CoT relay accepting a subscribe to a reaped asset (hence the silent wait) is the root the registry-aware-subscribe option would close.
- 2026-06-13: **Publish-intent persistence — remembered-published shares auto-republish on Unlock (caraka).** Publishing was session-scoped (ISC-S20 "online to share"): on restart you re-`[p]` each share. caraka asked the client to remember published shares and re-assert them automatically (motivation: many shares + a future autostart + anon-id sharing). **Decision:** persist a `publish <hex(root)>` directive in the encrypted seeds blob (`Seeds::{add_published,remove_published,published}`) — set on `PublishStarted`, cleared on `PublishStopped` — and on Unlock queue each remembered-published root for auto-republish, **drained only once connected+authed** (`App::take_pending_autopublish`, gated on `ConnectionStatus::Connected`; publish needs a live session). **Ephemerality is unchanged (key point):** this persists publish *intent*, not relay state — the relay stays RAM-only and reaps on disconnect (ISC-S20); the client merely re-asserts each launch, so each republish is a fresh, unlinkable `share_id`. **Stable share_ids explicitly REJECTED (caraka):** a stable id would be persistent correlatable metadata — a third long-lived identifier beyond pubkey + handle — linking content across sessions, against the ephemerality posture; discovery already rides `ListPublicShares` (keyed on identity), not a memorized id, and durable/repeat sharing rides the circle/seed-key channel. So rotating ids per publish are the *more* private behaviour, not a limitation. **Deferred:** the on-**boot** autostart variant needs auto-unlock (no human at boot) — out of scope here; this is the manual-Unlock slice. **Known edge:** `[u]` on a share whose auto-republish is still queued (the brief pre-connection window) does not yet dequeue it — smoke item, low-risk (the queue drains within a tick of connecting).
- 2026-06-13: **M16 post-smoke — `reconcile_into` replaces clear-then-rescan in the DefineShare path (completes the re-index fix).** The `scan_into`-preserve change below was necessary but NOT sufficient: `handle_define_share` ran `index.clear()` (drops the whole `share-index-v3` table) *before* the scan on every DefineShare — including the Unlock re-emit — so on restart the cache was wiped before the preserve-logic could run, then repopulated with `chunk_addrs: None`. The clear existed for "root changed wholesale." Replaced with `indexer::reconcile_into` (scan + prune only entries whose file no longer exists under the root): an Unlock re-define of the *same* root keeps the cached chunk addresses (no full re-hash on relaunch) while a genuine root change still drops every stale entry (none exist under the new root). A cancelled scan skips the prune (partial seen-set). +2 tests (preserve+prune same root; drop old entries on new root). Found because the `scan_into`-only fix did not change live behavior — I shipped the preserve fix without tracing that the define path clears first or live-testing the relaunch; corrected here.
- 2026-06-13: **M16 post-smoke — `scan_into` preserves cached chunk addresses across a metadata rescan (re-index-every-relaunch fix).** caraka hit a full re-hash of his Music share on every relaunch despite a stable profile, passphrase, and persistent redb. Root cause: the DefineShare-on-Unlock metadata rescan (`indexer::scan_into`) unconditionally upserted every file with `chunk_addrs: None`, clobbering the addresses a prior publish hash pass had cached — so the next `[p]` re-hashed the whole share. (Cross-launch only; in-session republish was warm because no rescan intervened.) **Fix:** `scan_into` now looks up the existing entry and carries `chunk_addrs` forward when `size + mtime` are unchanged, resetting to `None` only for new/changed files (mirrors `apply_event` and `mtime_rescan` — a stale address must never survive a content change). +2 tests (unchanged file keeps its blob; changed file drops it). Not the binary signature — nothing keys the cache to the build; any relaunch triggered it. The latent bug predates M16 (tiny M14 dogfood files hid the re-hash cost).
- 2026-06-05: **M15 — real ≥128-bit circle-entropy gate (resolves the M14 "carried, not yet ratified" item).** caraka ratified the replacement estimator: `passphrase::strength::estimate_circle` combines two independent honest models so EITHER path reaches green with **no format forced** — each DISTINCT BIP-39 word = log2(2048)=11 bits, plus a charset model on the non-wordlist residue (`len × log2(distinct_chars)`); a word credited at 11 bits is NOT also double-counted character-by-character. Because the additive sum over-credits periodic/sequential structure (`abcabc…`), zxcvbn is retained ONLY as a low-end anti-pattern veto (score < 2 ⇒ blocked) — its 2^64 ceiling is irrelevant here because the ≥128 magnitude comes from the key-space sum, not from zxcvbn. The circle-join gate now uses the real `CircleStrength::is_circle_green` (≥128), replacing the M14 interim score-4 proxy that wrongly accepted the public xkcd phrase; the join meter is bits-driven (`bits/128`, red<60 / yellow / green≥128) so the user "keeps adding until green" like the first-start passphrase meter. `Strength::is_circle_green` / `meets_circle_interim_floor` removed. **Acknowledged softness (caraka chose Option 2 over a diceware-only floor):** the charset model treats typed-but-non-random text as random; the zxcvbn veto closes the cheap-pattern hole, but a long *ordered* full-alphabet residue is the residual over-credit — accepted for the gate.
- 2026-06-05: **M15 D — TUI publish + serve + unpublish.** The net actor gains `NetCommand::PublishShare{root,name}` / `UnpublishShare{share_id}`: publish indexes the directory (`ShareContent::index_dir`, fails fast on a bad path), publishes the listing (`PublishShare` → server-assigned `share_id`, F25), then serves content by spawning `serve_share` as a `spawn_local` task (same pattern as the inbound circle readers) tracked by an abort handle. **Serve model (caraka-blessed):** "you must be online to share" preserved — the relay reaps the RAM-only share when the session drops (ISC-S20); explicit unpublish aborts the serve task and sends `UnpublishShare` (owner-scoped, ISC-A-S1). `AppSession` is `Clone` (Arc-backed channel), so the serve task owns a clone and the command loop is NOT restructured. UX: `MainFocus::Publish` input box (path|name; name defaults to the directory name) inserted after DefineShare in the Tab cycle; `[u]` in the Shares pane unpublishes the most-recent (serving is session-scoped). **MVP scope:** publish targets a typed path (decoupled from the M14 defined-share redb index, which serves the local browse view); per-share unpublish-by-selection is a documented follow-up.
- 2026-06-06: **M15 cleanup — publish via `[p]` on the defined share; the standalone Publish input pane removed.** The smoke test showed the separate Publish input pane (a second directory-path box, adjacent to DefineShare) made users type the share path twice with no indication the two panes relate. **Decision (caraka, 2026-06-06):** align with the original D PRD — define a share once (DefineShare), then publish it with `[p]` in the Shares pane (`[u]` unpublishes); no re-typing. `MainFocus::Publish`, `on_key_publish`, and the `publish_input` buffer are removed; the app retains the active defined root (`active_defined_share`, set on define and on Unlock-restore) which `[p]` publishes. The Tab cycle loses the Publish slot (Shares → DefineShare → Hide → …). This subsumes the "publish focus-bounce" wart (that pane no longer exists).
- 2026-06-06: **M15 cleanup — fetched downloads land as named files; reverses the C CAS design.** The pre-merge smoke test showed the original C design (chunks → `<profile>/fetched/cas` `FileChunkStore` + a manual extract step) leaks implementation to the user: a `cas/` folder, hex-named chunk files, stripped extensions, `<hex>.rc` refcount sidecars, and all shares mingled by content-addressed dedup. **Decision (caraka, 2026-06-06):** a fetcher's explicit downloads should be *named files in a folder*, not a content store. `core::storage::fetched` now writes each verified file **directly** to `<downloads-root>/<share-name>/<rel_path>` under its real name — no CAS, no `.rc`, no extract step; the Downloads pane is read-only (browse + show the folder). Downloads root = `<profile>/downloads` under `--portable`, else the OS Downloads dir namespaced under `daemonseed/` (`$XDG_DOWNLOAD_DIR`/`$HOME/Downloads`; a richer per-platform resolver is ShareUX/M16). Path-traversal safety extended to the share-name folder (ISC-A-C32). `NetCommand::ExtractShare` + `NetEvent::ExtractComplete`/`ExtractError` removed; the extract UI removed. The CAS earns its keep on the *serve* side (dedup/integrity) but is over-engineering for the fetch side. ISC-C63/C64/C65 revised to the named-files model. **Deferred to ShareUX/M16:** pre-fetch manifest preview (names+sizes), selective/per-file fetch, a user-chosen download directory.
- 2026-06-05: **M15 E — xtask live ISC-coverage; single-sourced TOTAL/COVERED via a leaf crate.** The ISC registry (`IscClass`, `ISCS`, `TOTAL`, `COVERED`, `Coverage`) moved into a new zero-dependency leaf crate `daemonseed-isc`; `daemonseed-integration-tests::isc_coverage` is now a thin `pub use daemonseed_isc::*;` re-export (every `m*_isc_coverage` test unchanged), and `xtask isc-coverage` depends on the leaf crate and reads `daemonseed_isc::{TOTAL, COVERED}` LIVE — the hand-maintained `TOTAL_ISCS` / `COVERED_ISCS` mirrors in xtask (which had silently drifted) are deleted. **Honesty reconciliation:** `ISCS`/`TOTAL` was reconciled to the repo `ISA.md` `## Criteria` for the BUILT, non-deferred surface — the 11 built ISCs M13/M15-C had added to the ISA but never to the registry (C59-C62, A-C29/A-C30, C63-C65, A-C31/A-C32) are now tracked, so `TOTAL` 122→133 and coverage reports the true denominator (100/133 = 75.2%, was a flattering 100/122). The deferred DM family (C38-C46 / A-C20-A-C25) and vacant C5 stay EXCLUDED by design (not built). **COVERED single-sourcing + drift guards:** `COVERED=100` is the distinct integration-*registered* count (the `m*_isc_coverage` test slices); `MILESTONE_COVERED` records the per-milestone provenance and a `covered_sum_matches` test asserts its sum == COVERED, plus a compile-time `COVERED <= TOTAL` assert. **Known gap (made honest, not hidden):** the 11 newly-tracked built ISCs have UNIT coverage (core/tui) but no integration `register()` call yet, so they raise TOTAL without raising COVERED — adding their integration coverage is a tracked follow-up; the number is now truthful rather than flattering. The fully-live numerator (xtask running the suite to tally `register` calls) remains the deferred end-state; `covered_sum_matches` keeps the static COVERED honest until then.
- 2026-06-05: **M15 C — fetched content → on-disk CAS + browse/extract (ISC-C63 / C64 / C65, ISC-A-C31 / A-C32).** A fully-verified fetch (which until now dropped its bytes after accounting) now persists to `<profile-root>/fetched/`: a `FileChunkStore` at `fetched/cas` holds each file's bytes, and a hand-rolled line-based manifest at `fetched/shares.idx` records `share_id → name → [(rel_path, chunk_addr, size)]`. New `daemonseed-core::storage::fetched::{FetchedStore, FetchedShare, VerifiedFile}`; net actor threads `name` + `fetched_root` into `FetchShare`, accumulates each verified file's bytes, and persists once after the whole fetch verifies (`NetCommand::ListFetched` / `ExtractShare`; `NetEvent::FetchedShares` / `ExtractComplete` / `ExtractError`); a `MainFocus::Fetched` browse pane (end of the Tab cycle) lists downloads and extracts a selected one to a typed dir. **At-rest posture (caraka-ratified 2026-06-05):** fetched content + manifest persist as **plaintext explicit downloads** — downloaded files are user-chosen artifacts the user fetched to open, NOT message/post/session history, so the no-client-history invariant (ISC-A-C1) holds; the larger seized-blob surface is acknowledged and is the R-PANIC erasure target. Manifest encryption was rejected as theatre while the chunk bytes sit beside it in plaintext; a future at-rest folder-encryption feature wraps the whole `fetched/` tree (additive, post-MVP). **Security:** extraction is path-traversal-safe (ISC-A-C32 — absolute / `..` / prefix rel_paths refused, validated before any chunk read); a fetch that fails verification persists nothing (ISC-A-C31 — no partial/poisoned downloads). **MVP scope:** one-chunk-per-file (inherited); re-fetching a share replaces its manifest record but leaves prior chunks in the CAS (refcount-correct eviction on replace is a documented follow-up, harmless because chunks are content-addressed); persistence/extract run inline (download-sized, local-disk) — `spawn_blocking` for very large extracts is a follow-up.
- 2026-06-05: **M14 share-management surface — share-index key as a sibling of the at-rest key; single active index for MVP (caraka-reviewed crypto).** The TUI gains a Define-Share input box; defined roots are indexed by the net actor and persisted. **Crypto/trust-boundary call (caraka reviewed the diff before commit):** the share-index key (ISC-C3 / A-C6) is derived as a *sibling* of the at-rest key from the *one* Argon2id run — `derive_session_keys` expands the single high-entropy intermediate twice under distinct HKDF `info` (`at-rest` vs `share-index`), so the at-rest key output is byte-identical (existing blobs open unchanged) and the index key costs no second Argon2id (Pi-4 floor). `IndexKey` is a zeroizing newtype carried on `Opened` + `SessionMaterials`, threaded through first-start and unlock. **Forks locked (caraka):** (1) share UX = a JoinCircle-style input box with an example-path hint; (3) fingerprint-only, rename deferred to the GUI era; (4) block-below-floor on circle join (precautionary). **MVP scope pin:** a single active share index (latest `DefineShare` wins); multi-root concurrent indexing (one index per root) is a documented follow-up; the actor opens the index off-thread and cold-scans on a dedicated blocking thread (redb MVCC) so the command loop never parks (ISC-A-C7). **Persistence (ISC-C21):** a `share <hex(root)> <hex(label)>` directive mirrors the `circle` directive; defining write-throughs the root, and Unlock re-emits one `DefineShare` per persisted root via a queue that re-indexes WITHOUT re-persisting. **Carried, not yet ratified:** zxcvbn saturates at 2^64 so the literal ISC-C9 ≥128-bit floor is unmeasurable; the join gate uses an interim score-4 proxy. The real estimator (key-space / word-count) + restoring a true ≥128 check await caraka's threshold ratification — deliberately NOT recorded as an ISC-C9 change here until then.

- 2026-06-05: **M13 persistence keystone — remember-all (A) over per-circle opt-in (B) (caraka).** The
  at-rest blob now persists display name (ISC-C4b), mute (ISC-C15), hide (ISC-C16), and **circle
  membership** (ISC-C59: entropy + label) via a session write-through, so a daily-login Unlock stops
  forgetting them. The trust-boundary fork was whether to persist *every* joined circle's entropy by
  default (A — friction-free returning daemons, larger seized-blob surface) or only user-pinned circles
  (B — precautionary, but default still "forgets"). caraka chose **A for alpha** ("see if the daemons
  share my paranoia; we can add B if users request it"), explicitly accepting the larger at-rest surface;
  B (a per-circle "ephemeral" opt-out) stays a fast-follow. This revises **ISC-C59** (membership is now
  persisted, not session-scoped) and clarifies **ISC-A-C2** (entropy persists in the blob but stays
  non-mnemonic-derivable, so mnemonic-only recovery never reconstructs the social graph). **Backward
  compat waived during alpha (caraka)** — no blob-format migration; new `name`/`circle` directive lines
  are additive and existing testers re-enroll if needed. **Design pins:** circle entropy + label stored
  as hex-encoded directive lines (space/newline-safe); a cached `SealingKey` re-seals on each mutation
  with no second Argon2id (Pi-4 floor would stall ~1-2s otherwise); `CircleJoined` echoes the entropy
  in-process so rejoin pairing needs no fragile dispatch-ordering; persisted label (ISC-C62) wins over the
  actor-supplied label on rejoin.

- 2026-06-03: **Step-6 scope decision — surface introducer-discovered candidates in the TUI Servers
  pane (caraka).** Chosen over "session-level test is enough" / a `cli introducer` subcommand: the
  Servers pane gains a refresh action (`NetCommand::RefreshIntroducer` → `session.refresh_introducer` →
  `NetEvent::IntroducerSnapshot`) and renders each candidate as `server-id  address` (no key bytes,
  ISC-S6/A-C19), with promotion staying the explicit user action.
- 2026-06-01: **`PublishShare` — RAM-only, connection-reaped, owner-scoped, blind-relay.**
  M12 has no dedicated `ISC-38`; the user-publish wire surface was designed against the existing ISCs.
  Forced choices: **RAM-only** (ISC-A-S1 — a published share is user data, not an operator carve-out, so it
  is never persisted; it lives in process memory and is **reaped on disconnect**, mirroring the CoT live
  relay's model); **server-assigned opaque `share_id`** (the M6 `PublicShareListing` doc already specifies
  "opaque server-scoped identifier"); **self-asserted `sharer_handle`** relayed verbatim and **not bound to
  the connection's authenticated identity** (M6 doc + ISC-A-S5b — the relay is a blind forwarder, never
  polices); the RPCs extend the existing **`PublicSpace`** service (additive). Decision (caraka,
  2026-06-01): **`PublishShare` + `UnpublishShare`** (not publish-only) so a daemon can stop sharing one
  folder without dropping the connection. `UnpublishShare` is **owner-scoped** — an unknown or other-owned
  `share_id` is a silent no-op, so the relay never reveals another connection's share ownership (ISC-A-S1).
  Implementation: `daemonseed-server::share::SharePublishRegistry` (RAM-only, per-connection owner token) +
  `ShareReapGuard` (reaps on connection close, every exit path incl. panic); `ListPublicShares` now serves
  the live registry. Additive SemVer **MINOR** (wire version stays 1.0).
- 2026-06-01: **Introducer refresh is precautionary — discovered peers are candidates the user promotes,
  never auto-trusted (ISC-C22 / ISC-A-C19).** The spec settled the *trust semantics* of an
  introducer-discovered server ("treated identically to manual entry — never auto-trusted from the
  introduction", ISC-C22) but left the *refresh behaviour* open: when the client calls a relay's
  introducer, does it auto-add every discovered peer to the active trust set, or surface them as
  candidates? **Decision (caraka, 2026-06-01): candidates.** `daemonseed-core::federation::discovered`
  holds a RAM-only `DiscoveredPeers` cache, structurally distinct from the `TrustStore`; `merge()`
  records candidates and reads `known` only to skip already-configured servers — it **never writes the
  trust set**. `promote_trusted` / `promote_untrusted` are the explicit user actions that move a
  candidate into the active set (the key is then established by first-contact hash verification or an
  out-of-band key, identical to a manual paste). Rationale: auto-adding would let a relay you connect to
  populate your active server list with addresses it controls and silently get its own key TOFU-pinned —
  exactly the transitive-trust hole ISC-S6's no-keys invariant and ISC-A-C19's anti-auto-discovery bias
  exist to close. The precautionary option keeps discovery and trust strictly separated at every point in
  the client lifecycle, not just at first-start.
- 2026-06-01: **Clean-device recovery (ISC-C29 recover branch / C30 / C32 / C36 /
  A-C2).** Recovery is the mirror of cold first-start: `FirstStart::<Welcome>::recover(mnemonic, passphrase,
  argon)` accepts a phrase the user already holds instead of generating one, and lands directly in
  `BackupVerified` — backup is verified-by-possession, so the C33/C34 backup-verify steps are skipped —
  rejoining the shared `finalize` / `into_session_materials` tail. The TUI exposes **both** input paths
  (caraka's call 2026-06-01): typed 24 words, or an `identity.dseed` file decrypted with the passphrase;
  both converge on one phrase, so the core flow is source-agnostic. The recovered device mints a **fresh
  local `profile_id`** (C36) and re-seals its own at-rest blob + `.dseed` under it, yet the identity is
  **byte-identical** to the source device: the ML-DSA-87 / ML-KEM-1024 keys and handle derive from the
  mnemonic alone via fixed HKDF `info` strings, independent of `profile_id`. This is the load-bearing fact
  — recovery is identity-from-mnemonic, and the profile is just local scaffolding around it. Circle-of-trust
  seeds are **not** recovered (A-C2: user-chosen entropy not in the mnemonic), surfaced in the recover UI
  copy. Failure paths fail closed and stay on-step (bad BIP-39 checksum, wrong `.dseed` passphrase, weak new
  passphrase); a fresh `Welcome` per recover attempt means a wrong passphrase costs only a retry, never the
  session. The cold-enrollment path is byte-identical to before — gate steps 1/2/4 unaffected. The gate test
  `fresh_daemon_recovers_identity_from_mnemonic` models real device loss: daemon A enrolls → chats → is
  dropped → a fresh daemon B recovers from A's captured mnemonic → B's own `#<12hex>` floor handle must equal
  A's (identity observed via the floor-handle self-echo, comparable across devices because both pick the
  floor handle).
- 2026-05-30: **Introducer was specced but never socket-wired in M5 — finishing it adds protocol
  surface.** Investigation found the federation introducer was never wired to the socket in M5. The
  `IntroducerQuery` / `IntroducerResponse` messages, the `introducer_response()` builder, and operator
  peer-config all shipped — but there is **no endpoint**: no gRPC RPC and no post-auth frame handler. The
  entire post-Authenticated surface is tonic gRPC (`PublicSpace` + `CircleOfTrust`); `IntroducerQuery` is
  referenced only inside `federation.rs` (builder + its unit tests), never read off a socket. So gate
  step 6 (introducer refresh) was never "client-only over already-shipped server APIs" as the 2026-05-29
  Path-2 scope assumed — completing it necessarily adds protocol surface.
- 2026-05-30: **Step-7 deprecation surface — version-gated trust-event emission (ISC-C25 / C28).** The
  warning *panel* (`DeprecationWarningRow` rows) is the always-on persistent non-blocking surface: an
  idempotent snapshot-replace, never an append, so a re-fetch can't duplicate or resurrect rows. Separately,
  *trust events* (the C28 taxonomy) are emitted from the net actor only on a meaningful transition — a
  `policy_version` bump (gated on `PolicyCache::cached_version` read *before* the cache insert) **or** a
  blocking cutoff-hit — so a passively re-fetched, already-dismissed warning never resurrects while a real
  escalation always breaks through. A relay that *withdraws* a previously-served policy (serves `None` after
  we held a version) is treated as a rollback (`ServerDeprecationPolicyRollback`), not a benign empty state,
  closing the strip-the-policy downgrade hole. A missing/short pinned key and any signature failure fail
  closed to `ServerDeprecationPolicyUnreadable` — an unverifiable policy is never accepted (ISC-A-C9). The
  decision logic lives in a pure `decide_deprecation(prev_version, artifact, server_pubkey, in_use, now)`
  so every branch (rollback, withdrawal, missing-key, version-gating, escalation) is unit-tested without a
  live session; the surface reuses core's `assess_deprecation` bridge rather than re-deriving the
  pending-vs-cutoff mapping. The single-relay alpha collapses the C25 `(suite_id, server-id)` dismissal
  scope to per-server, which is **safe only because** `assess_deprecation` filters the policy against the
  one-element in-use set `&[CNSA_2_0.id]` — at most one warning can ever surface, so a dismissal cannot
  swallow a sibling-suite deprecation. Adding a second in-use suite **requires** restoring the per-suite
  dismissal key before that collapse is removed. **Known limitations (coupled to the deferred client
  profile-persistence path, not independent):** the `PolicyCache` is in-memory only, so the anti-rollback
  and withdrawal-as-rollback guarantees hold **within a session only** — a restart wipes the cached version,
  after which a server that silently withdraws a previously-committed deprecation reads as a fresh clean
  state rather than a rollback. The one-hour TTL is likewise not cross-restart enforced (the actor refetches
  on every user action, stricter within-session). Multi-server earliest-cutoff governance is also post-MVP.
  The unverifiable/unreadable path is deliberately **visible and non-destructive**:
  `ServerDeprecationPolicyUnreadable` is PersistentNonBlocking (a status badge, never a silent no-op) and
  flows through the same `DeprecationError` path that keeps the last good cached warning, so an attacker
  forcing the unreadable branch cannot erase a previously-surfaced pending deprecation.
- 2026-05-29: ISC numbering is permanent — never renumber or reuse; vacated IDs (e.g. `ISC-C5`) stay
  reserved; withdrawn ISCs get a `DEPRECATED` marker plus a successor pointer. Commit/PR bodies cite ISC IDs
  only when a boundary is strengthened, deployed, or altered — never as decoration.
- 2026-05-29: **DM family folded into the main client line from conception.** The direct-messaging ISCs
  were drafted under a `ISC-C-DM*` / `A-C-DM*` namespace; they are renumbered once, here, into the
  main client line (C38–C46 / A-C20–A-C25) **before** this ISA's first commit makes any ID citable.
  Rationale: it follows the project's own first-start precedent (ISC-C29–C37 folded the same way), and
  deferral is better signalled by Out of Scope + a subheading than by an ID namespace. This is the last
  pre-citation renumber; the never-renumber rule binds from the promotion PR forward.
- 2026-06-07: **ISC-C57 is public-room-only by design; intra-circle sender authentication is a separate,
  deferred concern.** Implementing C57 (M16 B1) surfaced that `wire::PublicRoomMessage` carries
